- **Selection rules are checked twice:** a pure function (`selectionProblem`) before writing, so the admin gets the exact reason; and one guard statement in the batch (`requireSelectionRules`: active count within 1..30 and at least `min_select`; defaults within `max_select`), so a change landing in between can't leave a group impossible to order from. When that guard fails without a version change, the message asks to reload. The table also has a CHECK on `min_select` / `max_select`.
- **Limits** [Choice]: 1–30 active add-ons per group; add-on prices 0 to $100 in whole cents (a typo guard, like `MAX_PRICE_MINOR` for items). Easy to change.
- **Pre-selected add-ons:** moving the default in a "choose 1" group is two changes (unset, then set), because both steps are checked against the maximum. The admin screen can do both in one click later if needed.
- **Deferred to 3.5:** "used by N items" and per-item overrides of rules and prices.
- **Tests:** 17, including five forced races. All 11 guards fail a test when removed (the defaults guard needed its own race test, added).

### D60: Menu items and the price grid (step 3.5a), 2026-09-27

- **Split:** step 3.5 became 3.5a (items, option sets and the price grid, states, order) and 3.5b (add-on groups on items with per-item overrides), to keep each review reasonable.
- **The whole grid, every time:** a save sends every combination of the chosen option sets' active values exactly once; a missing one is refused with how many are missing. A grid is never half-saved, and a value added to a set later shows up as missing on each item until it's priced or switched off (data-model.md → Rules).
- **Stable variation ids by combination:** `combination_key` (sorted value ids, unique per item) matches submitted cells to existing variations, so prices change in place and ids survive for orders and sold-out switches. A third status, `retired`, marks variations whose option set left the item (out of the grid, kept for history; revived if the set returns). Variations using an archived value stay as they are, hidden and not sellable, until the value is restored.
- **States by action** (architecture.md → business actions): new items are drafts; `publish` (needs a sellable variation and an active leaf category; permission `menu:publish`), `unpublish`, `archive`, `restore` (to a draft). An archived item keeps its image and variations.
- **"Items only in leaf categories", both sides:** an item needs an active category without sub-categories (top-level or not); a category holding drafts or active items can't get sub-categories (create or move under it). Both are checked early and guarded in the batch; archived items don't hold a category. The category service's failure handler now re-runs its parent check to name the real cause (e.g. `CATEGORY_DEPTH` instead of a generic conflict when a sub-category appears mid-move).
- **Guards re-checked in the batch:** the category (active leaf), newly attached option sets (active), the grid's values (active), the image (via media's attach guard), the item's version and state, and for an active item "still sellable". When one fails, the service re-reads to name the cause.
- **Prices** in whole cents, 0 to $1,000 per variation (a typo guard). An option set archived in the library stays on items that use it; it can't be newly attached.
- **Tests:** 26 service tests (six forced races) and 9 grid-rule tests. 18 of 19 guards fail a test when removed; the survivor is an early check whose in-batch guard gives the same error. The "move under a parent that gets an item" race needed its own test (added after its mutant survived).

### D61: Add-ons on items (step 3.5b), 2026-09-27

- **One list, replaced whole:** an item's add-on groups, their order, the item's own rules and its own prices travel together as `modifierGroups` and are replaced together, like the price grid (D60). They're under the item's `version`, so two admins can't interleave edits of one item's add-ons.
- **Overrides are explicit:** `rules_overridden` marks an item that uses its own `min_select` / `max_select` (so "no maximum" on the item can differ from the group's limit); an add-on price row exists only where the item has its own price. The response shows what applies on the item next to the library's default, so the screen can show "own price" and "reset to default".
- **Checked when the item is saved, not when the library changes** [Choice]: the item's own rules must fit the group's active add-ons and defaults at save time (same `selectionProblem` as the library). A later library edit (archiving add-ons, changing defaults) doesn't re-check or block on items' own rules; the quote (step 6.1) caps an item's own minimum at what's active. Blocking library edits on every item that overrides would make the library hard to change for a rare case.
- **Archived library entries stay where they are:** a group archived in the library stays on items that offer it (not newly added); an add-on archived in the library keeps an item's own price, listed after the active ones, and returns when restored. Same rule as option sets and values (D60). An archived add-on without its own price on the item isn't listed.
- **Guards re-checked in the batch:** newly offered groups and newly priced add-ons are still active. Own rules aren't guarded against a concurrent library change, per the choice above.
- **Limits** [Choice]: at most 10 add-on groups per item; own prices 0 to $100 like the library's.
- **"Used by N items"** on add-on groups counts drafts and active items (like option sets).
- **Tests:** 14 (two forced races). All 6 guards and checks without an in-batch twin fail a test when removed; the 2 survivors are early checks whose in-batch guard gives the same error. `expectApiError` now takes an optional list of expected field-error paths.

### D62: D1's limit of 100 parameters per statement, 2026-09-27

- **Found while planning step 3.6:** D1 refuses a statement with more than 100 bound parameters ("too many SQL variables"). Checked on the staging database: an `IN` list of 100 works, 101 fails. libsql (local and tests) allows 32,766, so no test could see it.
- **What could fail on staging:** creating an option set with more than 14 values, or an add-on group with more than 12 add-ons (one multi-row insert); an item with many own add-on prices; retiring more than 99 versions of a price grid; reordering more than 99 sibling categories (the re-read); listing more than 99 option sets or add-on groups (values, add-ons and "used by" counts by id); a staff member with more than 20 branches.
- **Fix:** three helpers in `server/utils/batch.ts`. `readInChunks` runs a read by ids in pieces of 90. `insertPieces(table, rows)` splits a multi-row insert so rows × columns stay within 100. `chunk` splits `IN` lists in updates and guards, one statement per piece, in the same atomic batch.
- **Kept honest by the tests:** the test database refuses more than 100 parameters too (a proxy on the libsql client). `server/tests/d1-limits.test.ts` runs each of these at the largest size the contracts allow. Undoing any one fix fails a test, except the paged staff read, where a page of at most 100 ids is within the limit; its chunking is a safety margin.
- **Not changed:** the legacy menu (`server/legacy`, removed in 3.8) and Better Auth's own queries (small, fixed lists).

### D63: Availability rules (step 3.7), 2026-09-27

- **A library, chosen from the item and category forms:** rules ("Breakfast") are a library page like Options and Add-ons (`/api/admin/menu/availability-rules`); an item or category picks up to 5 with `availabilityRuleIds`, under its own `version` (like `modifierGroups`, D61). The link is written only from the item or category, never from the rule.
- **Windows:** ISO weekday (1 = Monday) + start and end minute in the branch's time zone; `endMinute` 1440 is midnight at the end of the day. An end before the start runs past midnight and belongs to the day it starts on (D45); Sunday night runs into Monday. A rule's `version` covers its windows, which are replaced whole. At most 21 windows per rule [Choice]. **Overlapping windows in one rule are refused** (422 `AVAILABILITY_WINDOWS`, naming the window) [Choice]: an overlap is always a typo and would make the admin screen ambiguous; touching windows (07:00–11:00, 11:00–14:00) are fine.
- **Levels** [Choice]: an item is available when its own rules, its category's and, for a sub-category, the parent's all allow it; a level without rules doesn't limit, a level with several needs any one to match (D45). The data model already said "an item is available only if its category is too"; the parent level follows from sub-categories inheriting their parent's context. The branch being open (step 5.1) and sold-out (step 3.6) are separate checks.
- **Archiving a rule in use is refused** (409 `AVAILABILITY_RULE_IN_USE`, with how many items and categories) [Choice], checked early and guarded in the batch. Why: with "no rule = always", treating an archived rule as absent would silently put breakfast items on sale all day, and treating it as still valid would make archiving meaningless. Archived items and categories don't count, so their links can outlive the rule: **an archived rule never matches** (fail closed: the item isn't sold), and the item or category shows it with `status: 'archived'` until it's removed. A form can send back an archived rule the item already has (like add-on groups, D61); it can't add one.
- **Evaluation is pure** (`availability.rules.ts`: `isInWindow`, `isAvailableAt(levels, at)`, `localTime(instant, timeZone)` with `Intl`, so daylight saving is the zone's). The public menu (3.8) and checkout (6.2) call it; nothing reads it yet.
- **Rejected:** per-window routes (a rule is edited as one form); storing windows as a weekday bitmask like the legacy schedules (can't express different hours per day, and overnight windows need their start day); allowing overlaps (harmless to "any matches" but always a mistake).
- **Tests:** 14 rule tests (same-day, overnight, Sunday into Monday, whole day, overlaps, levels, archived rules, time zones incl. a DST jump) and 19 service tests (seven forced races, plus two creates at once). 13 of 14 guards and checks fail a test when removed; the survivor is the early "in use" check, whose in-batch guard gives the same error. D1 limits: a rule with 21 windows, an item and a category with 5 rules, and a list of 120 rules; each chunked read fails a test when unchunked.

### D64: Sold out per branch (step 3.6), 2026-09-27

- **Per version, per branch:** `branch_item_states` (PK branch + variation) is the counter's "86" switch: "Large" sold out at one branch, "Regular" still sold, other branches unaffected. The menu stays brand-wide. Switching a whole item sends all its versions in one request (up to 400, the largest price grid).
- **Route on the counter surface:** `GET` / `PUT /api/counter/{branchId}/sold-out`, behind `requireBranchPermission` (`menu:read` / `menu:setSoldOut`: staff, managers, admins). The branch comes from the path and is checked against the caller's membership; staff of another branch get 404. The service only ever uses the actor's branch.
- **Sets a state, never toggles:** `{ variationIds, soldOut }`. So no `version` check and no idempotency key: two cashiers pressing at once, or a retry, both end in the state asked for. The write changes only rows not already in that state (an upsert with `ON CONFLICT … DO UPDATE … WHERE sold_out = 0`, and an update `WHERE sold_out = 1`), so `updated_by` stays the person who actually switched it. Every request is audited (`menu.sold_out.set`, with the branch).
- **Rows stay** after a version is back on sale (`sold_out = 0`, who did it). Switching back on something never switched off stores nothing. `updated_by` is plain text like the audit actor, so it survives an account's removal.
- **What's accepted** [Choice]: drafts and switched-off versions (the switch applies once they're sold); refused: unknown and retired versions and archived items (422 `VARIATION_NOT_AVAILABLE` on `variationIds.<i>`). Not re-checked in the batch: a switch on an item archived meanwhile is harmless (never sold) and reappears if the item returns. The list hides switches of archived items and retired versions for the same reason.
- **Stays until switched back** [Choice; confirmed by the owner as Q37, D105]: no automatic reset at the start of the business day yet. That needs branch hours (step 5.1); the table fits either answer (a later `sold_out_until` or a nightly job).
- **Found while building:** `insertPieces` fills D1's 100 parameters with the rows, so the upsert's conflict clause must bind nothing (it uses `excluded.*` and a literal). With bound values there, switching a full grid fails on D1; the D1-limit test catches it.
- **Branch foreign key:** `#auth/schema` declares only Better Auth's core tables by name; its `schema` object holds every table at runtime, so `menu.schema.ts` references `authSchema!.organization.id`.
- **Tests:** 12 service tests (per branch, idempotent repeats, two at once, labels in option-set order, drafts, refusals, archived and retired versions coming back) and 2 access tests. All 11 guards and filters fail a test when removed. D1 limits: 400 versions off and on again; each chunked piece fails the test when undone.

### D65: The public menu, no cache yet (step 3.8a), 2026-09-27

- **Route:** `GET /api/public/menu?branchId=…`, for anyone. What the branch sells **right now**: active items in active categories, available at this moment on the **branch's clock** (the item's, its category's and the parent's rules, D63), with their sellable versions (active, priced, no archived value) that aren't sold out at that branch (D64). Empty sub-categories and categories are left out, as are option values no listed version uses, archived add-on groups and add-ons. An item's own add-on minimum is capped at the add-ons offered (D61). Unknown and archived branches are 404.
- **Hidden, not greyed out** [Choice]: the step's "done when" says the menu shows only available, in-stock versions, so a sold-out or out-of-hours item isn't listed at all. If the customer site later wants "Sold out" or "From 7:00" labels, the menu can return them instead; `menuAt` is the one place to change. **Changed for sold out by D93:** sold-out versions are listed and marked; out-of-hours items stay hidden.
- **No cache yet** (owner, 2026-09-27; the plan said "cached, purged on writes"): no KV binding exists, KV purges take up to about a minute to reach every location (a sold-out switch would lag), and operations.md asks to measure before caching. The read is about 12 queries in parallel, all joins on status (no id lists that grow, except image ids, chunked; D62), and the response stays `no-store`. **Made cache-ready:** `loadCatalog` (time- and branch-independent) is the one function a cache would wrap; availability and sold-out are applied per request by the pure `menuAt`, so a cached catalog would never serve a stale sold-out state.
- **Layers:** `catalog.repository.ts` (reads), `catalog.rules.ts` (pure: `menuAt`, `groupOnItem`), `catalog.service.ts`. The branch comes from the branches feature (`getActiveBranch`, with its time zone), image URLs from media (`assetUrls`), both through their public `index.ts`.
- **Legacy removal split off** (owner, 2026-09-27): step 3.8 became 3.8a (this) and 3.8b. The owner asked **not** to delete the admin screens but to move them onto the new API and verify them in the browser; the legacy server code goes only once nothing uses it.
- **Tests:** 8 pure tests and 11 service tests (levels of rules in the branch's zone, sold out per branch, drafts and archived records, switched-off versions and archived values, archived add-ons with a capped minimum, option-set order, 404). 20 of 21 filters fail a test when removed; the survivor is the active-values filter, which only trims the catalog (a version using an archived value isn't sellable, so its value never shows). D1: 120 items with images. Real-server: over HTTP on `pnpm dev` (publish → listed; sold out on the counter → gone and in the counter list; back on sale → listed; a Tuesday-only rule on a Monday → hidden; archiving that rule → 409 in use; missing `branchId` → 400; unknown branch → 404; `Cache-Control: no-store`).

### D66: Moving the admin screens onto the new API (step 3.8b), and the Availability page (part 1), 2026-09-27

- **Owner's direction (2026-09-27):** the legacy menu's admin screens are **kept and moved onto the new API**, not deleted; the legacy server code goes only when nothing uses it.
- **Order, from what the code showed:** Categories and Menu items can't move separately: the old item form picks its category with `CategorySelect`, and old items reference `legacy_menu_categories`. Moving the item form honestly means the new model (option sets and the price grid, library add-on groups), which needs the Options and Add-ons pages. So 3.8b absorbs phase 4: part 1 the Availability page (replaces Schedules as a screen; no legacy coupling), part 2 the Options and Add-ons pages, part 3 Categories and Menu items together, then the removal. Each part is reviewed on its own.
- **Availability page** (`app/features/availability-rules/`, [plan](plans/availability-rules.md)): the form edits **rows** (some days + one start and end) and the API stores one **window per day**; the API's windows are grouped back into rows by identical times. An end of 12:00 AM is sent as 1440 (midnight at the end of the day). The server's overlap error names a window index, mapped back to its row (`rowOfWindow`). The whole library is loaded once (it's small and unpaginated): search and tabs work on the client, filters in the URL. Archive is disabled while in use (the server refuses anyway, D63). No bulk actions [Choice].
- **`StatusTabs` takes its tabs** (`tabs` prop, default Active / Inactive), because every new-API screen has `active` / `archived` (items add `draft`). Existing screens are unchanged.
- **The Schedules screen stays** in the sidebar until part 3: the old item form still picks schedules.
- **Verified:** unit (row ↔ window mapping, midnight end, overnight, form rules, wording); e2e (9, two of them checked to fail with the behavior removed: the in-use lock and the overlap row); real-server in headless Chromium on `pnpm dev` (create, an overlap refused and shown on its row, an overnight edit, archive, restore; the stored windows match).

### D67: The Options page (step 3.8b, part 2a), 2026-09-27

- **Each change is saved at once** ([plan](plans/option-sets.md)): the API has one call per action on a set and answers with the whole set, whose `version` covers its values (D58). A draft-and-save form would have to replay several calls and could fail halfway, so the editor (a slide-over) sends each change on its own and keeps the latest set it got back; the next call sends that version. New sets are created in a modal with their first values (one request), then open in the editor.
- **All changes to one set share one mutation key and lock** (`option-set:<id>`), so two never run at once: the second would fail its version check anyway.
- **The unsaved-changes guard watches only pending text** (a changed name, a value being renamed, a value being added), not the whole editor: saving one of them clears it and leaves the others guarded.
- **A version conflict shows an alert with Reload inside the editor**, not a toast action. Found by the browser test: while a modal or slide-over is open, the rest of the page (toasts included) is `aria-hidden`, so a toast's button can't be reached by keyboard or screen reader. The existing "Reopen" toasts are unaffected (they appear after their modal closed).
- **Archiving a set that items use is allowed** (they keep it, D60); the confirmation says how many. The last active value's Archive is disabled, and Add at 20 values.
- **Verified:** unit (the create form); e2e (9; the version forwarding and the last-value lock checked to fail with the behavior removed); real-server in headless Chromium on `pnpm dev` (create, add, reorder, rename, archive and restore a value, rename the set, a duplicate value refused by the server, archive and restore the set: 8 writes in a row, each with the previous answer's version).

### D68: The Add-ons page (step 3.8b, part 2b), 2026-09-28

- **Same editor model as Options (D67):** each change is saved at once with the version of the last answer; all changes to one group share one key and lock (`modifier-group:<id>`); the unsaved-changes guard watches only pending edits (name, rules, an add-on's name and price, a new add-on). "Pre-selected" saves when ticked. Folder `app/features/modifier-groups/` (the API's term), shown as "Add-ons" at `/add-ons` ([plan](plans/modifier-groups.md)).
- **One selection rule on both sides:** `selectionProblem` (minimum ≤ active add-ons, maximum ≥ minimum, pre-selected ≤ maximum) moved from `server/features/menu/modifiers.rules.ts` into `shared/contracts/menu-modifiers.ts`; the server file re-exports it, so the server is unchanged. The create form and the editor's rules check use it before sending, so the admin sees the server's exact message without a round trip. The server still checks, and again in its batch.
- **Every refused change shows inside the editor**, not only in its toast (extends D67 from conflicts to all errors): while a slide-over is open the toasts are `aria-hidden`, so their text doesn't reach screen readers. The alert shows the server's message, or "Someone else changed…" with Reload for a version conflict. The Options editor now does the same.
- **Money helpers moved to `app/utils/money.ts`** (AGENTS.md: promote when a second feature shows prices). `CURRENCY` is defined there instead of coming from the legacy `shared/contracts/menu.ts`, which part 3 removes. The unit test project got the `~` alias, so pure form files can import a root util explicitly.
- **Verified:** unit (7: the form's rules with the server's messages, cents, wording); e2e (8; version forwarding, the rules check before sending and the in-editor error checked to fail with the behavior removed; plus one for the Options editor's in-editor error); real-server in headless Chromium on `pnpm dev` (create with rules; a second pre-selection refused under "at most 1" and shown in the editor; price change, add, reorder, rules change, pre-select, archive: 6 writes, version 7).

### D69: Categories on the new API (step 3.8b, part 3a), 2026-09-28

- **Part 3 is three commits in one pull request** ([plan](plans/menu-screens-move.md)): 3a Categories, 3b Menu items, 3c remove the legacy menu. Categories and Menu items must ship together (D66). In 3a the old Menu items screen keeps a **temporary legacy category picker** inside the products feature (`LegacyCategorySelect`, `useLegacyCategoryOptions`), removed in 3b.
- **Delete becomes Archive and Restore** (the API archives; archiving a main archives its sub-categories, and the confirmation says so). Restoring restores only the category chosen. Tabs are Active / Archived (default Active). A main whose sub-categories match the tab stays in the tree as their parent, as with search.
- **One query for the tree and every picker** (`categories:all`, `GET /admin/menu/categories?status=all`): both need every category (archived ones name a current value), so a page with both makes one request and refreshes once. Found by the freshness e2e tests (they counted the picker's request as a second list load). Because the key is shared, no caller sets a `default` (every caller of a key must pass the same options); `useCategoryOptions` maps `undefined` to `[]`.
- **Order is saved per level:** the API orders one parent's children at a time, with each child's version. Save sends one request per changed level, one after the other; a failure stops, and the refetch shows what was saved. Only active categories have a position: reordering works on the Active tab with no search, and archived categories follow the active ones at each level.
- **`CategorySelect` levels are `main` and `leaf`** (was `main` / `sub`): a menu item goes only in a leaf (D44), and a category with only archived sub-categories isn't a leaf either (the server counts them). Sub-categories are labelled "Parent › Child". The form gains a description and availability rules (`AvailabilityRuleSelect`, now public in the availability-rules feature), and the parent can't change while a category has sub-categories (the server refuses a third level).
- **Verified:** unit (form mapping, tree filters and counts, reorder requests); e2e (Categories 18, pickers 3, and the shared-behavior suites moved to the new API; the "keep active subs of an archived main" and "archived subs have no position" fixes each checked to fail a test when removed); real-server in headless Chromium on `pnpm dev` (create mains and subs, a sub with a description and a rule, reorder both levels and reload, archive a main with its subs, restore one, tab counts matching the server).

### D70: Menu items on the new API (step 3.8b, part 3b), 2026-09-28

- **The form edits the whole item and saves it at once** (unlike the libraries' editors, D67): the API's PATCH takes the item, its option sets and price grid, add-on groups and availability rules under one `version` (D60, D61), and sending back what it read is accepted (an archived set, group, add-on price or rule already on the item stays). The list has summaries only, so the slide-over loads the item (`GET /items/{id}`) and keeps that copy: its version is what the save names, and later refetches don't touch the form. A conflict shows in the panel ("close and open it again"); no merge UI, as before.
- **The price grid is rebuilt in the form** from the chosen sets' active values (`buildGrid`, first set varies slowest, like the server), keeping the price and switch of every combination that was already there. New combinations start **on without a price** when the admin changes the sets (they must price them or switch them off), and **off** when they come from a value added to the library after the item was saved, so saving an unrelated change never sells something by surprise (D60's "missing until priced or switched off"). Versions hidden by an archived value aren't in the grid; the server keeps them.
- **Own add-on prices are "a price different from the library's"** [Choice]: the form shows each add-on's price; one equal to the library's is sent without an override, so a later library change applies to it. An item that had an override equal to the library's loses the override (the customer price is the same). Own rules are a checkbox that starts from the library's rules, checked with the shared `selectionProblem` before sending (D68).
- **States are actions from the list:** Publish (draft), Unpublish, Archive (with bulk archive), Restore (to a draft). An archived item opens read-only (the server refuses edits). "Published" is the screen's word for the API's `active`. The status tabs are All / Draft / Published / Archived, and "All" asks for `status=all` (the API's default leaves archived items out).
- **Dropped:** the "grouped by category" grid (the public menu, 3.8a, is the grouped view; the list filters by category), the legacy variant editor and its per-option prices (replaced by option sets and add-on groups), and the schedules picker (replaced by availability rules). Publish from inside the form and bulk publish aren't built.
- **Library data for the form** comes from new public composables that share the library pages' queries (`useOptionSetOptions`, `useModifierGroupOptions`, like D69); `describeRules` is public for the rules wording. Item mutations invalidate `products` and the three libraries (their "used by N items").
- **Verified:** unit (the form mapping: grid building and kept prices, missing combinations, add-on overrides, own-rules check, bodies; money tests moved to `test/unit/money.test.ts`); e2e (`products.test.ts` 19, `list-bulk.test.ts` 5 on bulk archive; the in-panel error, the price-error mapping and `status=all` each checked to fail when removed); real-server in headless Chromium on `pnpm dev` (upload an image, a 2 × 2 grid with one version off, an add-on with its own price, a rule; publish, seen on `GET /api/public/menu`; edit a price as version 3; archive, gone from the public menu).

### D71: The legacy menu removed (step 3.8b, part 3c), 2026-09-28

- **Removed, now that nothing uses it:** the `/api/v1` routes and `server/legacy` (the D41 menu and its upload route), their tests (`test/server`), `shared/contracts/menu.ts`, the D41 schema (`server/db/schema/`, `server/db/columns.ts`), the Schedules screen and feature (replaced by Availability, D66), and the ESLint rule that fenced off `server/legacy`. With the last ACTIVE/INACTIVE screens gone, the shared helpers built for that model went too (`StatusBadge`, `STATUS_ITEMS`, `STATUS_FILTER_ITEMS`, `useStatusCounts`): the new resources' statuses differ (`active` / `archived`; items add `draft`), so each feature names its own and `StatusTabs` now requires its `tabs`. `docs/reference/api.md` (the `/v1` routes) is deleted; `apiFetch` is documented in data-fetching.md, routes and errors in docs/server/.
- **Migration `0011_drop_legacy_menu` drops children before parents, and empties the self-referencing categories first.** drizzle-kit generated the drops parent-first. On a database with rows (staging) that fails: with foreign keys on (always on D1), `DROP TABLE` first deletes the rows, and a `restrict` reference (items → categories, sub → main category) is checked row by row. The order was fixed by hand, and `pnpm nuxt db generate` still reports no changes. `server/tests/migrations.test.ts` applies it over linked legacy rows (a category tree, an item with an image, schedule and variant); it fails with drizzle's order and without the sub-category delete.
- **No data is carried over** ([plan](plans/menu-screens-move.md) → Data on staging): nothing is in production, and the new model (option sets, the price grid, library add-ons) differs; staging's test data is re-entered through the new screens.
- **Tests moved:** the shared list-page e2e tests run on Menu items (they ran on Schedules), bulk-action ones on Menu items' bulk archive.

### D72: The Categories page redesign, 2026-09-28

- **Modes instead of always-on controls** (after the owner's mockups): the everyday view is the tree with row menus; **Select** reveals checkboxes and a bar with "Archive selected" (Active view) or "Restore selected" (Archived view); **Reorder** reveals drag handles and Move up / Move down per level, then Save order / Discard. The All view mixes statuses, so it has no bulk actions (Select is disabled there with a tooltip). Starting Reorder from a search or another tab clears the search and shows Active: the order is only meaningful over every active sibling. Moving to another parent stays in the Edit form, never by dragging.
- **Layout:** top-level categories are groups, a bordered card each on small screens and sections of one bordered list with column headings from `md` (Category · Subcategories · Availability · [Status] · Actions). Subcategories hang off dashed connector lines; an active parent with subcategories ends with "Add subcategory". The mode bars sit above the tree on desktop and are fixed to the bottom on small screens (safe-area padding). Icon buttons are 44 px on touch screens. The row itself isn't clickable: the name is the button (open in browse mode, toggle in select mode), so nested controls never compete.
- **No invented data:** the mockups' "Items" column isn't in the contract, so the page shows `childCount` ("2 subcategories"), the description and availability instead; no API change.
- **Availability wording:** a top-level category without rules is "Always"; a subcategory without rules is "Inherits parent", never "Always", since its parent may limit it. With rules: the first name and "+N", the full list in a tooltip and for screen readers (`availabilityLabel`).
- **Status wording:** Active / Archived only (no Activate, Deactivate or Delete). Badges show in the All and Archived views; the Active view doesn't repeat "Active" on every row.
- **Conflicts keep the user's work:** a refused Save order (409) keeps the new order and offers **Reload**, which refetches versions and merges new or removed categories into the pending order, so Save works again. The form shows a refused save inside the modal (toasts are out of reach while it's open) and, for a version conflict, **Reload** takes the latest version while keeping the input. Bulk restore runs parents before subcategories (the server restores a subcategory only under an active parent); a subcategory whose parent is archived shows a disabled "Restore (restore the parent first)".
- **Shared change:** `StatusTabs` takes an optional `size` (`md` here), backwards compatible. (Removed in D74: every page uses the default.)
- **Verified:** unit (availability wording, counts); e2e `categories.test.ts` 29 (the modes, terminology, parent archive warning, expand/collapse all, reorder by keyboard, buttons and drag, the 409 Reload for the order and the form, search clearing on Reorder, phone-width layout: no overflow, icon-only add button, 44 px move buttons, bars pinned to the bottom); five behaviors checked to fail their test when removed. Screenshots on `pnpm dev` at 1440, 768, 375 and 320 px against the mockups, no horizontal overflow.

**Follow-ups (owner's answers, 2026-09-28):**
- **Item counts:** `MenuCategory.itemCount` (drafts and published items; one grouped query, no id list). The list's "Contains" column reads "2 subcategories", "12 items" or "Empty" (a category holds one or the other, D44). "Add subcategory" is disabled with its reason on a category holding items, the parent picker (`CategorySelect level="main"`) no longer offers one, and archiving a leaf says how many items customers stop seeing.
- **Restore with subcategories:** `POST …/restore` takes `withSubcategories` (top-level only): its archived subcategories come back in the same batch, in their previous order, audited with the count. Restoring a parent that has archived subcategories opens a dialog with "Also restore its N archived subcategories" ticked; a bulk restore still restores only what is selected.
- **Polish:** collapsed groups are remembered per viewer in this browser (`localStorage`, `categories:collapsed`); search matches descriptions too; availability tooltips list each rule's times (`describeWindows`, public in availability-rules); `S` / `R` open Select / Reorder, `Esc` leaves them.
- **Verified:** server (27 category tests: restore with subcategories, nothing restored on a stale version, item counts; the child restore and the archived-items filter each fail a test when removed); unit; e2e `categories.test.ts` 37 (the collapse memory and Escape checked to fail when removed); real-server on `pnpm dev` (an item counted, a parent restored with its subcategory, rule times in the tooltip).

### D73: The Options page and option-set editor redesign, 2026-09-28

- **Library:** header "Options" with a subtitle and a New button (icon-only on phones), an info panel ("Options create menu-item versions. Prices are configured on each menu item."), search over set names **and active value names** (a card lists the values that matched: "Matches: Oat, Oat barista"; archived values don't match, as they aren't on the card), All / Active / Archived tabs with counts that follow the search (Active by default, in the URL). Cards in a 2-column grid from `lg`: one generic icon (no name-based icons), the name, "3 values · 1 archived", the active values as chips in stored order (3 on phones, 5 from `sm`, then "+N more"; CSS decides, no resize listener), "Used by N menu items" or "Unused", an explicit **Edit** / **View** button (a large **Manage** / **View** on phones). The ⋮ menu holds only **Archive** or **Restore** (owner, 2026-09-28): the button opens the set. No bulk actions, no order of sets, no Delete, no prices.
- **Editor:** a right slide-over from `sm`, full screen on phones (safe-area padding). The header shows the name, an Active / Archived badge, what uses it and a real status (**Saving…**, **Saved**, **Couldn't save**) from the set's mutations and the pending reorder. The name and each value are **read-only until Edit / Rename** opens an inline edit (one at a time; Enter saves, Escape cancels only that edit; replacing one with typed text asks first). "Add value" opens an inline input that stays open for the next value; at 20 active values it is disabled with the reason. Archived values sit in a collapsed "Archived values (N)" section with Restore (disabled at 20, with the reason). A **Danger zone** archives the set. An archived set is read-only with a prominent **Restore option set**.
- **Confirmations:** archiving a set says what happens by `itemCount` ("3 menu items use this set and will keep it, but it cannot be added to other items until restored." or "This set cannot be added to menu items until restored."); archiving a value says its menu-item versions will be hidden until it is restored. The last active value's Archive is disabled in its menu with "An option set needs at least one active value." (the server refuses it too).
- **Reorder mode, saved shortly after moves** (owner, 2026-09-28): drag handle, position, name, Move up / Move down (44 px on phones); ↑/↓ on a focused handle move too. Each move restarts a 0.5 s timer; the latest order is then sent in **one** request with the version of the previous answer, and requests never overlap (`createOrderAutosave`, `app/features/option-sets/utils/order-autosave.ts`, framework-free and unit-tested with fake timers). So a burst of moves is one write instead of one per click, and the move buttons are never disabled while saving (a disabled button loses the keyboard focus). Focus stays on the moved row; an `aria-live` region says "Large moved to position 3"; the row is highlighted for 1.5 s. A refused save drops the waiting moves, puts back the last saved order, stays in the mode and shows the error inside the editor, with Reload for a version conflict. **Done reordering** (a sticky full-width bar on phones) sends what's waiting first and leaves the mode only if it saved; Escape does the same. Closing the editor during the wait still sends it (mutations outlive the component). The reorder mutation has no success toast: the header says "Saved".
- **Why not "Save order" like Categories (D72):** a category reorder touches many siblings under two levels and is rare; an option set's values are one short list whose every other change already saves at once. A separate Save step would be the only unsaved state in this editor.
- **Create modal:** full screen on phones, "Examples: Size, Temperature, Serving style", first values move with Move up / Move down (no drag: a short list), 1–20 unique values as before; the editor opens after creation.
- **Deviations from the mockups, where the spec wins:** one generic icon for every set; an outline "Add value" also on phones; an archive icon (not trash) in the danger zone. The Add-ons editor (D68) is unchanged in this step.
- **Verified:** unit (search and wording 8, reorder autosave 5); e2e `option-sets.test.ts` 22 (search by name and value, counts, chips per width, menu without Delete, create order, editor saves with versions, inline Escape, last-value and 20-value limits, both confirmations, archived read-only, reorder by buttons, keys and drag with one debounced request, failure restore, conflict Reload, phone layout); the debounce, the failure restore and the inline Escape each checked to fail their test when removed. Real-server on `pnpm dev`: screenshots at 1440, 768, 375 and 320 px; keyboard-only reorder (Tab, Enter, ↓, ↑, Escape) saved twice with versions 1 → 2 → 3.

### D74: Design system first: Categories and Options back on Nuxt UI defaults, 2026-09-28

- **Context:** the Categories (D72) and Options (D73) redesigns copied the mockups' styling as well as their layout: a custom page header (`h1` and subtitle inside the navbar), the search moved out of `UDashboardToolbar` with a larger size and custom width, `size="lg"`/`"xl"` buttons with `min-h-11`/`min-w-11` touch targets, a larger `StatusTabs`, and hand-made parts (an accordion button, a bordered "Danger zone", a colored icon box). The two pages no longer matched Staff, Menu items, Add-ons and Availability.
- **Decision (owner, 2026-09-28):** mockups decide layout, positions and content; the Nuxt UI theme and our shared page structure decide styling. Rule added to AGENTS.md → Conventions.
- **Changes:** both pages use `UDashboardNavbar title`, the standard New button, a `UDashboardToolbar` with `SearchInput` (`w-64`) and, on Categories, the Expand / Reorder / Select buttons in its `#right` (labels from `lg`); the subtitle is the page's muted intro paragraph (Options folds its info panel into it); `StatusTabs` at its default size (its `size` prop removed). Every `size="lg"`/`"xl"` and `min-h`/`min-w` override is gone, so phones get Nuxt UI's default button sizes rather than 44 px targets. The option-set card is a `UCard` with one Edit/View button at every width (no separate Manage button); a value's position is a `UBadge`; "Archived values" is a `UCollapsible`; the danger zone is a `USeparator` and a section with a soft error button; the editor keeps Nuxt UI's slide-over width (already full width on phones). Kept, as layout: the Categories mode bars pinned to the bottom on phones, the reorder modes, the card grid, the chips per width, and the Categories toolbar's `ui.left: 'min-w-0'` so its search shrinks and the view buttons stay on screen at 320 px (the toolbar otherwise scrolls them away).
- **Consequence:** touch targets on phones are Nuxt UI's defaults (about 32 px for icon buttons). If they prove too small in use, change it for the whole app in `app.config.ts`, not per page.
- **Verified:** lint, typecheck, unit + server 492, e2e 174 (the size assertions replaced by "fits the screen"; the unsaved-changes forward test failed once under the full `pnpm test` and passed alone twice and in a full `pnpm test:e2e`); screenshots on `pnpm dev` at 1440, 768, 375 and 320 px, no horizontal overflow, every Categories toolbar button on screen at 320 px.

### D75: The Add-ons library and the add-on group page, 2026-09-28

- **Context:** the owner's Add-ons redesign (library and group detail, desktop and phone mockups). Styling follows D74 (Nuxt UI defaults, the shared page structure); layout and content follow the mockups.
- **Owner's answers (2026-09-28):** (1) a group opens on **its own page, `/add-ons/[id]`** (breadcrumb, deep links, Back), the app's first detail route, instead of the slide-over; (2) **saving:** the settings (name, Optional / Required, minimum, maximum) are a draft saved by **Save changes** in one PATCH; each add-on change is its own confirmed action (the Add / Edit dialog's Save, ticking Preselected, Archive, Restore, Save order), one call each, so nothing is ever half-saved (the API has no "save everything" call); (3) **the "Used by" list is built now:** the admin item list takes `modifierGroupId` (an `exists` over `menu_item_modifier_groups`, drafts and published like `itemCount`), the page lists the first 5 items, each opening that item on Menu items (`/products?item=<id>`), and "View all" opens Menu items filtered by the group (`?modifierGroupId=`, a removable "Offers Milk choices" button); (4) **row actions on phones are a bottom sheet** (`UDrawer`); a dropdown from `sm`.
- **Library:** cards (`UCard`) in a 2-column grid from `lg` with the generic Add-ons icon, a Required / Optional badge and the rule ("Choose exactly 1"), the active add-ons as compact rows (name, price aligned right, a Preselected mark; 3 rows on phones and 4 from `sm`, then "+N more"), "Offered by N menu items" or "Unused", and Manage / View (a link to the page). The ⋮ menu holds only Archive or Restore. Search covers group and active add-on names, with a "Matches" line. "Every add-on group is archived" gets its own empty state with Show archived. No bulk actions, no order of groups, no Delete.
- **Rule wording** (spec): "Optional · choose any", "Optional · up to N", "Required · choose exactly N", "Required · choose at least N", "Required · choose X–Y". `describeRules` is public, so the menu-item form uses the same words. A sentence under the settings explains it ("Customers must choose exactly 1."); no stored "exactly one" flag.
- **Group page:** heading with status and rule; an Add-ons card (search in the group, Reorder, Add add-on; table rows from `sm` with name, default price, a Preselected checkbox that saves when ticked, Edit and ⋮; stacked rows on phones; archived add-ons in a `UCollapsible` with Restore); a Used-by card; a Settings card (name, Optional / Required as a table radio group, minimum, maximum or "No maximum", the rule, status with Archive group / Restore group, last updated). On phones Add-ons and Settings are tabs, the header has Back and a ⋮ with Archive / Restore, and Save changes is a bar at the bottom while the settings have changes.
- **Checks before sending, with the server's messages:** add-on names unique among the group's active add-ons (the server's index), price 0–$100, pre-selected ≤ maximum, minimum ≤ active add-ons, maximum ≥ minimum (`selectionProblem`, shared). A disabled action says why (Set as preselected past the maximum; Archive on the add-on the group needs). The server's field errors land on their fields; the dialog stays open with the input.
- **Settings conflicts:** the draft remembers the group's settings when it started. If an answer or a refresh shows someone else changed them, Save changes doesn't send and the page offers Reload (our own add-on changes move the version but not the settings, so they don't block a save).
- **Reorder mode:** drag handle (↑/↓ on it too), position, name, price, Move up / Move down; nothing is sent until **Save order** (one PUT); Cancel drops the moves; a refused save keeps the moves on screen to retry, with Reload for a conflict; leaving with an unsaved order asks first. Unlike Options (D73, saved after each pause), because the spec asks for an explicit save here.
- **Not built (no API):** Preview, Duplicate, permanent Delete. Archive and restore only.
- **Removed:** `ModifierGroupEditor.vue` (the slide-over).
- **Verified:** unit (rule wording, search, prices, settings and add-on checks: 18); server (the item filter, checked to fail without it); e2e `modifier-groups.test.ts` 31 (library, search, previews per width, menus without Delete, archived-only state, load error, create → page, table and usage links, add / edit with checks and field errors, preselect limit, archive / restore, settings in one PATCH with checks, a taken name, a settings conflict, leave guards, archived read-only, not found, reorder by buttons, keys and drag with one PUT, conflict Reload, phone tabs, bottom sheet and Save bar, Menu items filter and `?item=`); the preselect limit and the settings conflict checked to fail their tests when removed; `pnpm build`; screenshots on `pnpm dev` at 1440, 1024, 768 and 390 px without horizontal scrolling; keyboard-only reorder saved on the real server.

### D76: The Availability page as a weekly agenda, 2026-09-28

- **Owner's answers (2026-09-28):** show the timezone in the form but **no "Open now"** (optional in the spec; it would duplicate the server's window check in the app); **D74 wins over the mockups** on phones (standard header and labelled New rule button, search in the toolbar, the note as the muted intro, Nuxt UI default sizes).
- **Timezone without a server change:** the owner chose "add the timezone to the branch options API", but the zone is already configured as `runtimeConfig.public.cafeTimeZone` (`NUXT_PUBLIC_CAFE_TIME_ZONE`), the value the seed gives the branch and the form already read. The form shows it; no API change. Revisit when branches get their own settings (step 5.1).
- **Library:** one full-width `UCard` per rule, one reading path (identity and usage → week at a glance → times → actions), stacked on phones with ⋮ top-right. The week is seven `UBadge`s: open days outlined in the primary color with a dot, the others muted, each named for screen readers ("Friday: open"). Times are grouped by days with the time right-aligned and "next day" as quiet text for overnight windows (`scheduleLines`). Usage reads "18 items · 2 categories" (`usageSummary`); the disabled Archive keeps "In use by …".
- **Wording change (public):** consecutive days are always a range, "Sat–Sun" and "Fri–Sat" instead of "Sat, Sun" (the spec's examples). `describeWindows`, used by the Categories tooltips, follows.
- **Form:** day toggles are buttons with `aria-pressed` (not checkboxes) with Weekdays / Weekend / Every day / Clear presets per row; "next day" note under an overnight row; a weekly preview built from the same helpers as the cards; full screen on phones; an archived rule opens read-only with Restore (it used to be unreachable: the server refuses edits). The data model, validation and background-save / Reopen behavior are unchanged.
- **Rules without windows:** the local database had one (from before the contract required a window). The card says "No times yet" and the form opens with one empty row, so it can be fixed.
- **Verified:** unit (17: agenda lines, midnight vs overnight, open days, usage wording, a rule without windows); e2e `availability-rules.test.ts` 15 (counts with search, archived read-only + Restore, toggles and per-row presets, overnight note and preview, the 21 limit and row checks, leave guard, phone layout and full-screen form, plus the earlier overlap / conflict / archive cases); `aria-pressed` and the read-only guard each checked to fail their test when removed; screenshots on `pnpm dev` at 1440, 1024, 768 and 390 px without horizontal scrolling.

### D77: Responsive UI standard (documentation), 2026-09-28

- **Context:** the owner asked for one canonical responsive UI standard before migrating pages. The audit found layouts switching at 640, 768 and 1024px, three editing surfaces chosen per feature, tables that scroll sideways on phones, four bottom-bar implementations and a touch-target rule (D74) in tension with the owner's new 44–48px requirement ([plan](plans/ui-standardization.md)).
- **Principle:** **Nuxt UI is the component design system; Tailwind CSS tokens are the layout and styling language.** Components are configured globally in `app.config.ts`; layout uses Tailwind's standard spacing, breakpoint, container-query, typography and color-token utilities; no parallel library, re-skin, arbitrary values, hex colors, one-off CSS, gradients, custom shadows or per-page component overrides. Custom CSS only for theme variables, safe areas and behavior utilities can't express.
- **Decision (owner-directed in the request):** width-based layouts in three classes (compact <640, medium 640–1023, expanded ≥1024); a 4px spacing ramp; semantic Nuxt UI colors; one primary action per page and at most one per card; at least 44px touch targets on compact with WCAG 2.2 AA as the floor; tables → grouped rows or cards on compact; master–detail → pushed screens or bottom sheets; long compact forms → full-screen routes; bottom sheets for brief contextual actions; gestures only as enhancements; sticky controls never obscure focus or content; a defined set of states; no decorative styling; no parallel component library.
- **Where the rules live:** [ui.md](reference/ui.md), [responsive-layout.md](reference/responsive-layout.md), [page-patterns.md](reference/page-patterns.md), [ui-review-checklist.md](reference/ui-review-checklist.md). The former `ui.md` helper reference is now [ui-helpers.md](reference/ui-helpers.md).
- **Approved in the owner's review (2026-09-28):** the Nuxt UI dashboard app bar and drawer on compact and medium, no admin bottom navigation; a drawer, not an icon rail, on medium; compact targets of at least 44px (`min-h-11`/`min-w-11`; 48px `min-h-12`/`min-w-12`) configured centrally in Nuxt UI configuration, not broad selectors or per-page overrides; light-mode primary-text contrast fixed through Nuxt UI's semantic theme variable (`--ui-primary`); token-compatible dark mode without a color-mode switch yet; a focused route for the long Menu item editor on compact; the Options editor stays a slideover with its inner layout adapting through Tailwind responsive and container utilities; the 4px spacing system and semantic color roles preserved.
- **Review findings resolved:** (1) viewport classes decide the shell, pages and surfaces; a constrained surface's **contents** follow its own width through Tailwind container queries (`@container`, default container variants), and JavaScript choices use one layout-context composable or an explicit `compact` prop, never device detection; (2) a compact record row is two sibling targets, a large record link or content button and a `UDropdownMenu`/`UButton` actions trigger, nothing nested and no styling-only row component; (3) a settings page's save model is the same at every width (recommended: one shared draft and one final Save, also across pushed compact sections).
- **D74 amended:** components stay at Nuxt UI defaults and pages still don't add `min-h`/`min-w` overrides; the compact 44px minimum is part of each component's central configuration in `app.config.ts`. (Replaced by D81: Nuxt UI's default sizes at every width.)
- **Also approved (owner, 2026-09-28):** (1) the Settings blueprint: one page-wide draft and one final Save at every width, pushed compact sections editing the same draft, moving between sections neither saves nor discards, one request for the whole draft, the unsaved-changes warning only when leaving the page; (2) more than two filters on compact: a labelled Filters `UButton` with the active count opening a `UDrawer` with Clear and Apply, the same URL/query state at every width; (3) one shared bottom action bar owning only positioning, safe-area and body padding, focus and keyboard handling, and one bar at a time, rendering the features' Nuxt UI buttons with no styling of its own; (4) Menu item URLs: `/products/[id]` (canonical) and `/products?item=<id>` both open the item at every width, compact lists open the route and wider lists the slideover, resizing never changes the URL, Back keeps the list's query.
- **No open decisions.** The complete table: [the plan's owner decisions](plans/ui-standardization.md#6-owner-decisions). Implementation starts only after this documentation is reviewed and merged into `main`.
- **No application code changed.**

### D78: UI foundations (Phase B step 1), 2026-09-28

- **Context:** the first step of the UI rollout ([plan §7](plans/ui-standardization.md#7-phase-b-migration-sequence-after-this-documentation-is-merged-into-main)): the global pieces every page migration builds on, with no page redesigned.
- **Touch targets (A3; replaced by D81):** `app/app.config.ts` adds `max-sm:min-h-11` to the slots of every Nuxt UI control the admin uses (button, input, number, time, select and select-menu fields, tabs trigger, dropdown menu item, navigation link) and `max-sm:min-w-11` to square (icon-only) buttons. Checkboxes, radios and switches keep their visual size: a `max-sm:after:` inset gives them a 44px hit area (the checkbox and radio box need `overflow-visible` for it, and their indicator keeps the rounding). From `sm` up nothing changes. Native elements a page draws itself aren't reached; the Categories record names (24px) are fixed by their page's migration.
- **Primary contrast (A4): the 800 shade, not 700.** Measured in the browser: amber-700 passes on white but gives 4.4:1 on its own tint (`text-primary` on `bg-primary/10`, the subtle badges); amber-800 passes on white, on the tint and behind the white label of solid primary buttons. Set as light mode's `--ui-primary` in `tailwind.css` (unlayered, so dark mode's 400 is restated). Visible effect: primary buttons, links and active states are a deeper amber in light mode.
- **`useLayoutContext()`** replaces six hand-written `useMediaQuery('(max-width: 639px)')` calls. It queries Tailwind's own breakpoints in `rem` (`width < 40rem`, `width >= 64rem`), so JavaScript and CSS agree even with a larger browser font.
- **`<BottomActionBar>` (decision 3):** fixed below `lg`; from `lg` `inline`, `pinned` (sticky in the panel) or `hidden`. It pads its scroll container and sets `scroll-padding-bottom` while fixed (restored afterwards), steps aside while a text field outside it has focus (keeping the padding, so nothing jumps), and shows one bar at a time (a `useState` stack; the latest wins). `max-lg:m-0` because a parent's `space-y-*` margin lifts a fixed bar off the edge. The Categories mode bars, the Add-on Save bar and `BulkActionsBar` use it; `CategoryModeBar` and the page-level bottom paddings are gone. **The Options editor's footer stays the slideover's own `#footer`:** it's an overlay footer that Nuxt UI already places inside the slideover (a fixed bar inside an animated overlay would be placed against the wrong box). This narrows decision 3 to page bars; **confirmed by the owner (2026-09-28): overlays use their own `#footer`.**
- **Behavior change:** the bulk bar on Menu items is now full width at the bottom of the screen below `lg` (it floated at every width), and it no longer fades.
- **Owner confirmation (2026-09-28, after merge):** the 800 shade, the overlay-footer exception and the bulk bar change are accepted.
- **Verified:** unit (`bottom-bar.test.ts`), e2e (`ui-foundations.test.ts`, 7; the size and hit-area tests fail with the configuration removed; the Categories and Add-on phone tests), screenshots at 390 and 1440 px in light and dark mode.

### D79: Staff on the UI standard (Phase B step 2), 2026-09-28

- **Context:** the first page migration and the reference for a resource index with a table ([plan §7](plans/ui-standardization.md#7-phase-b-migration-sequence-after-this-documentation-is-merged-into-main)). Before: a `UTable` at every width (sideways on phones), rows that opened on click with a `⋮` button inside them, a toolbar that scrolled sideways, a centered form modal on phones.
- **Phones (below `sm`):** a list of rows in the [compact row composition](reference/page-patterns.md#compact-row-composition): a ghost `UButton` holding the name ("(you)"), the email, the access as one line of text ("Manager at Airport · Staff at Riverside") and the Temporary password badge, named by the person's name; beside it the `⋮` menu (or the busy spinner). Rows or table is a `v-if` on `useLayoutContext().isCompact`, so only one exists in the DOM.
- **Table (from `sm`):** the name cell is the record's button and the row no longer opens on click, so the record target and the actions trigger are siblings at every width (page-patterns §2). `shrink-0` on the table: in the flex panel body it was squeezed into its own scroll box above the pagination (one row visible on a landscape phone).
- **Toolbar, all pages:** `UDashboardToolbar` is `overflow-x-auto` in Nuxt UI. `app.config.ts` makes it wrap below `sm` (its left group takes the room, controls wrap), so filters never scroll sideways (responsive-layout §5). It also stops the Menu items category filter from being cut off at 390px. Staff's controls are `w-full` / `flex-1` below `sm`.
- **Form:** full screen below `sm` (`useLayoutContext`), safe-area padding in the footer, the footer wraps.
- **Found, not fixed here:** Nuxt UI's `warning` color as text (the outline "Temporary password" badge) looks well below 4.5:1 on white, the same kind of gap as the primary (D78). It affects every page: fixed app-wide in D80 at the owner's request.
- **Verified:** e2e `staff.test.ts` (13, 4 new: rows and no sideways scrolling at 320 and 390 px, the toolbar wraps, pagination fits, 44px targets with the actions beside the row, the full-screen form, the row menu); screenshots at 320, 390 (light and dark), 768, 1024, 1440 and 844×390.

### D80: Light-mode warning contrast, 2026-09-28

- **Context:** found in D79. Nuxt UI's `warning` is yellow-500 in light mode: text in the offline banner, the Add-on group page, the Options editor, Menu items (the "Draft" badge) and Staff (the outline "Temporary password" badge) was far below AA. The owner asked for the fix (2026-09-28).
- **Decision:** light mode's `--ui-warning` is the **800** shade, next to the primary override in `tailwind.css` (dark mode keeps 400, restated). Measured in the browser, text on white / on its 10% tint: 500 1.9 / 1.8, 600 2.9 / 2.7, 700 4.9 / **4.3**, 800 6.8 / 5.8. 700 fails on the tint (subtle badges), as primary did (D78). Solid warning badges (the Menu items "Draft") now carry their white label at 6.8:1.
- **Success, error and info (owner, 2026-09-28, a follow-up):** they failed the same way at Nuxt UI's 500 shades (text on white / on the tint: success 2.2 / 2.0, error 3.8 / 3.3, info 3.8 / 3.3). Each now uses the lightest shade that passes both: success **800** (7.1 / 6.1), error **700** (6.4 / 5.3), info **600** (5.3 / 4.5). Visible effect: validation messages and destructive actions are a deeper red, Active badges a deeper green. Dark mode keeps the 400 shades, restated.
- **Verified:** `test/e2e/ui-foundations.test.ts`: each status color's light-mode shade, as text on white (also the white label of a solid badge) and on its tint; in dark mode, each on the dark background and on its tint.

### D81: Nuxt UI's default control sizes on phones, 2026-09-28

- **Context:** D78 made every Nuxt UI control at least 44px tall below `sm` (A3). The owner tried it on a phone and found the buttons too big and not friendly, and asked to use the Nuxt UI theme's own sizes instead.
- **Decision (owner):** buttons, fields, tabs, dropdown menu items and navigation links keep Nuxt UI's default sizes at every width. The per-component `max-sm:min-h-11` / `min-w-11` rules are gone from `app.config.ts`. WCAG 2.2 AA's 24×24px (2.5.8) is the floor; Nuxt UI's `md` controls are about 32px. 44px is Apple's guideline and WCAG's AAA level, not the AA floor.
- **Kept, because they change nothing visually:** the invisible hit areas of checkboxes, radios and switches below `sm` (their 16–20px box is hard to hit), and the toolbar wrapping on phones (layout, not size). Record rows stay large targets by their content (name and meta lines), not by overrides.
- **Still true (D74):** pages never add `min-h-*`/`min-w-*` or size props for touch; a size change is a theme change for every page.
- **Verified:** `test/e2e/ui-foundations.test.ts` (every Nuxt UI control on a phone is at least 24×24px; the same button is the same height on a phone and a desktop; the checkbox hit area), `staff.test.ts`; screenshots of Staff and Categories at 390px.

### D82: The Add-on group page on the UI standard (Phase B step 3), 2026-09-28

- **Context:** the reference detail route ([plan §7](plans/ui-standardization.md#7-phase-b-migration-sequence-after-this-documentation-is-merged-into-main)). Tabs below `lg`, Back and breadcrumb, the Save bar (D78) and bottom-sheet row actions already met the standard. What didn't: the add-on rows switched on the **viewport** (`sm:`) while living in a column much narrower than the viewport, so at 1024px four columns were squeezed beside the settings column; the rows and the page's two columns used arbitrary grid templates; a row's name opened nothing (Edit was a pencil button or inside ⋮); Reorder's Cancel and Save order sat in a sticky top bar positioned with negative margins.
- **Container query:** the add-ons section is an `@container`; `AddOnRow` and its column headings switch at `@md` (28rem). Narrower (phones, and beside the settings column at 1024px), a row stacks its default price under the name and marks a preselected add-on, with Preselect in the ⋮ menu; wider (from 640px while the columns are tabs, and at 1440px), it shows the price and the Preselected checkbox. Both layouts exist at every viewport width, chosen by room, not device.
- **Tokens, not arbitrary values:** the rows' columns are flex items with spacing-token widths (`w-24` price, `w-36` Preselected, `w-12` actions); the page's columns are `flex` with a `lg:w-88` settings column.
- **Record target:** an add-on's name is a ghost `UButton` that opens its Edit dialog, with the ⋮ actions beside it (page-patterns §2), at every width; the pencil button is gone (Edit stays in the ⋮ menu). On an archived group the name is plain text.
- **Reorder mode:** Cancel and Save order are in `BottomActionBar` ("Reorder"): the bottom of the screen below `lg`, inline above the list from `lg`, like Categories. The bar also says whether the new order is saved.
- **Verified:** e2e `modifier-groups.test.ts` (35, 4 new: the name opens Edit with the actions beside it, the reorder bar at the bottom of a phone, rows stacked at 1024px and in columns at 1440px with the reorder bar inline), screenshots at 390, 768, 1024, 1440 and 844×390, reorder at 390 and 1440.

### D83: A faster check workflow with the same gates, 2026-09-28

- **Context:** a UI step took 30–40 minutes of agent time, mostly waiting: every e2e run rebuilt the app first (47 s measured, even when only a test changed), the agent ran the full e2e suite locally (~6 min) before CI ran it again on the PR (~4–5 min in 3 parallel shards), and each step's docs repeated the same facts in 5–7 files. The owner asked for a faster workflow that keeps everything as reliable. Branch protection isn't available (private repository on the free plan).
- **Decision (owner):**
  - **Reuse the e2e build while nothing it reads changed.** `test/e2e/support/global-setup.ts` fingerprints every build input (`app/`, `server/`, `shared/`, `public/`, `nuxt.config.ts`, `.nuxtrc`, the package and lock files, `tsconfig.json`, `.env`: paths and contents) plus the `NUXT_*` and `NODE_ENV` variables, and skips `nuxt build` when the fingerprint matches the one saved next to `.output`. CI (`CI` set) always builds; `E2E_REBUILD=1` forces a build; any other `nuxt build` cleans `.output` and so the fingerprint. Measured on the Staff file: 89 s (build) → 27 s (reused); editing a test reuses, editing app code rebuilds.
  - **Local checks before a PR:** lint, typecheck, unit/server tests and the changed feature's e2e files; the full e2e suite locally too when a shared building block or app-wide config changes. **CI runs the full suite** on the PR and again on `main`, and the staging deploy needs every job to pass, so a red merge never reaches staging.
  - **Merge only when the PR's checks are green** (the owner checks the ✓; no PR watching, to save tokens). A red PR is fixed on the same PR, a red `main` before any new work.
  - **"Remove the guard, see the test fail"** is required for new guards (locks, permissions, money, idempotency, version checks), optional elsewhere.
  - **Docs per step:** one decisions entry and the step's progress line; reference pages only when a shared building block or a rule changes.
- **Rejected:** testing after merge (a merge to `main` deploys staging); dropping e2e (it caught real bugs, e.g. the staff session overwritten by Better Auth's state key); sharing one build across the CI shards (they run in parallel, little wall time to gain).
- **Where:** AGENTS.md → Checks; progress.md → How we work and How to verify.

### D84: Sign-in and change-password as task flows (Phase B step 4), 2026-09-28

- **Context:** the reference task flow ([plan §7](plans/ui-standardization.md#7-phase-b-migration-sequence-after-this-documentation-is-merged-into-main), page-patterns §5). Both pages were a centered card at every width (a small card in a gray frame on phones), and after a failed submit focus stayed on the button.
- **Decision:** `AuthFrame` (`app/features/auth/components/`, feature-local until another feature has a task flow) renders a centered `UCard` with the actions in its footer from `sm`, and on phones the full screen: title at the top, the fields, the actions at the bottom with safe-area padding (a flex column, not a fixed bar, so the on-screen keyboard never covers a field). The choice is `useLayoutContext().isCompact`. The submit button lives in the footer, outside the form, joined with `form="<id>"`: Enter still submits.
- **Focus:** a failed submit moves focus to the first invalid field (`UForm` `@error`), or to the server's error (`role="alert"`, focusable). Nuxt UI's form disables its fields while validating (`loadingAuto`) and re-enables them only after the error event, so the field is focused on the next frame; focusing at once silently does nothing.
- **Verified:** e2e `auth.test.ts` and `password.test.ts` (21, 5 new: both pages on a phone, Enter submits, focus to the invalid field and to the server's error), screenshots at 390 and 1440.

### D85: Categories on the UI standard (Phase B step 5a), 2026-09-28

- **Context:** step 5 brings the remaining pages to the references, one PR each. Categories switched its layout at `md` (768px, not a width class), used arbitrary grid templates (`md:grid-cols-[minmax(0,1fr)_9rem_…]`), and its record target was a hand-made 24px `<button>` holding only the name.
- **Container query:** the tree is an `@container`; rows and headings switch at `@2xl` (42rem of tree width, about what `md` gave with the page's padding). Narrow: a stacked row with a meta line; wide: Category · Contains · Availability · Actions. Columns are flex items with spacing-token widths (`w-36`, `w-44`, `w-12`); `rowColumns` is gone.
- **Status next to the name:** in the All and Archived views the Active/Archived badge sits beside the name instead of a fourth column, which left names about 100px at narrow widths.
- **Record target:** the name block (name, status, description, meta line) is a ghost `UButton` named by the category: it opens the edit form (browse) or toggles the checkbox (select, out of the tab order since the checkbox is the keyboard target). Archived and context-only rows show plain text, padded to line up.
- **Verified:** e2e `categories.test.ts` (40, 3 new: the name opens Edit with the actions beside it, stacked at 640px and in columns at 1024px, the status beside the name), `pickers` and `ui-foundations`; screenshots at 390, 768, 1024 and 1440.

### D86: Options on the UI standard (Phase B step 5b), 2026-09-28

- **Context:** the next page of step 5. The Options editor already met the standard (a slideover, A7: its value rows have no width switches; "Done reordering" sits in its header from `sm` and in the slideover's own `#footer` on phones, a thumb-reach choice by viewport). The library cards counted their value chips by the **viewport** (`sm:`), but from `lg` they sit in two columns, so at 1024px a card about 350px wide showed the wide count.
- **Decision:** each card is its own container (`@container` on the `UCard` root); chips switch at `@sm` (a 24rem card): 3 and "+N more" on a narrow card, 5 on a wide one. The search box is `w-full sm:w-64` like Staff's.

The search box had a fixed width on phones.

The search box is `w-full sm:w-64` like Staff's.

### D89: The Menu items list on the UI standard (Phase B step 5e, part 1), 2026-09-28

- **Context:** the last page of step 5, split in two PRs: this list, then the editor route (A6, decision 4). The List view was a `UTable` at every width (sideways scroll on phones), a click anywhere on a row or card opened the item, checkboxes were always on (page-patterns §2: selection is a mode), the grid's columns used an arbitrary value (`minmax(13rem,1fr)`) and the toolbar's search and category filter had fixed widths that overflowed at 390px.
- **Decision:**
  - **Record target:** the name is each record's one target (page-patterns §2). In the table it's a button in the name cell (the row is no longer clickable). On a card it's a button stretched over the card (`after:absolute after:inset-0`), so a click anywhere still opens it, with the ⋮ menu and the checkbox as siblings above it, never nested.
  - **List on phones:** rows (avatar, name, a status badge unless published, "category · price", ⋮), one tree in the DOM chosen by `useLayoutContext()` like Staff (D79); the table from `sm`.
  - **Grid:** an `@container` whose columns follow the width it has, at the sizes where a 13rem card fits: 1, then 2 from `@md`, 3 from `@2xl`, 4 from `@4xl`, 5 from `@6xl`.
  - **Select mode:** bulk archive is a mode, like Categories: a **Select** toolbar button (`S`), checkboxes and the name selecting while in it, the ⋮ menus hidden, Escape or ✕ leaving it, archived items not selectable (the Archived tab ends the mode). `BulkActionsBar` becomes the shared Select mode bar ("N selected · Select all · actions · ✕", Categories' bar moved to the root now that a second feature needs it); Categories uses it unchanged in behavior.
  - **Toolbar:** search `w-full sm:w-64`, category `flex-1 sm:w-56`, like Staff's filters.
- **Alternatives:** keep always-on checkboxes (against the standard, and noisy on phones); a separate Menu items bar (a second copy of Categories' bar).
- **Verified:** e2e `products.test.ts` (22, 3 new: Select mode, phone rows fit the screen and open by name, grid columns at 1440px; the card test now clicks the card's centre, which the stretched name receives), `list-bulk.test.ts` (5, now through Select mode), `categories.test.ts` (40, unchanged, on the shared bar); screenshots at 390, 1024 and 1440 px (grid, Select mode, List).

### D90: The Menu item editor route (Phase B step 5e, part 2), 2026-09-28

- **Context:** the owner's A6 (the long Menu item editor gets a focused route on phones) and decision 4 (`/products/[id]` canonical, `/products?item=<id>` also works, both at every width; the list picks one when opening; resizing never changes the URL; Back returns to the list with its query). Before this, `?item=` opened the slide-over and was removed from the URL at once, and there was no route.
- **Decision:**
  - **One editor, two surfaces.** `useItemEditor` holds the state, loading and save (the version read, Reopen after a failed background save, the refused-save message); `ProductFormFields` holds the `UForm` and its four sections (details, prices, add-ons, availability). `ProductFormSlideover` and the new `ProductEditorPage` only own their surface and their unsaved-changes guard, so data, rules and actions can't drift.
  - **The page** (`app/pages/products/[id].vue`, also `/products/new`): ← and a breadcrumb back to the list, Save in the navbar from `lg` and in `BottomActionBar` below. On phones the sections are tabs, one at a time; every section stays mounted (`v-show`), so a hidden one keeps its input and is validated, and an invalid field (the form's or the server's) switches to its tab (`sectionOf`, unit-tested). Saving returns to the list: `router.back()` when the list is the previous entry (its query kept), otherwise `/products`.
  - **The list:** opening an item on phones pushes `/products/<id>` (a new one `/products/new`); from `sm` it pushes `?item=<id>` (a new one opens the slide-over without a URL). `?item=` in the URL opens the slide-over at every width and stays while it's open; closing it removes only `item` (`router.back()` when the list pushed it, else a replace), and Back closes it (the route guard asks first about unsaved input).
  - **`useModalUnsavedChanges.requestClose` marks the form clean** before closing: the form stays registered until the overlay unmounts, and the list's URL change on close would otherwise ask a second time (forms.md case 22).
- **Alternatives:** a separate page form (two copies of a long form); removing `?item=` at once like before (against decision 4: the URL wouldn't say what's open, Back wouldn't close it).
- **Verified:** unit (`item-form.test.ts`: server field → form field, field → section); e2e `products.test.ts` (28, 6 new: `?item=` with filters kept, resizing to a phone keeps it, Back closes it; closing removes only `item` and asks once, checked to fail without the `markClean`; phones push `/products/<id>` with tabs and ← back to the filtered list; an invalid field in a hidden tab shows its tab on `/products/new`; `/products/<id>` wide saves the version read and returns; leaving with unsaved input asks), `modifier-groups.test.ts` (the deep link keeps `item` while open); screenshots at 390 (Details, Prices) and 1440 px.

### D91: Branch settings and dining tables on the server (step 5.1a), 2026-09-29

- **Context:** step 5.1 (branch settings, hours, dining tables with QR codes). The owner decided (this session): edit existing branches only (the seed task creates them, including staging's second test branch); hours are several windows a day and may run past midnight; closures ("closed today", holidays) wait for ordering (phase 6); a table's QR can be shown again at any time (the mockup's "View QR"), which the standard's "hashed and shown once" rule didn't allow; a table has an optional area. The mockups are a layout reference (D74) for 5.1b. Plan: [plans/branch-settings.md](plans/branch-settings.md).
- **Decision:**
  - **Rebuildable QR tokens (changes security.md's rule, owner-approved):** `token = base64url(HMAC-SHA256(NUXT_QR_SECRET, "<tableId>:<qrVersion>"))` cut to 128 bits; the database keeps only its SHA-256 (unique) and looks a scanned token up by it. A leaked database still reveals no working QR; the server rebuilds a table's QR to show it again. Rotating increments `qr_version`. Changing the secret invalidates every printed QR. A deployed build without the secret answers 500 `QR_NOT_CONFIGURED` on the table routes; the dev server uses a local one. Tokens are never audited or logged.
  - **Hours share the Availability rules' windows:** the pure window rules (overlap, "in the window", the branch's wall clock) moved from the menu feature to `server/utils/weekly-windows.ts`, and the window contract to `shared/contracts/common.ts` (`WeeklyWindow`); the menu re-exports them unchanged. `branch_hours` holds 0–21 windows; none means never open. The settings response says whether the branch is open now and today's weekday in its timezone, so the admin page needs no clock math.
  - **One version for a branch's settings and hours:** a new `version` on `organization` (a Better Auth additional field), guarded in the same batch as the hours and the audit event. Hours need `settings: ['manage']` besides `branch: ['update']` (security.md).
  - **Tables:** labels unique among a branch's active tables (case-insensitive partial index), natural order ("Table 2" before "Table 10"), at most 200 per branch (a soft cap, checked before the write), archived rather than deleted (their QR stops working; restoring brings the same QR back unless an active table took the label). The admin routes use `branch: ['update']` (admins only); managers get counter routes with the counter screen.
  - **Public:** `GET /api/public/tables/{token}` answers the branch and table; unknown tokens, archived tables and archived branches are the same 404.
- **Alternatives:** random tokens stored hashed and shown once (the old rule: no "View QR", re-printing a lost sticker needs a rotation); random tokens stored encrypted (a second key and a decryption step for the same result); tokens stored in plain text (a leaked database would hand out working QRs).
