- **Verified:** server tests `branches.service.test.ts` (22: settings and hours, overnight, timezone change, unknown timezone, overlaps on their field, stale and interleaved versions (both checked to fail with the guard removed), 21 windows under D1's parameter limit, deterministic tokens, the secret requirement, create/list/rename/archive/restore/rotate, label clashes, archived table and branch tokens, another secret's token, the 200 cap); the menu's server tests unchanged (174); real-server on `pnpm dev` (sign in, read and save settings, stale 409, unknown timezone 422, create a table, resolve its token without a session, rotate: old token 404, list, 401 without a session).
- **Deploy note:** set `NUXT_QR_SECRET` on staging (`wrangler secret put NUXT_QR_SECRET`) before the Branch page (5.1b) is used there; until then the table routes answer 500 `QR_NOT_CONFIGURED` and nothing else is affected.

### D92: The Branch pages (step 5.1b), 2026-09-29

- **Context:** the admin half of step 5.1 (D91), from the owner's two mockups (a layout and content reference, D74): Branch in the sidebar, **Settings** and **Dining tables** tabs, status cards, weekly hours with Open switches and copy buttons, table cards with QR codes, View QR and Rotate. The settings blueprint (page-patterns §3) applies: one draft, one Save changes.
- **Decision:**
  - `app/features/branches/`: `/branches` opens the only branch (launch has one) or lists them (staging's second test branch); `/branches/[id]` has the two tabs. The tab is **local state** (`?tab=tables` opens that one), and both tabs stay mounted: a tab in the URL would make every switch a route change, which the unsaved-changes guard asks about while the settings draft has changes.
  - **Settings:** the status cards show what's saved (the server says whether the branch is open now and today's weekday there). The draft covers details and hours and is sent whole with its version. Hours are per day (the mockup's row) with up to three windows each, since the owner chose several windows a day; a window ending after midnight says "Closes the next day". The server's overlap error (`hours.N`) is mapped to the window it names. A 409 keeps the input and offers Reload (like Categories, D72). On phones the three status cards become one card with a row each, so they don't fill the first screen.
  - **Dining tables:** loaded whole (few per branch) and filtered on the page. The QR is drawn in the browser from its link with `uqr` (UnJS, no dependencies) as an SVG path, **always black on white** in both themes (scanners need the contrast, so its colors belong to the image, not the theme). Downloads: SVG, and a 1024 px PNG drawn from it. A Print button is left out: printing a generated page runs into the CSP (D48), and the files print. "New QR" asks first (the printed code stops working), then shows the new code to print.
  - **Shared:** the clock formats (`formatClock`, `timeRange`, `isOvernight`) and the `UInputTime` conversions moved from the Availability feature to `app/utils/clock.ts` now that a second feature uses them (AGENTS.md rule 1).
- **Alternatives:** tabs in the URL (a "Discard?" question on every switch with a draft); a QR image from a server endpoint (a PNG library on Workers, and the token in another URL); `qrcode` through VueUse's `useQRCode` (heavier dependencies than `uqr`).
- **Verified:** unit `branch-form.test.ts` (6: form from settings, the complete draft, validation of open days only, `hours.N` to its window, copy, new windows, closed all week); e2e `branches.test.ts` (16); the Availability e2e file after the clock move; real-server on `pnpm dev` in headless Chromium (sign in, sidebar Branch, save details and copied hours, "Open now" after reload, add a table, its QR link resolves on the public route, New QR: old link 404, new 200); screenshots at 1440 (light and dark, next to the mockups) and 390.

### D93: The customer menu, and the admin workspace under `/admin` (step 5.3a), 2026-09-29

- **Context:** the owner chose to build the ordering-first customer menu next, from their five ChatGPT mockups and the review of them (a layout and content reference, D74): the menu is the home page, flat category tabs with an "All categories" tree, full-width "Add to order" that turns into a stepper, a detail for items with choices, search in the same cards, an order panel, a closed state. The admin app owned `/` and `/login`.
- **Decision:**
  - **Paths:** the admin workspace moved under `/admin` (`/admin/login`, `/admin/products`, …), as docs/server/architecture.md already drew it; the customer site owns every other path (`/`, `/table/<token>` from D91). `isAdminPath()` in the auth feature decides: the route middleware, the session-loss redirect and the cross-tab login only act on admin paths, so the customer site never reads `/api/admin/me`. After login, `?redirect=` must be an admin path (else `/admin`). Tab titles and the error page name the site they're on. The CI smoke check reads `/admin/login`.
  - **Server:** `GET /api/public/branches` (active branches, whether each is open now and when it opens next, on its own clock: `nextOpening { inDays, weekday, startMinute }`, pure `nextStart` in `server/utils/weekly-windows.ts`); the public menu's `branch` carries the same status. **Sold-out versions are now marked (`soldOut`), not hidden** (reverses D65's [Choice], which named `menuAt` as the one place to change): the mockups show "Sold out", and an item whose versions are all sold out is marked too. Out-of-hours items stay hidden.
  - **App** (`app/features/menu/`, pages `index.vue` and `table/[token].vue`, layout `shop`): sections per main category and sub-sections per sub-category; one scroll spy (`useScrollSpy`) drives the tabs and the tree. A chosen section is held as active until the customer scrolls themselves (a section near the end may never reach the top), and focus moves to it (closing the tree doesn't return focus to its button, which could stop the smooth scroll). Cards from `sm` in a grid laid out by the column's width, rows with a small "Add" on phones. An item with anything to choose (several versions or any add-on group) opens its detail (a dialog, a bottom sheet on phones); the add slot then says "2 in order" with "Add another", while a plain item's slot becomes a stepper. The detail starts from the first orderable version and the pre-selected add-ons, disables values that only lead to sold-out versions, keeps group limits, and its button names what's missing ("Choose Milk") before it shows the total.
  - **The order before checkout** lives in this browser (local storage, per branch; the table from a QR code in session storage, for the tab's visit). Lines store ids and a quantity, never prices: they're read against the current menu, so a price change or a sold-out switch shows at once and an unavailable line says so and doesn't count. Checkout (6.1–6.2) prices on the server; "Review order" is disabled until then ("Online ordering opens soon").
  - **Closed:** one warning banner ("Closed now · Opens today at 7:00 AM"), browsing works, adding is disabled everywhere.
- **Not built, on purpose:** customer sign-in and the account pages (step 5.2; sign-in is only needed to place an order), a note for the barista and "popular items" on an empty search (no contract or data for them yet), category icons (the owner dropped them).
- **Server rendering (D45) is not done in this step** [Choice, open as Q38]: the pages are client-rendered like the admin. The e2e harness mocks the API in the browser, which can't answer requests made while rendering on the server, so SSR needs its own test setup; the data layer (`useApiQuery`) works unchanged when it's switched on.
- **Alternatives:** the customer site on its own subdomain (a second origin for cookies and CORS for one small site); the order in `useState` only (lost on reload); prices stored in the order (stale after a price change).
- **Verified:** unit `app/features/menu/tests/menu.test.ts` (15: sections, search and highlighting, "from" prices, choices, add-on limits, what's missing, the order's merging, caps, reading against the menu, malformed storage, opening text); server (branches +4: open, next opening today / tomorrow / next week, overnight, no hours, archived; catalog tests changed to marked sold-out); e2e `shop-menu.test.ts` (15, three runs in a row), the admin e2e suite on `/admin/**`; real-server on `pnpm dev` in headless Chromium (a table's QR link opens the menu for "Table 12", add an item, a bad token says so, `/admin` goes to `/admin/login`); screenshots at 1440, 768 and 390.

### D94: The Sample data page (test data from the admin), 2026-09-29

- **Context:** the owner wants realistic data to try every screen (the admin and the customer menu), loaded **from the UI**, locally and on staging, and a way to clean it up. The owner's mockups (11 frames, reviewed the same day) set the page: a Sample menu card, a Branch hours and tables card, and a danger zone. Photos are left out (the owner uploads them), names are English, prices USD.
- **Decision:**
  - **An admin page, `/admin/sample-data`,** only where `NUXT_PUBLIC_SAMPLE_DATA_ENABLED` is on: the dev server (`$development`) and staging (`wrangler.vars`), never production. The routes check it first and answer 404; the sidebar lists the page (with a "Test" badge) only when it's on. Admins only (`menu:write/publish`, `branch:update`, `settings:manage`).
  - **Through the real services:** `server/features/sample-data/` creates every record with the menu's and branches' own services (`createCategory`, `createItem`, `publishItem`, `setSoldOut`, `updateBranchSettings`, `createTable`), so sample data obeys every rule a hand-made record does. The dataset is plain data (`sample-data.catalog.ts`): 8 categories, 3 option sets, 4 add-on groups, 2 availability rules (one overnight), and items in three sizes, **Small 12, Standard 40, Large 150** (the owner chose presets over a free number: realistic content and known cost). Every size has a draft, an archived and a sold-out item. Large adds generated flavour variants ("Pandan Latte"), all valid.
  - **Small steps, safe to repeat:** a Worker request may run only 50 queries on Cloudflare's free plan, so each call creates at most 2 records (tables: 4; reset: 20 uploads per call) and the page repeats the call until the server says it's done. Records are found again by name, so a step that failed halfway is just run again ("Try again" continues where it stopped); an item a crashed step left as a draft is finished on the next step. A `sample_data_runs` row remembers an unfinished load (its size, so "Continue loading" isn't refused because the menu isn't empty) and holds a 60-second step lock, so two tabs or two admins can't run steps at once (409 `SAMPLE_DATA_BUSY`); a crashed step's lock frees itself.
  - **Loading only on an empty menu** (or continuing its own unfinished load): no name clashes, no mix of sample and real data. **Reset** deletes every menu record (archived ones and sold-out switches too) and the load in one audited batch (`sample-data.reset`), then the uploads a batch per call. Branches, hours, tables, staff and accounts are kept. The admin types RESET.
  - **Hours and tables:** sample hours replace the branch's (the page asks first when it has some); the 12 tables T01–T08 and P01–P04 are added unless an active table has the label.
  - **The page** (the mockups' corrections): success is a status, never a button; one info alert naming the environment; "Current state" from real counts, so it's true after a reload or hand edits; one action at a time (a shared mutation lock; the others are disabled); leaving the page stops after the step in flight; lists elsewhere are refreshed once at the end, not per step. The reset dialog is full screen on phones.
- **Alternatives:** a script or a Nitro task (the owner wants everything in the UI; `/_nitro/tasks` doesn't exist on a deployed Worker); one request per load (over the query limit on the free plan, and all-or-nothing); a free item count (unbounded cost, and generated filler past a point); raw SQL inserts (would skip the rules the data should show).
- **Verified:** server `sample-data.service.test.ts` (9: standard load in states, Small and Large (150 items) loads, refused on a non-empty menu and when loaded, another size refused, a crashed step's draft finished without duplicates, the step lock and its expiry, reset with uploads and audit, hours and tables in steps keeping a used label); the lock and the draft fix-up each checked to fail with their guard removed; unit `state.test.ts` (4); e2e `sample-data.test.ts` (9); real-server on `pnpm dev` in headless Chromium with a fresh database (sign in, the sidebar entry, Standard loaded in 2.5 s and Large in 6 s through the page, hours and 12 tables, the customer menu shows it, reset through the dialog; screenshots at 1440 and 390).

### D95: Server rendering for the customer site (Q38), 2026-09-29

- **Context:** D45 says the customer pages are server-rendered; the menu (D93) shipped client-rendered because browser-mocked e2e tests can't answer requests made on the server (Q38). The owner asked for server rendering now.
- **Decision:**
  - **Per route:** `ssr: true`, with `routeRules` `ssr: false` for `/admin` and `/admin/**` (the workspace needs the staff session and never search engines) and `/table/**` (a QR link only stores the table in the tab and moves on). Counter screens will be SPAs too. `<html lang="en">`, and the menu sets its description (`useSeoMeta`).
  - **`apiFetch` on the server** fetches per request (`useRequestFetch`, in-process) with no session handling: the module-level browser client would be shared by every request the server handles. `plugins/api.ts` configures the browser client only.
  - **Server and browser render the same first page:**
    - What differs by width is CSS, never `useLayoutContext` (the server can't measure): the item card is a row or a card by Tailwind variants, its add slot rendered once per layout with the other hidden; both category triggers are rendered (sheet on phones, popover from `sm`); search field vs search button, and the order panel (`hidden lg:block`), likewise. The order panel's sticky offset is a CSS variable set in the browser, not a rendered style.
    - Browser storage (the order, the table, the chosen branch) is read after mounting (`initOnMounted`): the server's page and the first browser render show the default branch, no table and an empty order; the stored values apply right after.
    - The menu request names a branch only when the browser asks for one (a table, a choice); otherwise `GET /api/public/menu` answers for **the first active branch by name** (`branchId` is now optional), so the server can render without knowing the visitor.
  - **Testing:** the customer site's e2e tests use **no mocks**: the e2e build keeps its data in `.data/e2e` (`E2E_HUB_DIR` → `hub.dir`), and the global setup rebuilds that database from the migrations before each run and fills it through the real services with the Standard sample menu (D94): "Riverside" open around the clock, "Zeta Kiosk" with no hours, a table in each (QR tokens from a test secret). The admin's tests keep `mockApi`. A test asserts that the page hydrates with no errors and no "Hydration completed but contains mismatches" (checked to fail with a deliberate mismatch). The e2e server gets test values for `NUXT_PUBLIC_SITE_URL` and `NUXT_BETTER_AUTH_SECRET` (a production build's Better Auth answers 500 without them).
- **Alternatives:** SSR after checkout (Q38's suggested default; the owner chose now); seeding through a test-only server route (test code in the deployed bundle); fixture JSON loaded into the database by hand (a second dataset that would drift from the sample menu); keeping browser mocks and rendering the menu only in the browser (not server rendering).
- **Verified:** server (a test for the default branch); e2e `shop-menu.test.ts` rewritten on the seeded database (17: the page's HTML carries the menu, title and description; readable with JavaScript off; the admin page carries no content; hydration clean at 1440 and 390; tabs, tree, quick add, detail with price, add-on limits, sold out, the order after a reload, search, phone rows, sheet and search header, a table QR, a closed branch's table, a bad QR), twice in a row, and the full e2e suite; real-server on `pnpm dev` with the Standard sample menu (curl sees the menu in the HTML; no hydration warnings in headless Chromium at 1440 and 390; the stored order comes back; admin sign-in and a table's QR link still work).

### D96: Light and dark mode, light by default, 2026-09-29

- **Context:** until now the app followed the system's color scheme and had no switch (D77, `ui.md` §2: "token-compatible, no switch yet"). The owner asked for light and dark on both the admin and the store, with Nuxt UI's `UColorModeButton`, **light by default, ignoring the system**.
- **Decision:**
  - `colorMode: { preference: 'light', fallback: 'light' }` in `nuxt.config.ts` (Nuxt UI's `@nuxtjs/color-mode`). The system setting is never read; the person's choice is kept in this browser (`localStorage` `nuxt-color-mode`) and applied before the first paint by the module's inline script, on server-rendered and browser-only pages alike, so a dark choice doesn't flash light. One choice for the whole site: the store and the admin share it.
  - **Where the button is:** the admin sidebar's footer next to the account (stacked when collapsed); the admin's sign-in and change-password pages (top corner); the store's header; the customer's account pages (top bar). It's Nuxt UI's own button (`Switch to dark mode` / `Switch to light mode`), unstyled by us.
  - Nothing else changes: every screen already uses semantic tokens (D77), and both modes were already checked (`ui-foundations.test.ts`, whose dark tests now set the stored choice instead of the system's).
- **Alternatives:** follow the system with a three-way switch (system, light, dark) (the owner chose light unless changed); a switch per site (admin and store differing is confusing on one origin); the choice in a cookie (lets the server render the mode, but the inline script already applies it before paint).
- **Verified:** e2e `color-mode.test.ts` (2: light on a dark system for the store and the admin; the header's button switches, the choice survives a reload with no hydration errors and carries to the admin, where it switches back), `ui-foundations.test.ts` (dark shades), the full e2e suite; screenshots at 1440 and 390 in both modes.

### D97: The customer's account pages (step 5.2), 2026-09-29

- **Context:** customers need email and password accounts (D45, D51; the server side was step 1.6). The owner's mockups (17 frames, reviewed together the same day) covered sign-in, create account, check your email, verified and expired links, forgot and reset password, and the header's account states. The review's outcome: one card per width, errors on their fields, no social login, no "remember me" (sessions last 7 days, D45), no terms sentence until Terms and Privacy pages exist (launch checklist, 8.2), and the order gate (the "Sign in to place your order" sheet, the verify prompt on the order) deferred to checkout (6.2), when "Review order" has somewhere to go.
- **Decision:**
  - **Pages at the site's root** (D93): `/sign-in`, `/sign-up`, `/verify-email` ("Check your email", resend), `/email-verified` (where the verification link lands), `/forgot-password`, `/reset-password` (where the reset link lands), in a new `account` feature (`app/features/account/`), layout `account`, `noindex`. Server-rendered like the rest of the site; a `TaskFrame` (the admin's `AuthFrame` from D84, moved to `app/components/` and made CSS-only so it renders the same on the server) is a card from `sm` and the full screen with the action at the bottom on phones.
  - **Better Auth's own routes, no new server code:** the pages post to `/api/auth` (`sign-in/email`, `sign-up/email`, `sign-out`, `send-verification-email`, `request-password-reset`, `reset-password`) like the admin's `useAuth` (D52), and read the account from `GET /api/shop/me` (typed now: `CustomerAccount` in `shared/contracts/account.ts`). Better Auth's link handling decides the landing pages: the verification link verifies and signs in, then goes to `/email-verified` (`?error=TOKEN_EXPIRED` / `INVALID_TOKEN` when it doesn't work); the reset link goes to `/reset-password?token=` (or `?error=INVALID_TOKEN`, expired and used alike). The token leaves the address bar once read.
  - **Returning where the person was:** `?redirect=` carries a customer-site page (the header's Sign in passes the current one); anything else, another host (`//x`, `/\x`), the admin, or a sign-in or password page, goes to the menu (`accountRedirectTarget`, unit-tested).
  - **The header reads the account in the browser only:** the server's page is the same for every visitor (cacheable later, and no one's name can end up in another's page); the account slot shows a placeholder until the browser knows. Signed out is Better Auth's `get-session` answering `null`, so no visit logs a failed request; a session then reads `/api/shop/me`. Signed in: the initials open name, email, a verified or not-verified badge, the member code with Copy, and Sign out (neutral: not destructive). Not verified: a banner on the menu ("Verify your email to place orders", Resend email) and the same button in the account menu. Every resend shares one 60-second wait (the server allows about 3 per 10 minutes).
  - **Wording from the server's answers** (`accountFormError`): a wrong email or password is one message above the fields (Better Auth never says which), an existing email and a breached or too-short password go on their field, too many tries says so; focus moves to the field or the alert (page-patterns §5).
  - **One sign-in per browser:** the admin and the store share the origin and Better Auth's session cookie, so an admin signed in on `/admin` is signed in on the store, and signing out on either signs out both. Accepted for launch (staff use their own devices or browser profiles); separate sessions would need separate domains (Q4).
- **Alternatives:** `@nuxtjs/better-auth`'s client composables (a second way to call auth next to the admin's `useAuth`); rendering the signed-in header on the server (a per-visitor page, and a name in a cached page if caching is ever added); a "Confirm password" field (the show button covers typos with less typing); the order gate now (nothing to gate until checkout).
- **Testing:** the e2e seed creates three customers through Better Auth (without the breached-password lookup) and the tests read the real verification and reset links from the server's outbox. Sign-up and saving a new password ask Have I Been Pwned (an external API, blocked in the cloud containers), so only those two answers are mocked in the browser; their success paths are covered by the server tests (step 1.6) and a real-server run.
- **Verified:** unit `app/features/account/tests/account.test.ts` (11: redirect targets, links, initials, error wording, dead links, the form schemas); e2e `shop-account.test.ts` (13: sign in from the header and back, the account menu and member code, sign out, wrong password with focus, field checks, redirects refused, show password, the unverified banner → resend → the real link → verified, a dead verification link → sign in → verified, forgot → the real reset link → token out of the URL → save → sign-in says so, a dead reset link on opening and on saving, the same answer without an account, sign-up errors on their fields, links both ways keeping the return page, the phone layout), the full e2e suite (hydration clean on the menu with the new header); real-server on `pnpm dev` in headless Chromium with the breached-password lookup switched off for the run (not committed): sign up, the verification link from the outbox → verified and signed in, the account menu, sign out, forgot and reset with the real link, sign in with the new password, no console errors; screenshots at 1440 and 390, light and dark, and the admin sign-in in dark).

### D98: Pricing and the checkout quote (step 6.1), 2026-09-29

- **Context:** checkout (6.2) must never charge a price the browser made up or a menu that changed. The owner's answers (2026-09-29, the defaults offered): **at most 20 of one line, 30 lines per order, an optional note per line of up to 140 characters, prices shown for 10 minutes before the page asks again**. The 6.2 mockups (10 frames, reviewed the same day) show a signed-out visitor on Review order, so prices must be checkable without an account.
- **Decision:**
  - **`POST /api/public/checkout/quote`** (`server/features/orders/`, contract `shared/contracts/orders.ts`): the branch's public menu at this moment (`getPublicMenu`: availability rules in the branch's time zone, active versions and add-ons with the item's own add-on prices, the branch's sold-out switches, D65, D93), then each line priced against it (`quote.rules.ts`, pure). **Public:** it reveals only menu prices, which are public already; vouchers (7.5) will quote on the signed-in side. A POST only because lines don't fit a query string: it writes nothing, and the origin check applies as to every API POST.
  - **Problems per line, not a refused request:** a line is priced or carries its first problem (item not on the menu now, version gone, add-on gone with its ids, a group's rule unmet, sold out), in that order, so the page can show each line's state. Totals count the priced lines; a closed branch is an order problem (`BRANCH_CLOSED`) and still prices everything. `orderable` is true only with no problem at all.
  - **No quote token or stored quote:** the quote's only job is to show prices; placing the order (6.2) will price the lines again from the database and refuse when the total differs from what the page showed (the page then shows the price change). A signed token would add a secret and prove nothing the second pricing doesn't. `expiresAt` (10 minutes) tells the page when to ask again.
  - **Money:** whole cents; unit = version + add-ons on this item; line = unit × quantity; total = subtotal (no tax, service charge or vouchers, D45). The request carries no prices (strict schemas).
  - **Limits in the contract** (`ORDER_MAX_LINES`, `LINE_MAX_QUANTITY`, `LINE_NOTE_MAX`, `QUOTE_TTL_MINUTES`); the menu's order now stops at 20 per line (it allowed 99).
  - **Decided for step 6.2 (owner, 2026-09-29):** pickup numbers are 3 digits restarting at 001 each business day per branch, no letter; a customer may have **2 unpaid orders** at once (a third: "Pay for your waiting order first"); a signed-out visitor sees Review order with checked prices and a "Sign in to place order" button.
- **Alternatives:** a quote only for signed-in customers (hides prices behind sign-in, against the mockups); refusing the whole request on the first bad line (the page couldn't show which lines to fix); a stored or signed quote the order must cite (a table or a secret for no extra safety, since the order is priced again); sharing the browser's cart pricing with the server (the browser's is a display estimate; one authority is simpler to reason about).
- **Verified:** server `orders/tests/quote.rules.test.ts` (12: the written example 2 × Iced Latte Large with oat milk and an extra shot + a croissant + a Khmer iced coffee = $13.75; free add-ons and the 20 maximum; the 10-minute expiry; each problem; problem order; a closed branch; the request limits and that no price is accepted) and `quote.service.test.ts` (6, on the real menu services: an item's own add-on price, a price edit showing in the next quote, availability hours, unpublished, sold out at one branch only, an archived add-on, a branch without hours, an unknown branch 404). The sold-out, add-on-rule and item checks were each removed and the tests failed. e2e `checkout-quote.test.ts` (3, the built server on the seeded database: priced lines with a trimmed note, a closed branch, a foreign origin 403, an invalid body 400, an unknown branch 404).

### D99: Placing an order (step 6.2a, server), 2026-09-29

- **Context:** after the quote (D98), the order itself: stored so the counter (6.3) and the customer (6.5) can rely on it, never twice, never at a price the customer didn't see. The owner's answers (2026-09-29, the defaults offered): the **business day starts at 4:00** branch time (Q39); **online orders stop 15 minutes before closing** (Q40); a customer may **cancel while unpaid** (Q41, the button comes with 6.5); a table QR that no longer works is **refused, never turned into pickup** (Q42); **pickup numbers restart at 001** each business day per branch; **at most 2 unpaid orders** per customer. Step 6.2 is split: 6.2a the server (this entry), 6.2b the pages from the reviewed mockups.
- **Decision:**
  - **`POST /api/shop/orders`** (verified customer, `Idempotency-Key`) `{ branchId, tableToken?, lines, expectedTotalMinor }`: the lines are priced again with the quote's rules on the menu as it is now, then refused in this order: closed or last orders passed (`ORDERING_CLOSED`, the message says which), a line that can't be ordered (`ORDER_NOT_ORDERABLE`), **a total other than the one the page showed** (`PRICES_CHANGED`: a price is never charged unseen), a table that doesn't work at this branch (`TABLE_UNAVAILABLE`), a third unpaid order (`TOO_MANY_UNPAID_ORDERS`). All 409 with a message for the page. `GET /api/shop/orders/{id}`: the customer's own order; anyone else's is 404 like an unknown id.
  - **One batch** (atomic on D1): the order, its lines, the unpaid-orders guard (`requireAtMost`, new in `server/utils/batch.ts`, placed after the insert so it counts the new order) and the idempotency key (`withIdempotency`, D50). Two orders placed at the same moment can't both pass the limit, and two requests with the same key place one order. The stored response is only the order's id; the answer is read back from the database, so a retry (even after a price change) returns the order as placed.
  - **Pickup numbers** are computed **inside the insert** (`max + 1` for the branch and business day): SQLite runs one write at a time, so two orders can't read the same maximum, and a unique index on (branch, business day, number) is the final guard. The data model had planned a `branch_order_counters` table; one statement does the same job with one table less.
  - **The order is a snapshot:** each line keeps the item name, the chosen version and add-ons as text ("Large · Oat milk"), the add-ons with their prices (JSON: `[{ id, name, priceDeltaMinor }]`), the unit price, quantity, total and note. Menu ids are kept for reports without foreign keys (menu records can be reset on test environments, D94); the table id likewise (the branches feature owns tables and archives, never deletes, them; a feature's schema doesn't import another's, like the menu's image ids) with its label kept. The data model had planned an `order_line_modifiers` table; add-ons are only ever read with their line, and reports need items (8.1), so JSON is enough.
  - **Statuses:** the database allows the whole life (awaiting payment, preparing, ready, completed, cancelled) now, because SQLite can't change a `CHECK` without rebuilding the table; 6.2 creates `awaiting_payment` only. `payment_due_at` = placed + 30 minutes (the expiry task is 6.6); an unpaid order past it no longer counts toward the limit.
  - **Last orders:** `PublicBranch.closesInMinutes` (minutes to the end of the open stretch, through back-to-back windows and past midnight: `minutesUntilClosed` in `server/utils/weekly-windows.ts`); the quote and the placement refuse at 15 minutes or less (`LAST_ORDERS_PASSED` in the quote).
  - **Who may order:** any signed-in account with a verified email (staff too, for themselves); unverified is 403 `EMAIL_NOT_VERIFIED` (`requireCustomer`, D51).
- **Alternatives:** a counter table for pickup numbers (a second write per order for the same guarantee); a retry loop on a number collision (unneeded with the number computed in the insert); storing the whole order as the idempotent response (a retry would bypass the database, and the number isn't known before the insert); an app-side count for the unpaid limit (two simultaneous orders would both pass); turning a dead table QR into pickup (the customer would wait at a table nobody serves).
- **Verified:** server `orders.service.test.ts` (18: the full snapshot and 30 minutes to pay; the largest order (30 lines) within D1's 100-parameter limit; menu edits and archives never change a placed order; `PRICES_CHANGED`, `ORDER_NOT_ORDERABLE`, closed and last orders (20:44 accepted, 20:45 refused for a 21:00 close), each placing nothing; pickup numbers per branch and day, 3:59 vs 4:00 on an all-night branch; four orders racing at the commit get 1–4; the unique index refuses a copied number; the same key replays (also after a price change), two racing tabs with one key place one order, the same key with another order is 422; the third unpaid order refused and accepted again once the first two are past due, two racing orders can't both pass; dine-in at a table, another branch's table, an archived table and a made-up token refused; someone else's order 404). Races are made real by holding every racer at `db.batch` until all have arrived. Removing each guard (the unpaid guard, the total check, the owner check, the table's branch check, the closed check) made its tests fail. `quote.rules.test.ts` +2 (add-ons listed, last orders at 15 and 16 minutes), `server/tests/weekly-windows.test.ts` (5: closing time through back-to-back windows, past midnight and a gap; the branch's local date; the 4:00 business day), the branch test (closing time). e2e `shop-orders-api.test.ts` (3, the built server with real sessions: 201 then 200 with the same order on a retry, read back; signed out 401, unverified 403, no key 400, a changed total 409; someone else's order 404), `checkout-quote.test.ts`, `shop-menu.test.ts`, `shop-account.test.ts`, `branches.test.ts`.

### D100: Review order and the order placed (step 6.2b, the pages), 2026-09-29

- **Context:** the customer's side of placing an order (D99's `POST /api/shop/orders`), from the owner's reviewed mockups (frames 5, 8 and 9 as the base; the owner's answers: the gates as a dialog, a price change shown on the same page, the order page with only "Back to the menu"). Mockups are layout references; Nuxt UI decides styling (D74).
- **Decision:**
  - **`/checkout`, Review order** (`app/features/menu/`: it reads the menu's order in this browser): the order type (pickup at the branch as soon as possible, or dine-in at the table a QR code named, with Switch to pickup), the lines (quantity 1–20, Remove, "Add a note" up to 140 characters), and the summary beside them from `lg` or a `BottomActionBar` below with the total and "Place order · $x". Prices come only from the server's quote; a note isn't sent to the quote (it never changes a price), so typing doesn't ask again.
  - **Gates before the request:** signed out, the button reads "Sign in to place order" and opens a sign-in gate (Sign in / Create account, back to `/checkout`: the order stays in this browser); an unverified email opens the verify gate (Resend, "I've verified, continue"). A dialog, a bottom sheet on phones (`useLayoutContext`: these pages render in the browser only). The server's 401 and 403 `EMAIL_NOT_VERIFIED` open the same gates.
  - **Price changes the customer didn't make** (a new quote with other unit prices than the one they saw) are kept until the order is placed: a warning, the old unit price struck through on the line, the old total struck through, the new total on the button. Placing sends `expectedTotalMinor`; the server's 409 `PRICES_CHANGED` asks again and shows the change, so pressing the button again accepts it. No separate "Accept changes" step (the owner's review).
  - **Placing keeps its own state** (`placing`, `failure`) like the account forms (D97): its failures are shown on the page next to the order, never as a toast, which `useMutation` would add. The `Idempotency-Key` is made once per order as sent and made again whenever the body changes, so Try again after a lost answer returns the order already placed, never a second one.
  - **A dead table QR** is refused with the reason and "Switch to pickup"; never switched silently (Q42). The table token is now kept with the table context (`setTable(scanned, token)`), and the table counts only at its own branch.
  - **`/orders/<id>`** (`app/features/orders/`, the customer's orders; tracking grows here in 6.5): the pickup number as the hero (three digits), the status badge, what to do next, pay by the time 30 minutes on, the lines with notes, the total, the branch and order type; "Back to the menu" only. Its own address, so a reload or another tab shows it; placing replaces `/checkout` in the history.
  - **Browser-only** (`routeRules` `ssr: false` for `/checkout` and `/orders/**`): everything on them belongs to this visitor, so the server would render only a placeholder.
  - **The order in this browser** holds 30 different lines at most (the server's limit, D98): a new line past it isn't added and a warning toast says so; lines keep their notes across reloads.
- **Alternatives:** `useMutation` for placing (its error toast would repeat the failure the page already shows next to the order); an "Accept changes" screen for new prices (rejected in the review: the struck price and the new total on the button say the same with one step less); server-rendering the two pages (they are per visitor; a server render would only show a skeleton); keeping the idempotency key for the page's lifetime (a changed order would replay the old one).
- **Verified:** unit `checkout.test.ts` (7: notes only when placing, the 140 cut, the 30-line cap, what counts as a price change, each refusal's meaning), `order.test.ts` (3); e2e `shop-checkout.test.ts` (6, on the seeded database, no mocks: signed out → gate → sign in → back → note → placed → the order page, reload, the order emptied; the verify gate and "I've verified, continue"; a price changed in the database under the open page; dine-in by QR; a table archived under the open page → Switch to pickup; the phone bar and sheet); screenshots at 1440 and 390, light and dark. Found while testing: `shop-orders-api.test.ts` used the customer `shop-account.test.ts` verifies, so it failed when the account file ran first; it now has its own (`apiUnverified`).

### D101: The counter on the server: queue, payment and actions (step 6.3a), 2026-09-29

- **Context:** after an order is placed (D99), the counter takes the payment, which starts preparation (D45: no accept step), then marks it ready and completed, or cancels it. The plan had the queue (6.3) and the payment (6.4) as two steps, but without the payment nothing leaves "To pay", so the owner agreed to fold 6.4 into 6.3 (server 6.3a, pages 6.3b). The owner's answers (2026-09-29, the defaults offered): **Q36**, staff and managers may cancel a paid order before it's ready, recording how the money went back, and after that only an admin refund; riel is **rounded up to ៛100**; **KHQR** without a Bakong connection (the cashier checks the merchant app, then confirms, with an optional reference); the riel rate on an admin **Payments** page; the queue refreshes every 10 seconds (6.3b); the counter is for branch staff, managers and admins.
- **Decision:**
  - **Routes** under `/api/counter/{branchId}/orders` (the branch from the path, checked against membership, D47): the queue, one order, and `pay`, `ready`, `complete`, `cancel`, each with the branch permission the D45 roles already grant (`payment: ['collect']`, `order: ['ready' | 'complete' | 'cancel' | 'read']`).
  - **Every command** takes the order's `version` and an `Idempotency-Key`, and answers the order as it is now. The status change is a conditional update (same status and version) followed by `requireOneChange`, then its `order_events` row, the payment and the audit entry, in one batch stored under the key (`withIdempotency`, D50). A version or status that moved on is 409 `ORDER_CHANGED` with the order's state in words; a retry with the same key returns the first answer, even after the order moved on or the riel rate changed.
  - **Three layers against a double payment:** the version guard; the unique (order, version) of `order_events`; the unique `order_id` of `counter_payments`. Removing the first alone left the tests green (the second caught it); removing both failed the pay-versus-cancel race and the change-in-between test.
  - **The payment** is always the order's total. Cash in riel uses the rate in force, which must be the one the screen showed (409 `EXCHANGE_RATE_CHANGED`; `NO_EXCHANGE_RATE` without one), and stores the riel asked for (`toRiel`, rounded up to ៛100, integer arithmetic, shared with the app) and the rate. Paying past the 30 minutes is refused (`PAYMENT_EXPIRED`); expired unpaid orders leave the queue, and the expiry task (6.6) marks them cancelled.
  - **Cancel:** unpaid or preparing, with a reason (`customer_changed_mind`, `item_unavailable`, `other` with words). A paid one must say how the money went back (`cash` or `khqr`, the full amount, stored on the payment); an unpaid one may not (400 on `returnMethod`). Ready or completed: 409 `ORDER_NOT_CANCELLABLE` (an admin refund, later).
  - **`order_events`**: one row per version, from placing on (placing now writes version 1). The customer's tracking (6.5) and reports read it.
  - **The queue** (`GET …/orders`): to pay (not past their time), preparing and ready, oldest first, with lines, the customer's name and the payment, plus the riel rate and the server's clock (countdowns don't depend on the tablet's). The whole active list each time: a cafe's open orders are few, and one poll serves the board.
  - **The riel rate** (`exchange_rates`, append-only): `GET`/`POST /api/admin/exchange-rates` (`settings: ['manage']`); the rate in force is the latest set before now; setting the same rate again writes nothing.
  - **The retry key** lives in `idempotency_keys` (24 hours), like placing an order; the data model's planned `counter_payments.idempotency_key` column was dropped as a duplicate.
- **Alternatives:** a separate "accept" step (D45 says no); a partial or split payment (not in the launch scope: one payment of the full total); a pay command without the rate (the riel shown could differ from the riel recorded); keeping expired unpaid orders payable until the expiry task runs (the rule is 30 minutes; a customer past it places a new order); per-order polling (one list serves the whole board).
- **Verified:** server `counter.service.test.ts` (23: the queue's contents and order; each command and its event, payment and audit entry; riel rounding and the rate checks; KHQR reference; the 30-minute limit; a stale version; two cashiers paying at once (one payment); pay versus cancel at once (one wins); a change landing between read and write (the guard; checked to fail with the version guard and the event index removed); replays, including after a rate change, and a key reused with another body (422); ready, complete and their order; cancel unpaid, paid (with how the money went back) and ready (refused); the rate history); e2e `counter-orders-api.test.ts` (3, over HTTP on the seeded database with a real cashier: queue, pay without a key (400), replay, `ORDER_CHANGED`, ready, cancel refused, complete, the customer's view; a customer or another branch 404, signed out 401; "Other" needs words).

### D102: The counter app and the Payments page (step 6.3b), 2026-09-29

- **Context:** the counter's pages on D101's routes, from the owner's frames (queue board, Take payment in cash USD and KHQR, cancel dialogs, empty and closed states, the admin Payments page) and the review's answers (2026-09-29, the defaults offered): **no drag-and-drop** between columns (a payment needs its method, orders only move forward, a drag can fire by accident); **phones show one list at a time** as tabs; **KHQR without a code on screen** (no Bakong connection: the customer scans the counter's KHQR, the cashier checks the merchant app); the header's **Sold out button waits** for its own page (6.3c); **sign-in at `/counter/sign-in`**, one branch goes straight to its queue, several (or an admin) choose; a chime for new orders, mutable. Colors from Nuxt UI (D74): To pay warning, Preparing info, Ready success, like the customer's order page. The frames for cash in riel and the conflict and network states weren't drawn; they follow the prompts.
- **Decision:**
  - **A counter workspace** under `/counter` (`app/features/counter/`, layout `counter`, browser-only), for branch staff, managers and admins: the admin app stays admins only (D52). It has **its own session**, `useCounterSession()`: Better Auth's sign-in, then **`GET /api/counter/me`** (new, in the identity feature: the active branches the person works at with the role; every active branch for a platform admin; answers on a temporary password; 401 signed out; 403 `NOT_STAFF` for an account at no branch, which the sign-in page signs out again). State keys `counter-session:*`; an identity change fires `app:session-changed`, so the session boundary (D29) clears the previous person's data. The API plugin ends both sessions on a 401, passes a required password change to both, and discards responses across either session's change (the generations add). `middleware/counter.global.ts` protects `/counter/**` and sends a temporary password to `/counter/change-password` (the admin's password rules, now exported by the auth feature).
  - **The queue** (`/counter/<branchId>`): `useApiQuery` refreshed every 10 seconds while the tab is visible (and on return, D22). Three columns from `sm` (a tablet at the counter), tabs with counts below; `useLayoutContext` picks which renders (the page is browser-only, so no hydration concern, D95). "Pay by" and "time ago" run on the server's clock (`serverTime` in the answer). New orders after the first load: "New" and an outline for a minute, and a Web Audio chime (no file) unless muted in this browser. Search by number (with or without zeros) or name. The branch's open state and next opening come from `/api/public/branches` (`openingText`, now public in the menu feature).
  - **Mark ready and Complete** are one tap on a card: `useMutation` (key and lock per order, a toast if refused, the `counter` queries refreshed). **Take payment and Cancel** run in the order panel and the cancel dialog, which keep their own submitting state and show a refusal next to the order with Reload (changed meanwhile, the rate changed) or Try again (no answer: the same `Idempotency-Key`, a new one whenever the request changes), like Review order (D100). `useMutation` would add a toast repeating the panel's message.
  - **Take payment:** Cash USD with an amount received (nothing = exact; less = "Short by", Confirm waits), quick amounts at the next $5, $10, $20 and $50 above the total, and the change; Cash riel at the rate in force (`toRiel`, shared with the server), the rate and who set it when, quick amounts at the next ៛10,000 / ៛20,000 / ៛50,000 / ៛100,000, the change in riel; KHQR as an instruction and an optional reference. The amount received only computes the change: the server records the order's total.
  - **Cancel:** a reason (words with "Other"); a paid order records how the money went back and shows the full amount as text; a dialog, a bottom sheet on phones.
  - **Payments** (`/admin/payments`, `app/features/exchange-rates/`, in the Admin group of the sidebar): the current rate, Change rate (1,000–10,000 in the form, as on the server), the history with the current one marked.
- **Alternatives:** extending the admin's `useAuth` to branch staff (the admin's routes, sidebar and permissions are admin-only; one composable would mix two workspaces' rules); letting staff into `/admin` (D52 keeps it admins only); a generated KHQR code (needs a Bakong merchant integration, not in scope); drag-and-drop (rejected in the review); the board on phones (nine cards in one scroll; the review chose tabs); server push instead of polling (no push channel yet, D22; 10 seconds is enough for a counter).
- **Verified:** unit `counter.test.ts` (6: a card's words, time ago and the last 5 minutes, search, quick amounts, change and short, each refusal's meaning); server `identity.service.test.ts` (+3: the counter session's branches and roles, an admin's, temporary password, 401 and `NOT_STAFF`); e2e `counter.test.ts` (5, on the real seeded server with a cashier at Riverside: sign in → cash with short and change → paid → ready → completed; riel at ៛4,100 → ៛9,300 and change → cancel the paid order with the money returned; a payment recorded by another cashier meanwhile → the message → Reload shows it preparing; phone tabs and a full-screen panel; a customer refused at sign-in) and `payments.test.ts` (3, mocked); the full e2e suite (323) after the shared plugin change; screenshots at 1180 light and dark and 390.

### D103: Rate limits per real client address, and SQLite waits for a lock (found in CI on step 6.3b), 2026-09-29

- **Context:** PR #45's e2e shard 3 failed in CI, and locally one run in two. Two causes, both in shared setup, which the new counter tests only made visible:
  1. **Better Auth read the client address from `x-forwarded-for` only.** Without it (our node e2e server, and any host that doesn't send it), Better Auth logs a warning and puts **every request in one shared bucket**, so all visitors together get 5 sign-ins a minute. The e2e files sign in many times a minute from one machine and got refused. On Cloudflare the address Cloudflare sets itself is `cf-connecting-ip`; `x-forwarded-for` can be sent by the client, so trusting it would let anyone skip the limit.
  2. **Local SQLite answered `SQLITE_BUSY` at once** when another process held the write lock. In e2e, tests write to the server's database file directly (change a price, archive a table, set a riel rate) while the server handles requests, so a request failed with 500 ("database is locked", seen in the server's log). D1 (staging, production) has no file lock.
- **Decision:**
  - Better Auth reads **`cf-connecting-ip`** (`advanced.ipAddress.ipAddressHeaders`, `identity.auth.ts`). Hosting anywhere else needs that host's trusted header here.
  - Local SQLite waits **up to 5 seconds** for a lock (`hub.db.connection.timeout`, passed to the libsql client; D1 ignores it). The tests' own connections to that file wait the same way (`e2eDatabase()` in `test/e2e/support/database.ts`): CI then showed the test side refused while the server wrote.
  - e2e tests that sign in against the real server present **their own client address** (`test/e2e/support/client-address.ts`: `asNewVisitor(page)` for pages, `clientHeaders()` for `fetch`), as separate visitors behind Cloudflare would. The limits themselves stay as configured, and apply in the e2e build.
- **Alternatives:** turning rate limiting off in e2e (it would stop testing the real configuration, and the shared bucket would stay unnoticed); `x-forwarded-for` (forgeable); fewer sign-ins in the tests (the shared bucket is the real problem); retrying a busy write in the app (SQLite's own wait is the standard way).
- **Verified:** CI's shard 3 reproduced locally (fails with 429s, then with `SQLITE_BUSY` in the server's log); with both changes, shard 3 passed 4 runs in a row with no `SQLITE_BUSY`; the full e2e suite (323) passes; the build carries `timeout: 5000`. **Needs verification on staging:** that Workers requests carry `cf-connecting-ip` (Cloudflare documents it on every proxied request).

### D104: Unpaid orders cancelled after 30 minutes (step 6.6), 2026-09-30

- **Context:** D45 cancels an order still unpaid 30 minutes after it's placed. Since 6.3a the counter refuses its payment at the deadline and the queue hides it, but its status stayed `awaiting_payment`: the customer's order page kept saying "Pay at the counter", and reports would count it as open.
- **Decision:**
  - A Nitro task, **`orders:expire-unpaid`, every minute** (with the outbox; the operations doc had planned every 5 minutes: once a minute keeps the customer's page within a minute of the counter's refusal, at the cost of one indexed query).
  - It reads up to **100** unpaid orders due at or before now, oldest first (index `orders_status_due_idx` on (status, `payment_due_at`), migration `0016`), and cancels **each in its own batch**: the conditional update on status and version with `requireOneChange`, an `order_events` row with **no actor** (the system) and the note "Not paid within 30 minutes" (no reason code: the reasons are the counter's), and an audit entry `orders.order.expire` with no actor. A backlog clears over the next runs.
  - **A payment at that moment wins:** a cashier's payment landing between the task's read and its write stops that order's batch (the guard, or the event's unique version, D101), counted as skipped; the other orders go on. The deadline is the same instant as the counter's `PAYMENT_EXPIRED`, so there's no gap where an order is neither payable nor cancelled for longer than a minute.
- **Alternatives:** one batch for all (one changed order would roll back the rest); cancelling at read time in each request (no single place, and the customer's page would still read a stale status between visits); a reason code `expired` (a new `CHECK` value means rebuilding `order_events` in SQLite; the missing actor already says it was the system).
- **Verified:** server `expiry.service.test.ts` (4: only unpaid orders past their time, paid and counter-cancelled ones untouched, the event, audit and the customer's view; exactly at the deadline and a second run finding nothing; a payment landing between read and write wins (checked to fail with both the version guard and the event index removed, like D101); one changed order doesn't stop the others); all server and unit tests (674).

### D105: The counter's Sold out page (step 6.3c), 2026-09-30

- **Context:** staff switch versions off when they run out (D64, on the server since 3.6), but no screen did it. The owner's tablet frame shows a searchable list of the menu with one switch per row. Q37 was still open: should a switch reset itself each business day?
- **Decision:**
  - **Q37 answered (owner, 2026-09-30): it stays until someone switches it back.** No daily reset; D64's table already works this way. The page is titled "Sold out", not "Sold out today". Each switch's label says its current state ("Available" or "Sold out").
  - **Route `/counter/<branchId>/sold-out`**, reached from a "Sold out" button in the queue's header (an icon on phones). The queue's page moved to `app/pages/counter/[branchId]/index.vue`, so the two routes are siblings, not parent and child.
  - **The rows are the branch's menu as customers see it now** (`GET /api/public/menu`): one row per version ("Iced Latte · Large"), in the menu's order, under its top-level category. Drafts, archived items and items outside their time rule aren't listed, because they can't be ordered now. The server still accepts a switch on them (D64), so nothing is lost if one is added later.
  - **Who and when:** the sold-out list (`GET /api/counter/{branchId}/sold-out`) now carries `updatedByName`, the name of whoever switched it off, joined from `user` (`null` if that account no longer exists). A row shows "Since 10:15 AM by Sophea" (the first name). **Sold out or not comes from that list** once it has loaded, from the menu's flag until then. The badge and the "Since" line come from one source, so they can't disagree while the two queries refresh.
  - **Filters:** search by name, the categories as chips (they scroll sideways on phones), and "N sold out" to show only those. Switching a row back on in that view **keeps it on screen** until the filter, search or category changes, so a slip can be undone where it happened.
  - The switch is a `useMutation` (`counter:sold-out`), keyed and locked per version. It sends `{ variationIds: [id], soldOut }` and has no version check (D64 sets a state). Success shows no toast (the row changes). A failure names the version. It invalidates `counter`. A customer's open menu shows the change on its next load, and Review order re-checks every line (D100).
- **Alternatives:**
  - A daily reset (the owner chose "keep").
  - Listing every item the admin has, drafts included (the counter only needs what's on sale now).
  - A new counter endpoint combining menu and states (the public menu already has the rows and flags; one join added the name).
  - Whole-item switches (versions run out separately; a whole-item switch can come later as one request with all versions).
  - Letting the row disappear at once in the "sold out" view (found in the e2e test: a slip couldn't be undone).
- **Verified:**
  - server `soldout.service.test.ts`, 13 tests, including the name and a missing account.
  - unit `app/features/counter/tests/sold-out.test.ts`, 3 tests: rows, filters and the kept row, and which source decides sold out.
  - e2e `counter.test.ts` against the real seeded server, 2 new tests: switched off from the queue's button with "Since … by Sophea" shown, the customer menu following, "N sold out" listing it, switched back while filtered and still shown, then gone from the filter. The phone layout: chips, switches, no sideways scroll. Removing the kept-row logic makes the first test fail.
  - Screenshots at 1180 px, light and dark, and at 390 px.

### D106: Order tracking on the server (step 6.5a), 2026-09-30

- **Context:** the customer's order page (D100) knew only "waiting for payment". Tracking (the owner's 6.5 frames and review, 2026-09-30, "use your defaults") needs each step's time, the payment, who cancelled and why, a list of the customer's orders, and the customer's own cancel while unpaid (D45).
- **Decision:**
  - **`Order` grows** (contract `shared/contracts/orders.ts`): `version`; `paidAt`, `readyAt`, `completedAt`; `payment` (method, amount, riel, when, and how it went back if the order was cancelled after paying). The cashier's name and the KHQR reference stay at the counter.
  - **`cancellation: { by, reason }`**, read from the cancel event's actor:
    - `by` is `system` when there is no actor (D104), `customer` when the actor is the order's customer, and `cafe` otherwise. A staff member cancelling their own order counts as the customer.
    - The customer sees only the cafe's reason code, never the staff's own words (they may be internal).
  - **`GET /api/shop/orders?page=&pageSize=`** returns `{ inProgress, past }`:
    - `inProgress`: every order still in play, newest first (at most 50; in practice a few, since two can be unpaid).
    - `past`: a `Page` of completed and cancelled orders, newest first, with the id breaking ties.
    - Each entry is an `OrderSummary` with `itemCount` (units). The existing `orders_customer_idx` answers it, so there's no migration.
  - **`POST /api/shop/orders/{id}/cancel` `{ version }`**, with an `Idempotency-Key`, for a verified customer:
    - It is refused unless the order is waiting for payment. Paid meanwhile: 409 `ORDER_NOT_CANCELLABLE`, "Order 042 is paid now, so it can't be cancelled here. Ask at the counter." Already cancelled: 409 `ORDER_CHANGED`.
    - The event records the customer as the actor with the existing reason `customer_changed_mind`. A new reason value would mean rebuilding `order_events` for its `CHECK`, and the actor already says who did it.
    - It is audited as `orders.order.customer_cancel`.
  - **One command runner:** the counter's guarded batch moved to `server/features/orders/commands.ts` (`runOrderCommand`) and is shared by the counter's commands and the customer's cancel:
    - the find and ownership check;
    - the version check;
    - the update guarded by status and version, with `requireOneChange`;
    - the event, whose unique version is a second guard;
    - the audit entry;
    - idempotency, and the "what it is now" refusal. Ownership and the refusal's wording are per caller.
  - **Correction for 6.5b:** the order page's "we'll bring it to Table T12" (D100) becomes "collect at the counter". No table-service step exists, and the owner accepted that default for 6.5.
- **Alternatives:**
  - A separate "my orders" endpoint per status (two requests for one screen).
  - Cursor pagination (the API's convention is `page` and `pageSize`; the page drops an entry it already shows).
  - A new cancel reason `customer` (a table rebuild for what the actor already says).
  - Showing the staff's note (it may be internal).
  - Copying the counter's runner (the race handling is the subtle part and belongs in one place).
- **Verified:**
  - server `tracking.service.test.ts`, 8 tests:
    - each step's time, version and payment;
    - the three kinds of cancellation, with the money returned and the staff note hidden;
    - the list: in progress, pages, only their own, item counts;
    - the customer's cancel: event, audit, and the unpaid limit freed;
    - someone else's order is 404;
    - paid, whatever version is sent, and already cancelled;
    - a replay answers the same and cancels once;
    - a payment between read and write wins. With `requireOneChange` removed the event's unique version still refuses it; with both guards removed the test fails, like D101.
  - The counter's tests pass unchanged on the shared runner.
  - e2e `shop-orders-api.test.ts`, +1 test over HTTP: list, 401, 404, 400 without a key, cancel, replay, then 409.
  - All server and unit tests pass (686).

### D107: An AI assistant for admins, on any provider (phase 9 plan), 2026-09-30

- **Context:**
  - The owner wants an assistant that helps with the portal: answering questions, guiding through workflows, drafting menu items from a description, and improving or translating menu wording.
  - They will use their own API key and may choose **any AI provider**.
  - Asked to build it before 6.5b.
  - Review answers: "use your defaults", with the panel as `USidebar` instead of a slide-over.
- **Decision:** the plan in [plans/ai-assistant.md](plans/ai-assistant.md), steps 9.0–9.3. Its rules:
  - **The AI suggests, a person saves.** No write tools. Drafts open in the existing item editor; wording suggestions replace a field only when chosen. Every save goes through the existing services, with their validation, `version` and audit.
  - **Provider-neutral through the Vercel AI SDK** (`ai`, with `@ai-sdk/anthropic` / `openai` / `google` / `openai-compatible`, `@ai-sdk/valibot`, `@ai-sdk/vue`):
    - The provider, model and key are settings. One file (`assistant.model.ts`, built in 9.0) names providers; no other feature imports AI.
    - Supported means covered by the plan's quality check.
    - Nuxt UI 4.11's chat components are built for it; it runs on Workers (checked on staging in 9.0).
  - **Knowledge is Markdown in the code** (`server/features/assistant/help/`), sent whole with each question, most stable text first so the provider's prompt caching can reuse it:
    - A table would need an editor and a second source of truth for text that changes with the app.
    - There is no search index (RAG) while the guide is small.
    - There is no answer cache on our side.
  - **No stored conversation or preferences:**
    - The chat lives in the browser tab and is cleared at the session boundary (D29).
    - The server records only usage metadata (`assistant_usage`), for a daily limit per admin (100, guarded against two requests taking the last slot) and cost reports, kept 90 days.
  - **Admins only** (`assistant: ['use']`). The assistant is off, with routes 404 and buttons hidden, without a key.
  - The panel is **`USidebar`** on the right (owner). It is docked from `lg`, so the page stays usable while following the steps, and is a slide-over below `lg`.
- **Alternatives:**
  - Each provider's own SDK: the Anthropic SDK is Claude-only, and writing our own adapters duplicates the AI SDK.
  - A router service with one key for every provider (another company sees all the data).
  - Cloudflare Workers AI only (weaker models, especially in Khmer).
  - A help-content table.
  - Saved history.
  - A slide-over panel (covers the page being explained).
  - Each alternative, and every other option deferred, with what would bring it back, is in [plans/ai-later.md](plans/ai-later.md).
- **Open:** Q43, the provider account and key for staging and production, with a spending cap. Translation waits for decisions on languages and the customer site's language switch ([ai-later.md](plans/ai-later.md)).

### D108: The AI assistant's groundwork (step 9.0), 2026-09-30

- **Context:** the plan (D107, [plans/ai-assistant.md](plans/ai-assistant.md)) starts with what every later step needs: the packages, the provider setting, the feature switch, the permission, and a daily limit per admin with usage records.
- **Decision:**
  - **Packages:** `ai` 7.0 with `@ai-sdk/anthropic`, `@ai-sdk/openai`, `@ai-sdk/google` and `@ai-sdk/openai-compatible`. `@ai-sdk/vue` comes with 9.1, and `@ai-sdk/valibot` with 9.2, when they're used.
  - **Settings:** runtime config `ai` (`NUXT_AI_PROVIDER`, `_MODEL`, `_API_KEY`, `_BASE_URL`, `_DAILY_LIMIT`), read by `assistantSettingsFrom`:
    - **no key** means the assistant is **off**: its routes answer 404, like Sample data;
    - **a key with an unknown provider, no model, or an OpenAI-compatible provider without its URL** is refused with 500 `AI_NOT_CONFIGURED`. The key shows the assistant was meant to be on, so it isn't silently switched off;
    - a daily limit that isn't a positive whole number falls back to 100.
  - **`assistant.model.ts` is the only file that names providers.** It turns the settings into the AI SDK's `LanguageModel`.
  - **Route glue `requireAssistant(event)`** (`server/utils/assistant.ts`): switched off first (404 for everyone), then `assistant: ['use']`, which only admins hold.
  - **`assistant_usage`** (migration `0017`):
    - A row is inserted **before** each call to the provider, with its local `day` (Asia/Phnom_Penh), in a batch with a `requireAtMost` guard: that admin's rows that day, this one included, at most the limit. Past it: 429 `AI_LIMIT_REACHED`, and the provider is never called.
    - After the call the row gets the tokens, including cached input where the provider reports it, and `ok` or `error`. A failed call still counts, since it may have cost tokens.
    - Metadata only: never the question or the answer.
    - The user foreign key **cascades**: an admin's usage is their own data and goes with the account.
    - `assistant:purge-usage` (daily, 03:15 UTC) removes rows older than 90 days.
  - **Streams:** usage is finished in `streamText`'s `onFinish` / `onError` through `event.waitUntil`, so the Worker writes the row after the response has streamed.
    - The provider's error is logged; the admin sees only "The assistant can't answer right now."
    - One request may take up to 30 s (`AbortSignal.timeout`), with one retry.
  - **`POST /api/admin/assistant/ping`:** a tiny streamed answer (the AI SDK's UI message stream) to check settings, provider, streaming through the Worker and usage on staging. It counts against the limit and is removed in 9.1.
- **Alternatives:**
  - Counting the limit from midnight timestamps instead of a stored `day` (timezone arithmetic in SQL; a text day is simpler and indexable).
  - A limit kept in KV (outside the database's atomic batch).
  - Switching the assistant off when its settings are incomplete (hides a deployment mistake).
  - Restricting user deletion because of usage rows (the rows aren't worth keeping without the person).
- **Verified:**
  - server `assistant.service.test.ts`, 11 tests, with the AI SDK's `MockLanguageModelV4`, so no test calls a provider:
    - settings: off without a key, refused when incomplete, the limit's fallback;
    - each provider built from its setting;
    - the limit per admin and per local day, with midnight in Phnom Penh starting a new day;
    - two requests racing for the last slot: one passes. The test fails with the guard removed;
    - a streamed ping records its tokens, with cached input;
    - a provider failure is logged and recorded as `error`;
    - past the limit the provider is never called;
    - the 90-day cleanup;
    - an account removed takes its usage with it.
  - `identity.permissions.test.ts`: `assistant: ['use']` for admins only.
  - The staging Worker builds with the packages: 6.66 MB, 1.73 MB gzipped, within Cloudflare's limits.
  - **Verified on staging (2026-09-30):** the owner's `POST /api/admin/assistant/ping` as an admin streamed OpenAI `gpt-5.4-mini`'s answer through the Worker. The `assistant_usage` rows show `ok` with 20 input and 12 output tokens, written after the stream through `waitUntil`. The earlier attempts with `google` and the same OpenAI key were refused by Google and recorded as `error` with no tokens, counting against the limit as designed.

### D109: The help assistant (step 9.1), 2026-09-30

- **Context:** the first feature on the groundwork (D108): admins ask how to do something in the portal and get short steps with buttons to the right pages. The owner skipped the mockup round for it: Nuxt UI's own chat components, as they are.
- **Decision:**
  - **The help guide** is Markdown in the code, one file per screen (`server/features/assistant/help/*.md`): general use, how the menu fits together, Menu items, Categories, Options, Add-ons, Availability, Staff, Branch, Payments, the counter app and how orders work, Sample data. Each holds the screen's purpose, its tasks as numbered steps with the exact labels, and its errors in plain words. It was drafted from the code (labels, limits and server messages checked against it) for the owner to review. **A change to a screen updates its page in the same pull request.**
  - **What the model gets**, most stable first, so the provider's prompt cache reuses the start (Anthropic's cache point marked on it; OpenAI and Gemini cache long starts by themselves):
    1. the rules: answer only from the guide, say "I don't know" rather than guess, exact labels in bold, numbered steps, the question's language, never claim to have done something, plain text;
    2. the page list;
    3. the whole guide (Sample data only where it exists, D94);
    4. then the page the admin is on;
    5. then the conversation.
    
    The rules and the page are the SDK's `instructions` (AI SDK 7 refuses system messages among the messages).
  - **Links:** a `link_to_page` tool whose input is a key of the page list (`pages.ts`: the admin's fixed routes and the counter app), checked by a validator on the tool. An invented key becomes a tool error, never a button. At most 3 steps (text, then links).
  - **The conversation** lives in the browser tab and is sent whole, its latest 20 messages, each checked with the SDK's `safeValidateUIMessages` (message shapes, and the link tool's inputs and outputs, so a forged link to another site is refused). It must end with a question of at most 2,000 characters, and no system messages are accepted. It's checked **before** a daily slot is taken; nothing is stored (A4).
  - **`GET /api/admin/assistant`** (the limit and today's use, 404 when off) tells the app whether to show the assistant. This replaces the plan's public `assistant.enabled` flag, which would have been a second setting to keep in step with the key.
  - **The panel:** `USidebar` on the right with `collapsible="offcanvas"`, 26rem wide, beside the page from `lg` (the page narrows) and a slide-over below, full width on phones.
    - Contents: suggested questions per page, `UChatMessages`, `UChatPrompt` with `UChatPromptSubmit` (Stop while answering), Clear chat, an error row with Try again (not after the daily limit), and "AI can make mistakes: check before you act", with the questions left today once 10 or fewer remain.
    - The panel is mounted with the shell, closed. Mounted at the moment it opened, `USidebar`'s own phone handling closed it again.
    - Closed, it's `inert` and renders no contents. Otherwise its question box, which focuses itself, took the focus from every page: the full e2e suite caught it, with typing and single-key shortcuts going into the hidden panel.
    - It's opened by an **Assistant** button in the sidebar footer (on phones, inside the menu) and `Ctrl`/`⌘`+`/`, which also works while typing. **Not the navbar**, as the plan said: each page owns its navbar, and the button would have had to be added to every page.
  - **Answers** are shown as text: one paragraph per line, `**bold**` as bold, everything else as written (Vue text nodes, never HTML), with no Markdown library.
  - **The browser's request** goes through `apiFetch` (`responseType: 'stream'`). A lost session, a required password change and an old identity's answer are handled like any request. An error answer's JSON is read, so the limit's message shows. A session change clears the chat.
  - **Build pitfall found:** Nitro imports `.md` as text, and a chunk holding such text skips Nitro's `import.meta` rewrite. With the guide bundled into Nitro's main chunk, the server looked for its static files in `server/chunks/public` and every page lost its scripts and styles. The guide is now imported dynamically (its own chunk). The server tests load `.md` the same way through a small Vite plugin (`vitest.config.ts`).
  - **The quality check** is `server/features/assistant/eval/help.eval.ts`: 25 questions (5 in Khmer, one outside the guide, one prompt-injection) with the facts a good answer names. It runs only on request (`ASSISTANT_EVAL=1`, with the provider settings) and never in CI.
- **Alternatives:**
  - Markdown links written by the model, checked in the browser (a model can write any address; the tool's key can't be anything else).
  - Rendering the answer with a Markdown library (`@nuxtjs/mdc`: a module for two formats the prompt allows).
  - Keeping the guide in the database (D107: it changes with the code).
- **Verified:**
  - server `assistant.service.test.ts`, 23 tests, with `MockLanguageModelV4`:
    - the prompt's parts and order, the same start on every page and question, the cache mark;
    - Sample data in the guide and the page list only where it's on;
    - a link becomes a button and the answer continues; an invented page and a Sample data link where it's off are refused. Both tests fail with the tool's validator removed;
    - the earlier conversation is sent with its links;
    - bad conversations are refused before a slot is taken: an answer last, an empty or too long question, a system message, broken parts, a forged link;
    - provider failure, the limit, and the status per admin;
    - the page list and every `/admin` or `/counter` path in the guide exist as routes; every help file has a title.
  - unit `chat.test.ts` (5): the latest 20, links once each and only finished ones, text around links, bold, suggestions per page.
  - e2e `assistant.test.ts` (6), the chat route mocked with the SDK's event stream:
    - hidden when off, the shortcut included;
    - docked beside the page, a suggestion answered with bold labels, a link that navigates with the panel and conversation kept;
    - follow-ups carry the conversation, and Clear chat;
    - a failed answer and Try again;
    - the daily limit without Try again;
    - on a phone, opened from the menu, a link closes the sheet and opens the page.
  - Screenshots 1440 and 390.
  - **Not verified yet:** Stop in the browser (a mocked answer arrives whole).
  - **Quality check against OpenAI `gpt-5.4-mini` (owner, 2026-09-30): 23 of 25.** English and Khmer answers used the right labels and links. Out-of-scope and injection questions were refused. Tokens: 351,630 in (329,728 cached, 94%), 2,163 out; 1–6 s per answer. Two misses, fixed afterwards:
    - "How do I add a cashier?" stopped at **Add** and left out handing over the temporary password. The guide now puts it in the same step, and the rules say to give the steps up to the end of the task.
    - "Where are the sales reports?" answered "I don’t know" correctly; the check missed the curly apostrophe. The check now normalizes apostrophes.
    - Also tightened: no link to a page the answer doesn't send the admin to (one answer linked the Dashboard), and no offers of more help.

### D110: Reports on the server (step 8.1a), 2026-09-30

- **Context:** the owner's reporting scope (sales, best sellers, order history, print, CSV, Telegram), reviewed against the repository ([plans/reports.md](plans/reports.md)). The owner accepted the defaults R1–R6 (admins only, Open order in the counter app, first names only, no summary on closed days, 93 days, gross item sales with a Refunded column). Stage 1: the numbers, on the server.
- **Decision:**
  - **The order lifecycle is unchanged.** One status per order (`awaiting_payment → preparing → ready → completed`, or `cancelled`) and one payment row. Reports derive two views:
    - **Payment:** `unpaid`, `paid`, `refunded` (the money returned), `not_paid` (cancelled with no payment).
    - **Progress:** the status.
  - **Definitions** are in the plan's table. The main ones:
    - **Paid sales:** counted when the payment was collected, each order once (the payment's unique index).
    - **Refunds:** counted when the money went back.
    - **Net sales:** paid sales minus refunds.
    - **Average order:** paid sales ÷ paid orders, whole cents, rounded half up.
    - **Cash riel:** keeps the riel taken at each payment's own rate.
    - **Cancelled, not paid** and **Orders placed** count by the business day the order was placed.
    - **Current orders:** live, and only when the period includes today.
  - **Business days** reuse D99's rule: 04:00 to 04:00 in the **branch's** time zone. A period's instants are `[from 04:00, to+1 04:00)`, found with `zonedInstant`, which works in any zone, daylight saving included. At most 93 days.
  - **The category as sold:** `order_lines` gains `category_id` and `category_name`, filled at placement from the menu. Migration `0018` fills existing lines from the current menu (only staging test orders exist) and adds indexes on `order_lines.item_id` and `counter_payments(branch_id, returned_at)`. Names, versions, add-ons and prices were already kept as sold.
  - **Where the code lives:** in the **orders** feature (`reports.rules.ts` pure, `reports.repository.ts`, `reports.service.ts`), because every number comes from order tables. Sums are integers in SQL. Sales by item groups in SQL and filters, sorts and pages in the service: one branch sells a few hundred items at most, and the totals then cover every matching row.
  - **Routes** (`report: ['read']`):
    - `GET /api/admin/reports/summary`
    - `GET /api/admin/reports/items`
    - `GET /api/admin/reports/orders`
    - `GET /api/admin/reports/orders/{id}`
    
    Every answer carries the branch, the period and its instants, and `asOf`.
  - **Permissions:** `report: ['read', 'export']` for platform admins. `export` guards CSV and sending, from stage 2.
  - **Privacy:** the order detail shows the customer's first name only (R3).
- **Alternatives:**
  - A separate payment status column on orders (a second source of truth for the same fact).
  - Grouping by the UTC date or the browser's zone (wrong around midnight and 04:00).
  - A reports feature reading another feature's tables (the numbers are the orders feature's).
  - Pre-aggregated daily tables (not needed at one branch's volume).
- **Verified:**
