  - server `reports.service.test.ts`, 9 tests, on a hand-worked Monday of orders: cash USD, KHQR, cash riel, an expiry, a customer's cancel, a paid order refunded the next day, and a payment at 00:30 that still belongs to Monday. It checks:
    - business-day boundaries (03:59 and 04:00; New York in daylight saving);
    - period limits;
    - paid sales, the average and the payment split with riel;
    - the refund on the day the money went back, and net sales;
    - hourly and daily trends;
    - live counts, and an expired wait not counted;
    - names, categories and prices as sold after the menu was renamed and repriced;
    - filters, sorting, paging and totals;
    - order history filters and paging;
    - the detail's payment, riel, and a timeline naming the customer, the cashier and the system.
  - `identity.permissions.test.ts`: `report: ['export']` for admins only.
  - unit + server 725 pass; lint and typecheck clean.

### D111: The report pages, CSV and Print (step 8.1b), 2026-09-30

- **Context:** stage 2 of [plans/reports.md](plans/reports.md): the pages on D110's routes, from the owner's frames, with the deviations agreed in the review (Cash riel, the existing shell, `$` chart labels trimmed to the hours with sales, no Send to Telegram yet, Nuxt UI's selected row, a timeline naming only who acted).
- **Decision:**
  - **Pages:** `app/features/reports/` with **Reports → Summary, Sales by item, Order history** in the sidebar between Menu and Admin (`/admin/reports/summary`, `/items`, `/orders`; `/admin/reports` goes to Summary).
  - **The period and branch are in the URL** (`?from=&to=`, `&branch=` only when there are several) and remembered in the tab (`useState('report-scope')`), so moving between reports keeps them. Presets (Today, Yesterday, Last 7 days, This month) and a calendar range come from **the branch's business date sent by the server** (`GET /api/admin/reports/branches` gives `today`), never the browser's clock. A period no report can show (reversed, over 93 days, in the future, not a date) falls back to today.
  - **Summary:** four stat cards with the change against the period before, net sales, the chart (a few `div` bars with dollar gridlines, not a chart library: at most 24 or 93 bars; a screen-reader table with the same numbers), Payments, Current orders (live, only for today) or Orders placed, Best sellers, Cancelled not paid, and "How these are counted".
  - **Sales by item:** sorted **on the server** by each column's header (`SortButton`), the totals of every matching item as the table's footer, rows on phones with a sort select.
  - **Order history:** filters at once from `sm`; on phones in a bottom sheet applied together with **Apply filters**, shown as removable chips (the UI standard's compact filters). `?order=<id>` opens the order (a side panel, full screen on phones); Back closes it when this list opened it. Letters in the number search are dropped before asking (the server accepts digits only).
  - **CSV:** `GET /api/admin/reports/{summary,items,orders}.csv` (`report: ['export']`), the same query as the page without paging; every matching row in the page's order; UTF-8 with a byte-order mark, CRLF, RFC 4180 quoting; text starting with `= + - @`, tab or CR prefixed with `'`; money as exact decimals from cents; times in the branch's zone; `no-store`, never saved on the server. Orders stop at 20,000 with a 422 saying to narrow the period (R5). The file name (`riverside-2026-09-30-summary.csv`) is one shared function (`csvFilename` in the contract). The app downloads through `apiFetch` inside a `useMutation` (the session, `ApiError`, pending state and toasts), then saves a `Blob`; the browser's text decoding drops the byte-order mark, so it's put back.
  - **Print:** the browser's print (`window.print()`). A print-only header (cafe, branch, report, period with business day and zone, figures as of, printed at and by), toolbars and the shell hidden (`print:hidden`), the dashboard's fixed scrolling frame released, A4 portrait, black on white in either color mode (the color tokens restated under `@media print` in `tailwind.css`), cards kept whole (`break-inside-avoid`). Page numbers are the browser's.
  - **The help assistant** knows the reports: `help/reports.md`, and the three pages in its link list.
- **Alternatives:**
  - A chart library (`@unovis/vue`, Nuxt UI's dashboard template): a dependency for two bar charts. Superseded by D207: the chart is now Nuxt UI's `UChart`.
  - A plain `<a href="….csv">` download: a refusal (over 20,000 orders, a lost session) would open a JSON page instead of a message.
  - A server-rendered PDF: printing to PDF from the browser already gives one.
  - Dates from the browser's clock: wrong between midnight and 04:00, and for a browser in another zone.
- **Found while building:** the scope watcher first watched the scope **object**; it wrote the remembered scope, which the scope reads, so each run made a new object and ran again: the page hung. It now watches the values as a string. Every reports e2e test would time out again with the old watcher.
- **Verified:**
  - server `reports.service.test.ts` 12 (3 new: the CSV cell rules, formula guard, quoting, Khmer text, exact amounts, file names; the three exports on the worked Monday; `today` at 03:00 still the day before);
  - unit `app/features/reports/tests/reports.test.ts` 8 (presets, labels, the period fallback including 93 and 94 days, the chart trimming and scale, badges, the timeline's words);
  - e2e `reports.test.ts` 13 on a mocked API (the Summary for today and a preset, the period kept across reports, a bad URL period, empty and error states, the CSV download's bytes, BOM and query, a refused download, print media, Sales by item sort and category, Order history filters, the panel with Back, `?order=` from a link, the number search, and the phone's filter sheet, chips and full-screen order);
  - screenshots at 1440 and 390 px, print media;
  - lint, typecheck, unit + server 736, the full e2e suite 345.

### D112: Telegram connection and Send to Telegram (step 8.1c), 2026-09-30

- **Context:** stage 3 of [plans/reports.md](plans/reports.md): connect the cafe's Telegram chats and send a report there, from the owner's frames (the Telegram page, the connect dialog, Send to Telegram), with the review's changes (a group warns everyone sees it, Disconnect asks first, every admin sees every chat, the preview is the real message, a failure stays in the dialog with Try again).
- **Decision:**
  - **grammY's `Api`** (the owner suggested grammY) for calls to Telegram: typed Bot API methods, `GrammyError` with Telegram's `error_code` and `retry_after`, multipart uploads for the CSV (`InputFile`), and a `fetch` option the server tests replace with a fake Telegram. Its web build runs in the Worker (checked in the staging build). **Not** its `Bot`/`webhookCallback`: our webhook handles three kinds of update against our own services, and `Bot` would add a middleware framework and a `getMe` call per cold start.
  - **Feature `notifications`** (server and app; shown as "Telegram" under Admin): `telegram_destinations` (one row per chat, `connected` / `blocked` / `disconnected`) and `telegram_links` (one-time connect codes). Contracts in `shared/contracts/notifications.ts`.
  - **Connecting:** the portal makes a link (`t.me/<bot>?start=<code>` for a private chat, `?startgroup=<code>` for a group). The code is 32 random bytes; only its SHA-256 is stored; it works **once** for **10 minutes** (a conditional update guarded in its batch, so two chats racing with one code connect one). A private chat is connected when its owner presses Start. A group needs the Telegram user who added the bot to be **an admin of that group** (`getChatMember`), then a **portal admin confirms** it ("Connect NUK Riverside Staff (group, 8 members)?"); Cancel makes the bot leave. The portal polls the link every 2 s.
  - **The webhook** `POST /api/webhooks/telegram` (no session, exempt from the origin check) checks `X-Telegram-Bot-Api-Secret-Token` in constant time first. It handles `/start` codes, `my_chat_member` (the bot removed or blocked → the chat is **Blocked**) and `migrate_to_chat_id` (a supergroup upgrade keeps the chat). An error answers 500 so Telegram retries; every handler takes a repeat.
  - **Setup is the owner's, once per environment** (docs/server/operations.md → Telegram): @BotFather, three settings (`NUXT_TELEGRAM_BOT_TOKEN` and `NUXT_TELEGRAM_WEBHOOK_SECRET` as Worker secrets, `NUXT_TELEGRAM_BOT_USERNAME`), then one `setWebhook` call from the owner's machine. Without a token Telegram is off (404; the page says it isn't set up); a half-set configuration is 500 `TELEGRAM_NOT_CONFIGURED`. A Nitro task can't run on the deployed Worker, so there is none.
  - **Send to Telegram** (Summary and Sales by item, `report: ['export']`): the dialog sends the page's query **as text** (as its URL has it); the server checks it with the report's own schema and builds the message (Telegram HTML, names escaped, never an email or member code) and, when asked, the CSV (the 8.1b export). The preview is that message as plain text. The send runs once per `Idempotency-Key` (`withIdempotency`: a lost answer replays the first result; a failure stores nothing, so Try again sends).
  - **Telegram's refusals are 4xx:** blocked → 409 `TELEGRAM_BLOCKED` (the chat is marked blocked), Telegram's flood control → 429 `TELEGRAM_RATE_LIMITED` with its wait, anything else → 424 `TELEGRAM_UNAVAILABLE`. The app shows only 4xx messages (5xx is a crash), and these are our words for the admin.
  - **Permissions:** the Telegram page uses the existing `settings: ['manage']` (admins; no new statement until managers need it); sending uses `report: ['export']`. Audit: connect, test, disconnect, and each send (the report and period, never figures).
- **Alternatives:**
  - Our own `fetch` calls to the Bot API: fewer dependencies, but hand-written types, error parsing and multipart uploads.
  - grammY's `Bot` with `webhookCallback`: more than three update kinds need.
  - Connecting a group without the portal's confirmation: anyone who saw the link could attach a group of theirs.
  - Long polling (`getUpdates`) instead of a webhook: a Worker isn't a long-running process.
- **Verified:**
  - server `notifications.service.test.ts` 14 with grammY's real `Api` against a fake Telegram: settings, start codes, the secret compare; private connect (code stored only as a hash, once); an expired link, a group's link in a private chat, another admin's link; a group needing a group admin then the portal's confirmation; confirm after expiry; Cancel leaving the group; blocked and supergroup updates; Send test with Telegram's 429 and 403; disconnect with a stale version, leave and reconnect; a send once per key, a failed send tried again with the same key, a disconnected chat. **Two chats racing with one code connect one**: checked to fail with the link's guard removed.
  - server `reports.service.test.ts` +3 (the Summary and Sales by item messages on the worked Monday, escaping, "…and N more");
  - e2e `telegram.test.ts` 6 on a mocked API (not set up, private connect with polling, a group's confirm and Cancel, Send test, Reconnect, Disconnect asked first with the version; Send to Telegram's preview, group warning, blocked chat disabled, a refused send tried again with the same key; the button hidden where Telegram is off);
  - the staging build bundles grammY's web build; screenshots at 1440 and 390 px.
  - lint, typecheck, unit + server 753, the full e2e suite 351.
  - lint, typecheck, unit + server 753, the full e2e suite 351.
  - **Not verified yet:** a real bot. That is 8.1e, on staging, after the owner's setup.

### D113: New-order and payment alerts, the closing summary and the delivery history (step 8.1d), 2026-09-30

- **Context:** stage 4 of [plans/reports.md](plans/reports.md), from the owner's frames and the review's changes: "Retrying at 9:05" without Retry, Failed only after the last try with Retry; a group chosen for the closing summary warns that everyone sees the sales; the new-order message with the table and each note under its line; Open order into the counter app (R2); the closing summary's CSV as a second message from the same snapshot; nothing on a closed day (R4).
- **Decision:**
  - **Orders don't know Telegram.** Placing and paying an order write the neutral outbox events `orders.placed` / `orders.paid` (`{ orderId }`) in the same batch (`orders.events.ts`); `platform:deliver-outbox` hands them to the notifications feature, which reads the order through `orderAlert` (first name only, R3). Without a bot, the handler does nothing and the event is done.
  - **One delivery per chat and subject, saved before it's sent** (`notification_deliveries`, unique `(destination, dedupe_key)`): the outbox's repeats, and two runs at once, add nothing. The message is a snapshot (HTML, a link button, a file), so a retry sends the same thing; the closing summary's figures are read once. The CSV is a delivery of its own, so a failed file never resends the text.
  - **Own delivery loop, not the outbox's:** `deliverDue` claims each due delivery with a conditional update, sends it, and records the state the page shows: sent; Telegram's `retry_after` respected (not counted as a failure); other failures back off 1, 2, 4 … minutes (the outbox's schedule), **Failed** after 8 tries; a chat Telegram says is gone is marked **Blocked** and its delivery fails at once. Retry (only on Failed) puts the same message back and sends it. New alerts are sent right after they're queued; the rest by `notifications:deliver` every minute. At least once: a lost answer can repeat a message (Telegram has no idempotency key).
  - **Which chat gets what:** `notification_rules` (kind × chat, a row = on; the closing summary's `attach_csv`), switched on the Telegram page one at a time (`PUT /api/admin/telegram/rules`, `settings: ['manage']`).
  - **The closing summary** is due 30 minutes after the end of the business day's last opening window: the windows starting that date from 04:00 and the next calendar day before 04:00, an overnight one ending the next day (`closingInstant`). None on a closed day (R4); not sent more than 12 hours late (after downtime); once per branch, business date and chat. It's the Summary message titled "Closing summary", with "View full report" to that day's Summary (https only).
  - **Messages:** new order "🧾 New order #042 · Riverside", "Pickup · Sokha" or "Dine-in · T4 · Sokha", the lines with version and add-ons, "↳ note" under its line, total, "Waiting for payment…"; payment "✅ Paid #042 · …", the method and amount (riel as riel); **Open order** to `/counter/<branchId>?order=<id>`, left out without an https site (Telegram refuses the button). The counter app opens `?order=` once its queue has loaded, then drops it from the URL; an order no longer in the queue says so.
  - **The history** (the latest 50, kept 90 days by `notifications:purge-deliveries`) also records reports sent from the portal (`kind: 'report'`, sent), so everything sent to Telegram is in one list; View shows the saved message.
- **Alternatives:**
  - Sending each alert as its own outbox message: the outbox can't wait for Telegram's `retry_after`, and its rows aren't what the page needs to show.
  - The orders feature calling Telegram in its request: a slow or down Telegram would slow or fail the order, and orders would depend on notifications.
  - Rebuilding the closing summary on each retry: the figures could change between tries.
- **Verified:**
  - server `notifications.delivery.test.ts` 16 with the real order services and grammY's `Api` on a fake Telegram: closing times (overnight, after midnight, closed day); the outbox events of placing and paying; no rule, no delivery; the new-order message word for word with its note and Open order, and no email; a repeated event adding nothing; **the same event handled twice at once saves one delivery** (checked to fail without the dedupe index's `on conflict do nothing`); no button without https; the payment message; Telegram's 429 wait; the 1, 2, 4 … backoff to Failed after 8 tries and Retry sending the same text; a 403 failing at once and blocking the chat; a claimed delivery not sent twice; the closing summary at 21:30 and not 21:29, once, with its CSV and View full report; none on a closed day or 12 hours late; a chat added later still getting it; rules on and off; a portal send in the history.
  - e2e `telegram.test.ts` +2 (switches with the group warning and Attach CSV; Sent, Retrying at, Failed with its reason, View, Retry), `counter.test.ts` +1 (`?order=` opens the order and leaves the URL; a finished one says so); screenshots at 1440 and 390 px.
  - lint, typecheck, unit + server 769, the full e2e suite 354.
  - **Staging (8.1e, the owner, 2026-09-30)** with the real bot `@nuk_cafe_bot` and the scheduled task: alerts in a private chat and a group, Open order, Blocked and Reconnect, the closing summary with its CSV, the history. A Failed delivery to a blocked chat wasn't exercised there (the server tests cover it).

### D114: Order tracking pages, polled; live pushes planned for later (step 6.5b), 2026-09-30

- **Context:** the customer's side of tracking, on the server since 6.5a (D106), from the owner's 6.5 frames and their review ("use your defaults"). The owner asked to look at Cloudflare Durable Objects and WebSockets for live updates, and to keep them as a plan for later ([plans/order-tracking.md](plans/order-tracking.md), [plans/live-updates.md](plans/live-updates.md)).
- **Decision:**
  - **`/orders/<id>` tracks the order:** the number as the hero, the status badge, the four steps (Paid → Preparing → Ready → Picked up; none for a cancelled order), what to do next, "Pay at the counter by …" with the minutes left (red in the last 5, "Time's up" at 0 until the expiry task, D104), the payment line, "Updated …", then the lines as the receipt. Two columns from `lg`. Ready: a success panel and "Ready: 042" as the tab title. A table order is collected at the counter (D106's correction). Status colors: cancelled is **neutral**, completed success (the review).
  - **Polling, not pushes, for now:** the order is read again every 10 s while it's in progress and the tab is visible, and stops once it's completed or cancelled; the menu's bar polls only while an order is in progress; Your orders doesn't poll (return-to-tab refresh, D22). An order changes about 3 times in 15 minutes, so 10 s is enough, costs ~12,000 small requests a day at one cafe, and needs no new infrastructure.
  - **Cancel:** a confirm sheet (a dialog from `sm`) with its own state, like the counter's panel (D102): the same `Idempotency-Key` for Try again; a refusal (paid meanwhile: 409) is shown in the server's words with Close, and the page reads the order again. The sheet stays mounted after the refusal, so its words don't vanish when the page switches to Preparing.
  - **Order again:** `OrderLine.variationId` is added to the contract (already stored per line; the counter's lines carry it too). The menu feature exports `useOrderAgain()`: it reads the branch's public menu and adds the lines still offered as they were (item, the same version not sold out, every add-on still offered, notes kept) to that branch's order in this browser, then the page opens the menu with "Added 2 items … Banana Bread isn't available now." The table isn't carried over.
  - **Your orders (`/orders`):** In progress as cards, Past as rows, 20 at a time with Load more; each refresh reads every page shown again and drops a repeated row, so an order moving from In progress to Past can't be lost between pages. Linked from the account menu ("Your orders").
  - **The menu's bar:** the orders feature exports `ActiveOrdersBar`; the menu page mounts it only for a signed-in customer (the account is read in the browser, D97), so signed-out visitors make no orders request and the server-rendered page stays the same for everyone.
- **Alternatives:**
  - **Durable Objects with WebSockets now:** instant updates, but a new deploy preset, an authenticated upgrade, a publish path for Node (local and e2e), reconnects, and polling kept anyway as the safety net (locked phones drop sockets). Planned for the counter and tracking together when the 10 s delay matters ([plans/live-updates.md](plans/live-updates.md)); crossws' server-side publish was found to miss sockets after hibernation, so the plan uses Cloudflare's socket tags.
  - Stitching list pages client-side without re-reading them: a row can move between pages when an order finishes.
  - Order again through the checkout quote: it prices lines but doesn't say which add-ons are still offered for the cart's own format; the public menu does, and checkout prices everything again anyway.
- **Verified:**
  - e2e `shop-tracking.test.ts` 6 on the seeded server: the counter's API pays, readies and completes while the page follows through real 10 s polls (payment line, the current step, "Ready: 00N"), Order again refills the order; cancel from a phone sheet and Your orders lists it; a payment wins over a cancel with the sheet's message; another customer's order is not found; the bar ("Order 00N · Waiting for payment", then "is ready") to the order and Your orders from the account menu; signed out, no orders request (checked to fail with the bar mounted for everyone).
  - `shop-checkout.test.ts` updated to the tracking wording (pay by, minutes left, a table order collected at the counter).
  - unit `order.test.ts` 9 (steps, countdown edges, cancellation wording, payment line, dates, the bar), `menu.test.ts` +2 (Order again keeps and skips lines); server `orders.service.test.ts` expects the version on each line.
  - lint, typecheck, unit + server 777; e2e locally: `shop-tracking`, `shop-checkout`, `shop-menu`, `shop-account`, `shop-orders-api`, `counter`, `counter-orders-api`, `reports`, `color-mode` (the full suite runs in CI). Screenshots at 390 and 1440 px.

### D115: Points and vouchers deferred; launch essentials first; an admin resets a staff password (step 10.1), 2026-09-30

- **Context:** the owner decided that points and vouchers (phase 7, the blueprint's "part of launch", D45) aren't important for a coffee shop's launch, and asked to focus on what a working cafe runs into first. The first of those: a staff member who forgets their password has no way back in. Resetting by email can't reach them (staging mail goes only to the Resend account's own address until the cafe has a sending domain, Q4), and the owner can't buy a domain yet.
- **Decision:**
  - **Phase 7 is deferred**, not dropped: the blueprint's rules stay as the design for when the owner asks for it. The member code on the customer's account stays (harmless, and loyalty would use it). A new **phase 10, launch essentials**: 10.1 reset a staff password, 10.2 the counter's finished orders today (with a mockup round), 10.3 the order of items within a category, 10.4 production groundwork that needs no domain (a restore drill, error alerts).
  - **10.1:** `POST /api/admin/staff/{userId}/reset-password` `{ version }` gives a staff member a new temporary password (the same generator as a new account), returned once and shown in the Staff page's existing one-time dialog. In **one batch**: `mustChangePassword` set and the version moved on (guarded by the version read, `requireOneChange`), the credential password replaced (or added, for an account without one), every session deleted, and an audit entry `staff.password.reset` without the password.
  - **A permission of its own,** `staff: ['reset-password']` (admins only): setting someone's password can take over their account, so it can be granted separately from editing access later (to managers, say).
  - **Only for staff:** a customer (including a disabled staff member, who is a customer again) is "not found" here; customers reset by email. **Never one's own** (409 `OWN_ACCESS`: "Use Change password in your account menu"), which the row menu also shows.
  - **Another admin can be reset too:** admins can already change each other's access, and the audit entry records who did it.
  - The Staff page: **Reset password** in each row's menu (desktop and phone), a confirm that says what happens (their password stops working, signed out everywhere), then the temporary password once, with "Their old password no longer works…".
- **Alternatives:**
  - Better Auth's admin `setUserPassword` endpoint: it doesn't set our `mustChangePassword`, end sessions and audit in the same batch as our version guard.
  - Reuse `staff: ['update']`: ties a takeover-capable action to editing roles.
  - Email reset for staff: needs the sending domain (Q4).
  - Let admins reset their own password here: Change password already exists and asks for the current one.
- **Verified:**
  - server `staff.service.test.ts` +5: the new password works and the old one doesn't, sessions end, must change; a person who had changed theirs and another admin; the audit entry without the password; own account, stale version, a customer, a disabled staff member and an unknown account refused; **two admins resetting at once: one wins and only its password works** (checked to fail with `requireOneChange` removed: both "succeed" and one handed-over password is dead). `identity.permissions.test.ts`: only admins hold `reset-password`.
  - e2e `staff.test.ts` +3 (mocked API): confirm, the version sent, the password shown once with the reset wording; Cancel sends nothing; one's own row has it disabled with the reason.
  - lint, typecheck, unit + server 783.

### D116: Consistent Nuxt server import aliases, 2026-10-01

- **Context:** the owner requested a server-only standards update and import cleanup. The standard recommended `../../utils/<file>` and examples used `~~/server/`, while Nuxt 4 documents `#server` and `#shared`.
- **Decision:** use `#server/` and `#shared/` for parent and root paths throughout server code, including tests, re-exports and dynamic imports; retain same-directory `./` imports. This is a project convention, not a Nuxt ban on relative imports. Keep explicit imports, public feature boundaries, D47's direct build-time auth-options import and D49's lazy customers import. Mirror `#server` in plain Node Vitest projects. ESLint rejects old path spellings and cross-feature deep imports through the new alias.
- **Scope:** server imports, lint/test alias configuration, and server/agent documentation; no client changes or business behavior changes.
- **Verification:** Nuxt preparation, lint, typecheck, all 783 unit/server tests and the production Node build pass. A target-equivalence scan checked all 671 rewritten imports in 243 server files. Sixteen temporary lint probes rejected forbidden static/dynamic paths and cross-feature internals; disabling the guards removed the rejection. Seven HTTP e2e checks (`shop-orders-api`, `counter-orders-api`) pass against the built server; their shared harness was temporarily set to `browser: false` because these tests use HTTP only, then restored unchanged. Browser e2e could not run here: Chrome is absent and downloads failed; the existing CI runs the full browser suite before merge.

### D117: The counter's finished orders today (step 10.2), 2026-09-30

- **Context:** once an order is completed or cancelled it leaves the queue, and a cashier had no way to look it up ("I paid, why was it cancelled?", "did we give the money back?"). The owner's frames (2026-09-30): a Queue | Finished today (24) switch, a table with the payment note, a read-only panel with the cancellation and a timeline of who did each step, rows on phones. Reviewed: one search (the list's, not also the header's), a phone row opens the order full screen.
- **Decision:**
  - **`GET /api/counter/{branchId}/orders/finished`** (`order: ['read']` at the branch): today's business day's completed and cancelled orders (the day they were placed, from 4:00 in the branch's zone, like reports), the most recently finished first, at most 500, as `CounterOrder`s. The queue answer gains `finishedToday` for the switch's count. A new index `orders_branch_date_idx (branch_id, business_date)` (migration `0021`) serves both.
  - **`GET /api/counter/{branchId}/orders/{id}/history`**: the order, every step from `order_events` with who took it (the system for the expiry, the customer by first name, a staff member), the cancel's reason and the **staff's own note** (the counter sees it; customers never do, D106), and who returned the money. Any of the branch's orders, not only today's.
  - **Read only.** No refund or reopen from here: no rule exists for either.
  - **The app:** `/counter/<branchId>/finished`, its own address (the switch is two links, so Back works); search by number or name and All/Completed/Cancelled chips with counts, filtered in the browser (a day's orders are few); a table from `sm` where the order number opens the order (the Reports pattern), rows on phones; the panel beside the list from `lg`, a side panel below, full screen on phones. Staff are named by first name ("by Sophea"), like the rest of the counter. No timer: the list is read again when a counter command changes an order (they invalidate `counter`) and on return to the tab.
  - **Telegram's Open order** (D113) on an order no longer in the queue now opens it on Finished today, instead of a toast saying it isn't in the queue.
- **Alternatives:** a fourth column on the board (the board is for work still to do, and would grow all day); a date picker (older days are the admin's Order history, with the same timeline); the admin's report route (admins only, R1).
- **Verified:**
  - server `counter.service.test.ts` +3: completed, cancelled by the cafe (paid, money returned), by the customer and by the expiry are listed, the most recently finished first; not the orders in play, another branch's or another day's; the count on the queue; the business day's edge (still Monday at 03:59 Tuesday, empty at 04:00); the history's steps with who took each, the staff note and who returned the money; the system's cancel; another branch's order 404.
  - unit `finished.test.ts` 5 (payment notes, chips, the cancellation card's wording, the timeline).
  - e2e `counter.test.ts` +2 and one updated (real seeded server): the switch's count, the table row with its payment, the panel's steps, the chips and search; the phone rows and full-screen order; `?order=` for a finished order opens it on Finished today. Screenshots at 1440 and 390 px against the frames.

### D118: Reorder menu items within a category (step 10.3), 2026-09-30

- **Context:** the customer menu shows a category's items by their position, but no screen could set it: items appeared in the order they were created, so a cafe couldn't put best sellers first. The server has had `PUT /api/admin/menu/items/order` since step 3.5a (every listed item with its version, one batch, audited; the public menu sorts by the position).
- **Decision:**
  - **A Reorder dialog on Menu items**, not a mode of the list: the list is paginated and filtered by status and search, while a reorder needs one category's whole set of drafts and published items. The dialog picks a category (a leaf, starting from the list's category filter), reads every page of it, and lists its drafts and published items (archived ones keep their place and aren't shown, as the server's rule expects). Drag handles and Move up / Move down, with the new position announced, as on the Add-on group page (D75). Toolbar button **Reorder** and the **R** shortcut (as on Categories).
  - **Saved in one request** with every item's version; someone else's change meanwhile is refused (409) and nothing moves. The dialog says so with Reload (the latest order; the moves are dropped). Its own saving state, like the other order dialogs.
  - **Unsaved moves are guarded** (`useModalUnsavedChanges`): a loaded or reloaded order is the baseline, so only moves count. The category can't be changed while there are unsaved moves.
  - Full screen on phones.
- **Alternatives:** a Reorder mode on the grid (it would have to leave pagination, status tabs and search aside, which is the dialog in disguise); ordering by name (no way to put best sellers first).
- **Verified:** e2e `products.test.ts` +3 (mocked API): the category's items in order without archived ones, a move announced, Save sends every item with its version in the new order; a 409 says so, and Reload reads the order again; Cancel with a move asks first. `shortcuts.test.ts` passes with the renamed `R` entry. Screenshots at 1440 and 390 px. The server's order rule is covered since 3.5a (`items.service.test.ts`).

### D119: Server error alerts on Telegram and the restore drill runbook (step 10.4), 2026-09-30

- **Context:** production groundwork that needs no domain (phase 10, D115). Staging and production have no alert when the app fails; the owner already uses Telegram (8.1). The restore drill (operations.md) had no written steps, and it needs the owner's Cloudflare login.
- **Decision:**
  - **A fourth notification kind, `server_error`**, switched per chat on the Telegram page like the others. The Nitro error handler, on every 5xx under `/api/**`, queues an alert in the background (`event.waitUntil`; the notifications feature imported only then, and a failure while alerting only logged, so the response never depends on it).
  - **What it says:** the method, the route (segments with a digit and 8+ characters become `{id}`), the status and the request id. **Never the error's text** (it can hold SQL or someone's data); Workers Logs have it under the request id.
  - **At most one per route and chat every 15 minutes:** the window is part of the delivery's dedupe key, so the existing unique index (D113) drops the rest, also when two failures land at once. Sent by `notifications:deliver` within a minute, with its retries; blocked chats get none.
  - **Migration `0022`** rebuilds `notification_rules` (SQLite can't change a CHECK in place): copy, drop, rename, written by hand without the foreign-key pragmas drizzle-kit adds (nothing references the table; D1 keeps foreign keys on); `db generate` reports no drift.
  - **Not covered:** a failure the Worker can't answer at all, and scheduled task failures (logged). Cloudflare's own 5xx alerts come with production (8.2).
  - **The restore drill is a runbook** (operations.md → Restore drill): note the Time Travel bookmark, make a visible change, restore and time it, check the app, write the result into progress.md. The owner runs it (it needs the Wrangler login and undoes staging's test data written after the bookmark).
- **Alternatives:** email alerts (no sending domain yet, Q4); Cloudflare notifications only (need production's plan and a domain; they'd say "5xx rose", not which route); the error's message in the alert (leaks internals to a chat).
- **Verified:**
  - server `notifications.delivery.test.ts` +4: route naming; nothing without a rule; the message with the route, status and request id and no error text, sent to the chat; one per route and chat per window, also for two failures at once, another route or the next window alerting again; a blocked chat none. `server/tests/migrations.test.ts` +1: the rebuild keeps every rule over existing rows, accepts the new kind, still refuses an unknown one, and a chat's rules still go with it.
  - The error handler's call is not exercised end to end (no route fails on purpose); the e2e build, which bundles it, passes the full suite (368).
  - **The restore drill on staging** (the owner, 2026-10-01): in the Cloudflare dashboard (D1 → Time Travel, restore by date; the runbook first assumed Wrangler only), about 2 seconds; admin sign-in, the customer menu and the counter worked afterwards. The free plan's window is 7 days.

### D120: The release check (step 10.5), 2026-10-01

- **Context:** the blueprint lists the scenarios the first release must pass (§8): money, double orders, the table QR, who sees what. Each feature had its own tests, but nothing tied scenario to proof, so "is it safe to open?" was a belief.
- **Decision:**
  - **One page, `docs/plans/release-check.md`:** each scenario, the tests that prove it, and its status. The loyalty rows wait for phase 7 (D115). Production (8.2) launches when every row is ✅ and the hand check passed.
  - **Gaps filled with tests, not new code:** a signed-out visitor at a table QR keeps the table through sign-in (e2e, real server); a wrong-branch command leaves the order, payment, events and audit untouched; two staff marking ready at once; completing retried with the same key; an upload whose item save fails stays unattached and reusable. All passed against the existing code: no bug found.
  - **A hand check on staging** (five steps, about 20 minutes) for what tests can't see: a real phone at a QR, two real devices at the counter, the real Worker and D1.
- **Found:** an order's status change has two guards, the batch's version check and the unique index `order_events_version_idx`. With the first removed, the ready race still ends with one winner (the second writer fails on the index).
- **Alternatives:** an end-to-end suite against staging itself (needs test accounts and data reset on a shared environment, and email we can't send yet, Q4); leaving the mapping to 8.2 (the gaps would surface at launch).
- **Hand check on staging (2026-10-01, NOT PASSED; results in release-check.md):**
  - **A counter hazard, fixed:** the queue refreshes every 10 s under an open order panel. When another cashier paid meanwhile, the panel's Confirm payment button became Mark ready in the same place, and the second cashier's click marked the order ready (orders 002 and 003: ready 1 s after paid; one payment each, checked in D1). The panel now remembers the status it opened with; a different one from a refresh shows "Paid meanwhile by …" and hides the actions until **OK**. Reload counts as OK (the cashier asked for the latest). Alternatives: disable the button for a second after a change (a timer guessing at reaction time); close the panel (loses where the cashier was).
  - **Error 1102 on the Workers Free plan** (10 ms CPU per request; server rendering, sessions and password hashing need more). Not an app error: nothing reached the error handler. The Paid plan is the fix; squeezing every request under 10 ms isn't realistic.
- **Verified:** server `counter.service.test.ts` +2 and one extended, `items.service.test.ts` +1; e2e `shop-checkout.test.ts` +1 (a new seeded customer, `tableGuest`); the race test run with `requireOneChange` removed (still passes, through the index, as above).

### D121: Rate limits on the public API without a domain (step 10.6), 2026-10-01

- **Context:** `/api/public/**` needs no sign-in, and the checkout quote reads the whole catalog on every call. The planned limits were Cloudflare WAF rules, which need a custom domain (Q4), so nothing limited them. On the Workers Free plan heavy use ends in Error 1102 (10.5); on the Paid plan it costs CPU.
- **Decision:**
  - **Cloudflare's Workers rate-limit bindings** (GA since 2025-09), which work on a `workers.dev` address: `RATE_LIMIT_QUOTE` 120 a minute for `/api/public/checkout/quote`, `RATE_LIMIT_PUBLIC` 600 a minute for the rest of `/api/public/**`, keyed by `cf-connecting-ip` (set by Cloudflare, not forgeable; the same key as Better Auth's limits, D103). The numbers live in `nuxt.config.ts`, the rules in `server/utils/rate-limit.ts`, a middleware applies them.
  - **Generous on purpose:** customers on the cafe's Wi-Fi share one address. A person ordering makes a few quotes a minute; a script makes thousands. Server rendering calls the API in-process with the visitor's headers, so a page view counts once per call against that visitor.
  - **429 `RATE_LIMITED`** with `Retry-After: 60` and "Too many requests from your connection. Wait a minute and try again." (the app's `ApiError` already knows 429).
  - **Fails open:** no binding (dev server, tests, the e2e build), no address, or a limiter error lets the request through (logged). Cloudflare counts per location and may let a few extra past: this bounds abuse, it isn't an exact quota.
  - **Not covered here:** `/api/shop/**` (signed in; orders already limited to 2 unpaid) and `/api/auth/**` (Better Auth's own limits). The WAF rules stay planned for when a domain exists.
- **Alternatives:** our own counter in D1 or KV (a write per request; KV isn't atomic); waiting for the domain and the WAF (no protection until launch).
- **Verified:** server `server/tests/rate-limit.test.ts` 5 (the quote's own limit, per address, nothing checked without a binding or address or outside `/api/public/`, the 429 body); a staging build's `wrangler.json` carries both bindings and `wrangler deploy --dry-run` lists them. The staging deploy after the merge accepted both bindings on the Free plan. **Not verified:** a 429 on the deployed Worker.

### D122: Load speed for customers: the menu page cached, photos made smaller (step 10.7), 2026-10-01

- **Context:** measured on a production build with the Standard sample menu: building the menu data is ~0.5 ms of our code, `GET /api/public/menu` ~8–11 ms (mostly SQLite, which on D1 is waiting, not CPU), but **server-rendering `/` ~119 ms**. Every visitor gets the same page (the account is read in the browser, D97), so the render is repeated work. Menu photos were uploaded as taken: a phone photo is 3–8 MB, shown on a card a few hundred pixels wide, over mobile data.
- **Decision:**
  - **The menu page `/` is cached for 60 s** with Nitro's `swr` route rule (`MENU_PAGE_CACHE_SECONDS` in `nuxt.config.ts`): one render serves every visitor of that Worker instance for a minute and is refreshed in the background. Cached: **2.1 ms** instead of 119 ms. Sold-out marks, the open state and menu edits can lag by up to a minute on that page; checkout's quote and placing an order read everything again (D98, D99), so a lagging page can't sell something it shouldn't. The cache is per instance and in memory (not shared between locations); the e2e build turns it off (`E2E_HUB_DIR`), because its tests change the menu and reload at once.
  - **Photos are made smaller in the admin's browser before upload** (`app/features/products/utils/shrink-image.ts`): the longest side at most 1600 px, WebP at quality 0.82 (JPEG where the browser can't write WebP), the phone's orientation applied; nothing enlarged, the original kept when the result isn't smaller or the browser can't decode it. Originals over 25 MB are refused before decoding (a phone's memory); the server's checks (type, 5 MB) are unchanged and apply to the result. "Preparing the photo…" shows while it works and counts as uploading (Save waits).
- **Considered and left for later:**
  - **Cloudflare's Cache API (`caches.default`)** does nothing on a `workers.dev` address; it works on a custom domain (Q4).
  - **Workers Caching** (the `cache` setting) works on `workers.dev`, but caches responses **without a `Cache-Control` header by heuristics** (a 200 for up to 2 hours). Every route would first need an explicit header (`no-store` on the API and the signed-in pages): part of production (8.2).
  - **The JavaScript the home page loads** (~420 KB compressed, admin and counter code among it): a bundle analysis step of its own.
  - **Resizing on the server or on the fly** (Cloudflare Images): a paid product and a domain; resizing once at upload covers the menu's single size.
- **Verified:** unit `shrink-image.test.ts` 4; e2e `products.test.ts` +1 (a 3200×2400 photo uploads as `IMG_2041.webp`, `image/webp`, under half the size, decoded in the page as 1600×1200) and the reject test now covers 25 MB; a production build (not the e2e one) with the e2e database: the cached `/` has the same cards, page data and preloads as an uncached render, and a browser visiting twice and adding to the order hydrated with no errors. **Not verified:** the cache on the deployed Worker (the first deploy shows it: a second load answers faster).

### D123: The customer menu stops loading the admin's calendar (step 10.8), 2026-10-01

- **Context:** measured with `nuxi analyze` on a production build (D122 listed it for later): the menu page `/` needed ~327 KB of compressed JavaScript before it could be used. The largest avoidable part was Reka UI (Nuxt UI's base): its calendar, range calendar, month and year pickers, date and time fields and `@internationalized/date`, which only the admin's Reports (the period calendar) and Branch hours (`UInputTime`) use. The cause: Nuxt UI 4.11 imports some primitives from `reka-ui/namespaced` (`Popover.vue`: `import { Popover, HoverCard } from 'reka-ui/namespaced'`; also `Calendar`, `InputTime`, `InputDate`, `InputMenu`, the dropdown and context menus). That barrel is **one module** defining every family as an object, so the bundler put everything any page used from it into one chunk, and the menu's "All categories" popover loaded the admin's calendar with it. No newer Nuxt UI or Reka UI changes this (4.11.3 and 2.10.5 are patch releases).
- **Decision:** a local Nuxt module, `modules/reka-namespaced.ts` (a Vite plugin, build only): it rewrites each `reka-ui/namespaced` import in a dependency into one import per family (`reka-ui/namespaced/Popover`), and serves that as a small module building the same object from `reka-ui`'s named exports, copied from the barrel's own definition. Each family is then chunked with the pages that use it. The components get exactly the objects they got before.
  - **Fails loudly:** a family whose definition isn't found in the barrel stops the build; a unit test checks every family the installed Nuxt UI imports against the installed Reka UI, so an upgrade that changes the barrel shows up in `pnpm test:unit` first.
  - **Resolved from Nuxt UI:** pnpm lets only Nuxt UI resolve Reka UI, so the plugin resolves both the barrel and `reka-ui` from the file that imported the family.
  - **Remove it** when Nuxt UI imports Reka UI by name: the plugin then does nothing, and the e2e test below keeps passing.
  - **Removed (2026-10-17, Nuxt UI 4.12.0 upgrade):** Nuxt UI 4.12.0 imports Reka UI by name only (neither it nor `vaul-vue`, the only other package using Reka UI, imports `reka-ui/namespaced`), so the module did nothing and its unit test found no families. `modules/reka-namespaced.ts` and `test/unit/reka-namespaced.test.ts` are deleted; the `shop-menu.test.ts` e2e guard stays and passes, and fails first if a later Nuxt UI goes back to the barrel.
- **Result:** the menu page's JavaScript **327 → 294 KB compressed (−33 KB, −10%)**; `@internationalized/date` and the calendar code now load only with the Reports pages.
- **Alternatives:** patching Nuxt UI (`pnpm patch`, redone on every upgrade, and it touches several components); `manualChunks` (can't split one module); replacing the popover on the menu (the dropdown and the account menu import the barrel too); waiting for upstream (no change in sight).
- **Measured, not changed:** the rest is mostly the framework (Vue, the router, Nuxt UI's runtime, Tailwind Variants ~200 KB) and the menu itself (~22 KB). Better Auth's client (~24 KB) loads on every page through `@nuxtjs/better-auth`'s plugin; the customer site reads the account through `/api/shop/me`, so this could go later, but it's the module's own setup.
- **Verified:** unit `reka-namespaced.test.ts` 5; e2e `shop-menu.test.ts` +1 (no script the menu loads, after opening "All categories", contains `CalendarRoot`, `DateFieldRoot` or `TimeFieldRoot`; checked to fail with the module removed); the full e2e suite (372) passes with it, Reports' calendar and Branch hours' time fields included.

### D124: The customer menu revised: the header at every width, the order always in reach, staff links (step 10.9), 2026-10-01

- **Context (the owner's frames, 2026-10-01):** on phones the header (logo, branch, search, Pickup, light/dark, account) was wider than the screen and the page scrolled sideways; with 10 or more lines the order panel grew past the bottom of the screen, taking the subtotal and Review order with it, and the phone's order sheet scrolled its title and Review order away; the white cards on a white page were hard to tell apart; there was no way to empty the order at once; staff signed in on the store had no way to their workspace.
- **Decision:**
  - **Header:** the branch and an Open/Closed badge under "NUK Cafe" below `lg` (the branch cut with "…", its full name in the tooltip), a chip beside it from `lg`; search an icon on phones; the light/dark button only from `lg`, below it **Appearance (Light, Dark) in the account menu**, also signed out (the account icon opens Sign in, Create account and Appearance; from `lg` "Sign in" stays a link). No "System": the site stays light unless the person picks dark (D96). Below 360 px the Pickup button drops its chevron. Pill tabs; the same light/dark rule on Review order and Your orders.
  - **Page:** gray (`bg-muted`) with white cards; on phones each section's rows in one white box.
  - **Item card:** photo and text, then under a line "N in order" (items with choices) and the price, and on the right **Customize** (items with choices) or **Add**, which becomes a stepper (0 removes, as before). Phones keep rows, with "Customize" for items with choices.
  - **The order:** the same parts everywhere (`OrderActions`, `OrderLines`, `OrderTotals`): from `lg` a panel at most the screen's height below the header; below `lg` a drawer from the right on tablets and a bottom sheet on phones, using the drawer's own header, body and footer (its padding). Only the lines scroll. Each line: price beside the name, a **trash** button and a stepper from 1 (only the trash removes). **Clear order** asks ("Remove all") first.
  - **Workspaces:** `GET /api/shop/me` returns `workspaces` (`admin`, `counter`), decided on the server by `workspacesOf` with the same rules as `/api/admin/me` and `/api/counter/me`; the account menu links to `/admin` and `/counter`. Only links: each workspace checks access on its own routes.
  - **The drawer's side** is chosen in script (`useLayoutContext`), the one exception to D95's CSS-only rule: only the open drawer's content depends on it, and the server's page has it closed.
- **Alternatives:** keeping "System" in Appearance (a phone in dark mode would see a dark store the owner designed light; D96 stands); hiding the member code behind dots (it isn't a secret, and the counter reads it); a separate Clear button per breakpoint (one `OrderActions` serves the panel and the drawer).
- **Verified:** server `identity.service.test.ts` +2 (admin both, staff the counter, a customer, an archived branch and an unknown role neither); e2e `shop-menu.test.ts` +7 (a 12-line order keeps title and Review order on screen at 1440 while the lines scroll; trash and the stepper from 1; Clear order cancelled then confirmed; the phone sheet; the tablet drawer from the right at full height; no sideways scroll at 320/390/768/1024/1440; Appearance in the signed-out phone menu), `shop-account.test.ts` (the phone's account menu), `counter.test.ts` +1 (the cashier's `workspaces` and the Counter link; a customer gets none); screenshots at 1440 light and dark, 768 with the drawer, 390 with the account menu, 320.

### D125: Publish from the item form, and bulk Publish (step 10.10), 2026-10-02

- **Context:** a new menu item was saved as a draft, then published from the list's ⋮ menu, one item at a time: setting up a real menu meant two trips per item.
- **Decision:**
  - **"Create and publish" / "Save and publish"** beside Save in the item editor (the slide-over and the page, D90), for a new item or a draft only. It saves, then publishes the version the save returned, through the list's own `products:publish` mutation (same lock, same route, the server's own checks). One toast: the save's is skipped for that call (`successMessage` may now return `false`, a shared engine change documented in mutations.md). If the server refuses to publish, the item **stays saved as a draft** and the toast says so with the reason (`"Chai" was saved as a draft, but not published`); the editor closes, since the save went through. Save keeps Ctrl/⌘+Enter; publishing is never the shortcut.
  - **Bulk Publish** in the Menu items Select mode (beside Archive selected): the selected **drafts** (the button says "Publish 2 drafts" when the selection mixes in published items), one confirmation, the usual progress, Stop, summary and Retry failed. Refused items stay selected with the reason.
  - **No server change:** `POST /api/admin/menu/items/{id}/publish` already checks draft, leaf category and a sellable version per item, guarded in its batch.
- **Alternatives:** a `publish` flag on create and update, saving and publishing in one batch (atomic, but a second path through the publish rules; a refused publish here leaves a valid draft, nothing half-done); keeping the editor open after a refused publish (its version and the `/new` URL would be stale after the save).
- **Found on the way:** `ProductImageInput` (D122) read `preparing` in a `watchEffect` declared above it; the effect runs during setup, so opening any item form threw a `ReferenceError` in production builds (the app's handler toasted "Unexpected error" over the form's footer). Fixed by declaring it first; e2e "opening the form, new or to edit, raises no error in the page" fails without the fix (checked).
- **Verified:** unit `mutation.test.ts` +1 (no toast when the message function answers `false`); e2e `products.test.ts` +4 (Create and publish sends the create then the publish with the returned version and shows one toast; a refused publish on a draft keeps it saved with the server's reason; a published item has no Save and publish, and Save on a draft doesn't publish; no page error on opening), `list-bulk.test.ts` +1 (drafts only, asked first, a refused one stays selected with its reason); screenshots of the editor page at 390 and 1440.

### D126: UI polish: the page width, sheets without dragging, the period picker, searchable dropdowns, surfaces (step 10.11), 2026-10-02

- **Context:** the owner's review of the admin on a large screen and a phone: pages stretched across a 1920px screen; the order sheet moved and closed while scrolling; the period picker's hover lit a whole block of days; the Branch tabs drew two lines and the sidebar lost "Branch" on the branch's own page; the time zone list lagged; dropdowns of records had no search; numbers sat in flat white boxes.
- **Decision (owner, 2026-10-02):**
  - **Page width without per-page breakpoints:** one side padding for the navbar, the toolbar and the body, `--page-gutter = max(Nuxt UI's padding, (100% − cap) / 2)` (`tailwind.css`, applied in `app.config.ts`). Every admin page is centered at 80rem; settings and form pages add `page-narrow` (56rem) on their `UDashboardPanel`. The inner `mx-auto max-w-*` wrappers are gone. A custom property's `var()` resolves where it's declared, so `page-narrow` restates the gutter (an e2e test caught the first version, which never narrowed).
  - **`<AppDrawer>`:** `UDrawer` with no handle and `handle-only`, so no sheet moves under a finger; X on by default. Every drawer and sheet uses it, also through `resolveComponent`; `UDrawer` is a lint error elsewhere (template, import and `resolveComponent`).
  - **The period picker:** Reka's `maximumDays` lights the whole allowed window from the first click; it's replaced by disabling days more than 93 away while a range is half chosen (`beyondRangeLimit`), so the hover follows the pointer. Two months from `lg` (last month and this one, today's on the right); on phones a bottom sheet of presets, and Custom range with a From/To line and Apply (nothing applies until then), on the branch's month (the server's business date, not the browser's).
  - **Tabs and the sidebar:** `<ToolbarTabs>` puts toolbar tabs on the toolbar's line (no second line, the underline kept inside the toolbar's clipping box). `useUrlTab` keeps a page's tab in the URL with `history.replaceState` (no route change, so Back leaves the page and the unsaved-changes guard doesn't ask); the Branch list passes `?tab=` on when it opens the only branch. `withActiveItem` marks the sidebar's section on pages under it (`/admin/branches/<id>`, `/admin/add-ons/<id>`, `/admin/products/new`): Vue Router saw those as siblings, not children.
  - **Dropdowns by where the items come from:** the cafe's records use `<RecordSelect>` (`USelectMenu`, search anywhere in the label, virtual scroll always, `pinned` "All"/"None", "No … match"); fixed sets stay `USelect`. The time zone list (about 420) is virtualized and built once from the saved zone, not rebuilt (420 date formatters) at every pick: 19 rows in the page instead of 420. The customer site's branch choice stays a `USelect` (D123's bundle).
  - **Surfaces:** `<StatCard>` (Nuxt UI's `subtle` card) for numbers (Reports Summary); a card's header is a `bg-elevated/25` strip (`app.config.ts`); Branch status cards and the Payments current rate on the same soft fill.
- **Alternatives:** a `<PageContainer>` wrapper in every page (the navbar and toolbar wouldn't line up, and every page would need it); one width for all pages (too tight for tables or too loose for forms); `UDrawer` with `handle-only` kept visible (a handle invites a drag that does nothing); routing the tab (`router.replace`) (the unsaved-changes guard asks on every route change); a custom calendar (Reka's highlight works once its window limit is off).
- **Verified:** unit (`navigation.test.ts` 4, `reports.test.ts` +1); e2e `branches.test.ts` +5 (the sidebar's section on both tabs, the tab in the URL across a reload and through `/branches`, one line under the tabs with the underline shown, 896px centered at 1920 with the navbar lined up, the time zones drawn only in view and found by name), `reports.test.ts` +3 (hover from the first click to the pointer, applied on the second; the phone sheet applying only on Apply; sheets without a handle that don't move when dragged, checked to fail with dragging back), `products.test.ts` +1 (search anywhere, All kept, no match said); existing tests moved to the new dropdowns; the full e2e suite; real-server screenshots at 1920, 1440 and 390 px.

### D127: Phone numbers: a country with the number, E.164 on the wire, libphonenumber on both sides (step 10.12), 2026-10-02

- **Context:** the Branch phone was free text (30 characters): nothing said which country, and nothing checked the number. The owner asked for a reusable phone field: a dial-code dropdown with flags (Cambodia, Hong Kong and Argentina for now), the number checked by a schema rather than by blocking keys, following the international standard.
- **Decision (owner, 2026-10-02):**
  - **Stored and sent in E.164** (ITU-T: `+`, the country code, digits, at most 15): `+85512345678`. Shown in international format (`formatPhone`: `+855 12 345 678`).
  - **Each country's numbering plan checked by libphonenumber** (Google's rules) through `libphonenumber-js` with its "min" data, in `shared/contracts/phone.ts`: `PHONE_COUNTRIES` (one line per country), `parsePhone(text, country?)`, `phoneSchema` (the request field: a full number in a supported country → E.164, blank → `null`). The server's `updateBranchSettingsSchema` uses `phoneSchema`; the form uses `parsePhone` with the chosen country. Hand-written rules would have been wrong for real numbers: Cambodia's trunk 0, Argentina's mobile 9 after +54 (typed locally with 15).
  - **`<PhoneInput>`** (root): the country as a `USelect` (a fixed set) with the flag and dial code in the input's leading slot, the number beside it as typed. Nothing is blocked; a full number typed or pasted (`+…`, `00…`, or `855…` when it isn't a valid local number) switches the country and keeps the local part; leaving the field drops the trunk 0 and groups the digits. The select sits inside the input because Nuxt UI hands a `UFormField`'s id and name to every control under it: side by side, the label pointed at the select and both posted `name="phone"`.
  - **The form** keeps `phone` (as typed) and `phoneCountry` in its draft and checks them together, the message naming the country ("Enter a valid Hong Kong phone number"); it sends `parsePhone(…).e164`.
  - **Flags:** the `circle-flags` icon set, bundled at build time like Lucide (D18); `shared/contracts/phone.ts` added to the icon scan, which looked at `app/` only.
  - **Existing data:** a phone saved as free text before this (staging only) shows as it is with the error, and must be fixed on the next save; the server refuses anything but a supported E.164.
- **Alternatives:** our own rules per country (wrong on real numbers, see above); libphonenumber's "max" data (exact type rules, about twice the size; length and prefix checks are enough for a contact number); custom metadata for only three countries (smaller, but a build step to keep in step with the library); a separate country field on the server (E.164 already carries the country).
- **Size:** the library is in admin chunks and the server only; the customer menu doesn't import it (the branches contract is admin-only).
- **Verified:** unit `phone.test.ts` 9 (local and full numbers, 00 and bare codes, Argentina's 9, wrong lengths named by country, unsupported countries, the server reading only full numbers, the request schema's E.164 and field errors), `branch-form.test.ts` +2 (E.164 out, the country's message, legacy free text); server `branches.service.test.ts` on E.164; e2e `branches.test.ts` +3 (a saved number under its flag, a pasted full number switching the country, the trunk 0 dropped and E.164 sent, a wrong number named and not sent); screenshots of the field in light, dark, 390 px, open and with an error.

### D128: Tabs that scroll, one pager, one quantity stepper, the order line card (step 10.13), 2026-10-02

- **Context:** the owner's next review: on a phone, tab labels were cut ("Arch…"); the lists' pager was a bare row of numbers with no way to jump to a page or change the page size; the quantity fields (`UInputNumber`) opened the keyboard on phones and tablets; and the order's line on the menu was a loose row. The owner sent frames for the pager and the line card (their shape, styled by Nuxt UI).
- **Decision (owner, 2026-10-02):**
  - **Tabs:** every `UTabs` row scrolls sideways with its scrollbar hidden and its labels whole (`app.config.ts`: list `overflow-x-auto scrollbar-none`, triggers `shrink-0`, labels `whitespace-nowrap`; `scrollbar-none` is a Tailwind utility in `tailwind.css`). `useCenteredTab` scrolls the row (only the row: `scrollTo` on the tablist, never `scrollIntoView`, which would move the page) to put the active tab in the middle: smoothly on a change (tap, keyboard, URL), at once on mounting and whenever the row or a tab changes size (a `ResizeObserver`: the counts arrive after mounting and widen the row). `StatusTabs`, `ToolbarTabs`, the counter's queue tabs, the Add-on group's and the item editor's section tabs use it.
  - **`<ListPagination>`** on Staff, Menu items, Sales by item and Order history: "Page [n] of N · Rows per page [20]" and first/previous/numbers/next/last (`UPagination`); a typed page applies on Enter or blur, clamped; on phones previous / "Page [n] of N" / next with rows per page under them. `usePaginatedQuery` owns `pageSize` now (a ref, `PAGE_SIZES` 10/20/50/100 within the server's `MAX_PAGE_SIZE` of 100), keeps it in the URL when not the default, and goes back to page 1 when it changes.
  - **`<QuantityStepper>`:** − n + in a pill (Nuxt UI `UButton` and `UInput`). The field has `inputmode="none"`: no keyboard on touch screens (− and + are the way), while a keyboard can still type a number (applied on Enter or blur, clamped, anything else reverts) and ↑/↓ step. One markup for every visitor, so the server-rendered menu stays hydration-safe; disabling the field by width would need a width check (D95). `UInputNumber` can't do this: Reka's number field sets its own `inputmode`. Default max 99; **customer lines stay at 20** (`LINE_MAX_QUANTITY`, D98: the server's limit; the owner accepted keeping it over 99).
  - **The order line card** (the menu's order panel and sheet): a soft `UCard` with the photo (a cup when there is none), the name, the choices, "No longer available" only when so, the line's total on the right in bold; a divider; "Add a note" on the left (the note box opens under it, the note kept with the line to Review order) and the trash with the stepper on the right. No ⋮ menu: its only actions are on the card already. Review order's lines take the same bottom row.
  - **The checkout gate's hidden X** was removed earlier on main (`:close="false"`); nothing more to do.
- **Alternatives:** `UTabs` with arrows at the ends (more chrome, and a phone swipes anyway); `scrollIntoView({ inline: 'center' })` (scrolls the page vertically too); a "Load more" instead of pages (the reports and Staff need a page to come back to, and CSV covers "all rows"); `readonly` on the quantity field for touch (it then can't be typed with a keyboard either, and needs a width check on a server-rendered page); 99 for customer lines (the server refuses over 20; raising it is a business rule for the owner).
- **Verified:** e2e `list-page.test.ts` +5 (a typed page, clamped; a page size back to page 1, asked of the API, kept across a reload; the phone pager; labels whole and the row scrolling, not the page, at 320 px; the URL's tab and a tapped tab centered), `shop-menu.test.ts` +1 (the line card: no keyboard on touch, + to 2 with the total, a typed 35 kept at 20, a note carried to Review order) with the stepper's names in the existing tests, `shop-checkout.test.ts` and `shop-tracking.test.ts` on the new controls; the full e2e suite; real-server screenshots of every admin, counter and customer page at 1440 and 390 px, light and dark (no page scrolls sideways, no page errors).

### D129: List toolbars at every width, modes as toggles, the bulk bar on phones (step 10.14), 2026-10-02

- **Context:** the owner's review of Menu items: on a tablet the toolbar scrolled sideways; on a phone search, category and four buttons wrapped into a lopsided block with the search cut off; in Select mode on a phone the bulk bar squeezed "0 selected · Select all" into about 60 px, a word per line and overlapping; and Select mode could only be left by the small ✕ at the far end of the bottom bar (the Select button was disabled while selecting).
- **Cause:** the toolbar's left side kept fixed widths from `sm` (search 16rem, category 14rem) with four buttons on the right, and `UDashboardToolbar` scrolls (`overflow-x-auto`) when its content is wider; below `sm` it wrapped the left side beside the buttons. The bulk bar put the count and three buttons that never shrink in one row.
- **Decision (owner, 2026-10-02):**
  - **Three toolbar layouts** (Menu items, the reference): phones put the search on its own full-width line, the category (`flex-1`) with `⋯` and the grid/list switch on the next, Reorder and Select behind `⋯` (`UDropdownMenu`); tablets one line, the search growing (`flex-1`, at most `max-w-xs`), the category 12rem, icon buttons with their tooltips; labels from `xl` (from `lg` the sidebar takes 16rem, so labels at `lg` overflowed at 1024 px).
  - **A mode is a toggle:** the button that starts it leaves it, in the same place. Select reads **Cancel** ("Cancel selection") while selecting, and on phones a **Cancel** button replaces `⋯` while selecting. Categories' Reorder reads **Done** ("Done reordering"), disabled until the new order is saved or discarded; its bar's Done is gone. `S` and `R` toggle; Escape still leaves. The label changes with the state, so the button has no `aria-pressed` (a toggle button must not change its name).
  - **`<BulkActionsBar>` carries actions only:** no ✕ (`@exit` removed). The count line never wraps ("None selected" with nothing selected, for mouse and touch alike); on phones the actions share the next line in equal widths (a grid of `auto-cols-fr` columns), side by side from `sm`. Categories gets the same bar.
  - The other list pages were checked at 320, 390, 680 and 1024 px on the dev server: none scrolls sideways (Categories' one line of search and three icon buttons fits; Order history's four filters wrap to a second line at tablet width).
- **Alternatives:** keeping Reorder and Select as two icon buttons on phones (one tap, but the category select gets narrow); a Filters sheet for one filter (the rule keeps one or two filters visible); a labelled toggle with `aria-pressed` and a fixed name "Select" (clearer to screen readers as a toggle, but "Select" pressed doesn't say how to stop); short labels ("Publish", "Archive") on phones (the full labels fit in equal halves at 390 px).
- **Verified:** e2e `products.test.ts` +7 (the toggle and `S`; the phone layout with ⋯ and Cancel, the bar's actions in one row of equal widths; no sideways scroll of the page, the toolbar or the bar, and the count on one line, at 320, 680, 768, 1024 and 1440 px), with the 680 and 1024 px tests checked to fail on the old toolbar; `categories.test.ts` (Cancel selection, Done reordering disabled while unsaved), `list-bulk.test.ts` (None selected); screenshots of every list toolbar at 390 and 680 px.

### D130: KHQR at the counter: a QR per order, confirmed by the cashier (step 10.15a), 2026-10-02

- **Context:** the counter took "KHQR" as an instruction: the customer scanned the cafe's printed KHQR, typed the amount, and the cashier matched the payment by eye. The owner asked for KHQR so customers in Cambodia can scan and pay. Research ([plan](plans/khqr.md)): KHQR is NBC's EMVCo-based payment QR; receiving is free; checking a payment needs the Bakong Open API (a 90-day token, reportedly refused outside Cambodia). The owner chose (2026-10-02): **a dynamic KHQR at the counter, not online payment** (D45's "pay at the counter before preparation" stays), **free and on Cloudflare only** (no server of our own), a personal Bakong account (not a registered business, so no ABA PayWay); the Bakong check is step 10.15b, once the owner's Bakong account is verified.
- **Decision:**
  - **Our own KHQR builder** (`server/features/orders/khqr.ts`, pure): an individual account (tag 29), the SDK's tag order, amount format ("12", "4.50", riel whole), bill number and store label (tag 62), creation and expiry times (tag 99), CRC-16/CCITT-FALSE; MD5 by `@noble/hashes` (already installed through Better Auth; Web Crypto has no MD5 outside Workers). Checked byte for byte against QR texts NBC's JavaScript SDK (`bakong-khqr` 1.0.20) made, each accepted by its `verify`. The SDK itself isn't a dependency: it pulls in axios 0.24 (published advisories) for an API we don't call from it.
  - **`khqr_settings`** (one row, versioned, audited, `settings: ['manage']`): on/off, the Bakong account ID (`name@bank`, lower-cased), the name customers see and the city (ASCII: the QR's text), USD and/or riel. `GET/PUT /api/admin/khqr`; Payments → **KHQR at the counter** (Reload after someone else's save keeps the input).
  - **`khqr_charges`**: `POST /api/counter/{branchId}/orders/{id}/khqr` `{ currency }` answers the order's open QR in that currency (a minute or more left, the account unchanged) or makes one: unpaid orders only, before the pay-by time, 15 minutes or until the pay-by time. Riel at the current rate rounded up to ៛100 (`toRiel`). The MD5 is unique (10.15b looks payments up by it).
  - **Pay** with `method: 'khqr'` takes an optional `chargeId`: refused (`KHQR_CHARGE_INVALID`) unless it's this order's, in this branch; kept on the payment (`counter_payments.khqr_charge_id`, migration `0023`) and in the audit. An expired QR is still accepted: the customer may have paid at the last second, and the cashier confirms only money they see. The payment's amount stays the order's dollar total (riel detail on the charge), so reports are unchanged.
  - **The counter:** Take payment → KHQR shows the QR (drawn by `uqr` like the table QRs; `<QrCode>` and `qrImage` moved to the root now that two features use them), the amount, "Order 042 · to NUK Cafe", and the minutes left by the server's clock (the queue's offset); US dollars / Riel when both are offered; New QR once expired; **Confirm KHQR payment · $2.25**. Not set up: the printed-KHQR instruction, as before.
- **Alternatives:** NBC's SDK as a dependency (the old axios, and `buffer`/`md5` polyfills, for a 100-line format); online payment before preparation (changes D45 and needs Bakong's check first); a static QR with the amount typed by the customer (today's mistakes stay); refusing a payment on an expired QR (punishes a customer who paid at the last second).
- **Verified:** server `khqr.test.ts` 6 (the SDK's four vectors, amount formats, the CRC's check value) and `khqr.service.test.ts` 10 (settings from the version read and a race of two saves, checked to fail without the guard; refused until on and in a currency not offered; the open QR answered again, a new one with under a minute left or after the account changed; riel rounded up, no rate refused; capped at the pay-by time, refused once paid, past due or in another branch; the payment names its QR, another order's refused and nothing recorded, checked to fail without the check); unit `khqr.test.ts` 2 (amounts, the countdown by the server's clock); e2e `payments.test.ts` +2 (saved lower-cased with the version; a Khmer name and an ID without @bank refused before sending; Reload keeps the input), `counter.test.ts` +1 on the real server (the QR named by order and amount, "Works for", Riel at ៛4,100 → ៛9,300, Confirm, the payment row joined to its QR).

### D131: KHQR checked with Bakong: the counter records a paid QR by itself (step 10.15b), 2026-10-02

- **Context:** with D130 the cashier still confirmed each KHQR payment by looking at the bank app. The owner's Bakong account is verified and has a Bakong Open API token (90 days). Bakong sends no webhooks: the payee asks (`check_transaction_by_md5`), and Bakong reportedly refuses servers outside Cambodia in production, which a Cloudflare Worker usually is. The owner's constraints stand: free, Cloudflare only.
- **Decision:**
  - **`NUXT_BAKONG_TOKEN`** (a Worker secret the owner sets; never in chat or the repository) turns the check on; **`NUXT_BAKONG_API_URL`** (default Bakong's) can point at a relay later. Without the token everything works as in D130.
  - **Our own client** (`server/features/orders/bakong.ts`, `fetch` passed in for tests): `POST /v1/check_transaction_by_md5` with `Bearer` and `{ md5 }`, 8 s timeout. `responseCode 0` with a transaction is **paid**; `errorCode 1` **not found**; `errorCode 6` or 401 **token**; 403 **refused**; anything else **error** (a "success" without a usable transaction is never taken as paid).
  - **`POST /api/counter/{branchId}/orders/{id}/khqr/{chargeId}/check`** (`payment: ['collect']`): the QR must be this order's, in this branch (`KHQR_CHARGE_INVALID`). Already recorded with this QR: `paid` without asking Bakong. Bakong's transaction must name the QR's account (any case), currency and amount (cents, or whole riel), or it's **`mismatch`** and nothing is recorded. Matching and the order unpaid: the payment is recorded **as the cashier who asked** (`recordCheckedKhqrPayment`: the same payment, event, outbox message and audit as `pay`, `checkedWithBakong` in the audit, Bakong's `externalRef` or hash as the reference, keyed `khqr-check:<chargeId>`), **even past the pay-by time** if the expiry hasn't run (the money arrived). Two cashiers checking at once record one payment (the version guard and the payment's unique index; the loser reads the order back and answers `paid`). Paid on Bakong but the order was paid another way or cancelled: **`refund_needed`**, the counter says to give it back. Bakong unavailable: **`unavailable`** with the reason.
  - **The counter** asks every 5 s while the QR is on screen, until 5 minutes after it expired, one request at a time; it stops on a final answer (`paid`, `mismatch`, `refund_needed`) or one a person must fix (token, refused; **Try again**), and keeps asking through a network blip. "Waiting for the payment: it's recorded as soon as Bakong confirms it." shows **above the QR** (below it, a tablet's 820 px panel hid it and the mismatch alert under the fold: found in the screenshots, guarded by the e2e test). Paid: the panel closes with "Order 042 paid · Bakong confirmed the KHQR payment", and only then is the queue refreshed (refreshed first, the order leaves the payment form and the answer was lost: found by the e2e test). Confirm by hand stays.
  - **Payments → Automatic check with Bakong:** On/Off (the queue and the settings carry `automaticCheck`), and **Test connection** (`POST /api/admin/khqr/test`, `settings: ['manage']`: a made-up MD5; "not found" means the token and this server are accepted).
- **Alternatives:** a cron that checks every unpaid QR (a minute late at best, and asks Bakong for QRs nobody is looking at); recording in the Bakong check only after a cashier's tap (safer against a wrong match, but the matching rules already require our account, currency and amount); refusing a verified payment past the pay-by time (the customer paid; the expiry then cancels and a refund is needed anyway); a paid relay server in Cambodia now (not free; kept as the fallback if staging shows the 403, via `NUXT_BAKONG_API_URL` and a Cloudflare Tunnel once there's a domain, Q4).
- **The 403 question, answered (owner, 2026-10-02):** the owner set `NUXT_BAKONG_TOKEN` on the staging Worker and **Test connection** said **Connected**: Bakong answers the Worker (served from the Cloudflare location nearest the owner in Cambodia), so no relay is needed. **A real payment worked** (owner, 2026-10-02): a QR paid from a banking app at the staging counter was recorded by the automatic check. A Telegram reminder before the token's 90 days is left for later; meanwhile the token's expiry shows on the counter and on Payments, and cashiers confirm by hand.
- **Verified:** server `bakong.test.ts` 4 (each answer's meaning, the request's URL, headers and body, a failure, a timeout, a non-JSON 403) and `khqr-check.service.test.ts` 12 (paid recorded once as the asking cashier with its audit, asked again without calling Bakong; riel; waiting; another amount, currency or account records nothing; no token, token, refused, error; past the pay-by time; already paid in cash, expired, cancelled → refund; two cashiers at once → one payment, checked to fail without the read-back; another order's or an unknown QR refused; the match rules; Test connection); unit `khqr.test.ts` +2; e2e `counter.test.ts` +1 against a stand-in Bakong the e2e server is pointed at (`test/e2e/support/fake-bakong.ts`: the real server and client, nothing mocked in the browser): the payment recorded with nobody pressing Confirm, with its QR and reference; a wrong amount said and not recorded; `payments.test.ts` +1 (Off, On, Test connection refused then connected).

### D132: The Bakong token's expiry on Payments and reminders on Telegram (step 10.16), 2026-10-02

- **Context:** Bakong's API token lasts 90 days (D131). When it expires the counter falls back to confirming by hand: no money is lost, but the automatic check stops, and the app only noticed once Bakong refused the token. The owner asked for a reminder and whether it would be Telegram or the Payments page. Their token starts with `eyJ`: a JWT, whose payload carries its expiry (`exp`).
- **Decision (owner, 2026-10-02: both):**
