  - **The expiry is read from the token** (`bakongTokenExpiry`, `server/features/orders/bakong.ts`): the JWT's payload is decoded for `exp`, never verified (it's our own secret; the date only drives the page and the reminders). Nothing to type in; a new token brings its own date. Not a JWT, or no `exp`: no date, and Payments says to note the day it was set.
  - **Payments → Automatic check with Bakong** shows "Token expires 21 Dec 2026 · 80 days left" (`GET /api/admin/khqr` → `tokenExpiresAt`), amber within 14 days with what to do, red once expired. The date in the cafe's time zone, like the messages.
  - **Telegram reminders** at 14, 7, 3 and 1 days before and on the day, to the chats with **Server errors and reminders** on (the `server_error` switch, relabelled: a reminder is an operational alert like a server error, and a kind of its own would need both tables' `CHECK` constraints rebuilt). Queued by `notifications:deliver` (every minute, already running); nothing is read from the database while more than 14 days are left. Each reminder goes once per chat and token (the dedupe key names the expiry and the stage, so a new token starts over); a server down on day 7 sends the 7-day one on day 6; an already expired token gets one "has expired" message. The message has an Open Payments button.
  - The token itself never leaves the server: the page and the messages carry only its date.
- **Alternatives:** Telegram only (nothing to look at when you wonder); the Payments page only (nobody opens it at the right time); an admin types the date the token was set (a step to forget, and wrong after a renewal); a new notification kind with its own switch (a table rebuild for two `CHECK` constraints, for one reminder).
- **Verified:** server `bakong.test.ts` +2 (`exp` read from a JWT in base64url; plain tokens, a broken payload, a missing, string or negative `exp` give no date), `notifications.delivery.test.ts` +3 (the stages including a late server; once per stage and chat with the date, the instructions and the button, sent; a new token starts over; two runs at once queue one per chat), `khqr-check.service.test.ts` (the settings carry the date); unit `bakong-token.test.ts` 2 (days counted up, the colours, expired); e2e `payments.test.ts` (5 days left in amber with what to do), with `telegram.test.ts`, `assistant.test.ts` and `counter.test.ts` passing.

### D133: A merge to main deploys without running the checks again, 2026-10-02

- **Context:** every change ran the full checks twice: on its pull request, then again on `main` after the merge, before the staging deploy (D54). The owner noticed and asked to drop the second run: the pull request already verified the change, and they work alone. Each run on `main` costs about 5–6 minutes before staging updates and about 15 runner-minutes (`check` plus three e2e shards; the repository is private, so Actions minutes are limited).
- **Why the second run is mostly a repeat:** a pull request's checks run on GitHub's merge preview (the branch merged into `main` as it was). If nothing else lands on `main` before the merge, the merged code is exactly what was checked. With one person and one pull request at a time (progress.md → How we work), that's the normal case.
- **Decision (owner, 2026-10-02):**
  - `check` and `e2e` run on pull requests and by hand (`workflow_dispatch`, Actions → CI → Run workflow), not on a push to `main`.
  - A push to `main` runs `deploy-staging` straight away; its smoke check (health, CSP header, JSON 404, 401 without a session) still guards the deploy.
  - Runs are cancelled by a newer push only on pull requests: on `main` the deploy now starts at once, and a cancel between the migrations and the Worker would leave staging half deployed.
  - When `main` moved after a pull request's checks (two merged back to back), press **Update branch** on the second one before merging, or run the workflow on `main` by hand afterwards (AGENTS.md → Checks).
- **Not covered:** a pull request merged without its checks on the current `main` (the gap above) reaches staging unchecked; staging is the only environment, and a bad deploy is fixed forward or restored (operations.md → Restore drill). **Revisit at production (8.2):** a production deploy should be gated by checks on exactly the code it ships (branch protection requiring up-to-date branches, or the checks before the production job).
- **Alternatives:** keep the full run on `main` (safe, but doubles the time and minutes for a solo, one-PR-at-a-time flow); keep only `check` on `main` (half the saving, and the e2e suite is where merge surprises would show); branch protection requiring branches to be up to date (closes the gap, but on a private repository it needs a paid GitHub plan).
- **Verified:** the workflow file parses (YAML lint); the effect shows on this pull request (checks run) and after its merge (only `deploy-staging` runs on `main`).

### D134: Multi-tenant (SaaS-ready): a tenant per cafe, paths on one domain, organization = tenant (phase T, T0), 2026-10-02

- **Context:** the owner's boss may sell the app to other cafes as a subscription (an online menu with ordering, counter payments and reports). The owner wants it ready to deliver when funding or a partner comes, and builds, tests and fixes alone. The app was built for one cafe: no table says which business a row belongs to, a Better Auth organization is a **branch**, the global `admin` role means *the cafe's admin*, and the Bakong token is one Worker secret. Nothing is in production, so this is the cheapest moment to add the boundary; every feature added first would add tables to rework. The only address is `nuk-cafe-staging.kimsur61.workers.dev` (no domain yet, Q4), where wildcard subdomains aren't possible.
- **Decision (owner, 2026-10-02; plan: [plans/multi-tenant.md](plans/multi-tenant.md)):**
  - **SaaS-ready, not SaaS-complete.** Now: the tenant boundary under every query (T1) and the platform console with per-cafe branding and secrets (T2). Self sign-up, plans and billing (T3) wait until the commercial plan is confirmed.
  - **One shared D1 database with `tenant_id`** on every tenant-owned table, composite foreign keys for links between a tenant's records, and a two-tenant isolation test in every feature (checked to fail with the filter removed). Not a database per tenant: D1 bindings are fixed in the Worker's configuration, migrations would run per database, and cafe-sized data fits one database for years.
  - **The tenant is in the path:** `/c/<slug>/` (customer site), `/c/<slug>/admin`, `/c/<slug>/counter/<branchId>`, and `/api/c/<slug>/…`; `/platform` for super admins; sign-in pages and `/table/<token>` stay global. The server resolves the tenant from the path and checks membership; `apiFetch` and one link helper add the prefix, so a later move to subdomains or cafes' own domains touches two places.
  - **A Better Auth organization is a tenant; branches become our own table** (same ids). The spike showed Better Auth's `additionalFields` can only reference its own models (`nuxt prepare`: `Model "menu_categories" not found in schema`), so branch-organizations couldn't carry a real foreign key to a tenant table of ours. Roles: `superadmin` on the platform (no access to a cafe's data); `owner` or `member` in a tenant (owner = today's admin); `manager` or `staff` per branch in our `branch_staff` (today's branch roles and access control, unchanged); a customer profile per tenant with one login for the platform.
  - **Owners manage memberships, not accounts:** a password reset or a disable (global effects) only for an account that belongs to that tenant alone.
  - NUK Cafe becomes tenant #1 (`/c/nuk`) by a hand-written migration; each T1 PR leaves the app working (the only tenant is the current tenant until T1.5 moves the addresses).
- **Alternatives:** stay single-cafe and retrofit later (every new table and feature makes it costlier, and real data would need migrating); a database per tenant (strong isolation, but dynamic D1 access needs the REST API or Workers for Platforms); subdomains now (impossible on `workers.dev`); the tenant remembered in the session (`activeOrganizationId`: two tabs on two cafes would fight, and a shared link wouldn't name its cafe); keeping organization = branch with our own `tenants` table (no foreign key from a branch to its tenant, tenant roles outside Better Auth's membership).
- **Verified:** the spike (the failing `nuxt prepare` above, reverted); no code changes in T0.

### D135: Tenancy core: organization = tenant, our own branches and branch staff, the migration (step T1.1), 2026-10-02

- **Context:** D134 planned the tenant boundary. T1.1 builds its core: the identity model, branches, and every table that points at a branch. Better Auth's organizations were branches; their ids were referenced by the branch hours, dining tables, sold-out switches, orders, payments and KHQR charges.
- **Decision:**
  - **An organization is a tenant; branches are our `branches` table** (the old branch-organizations' ids kept, so every `branch_id` still holds), with `tenant_id`. Who works where is `branch_staff (tenant_id, branch_id, user_id, role)`, and each such person is a `member` of the tenant. Tenant roles: `owner` (what the old global `admin` held, shown as "Admin" in the app; the contracts are unchanged) and `member`. Platform roles: `customer`, `superadmin` (tenants only, no tenant data; used from T2).
  - **Composite foreign keys:** the tables that point at a branch, and the orders family, carry `tenant_id` with `(tenant_id, branch_id)` → `branches(tenant_id, id)` and `(tenant_id, order_id)` → `orders(tenant_id, id)`: the database refuses a link across tenants even if a query forgot its filter. The menu, customers, settings, Telegram and the platform tables get `tenant_id` in T1.2–T1.4, each in its own PR.
  - **Migration `0024_tenants`, written by hand:** drizzle-kit's rebuilds rely on `PRAGMA foreign_keys=OFF`, which D1 ignores, and dropping the old `orders` would then run its children's ON DELETE rules (deleting order lines) or fail on `restrict`. Instead: the tenant "NUK Cafe" (`nuk`, another slug if taken) is created when the database holds anything; branches, branch staff and owners are copied; each rebuilt table is created under a temporary name **pointing at its new parent**, filled, then the old tables are dropped **children first** (no rule finds a row to act on), the old branch organizations deleted, and the new tables renamed (SQLite rewrites the children's references). Tested over linked rows with foreign keys on: every row moved, no temporary name left, `foreign_key_check` empty, the new keys refuse a cross-tenant link.
  - **Access:** the request's tenant comes from the server (`requireTenant`: the only tenant until T1.5 puts it in the path; 404 when none or suspended). `requirePermission` (same name, 91 routes unchanged) now checks the role **in that tenant**; `requireBranchPermission` checks the branch belongs to the tenant (another tenant's branch is 404) and the caller is on its staff or an owner. The actor carries `tenantId`; services scope to it. The route rules' admin gate asked for the global `admin` role, which no longer exists: it asks for a session only, and `server/tests/admin-routes.test.ts` checks every admin route calls an access helper.
  - **Staff:** owners manage memberships of their own tenant. Another tenant's people are 404. Adding an account that works at another cafe keeps that access; disabling removes only this cafe's. A password reset changes the account everywhere, so it's refused (`STAFF_ELSEWHERE`) for someone with access elsewhere or a super admin. The last-owner guard counts this tenant's owners.
- **Alternatives:** keep organization = branch with our own `tenants` table (Better Auth can't reference it: no foreign key from a branch to its tenant, D134's spike); drizzle-kit's generated rebuilds (unsafe on D1, above); a `tenant_id` default to fill old rows (a forgotten tenant would silently land in NUK Cafe); keeping the admin gate with a tenant-role check in the route rules (they can't see memberships).
- **Verified:** server 609 incl. the migration over linked rows (and an empty database, and a taken slug), two-tenant tests for access (`identity.service.test.ts`), staff (`staff.service.test.ts`: list, read, update, disable, reset, the owner guard, the database's key) and branches (`branches.service.test.ts`: reads, writes, a table's QR); each new guard checked to fail its test when removed (7); every admin route checked for an access helper (95); unit 889; lint; typecheck; the full e2e suite. **Needs verification on staging:** the migration over the real data (Time Travel noted before the deploy), then admin sign-in, the menu, a dine-in order and the counter by hand.

### D136: The menu and its uploads per tenant (step T1.2), 2026-10-03

- **Context:** after T1.1 (D135) the menu was still one shared menu: its 15 `menu_*` tables and `media_assets` had no tenant, and the sold-out switches pointed at a version by its id alone. A second cafe would have seen, changed and linked to NUK Cafe's menu.
- **Decision:**
  - **Every menu table and `media_assets` carries `tenant_id`.** Each menu parent has `unique(tenant_id, id)`, and every link inside the menu is a composite foreign key `(tenant_id, x_id) → x(tenant_id, id)`: a category's parent, an item's category, an item's option sets, versions, version values, add-on groups, add-on prices and rules, a category's rules, a rule's windows, an option set's values, a group's add-ons, and the sold-out switch's version (`branch_item_states`, planned in T1.1). Name uniqueness is per tenant (`(tenant_id, lower(name))` for top-level categories, option sets, add-on groups and rules; sub-categories, values and add-ons are already unique within a parent).
  - **An item's photo keeps no foreign key** (as before): the media feature owns its lifecycle (temporary, attached, purged), and its attach is now scoped to the tenant (`attachStatements(db, tenantId, …)`), so another tenant's upload is "not available" like a missing one.
  - **New uploads go under `t/<tenantId>/menu/<id>.<ext>`** in R2, so one cafe's objects can be listed, exported or removed together. Keys stored before stay `menu/<id>.<ext>`; `/media/**` serves both shapes and nothing else. `media:purge-temporary` stays a platform task over every tenant's expired uploads (the row says whose).
  - **Code:** repositories take `tenantId` where an id comes from the request (lookups, lists, counts, the batch guards on chosen sets, values, groups, add-ons, rules and categories, the reorder lists) and on every insert; children of a record already found in the tenant are read by that record's id, as in T1.1. Admin reads take `actor.tenantId`; writes take it from the actor. The catalog (`loadCatalog(db, tenantId)`), the sold-out check, the sample-data load and reset, and the upload count are per tenant.
  - **Migration `0025_menu_tenants`, written by hand** with 0024's technique (new `__new_…` tables pointing at the new parents, filled with the tenant; old tables dropped children first, sub-categories deleted before their parents for the `restrict` self-reference; renames; indexes). Every existing row goes to NUK Cafe (0024's tenant id, else the oldest organization); a database with menu rows and no tenant gets "NUK Cafe" here.
- **Alternatives:** `tenant_id` only on the library roots (categories, sets, groups, rules, items), leaving the link tables to follow by id (smaller, but the database couldn't refuse a cross-tenant link); a foreign key from an item to its photo (it would turn a purge or a lifecycle bug into a failed task; the scoped attach is the boundary); moving old R2 objects under the prefix (a copy per object for no gain: the row holds the key).
- **Verified:** unit and server 994 (server 716), incl. `menu.tenants.test.ts` (8: lists and reads, writes, links to another tenant's category, parent, option set, add-on group, rule and photo, reorders, the same names, the customer menu, sold out, uploads and the reset) and the migration over a linked menu (`0025_menu_tenants` in `migrations.test.ts`: every row gets the tenant, `foreign_key_check` clean, cross-tenant links refused, an item's children still cascade); each tenant filter checked to fail a test when removed (24, and the three backed by a second layer with both removed); e2e on the real seeded server and the menu screens (10 files, 129 tests), then the full suite (415). **On staging after the deploy:** the menu, an item's photo, a new upload (its key under `t/`), sold out and checkout checked by hand.

### D137: Orders, customer profiles, the KHQR settings and the riel rate per tenant (step T1.3), 2026-10-03

- **Context:** after T1.1 and T1.2 (D135, D136) the orders carried their tenant, but the reads didn't use it: a customer's own orders were found by their account alone, so one account ordering at two cafes would see both cafes' orders on either site, and the two-unpaid limit counted across cafes. The KHQR settings were one row for the whole app (`id = 'default'`), the riel rate one history, and a customer profile (member code) one per account.
- **Decision:**
  - **Orders:** `findOrder(db, tenantId, id)` and the customer's lists and unpaid guard filter by the tenant: another cafe's order is 404 for the customer, the counter and reports, and the limit of two unpaid orders is per cafe. Platform tasks that got an id from our own outbox (the Telegram alerts) read with `findOrderForTask` until the outbox names the tenant (T1.4). The counter's lookups were already scoped by the branch (a tenant's branch since D135); the KHQR charge lookup is scoped too.
  - **Customer profiles:** one per account **and tenant** (`PK (tenant_id, user_id)`), member codes unique per tenant: the account is the platform's, the member code, phone and marketing choice are the cafe's. Created at sign-up in the cafe signed up at (the current tenant until T1.5), in the batch that adds someone to a cafe's staff (an existing account joining another cafe gets one there), or on first use (`ensureProfile(db, tenantId, userId)`).
  - **KHQR settings:** one row per tenant (`tenant_id` the primary key). The Bakong token stays a server setting until T2 moves it per tenant.
  - **Riel rate:** `exchange_rates.tenant_id`; each cafe sets and reads its own rate and history.
  - **Migration `0026_tenant_settings`, written by hand** (drizzle-kit would ask whether `id` became `tenant_id`): nothing references these three tables, so each is copied with NUK Cafe's id, dropped and renamed.
- **Alternatives:** one global profile per account (a member code would leak across cafes, and loyalty is per cafe); keeping one KHQR account for the platform (every cafe's money would go to one Bakong account); a profile created for every cafe at sign-up (a customer of one cafe would get a member code everywhere).
- **Verified:** unit and server 1003, incl. `orders.tenants.test.ts` (5: the customer's orders and cancel across cafes, the unpaid limit per cafe, the counter and reports, the riel rate, the KHQR settings and QRs), profiles per cafe and for an account joining another cafe's staff (`identity.accounts.test.ts`), and the migration (`0026_tenant_settings` in `migrations.test.ts`: rows moved, member codes unique per tenant, one settings row per tenant); each tenant filter checked to fail a test when removed (8); the full e2e suite (415; the counter's rate and KHQR rows written by its tests now name the tenant). **On staging after the deploy:** the member code, Payments, a riel payment and a KHQR checked by hand.

### D138: Telegram and notifications per tenant; the outbox names its tenant (step T1.4a), 2026-10-03

- **Context:** after T1.3 (D137) Telegram was still one set of chats for the whole app: every cafe's admins would have seen, changed and sent to the same chats, and an order's alerts went to every chat with the switch on. The outbox didn't know whose change a message came from. T1.4 was planned as one step; it's split in two to keep each pull request reviewable: T1.4a (this) Telegram, notifications and the outbox, T1.4b reports, the assistant, sample data, audit and idempotency.
- **Decision:**
  - **Chats, links, rules and deliveries carry `tenant_id`**, linked by composite keys `(tenant_id, destination_id)`. A chat is unique **per tenant**: one Telegram chat (an owner's private chat, a staff group) can follow two cafes, each with its own row, switches and history.
  - **Telegram's own events apply to every cafe that has the chat:** the bot removed or blocked marks it blocked everywhere, a group upgraded to a supergroup moves everywhere. **The bot leaves a group only when no cafe still has it connected** (`chatConnectedAnywhere`): one cafe disconnecting it must not stop the other's messages.
  - **A link's code connects the chat to the cafe whose admin made it** (`telegram_links.tenant_id`): the code is the only key Telegram sends. Reading, confirming and cancelling a link are scoped to the tenant (the same person owning two cafes can't confirm one cafe's link from the other).
  - **The outbox names its tenant** (`outbox_messages.tenant_id`, nullable): the orders' events carry it, account emails are the platform's (`null`). The handler receives it, so an order's alerts go to that cafe's chats only (`orderAlert(db, tenantId, orderId)`, and `findOrderForTask` from D137 is gone).
  - **Tasks:** the closing summaries run for every active tenant (`activeTenants`); a server error alerts the request's tenant (the only one while a request hadn't resolved one, until T1.5 puts it in the path); the Bakong token's reminders go to the token's tenant, NUK Cafe's, until each cafe has its own token (T2). Delivery and the 90-day purge stay platform tasks over every tenant's rows (each row says whose it is).
  - **Migration `0027_telegram_tenants`, written by hand** like 0024: the four tables rebuilt with NUK Cafe's id; the outbox column added, the orders' pending events given the tenant. A link's `destination_id` is now `restrict` (was `set null`): chats are never deleted, and a composite key can't be half-nulled.
  - The bot's fixed texts still say "NUK Cafe"; they take the cafe's name with the platform's name (QT1, T2).
- **Alternatives:** one chat row shared by cafes with a cafe list per rule (a disconnect, title or history would leak across cafes); the tenant in the outbox payload instead of a column (the delivery code would trust a JSON field, and platform messages couldn't be told apart); leaving the group on any cafe's disconnect (simpler, but it silently stops another cafe's alerts).
- **Verified:** unit and server 1011, incl. `notifications.tenants.test.ts` (5: chats, rules and test messages per cafe; links for another cafe's admin and for one account owning both; one group following two cafes, the bot leaving only after both disconnect; an order's alert to its own cafe; delivery history and server errors per cafe), the outbox handing its handler the tenant (`platform.service.test.ts`), and the migration (`0027_telegram_tenants` in `migrations.test.ts`); each tenant filter checked to fail a test when removed (11, the link's with one account owning both cafes); the full e2e suite (415). **On staging after the deploy:** the Telegram page, Send test, and a new-order and payment alert checked by hand.

### D139: Audit, idempotency, the assistant's usage and sample data per tenant; reports' isolation tested (step T1.4b), 2026-10-03

- **Context:** after T1.4a (D138) four platform-wide tables still didn't know the cafe: `audit_events` (whose change it was), `idempotency_keys` (a retry key scoped by account and operation only), `assistant_usage` (the daily limit counted per account across every cafe) and `sample_data_runs` (one row for the whole app). Reports already took the tenant (D137) but had no two-cafe test.
- **Decision:**
  - **Audit events name their tenant** (`audit_events.tenant_id`, nullable like the outbox's: `null` is the platform's own, e.g. a future platform console). `AuditActor` gains a required `tenantId`, so every call site states it: the access helpers' actor carries it; the system's unpaid-order expiry uses the order's tenant; a chat connected from Telegram (no session) uses the link's tenant; the seed task its tenant.
  - **The idempotency scope includes the tenant** (`(tenant_id, actor_id, operation, key)` unique, `tenant_id` not null): one account acting in two cafes never replays one cafe's answer in the other, nor gets a mismatch from it.
  - **The assistant's daily limit counts per admin and cafe** (`assistant_usage.tenant_id`; the guard and "used today" count that cafe's rows). Usage is the cafe's cost. A limit for the whole cafe (whatever its admins) is a plan limit, left to T3 (QT2).
  - **The sample-data run is per tenant** (primary key `(tenant_id, id)`): a cafe's load, its step lock and its reset never touch another's.
  - **Reports:** unchanged; their isolation (another cafe's branch is not found for every report, export and Telegram message; a cafe's branches and figures only its own) is now tested.
  - **Migration `0028_platform_tenants`, written by hand** like 0024: the three tables with a NOT NULL tenant (or a new key) rebuilt with NUK Cafe's id; the audit column added and every existing event given NUK Cafe's id (all were its).
- **Alternatives:** the tenant inside audit `metadata` (as staff changes already put it; not indexed or enforced); the idempotency key left global (a key is a UUID, so a clash is unlikely, but the scope would rely on luck rather than the database); one daily limit per account across cafes (one cafe's use would block another's).
- **Verified:** unit and server 1020, incl. tenants tests in `platform.service.test.ts` (keys per tenant), `assistant.service.test.ts` (limit and "used today" per cafe), `sample-data.service.test.ts` (load, lock and reset per cafe), `orders.tenants.test.ts` (+3: reports, the same key placing an order at each cafe, audit rows with their cafe), `notifications.tenants.test.ts` (+1: a chat connected from Telegram audited in the link's cafe), the expiry's audit, and the migration (`0028_platform_tenants`); each tenant filter checked to fail a test when removed (12, the report branch lookups included); the full e2e suite (415). **On staging after the deploy:** a few admin changes, the assistant, Sample data, an order placed and paid.

### D140: Each cafe's API under its address, `/api/c/<slug>/…` (step T1.5a), 2026-10-03

- **Context:** after T1.4 (D135–D139) every tenant-owned query names its tenant, but a request's tenant was still "the only one" (`currentTenant`), so a second cafe couldn't be reached. T1.5 puts the cafe in the address (D134's scheme). It touches every API route and page; it's split to keep each pull request reviewable: T1.5a (this) the API, T1.5b the pages.
- **Decision:**
  - **A cafe's routes live under its address:** the `admin`, `counter`, `public` and `shop` surfaces moved to `server/api/c/[slug]/` (`/api/c/<slug>/admin/…`). `requireTenant` (which every access helper calls) reads the route's `slug`, loads the tenant (`tenantBySlug`: unknown 404 `NOT_FOUND`, paused 403 `TENANT_SUSPENDED`) and keeps it on `event.context.tenant`; the helpers then check the caller may act there, as before. The route parameter rather than a middleware (D134's plan said middleware): a test can check every route under `c/[slug]` calls a helper (`server/tests/routes.test.ts`), and no route outside it does.
  - **The platform's routes stay outside:** `/api/auth` (Better Auth), `/api/webhooks/telegram`, `/api/health` (was `/api/public/health`; the deploy's smoke check) and **`/api/tables/{token}`** (was `/api/public/tables/{token}`): a printed QR code names no cafe, so the scan finds the cafe from the token and answers it with the branch and table (`ScannedTable`). A paused cafe's tables are 404.
  - **The app adds the address in one place:** `apiPath` (`app/utils/api-path.ts`) puts `/admin`, `/counter`, `/public` and `/shop` calls under `/c/<slug>`; feature code still calls `apiFetch('/admin/…')`. Until pages carry an address (T1.5b) the slug is `NUXT_PUBLIC_DEFAULT_TENANT` (`nuk`, NUK Cafe's since 0024); the browser reads it once in the API plugin (`apiFetch` also runs from click handlers, outside Nuxt's context), server rendering per request.
  - **Rate limits** (D121) count a cafe's public routes and the table scan together per client address, whichever cafe (`rateLimitPath` drops the address).
  - **Route rules:** the session gate is on `/api/c/*/admin/**`, `…/counter/**`, `…/shop/**`.
  - **Unchanged for now:** pages and links (`/admin/…`, `/counter/…`, Telegram's Open order, the assistant's page links) until T1.5b; `currentTenant` (the oldest tenant) stays for work without an address: a sign-up's first customer profile, a server error outside a cafe's routes, the Bakong token's reminders.
- **Alternatives:** a middleware rewriting `/api/c/<slug>/admin/…` to the old files (fewer moved files, but the code's paths would no longer match the URLs, and a route could forget the tenant unseen); the tenant in a header or cookie (the client would name its tenant; a stale tab could act in the wrong cafe); the table scan under a cafe (a printed code has no cafe in it, and a renamed address would break every printed QR).
- **Verified:** unit and server 1142 (`tenantBySlug` incl. a paused cafe, the table scan incl. a paused cafe and an archived table, `apiPath`, rate limits across cafes, the 212 route checks); the full e2e suite (the mock strips the address; the seeded cafe is at `nuk`). **On staging after the deploy:** the CI smoke check calls `/api/health` and `/api/c/nuk/admin/me`; the admin, the menu, a table QR, checkout and the counter checked by hand.

### D141: Each cafe's pages under its address, `/c/<slug>/…` (step T1.5b), 2026-10-03

- **Context:** after T1.5a (D140) the API was per cafe, but every page still lived at the site's root and called NUK Cafe's API (`NUXT_PUBLIC_DEFAULT_TENANT`): a second cafe still couldn't be visited.
- **Decision:**
  - **A cafe's pages live under `app/pages/c/[slug]/`:** the menu `/c/<slug>`, checkout, orders, the admin and the counter. The platform's pages stay at the root: the customer's account pages and `/table/<token>`.
  - **Feature code names a page inside its cafe** (`'/admin/products'`) and turns it into an address with `useTenantPath()` (`tenantPath(…)`; `tenantUrl`/`splitTenantUrl` the pure helpers). Features' sidebar entries stay inside the cafe and the layout adds the address; the auth and counter paths are functions of the cafe (`loginPath(slug)`, `counterSignInPath(slug)`, …); after login only this cafe's admin pages are accepted as a redirect. **A lint rule** refuses a bare cafe page in a link (`to="/admin…"`, `navigateTo('/checkout')`, `router.push`, `{ to | path: '/orders' }`); API paths aren't links and aren't checked.
  - **`apiFetch` takes the cafe from the page's address:** in the browser from `window.location` (a route middleware calls the API before the router has moved, the first page included), on the server from the request's URL. **Moving to another cafe is a full page load**, so the address and the app's state change together; an in-app switch comes with T2's switcher.
  - **The server refuses a page for an address that names no cafe** (404) or a paused one (403) before rendering (`31.cafe-pages.ts`); their own pages come in T2. **Old addresses** (`/`, `/admin/…`, `/counter/…`, `/checkout`, `/orders/…`) answer 302 to NUK Cafe's, the query kept (`30.old-addresses.ts`), so bookmarks and Telegram messages already sent still work; 302, as `/` becomes the platform's own page later.
  - **A table's QR** (`/table/<token>`) asks the global scan (D140) and opens that cafe's menu at the table. **The account pages** (no cafe in their address) return to `?redirect=` when it's a cafe's customer page or a table link (never a workspace), and "Back to the menu" is that page's cafe, else NUK Cafe's.
  - **Server-built links:** Telegram's buttons (Open order, View full report, Open Payments) carry the cafe's address (`cafeSiteUrl(slug)`); the assistant's page list stays inside the cafe and the app adds the address to its answers' buttons.
  - **Browser storage keeps its keys:** the table, the chosen branch and the cart are keyed or guarded by branch, and branch ids are unique across cafes; the menu now shows a stored table only at its own branch, as checkout already did. The menu page's 60-second cache (D122) is per address, so per cafe.
- **Alternatives:** a router middleware prefixing bare links on navigation (the links' `href`s would still lack the cafe: a link opened in a new tab or copied would land in the default cafe); per-cafe storage keys (more keys for state that's already per branch); the cafe in a cookie or the session (two tabs on two cafes would fight, D134).
- **Verified:** unit and server 1148 (`tenant-path`, account returns, old addresses, Telegram links with the cafe); lint (the rule checked to report each kind of bare link); e2e `cafes.test.ts` (6, a second seeded cafe at `/c/brown-bean`: each address renders only its own menu, unknown addresses 404 for pages and API, old addresses redirect, a QR opens its own cafe at its table, one account sees each cafe's own orders, a cashier works only at their cafe's counter), with the server's and the browser's cafe detection each checked to fail a test when replaced by the default cafe; the full e2e suite (421). **On staging after the deploy:** `/` and `/admin/login` move to `/c/nuk/…`; the admin, the menu, a table QR, checkout, the counter and Telegram's Open order checked by hand.
- **Follow-up, 2026-10-03 (staging deploy after the merge):** the smoke check got 404 at `/c/nuk/admin/login`. A Worker build (`wrangler dev`, local D1) answers 200 there once a tenant has the slug `nuk`, and 404 "The cafe was not found." without one, so staging's NUK Cafe doesn't have the address `nuk`. The likely cause is 0024's fallback: it names the tenant `nuk-cafe` when an old branch organization already has the slug `nuk`, then deletes those organizations, so `nuk` is free again. **Migration `0029_nuk_address`** gives tenant #1 the slug `nuk` when it has `nuk-cafe` and no organization holds `nuk`, and does nothing anywhere else (test in `server/tests/migrations.test.ts`; it fails when the "nuk is free" condition is removed). **Rejected alternative:** setting `NUXT_PUBLIC_DEFAULT_TENANT=nuk-cafe` for staging. Every printed link, Telegram message and redirect would then name a different address on staging than in production, for a slug that only exists because of a migration conflict. The smoke check now asks `GET /api/c/nuk/public/branches` first and prints the Worker's answer when it isn't 200, so the next failure says which cafe check failed.

### D142: The platform console: cafes, their first owner, pause and resume, change of address (step T2a), 2026-10-03
- **Context:** T2 needed the platform's own console before a second cafe can exist on staging. The owner skipped the mockup round (2026-10-03: "the frames generated look bad; you decide the design"), so the screens follow the UI standard's patterns (page-patterns.md) and Nuxt UI's defaults. T2 is split into three pull requests: **T2a** the console (this entry), **T2b** choosing a cafe and the switcher, the cafe profile, the not-found and paused pages, **T2c** each cafe's own Bakong token.
- **Decision:**
  - **`/platform` (layout `platform`, the admin's dashboard shell with its own sidebar, "NUK Platform" until QT1 is answered):** `/platform/sign-in` and `/platform/change-password` like the counter's (D102), a session of its own (`usePlatformSession`, `GET /api/platform/me`; `platform-session:*` state, the session boundary and the API plugin end it like the others). Pages: **Cafes** (the reference index: a table from `sm`, rows on phones; status tabs All / Active / Paused with counts; search by name or address; the name opens the cafe), **New cafe** (a modal: the cafe's name and web address, which follows the name until typed by hand; the first branch's name and time zone; the first owner's name and email), its **result** (the owner's sign-in address and temporary password shown once, or "already had an account"), and a **cafe's page** (`/platform/cafes/<id>`: stat cards for branches, staff, orders in 30 days and the last order; the web address with Change and the earlier addresses; the owners; Pause in a danger card with a required reason; a paused cafe's alert with its reason and Resume, asked first).
  - **Server: the `tenants` feature** (`server/features/tenants/`) on `/api/platform/tenants` (list, get, create, `suspend`, `resume`, `slug`), every route behind `requirePlatformPermission` (the `superadmin` platform role, D134; a test checks every platform route calls it and none resolves a cafe). It orchestrates other features through their public APIs: `firstBranchStatement` and `activeBranchCounts` (branches), `planStaffCreate`, `staffCounts`, `tenantOwners` (identity; `createStaff` now builds on `planStaffCreate`), `orderActivity` (orders, with an index on `(tenant_id, placed_at)`). A new cafe, its address, its branch, its owner (a new account with a temporary password, or an existing one keeping its password), the owner's customer profile and the audit row are **one batch**.
  - **Addresses:** `tenant_slugs` keeps every address a cafe has ever had, the current one included, keyed by the address: a second cafe can never take one (an old printed menu or a Telegram message would land in the wrong cafe), even when two saves race. A page at a former address 302s to the cafe's current one (`31.cafe-pages`); the API doesn't follow (pages reload at the new address). A cafe may take back its own former address. Printed table QR codes never change: they don't name the cafe. Rules in `shared/contracts/tenants.ts`: 3–40 lowercase letters, digits and single dashes, and a reserved list (`admin`, `api`, `platform`, `www`, …) for a later move to subdomains.
  - **Pause:** `status = 'suspended'` with a required reason (for the platform team only) and a version check; every cafe route already answers 403 `TENANT_SUSPENDED` (D140) and its pages 403 (their own paused page comes in T2b). Resume clears the reason.
  - **Audit:** each console change is audited in the cafe's own history (`tenant.create`, `tenant.suspend`, `tenant.resume`, `tenant.slug.change`) with the super admin as the actor.
  - **Making a super admin** stays a hand step (operations.md → Super admins): the D1 console on staging; locally the seed task makes its first owner one too. No screen grants it, so the console can't widen its own team.
  - Migration `0030_platform_console`: `tenant_slugs` (every cafe's current address copied in), `organization.suspended_reason` (a Better Auth additional field), the orders index. `seedTenant` moved from identity to tenants (it records the address too).
  - `timezoneOptions` moved to `app/utils/timezones.ts`: the Branch form and the New cafe form both use it.
- **Alternatives:** a separate platform app or Worker (one deployment is the plan's choice, D134); deleting a cafe (orders and reports reference it: paused only, as the plan says); giving a renamed cafe's old address back to anyone after a while (a printed menu lives for years); granting `superadmin` from the console (a compromised super admin could add others).
- **Verified:** server (`tenants.service.test.ts` 18: create incl. an existing owner, taken and former addresses, an unknown time zone, two creates racing for one address, an address taken and left again meanwhile, an owner account created meanwhile, the list with usage, search, status and pages, pause and resume with stale versions, change of address incl. taking one back and a race; the address history and the version guard each checked to fail when removed); the platform session check; migration `0030`; every platform route checks the platform role; unit (address rules, the form, usage lines); e2e `platform.test.ts` (11, mocked API: sign-in and its refusal, a temporary password, the list and its filters, phone rows, New cafe with the password shown once and a taken address on its field, change of address, pause and resume, an unknown cafe; the sign-in tests checked to fail without the page guard) and `cafes.test.ts` (+1 on the real server: a former address redirects, the platform API refuses without a session).

### D143: Each cafe's own name and logo, and its not-found and paused pages (step T2b), 2026-10-03
- **Context:** "NUK Cafe" was written into the menu's header, the admin's sidebar, the counter, the sign-in pages, the tab titles, the printed reports and the error page. With a second cafe on the platform (D142), every cafe showed NUK Cafe's name. An unknown cafe address showed "Page not found" and a paused cafe a bare 403. The owner skipped the mockup round (D142), so the screens follow page-patterns.md. T2 is now four pull requests: T2a the console, **T2b** (this entry), **T2c** choosing a cafe and the switcher, **T2d** each cafe's own Bakong token.
- **Decision:**
  - **Profile:** a cafe's name is the organization's `name` (the console sets it at creation); its logo is `organization.logo_asset_id` (a Better Auth additional field, no foreign key, D134), an upload in the cafe's media, attached and released by the media feature in the save's batch with the audit row `tenant.profile.update` and a version check on `organization.version`. Migration `0031_cafe_profile`.
  - **Reading it:** `GET /api/cafes/{slug}` (`{ slug, name, logoUrl, status }`), a platform route outside the cafe surfaces, since the account pages have no cafe in their address and must name the cafe they came from. It answers for a paused cafe (its pages name it) and 404s for an unknown or former address; rate limited like the public routes. In the app, the root `useCafe(slug?)` (query `cafe:profile:<slug>`, server-rendered on the menu) and `<CafeLogo>` (the logo, or the coffee icon) replace every hard-coded name: the menu's header and SEO, Review order, the orders pages, the account layout, the admin's sidebar and sign-in, the counter's header and sign-in, the printed report header, the KHQR name's placeholder, and the tab titles (`app.vue`: "<page> · <cafe> Admin", "· <cafe> Counter", "· <cafe>"; the platform console "NUK Platform"). The assistant's help guide no longer names NUK Cafe.
  - **Cafe profile page** (`/admin/cafe`, `app/features/cafe/`, the settings pattern): the name, the logo (`<ImageInput>`), a preview, and the web address read-only ("only the platform team can change it": printed QR codes and links carry it, D142). One draft, Save in the navbar from `lg` and in the bottom bar below; 409 → Reload keeps the input; the unsaved-changes guard. Permission `settings: ['manage']` (owners). The save invalidates `cafe`, so the sidebar, the title and other tabs follow.
  - **Shared upload:** `ProductImageInput` became the root `<ImageInput>` (`alt`, `noun`), with `useImageUpload()` (`media:upload`) and `shrinkImage` moved to the root: the second feature needing uploads arrived (AGENTS.md's promotion rule).
  - **Error pages:** a cafe page whose address names no cafe is refused by `31.cafe-pages` with 404 and the page-only code `CAFE_NOT_FOUND` (the API keeps `NOT_FOUND`): "Cafe not found", no way back into a cafe that isn't there. A paused cafe's pages (403 `TENANT_SUSPENDED`) say "Ordering is paused: <name> isn't taking orders at the moment" to customers and "This cafe is paused … ask the platform team" in its admin and counter, with Try again. The page reads the code from the error's `data` (JSON text after a server render).
- **Alternatives:** the name in the runtime config (one per deployment, not per cafe); the profile inside each surface's own route (`/api/c/<slug>/public/…`, refused for a paused cafe, so its paused page couldn't name it); a logo URL typed by the owner (an outside image breaks the CSP and can change under us); letting owners change their web address (every printed QR and old link would need the platform's redirect history anyway, and the console already does it, D142).
- **Verified:** server (`tenants.service.test.ts` +5: the profile by address incl. paused, unknown and former; the name and logo saved, the new logo attached, the old one released, the change audited; another cafe's upload refused; a stale version or a change between the check and the write saves nothing; an upload cleaned up meanwhile saves nothing either; the version guard checked to fail when removed); unit (`cafe-form.test.ts` 3, `shrink-image.test.ts` moved); e2e `cafe-profile.test.ts` (5, mocked API: save with an uploaded logo and the sidebar and tab title following, a required name, a 409 with Reload, the phone's bottom bar, the sidebar entry) and `cafes.test.ts` (+3 on the real server: each cafe's profile and its menu's title, Cafe not found on a page and on an admin page, a paused cafe's pages for customers and for staff, by name); the full e2e suite (441).

### D144: Your cafes and the cafe switcher (step T2c), 2026-10-03
- **Context:** one account can own one cafe and work at another (D134), but nothing in the app showed it: each workspace knew only its own cafe, and an owner who signed in at another cafe's admin (the old `/admin` addresses lead to NUK Cafe's) was signed out with "no access" and no way on. The owner left the design to us (D142).
- **Decision:**
  - **`GET /api/me/cafes`** (a platform route: it names no cafe, behind the session gate `/api/me/**`): the signed-in account's cafes by name, each with its profile (D143) and the workspaces it may open there: `admin` and `counter` where it's an owner, `counter` where it works at an active branch (the same rules as `workspacesOf`, D124, for every tenant at once). A paused cafe is listed with its status. A temporary password is accepted (only links; each workspace asks for a new password itself, like the console's session, D142). Identity finds the tenants (`accountWorkspaces`), the tenants feature adds the profiles (`listAccountCafes`), one read per cafe (an account works in a handful; a list of ids would meet D1's parameter limit).
  - **Your cafes** (`/cafes`, browser only, the task-flow frame): a row per cafe with Admin and Counter buttons where they open, a paused cafe with "Paused by the platform team" and no buttons, an empty state for an account with no cafe, and sign-in first when signed out (`?redirect=/cafes`). Tab title "Your cafes · NUK Platform": it spans cafes.
  - **The switcher:** the admin sidebar's cafe name (D143) becomes a menu when the account owns another active cafe: the other cafes' admins and All your cafes. The counter's user menu gets **Another cafe** (to Your cafes) when the account works at another cafe's counter. With one cafe nothing changes.
  - **Every move to another cafe is a full page load** (`external` links), as D141 requires: the admin and counter sessions, query data and drafts belong to the cafe of the address. Unsaved forms are still asked about (the browser's leave warning).
  - **A refused sign-in that works elsewhere:** when the admin (or counter) sign-in is refused here (403), the app asks `GET /api/me/cafes` once; if the account opens that workspace at another active cafe, it **stays signed in** and the alert says "This account doesn't manage this cafe" with a **Your cafes** button (client code `WORKS_ELSEWHERE`). Otherwise it is signed out as before (D52).
  - The helpers both sign-ins and the cafe feature use live in the root `app/utils/account-cafes.ts` (`fetchAccountCafes`, `otherCafes`, `workspaceUrl`, `worksElsewhere`, `CAFES_PATH`); the cafe feature exports `useAccountCafes()` (query `cafe:mine`) and `<CafeSwitcher>`.
- **Alternatives:** Better Auth's `activeOrganizationId` (a cafe chosen per session: two tabs on two cafes would fight, and the path already names the cafe, D134); a cafe picker inside each workspace's sign-in (every workspace would repeat it); sending a refused sign-in straight to Your cafes (a person who typed the wrong cafe's address should see why first).
- **Verified:** server (`tenants.service.test.ts` +3: owned and staff cafes with their workspaces by name, archived branches and plain members left out, a paused cafe listed, a temporary password accepted, no session or a banned account 401; the owner filter and the active-branch filter each checked to fail when removed; `routes.test.ts` lists the route among the platform's); unit (`account-cafes.test.ts` 2); e2e `your-cafes.test.ts` (6, mocked API: the switcher's cafes and its full page load, one cafe a plain link, Your cafes with workspaces and a paused cafe, the empty state, signed out to sign-in, a refused sign-in kept signed in with Your cafes) and `cafes.test.ts` (+2 on the real server: each account's cafes and 401 without a session; Brown Bean's owner signs in at NUK Cafe's admin, goes on through Your cafes and lands in Brown Bean's admin); the full e2e suite (449).

### D145: The cafe menu in the sidebar, View menu and an item's address on the menu, 2026-10-03
- **Context:** after T2c the owner asked (2026-10-03) for switching cafes to be easier from the admin, and for a way to see the customer menu without copying its address into a new tab, especially after editing a menu item. The sidebar's cafe name was a menu only for an account with another cafe, and nothing showed it could be clicked.
- **Decision:**
  - **`<CafeSwitcher>` is always a menu** (a chevron beside the cafe's name): the other cafes the account owns (when there are any; each a full page load, D141), **View menu** (the cafe's customer menu in a new tab) and **All your cafes**. The sidebar's footer also has **View menu** on every admin page.
  - **An item's address on the menu:** `/c/<slug>?item=<id>` opens that item's detail once the menu of the branch this browser shows has loaded (in the browser only: the server renders the plain menu, so caching and hydration don't change). An item that isn't on the menu (a draft, archived, sold out of its hours) shows a toast saying so; closing the detail removes `item` from the address.
  - **View on menu** in the item editor (the slide-over's footer and the editor page's navbar) and in a published row's ⋮ menu opens that address in a new tab. For a draft or an archived item it is disabled with the reason ("Publish it to see it on the menu"); a new item has none. The tooltip says a change can take a minute to show (the menu page is cached, D122).
- **Alternatives:** a preview of drafts on the menu (it would need a signed preview mode on a public, cached page: not worth it for now); the item in a separate public page (a second rendering of the same item to keep in step with the menu).
- **Verified:** e2e `your-cafes.test.ts` (+1, the one-cafe menu with View menu and All your cafes, and the sidebar's View menu opening a new tab), `products.test.ts` (+2: a published item's link from its form and its row, a draft's disabled button with the reason and no row action), `shop-menu.test.ts` (+2 on the real server: `?item=` opens the item and closing it leaves the address, an unknown item says so), and the cafe menu's new name in `cafe-profile.test.ts` and `cafes.test.ts`; the full e2e suite (454).

### D146: New cafe for an existing owner, 2026-10-08
- **Context:** the owner asked (2026-10-03) for a clearer way to give someone a second cafe. New cafe (D142) asked for the owner's name and email, and an email that already had an account was quietly given access: the platform team had to know to type the same email, and the result didn't say what happened beyond "already had an account".
- **Decision:**
  - **New cafe → First owner** is a choice: **A new person** (name and email; a temporary password shown once) or **An existing owner** (a searchable list of everyone who owns a cafe, `<RecordSelect>`, with the cafes each one owns under the field). The form keeps both choices' fields, so switching loses nothing; only the chosen one is sent.
  - **The contract:** `createTenantSchema.owner` is a variant on `kind`: `{ kind: 'existing', userId }` or `{ kind: 'new', name, email }`. `GET /api/platform/owners` lists the owners with their cafes (`tenant: ['create']`).
  - **Owners only.** The list and `kind: 'existing'` take only accounts that own a cafe and aren't disabled; a customer, branch staff, a disabled owner or an unknown id is 422 on `owner.userId`. A new person's email that belongs to an owner is 409 `OWNER_EXISTS` on `owner.email` ("choose An existing owner"), so adding an owner is always the explicit choice. A new person's email that belongs to a customer still gets access and keeps its password, as before.
  - **A cafe's page** has **New cafe for this owner** beside each owner: New cafe opens with that owner chosen.
  - **The result** names every cafe the owner has now ("Zoe Tran now owns 3 cafes: Tea Kiosk, Old Mill and Zoe Corner."); `CreatedTenant.owner` carries them. An existing owner finds the new cafe under Your cafes and in the admin's cafe menu (D144, D145).
  - Cafes are still created only by the platform team; an owner creating their own cafe waits for self sign-up (T3).
- **Alternatives:** an owner adding a cafe from their own admin (that is self sign-up with billing, T3); searching owners on the server (the list is one row per cafe owned, loaded once: a search parameter comes when the platform has thousands of cafes); keeping the email-only form with a better result message (the platform team would still have to know the email trick).
- **Race:** the chosen owner's account changing between the check and the write (the same version guard as D142's existing account) is 409 `VERSION_CONFLICT`, nothing saved.
- **Verified:** server (`tenants.service.test.ts` +6: the owner list leaves out customers, branch staff and disabled owners; an existing owner keeps their account and the answer names both cafes; refusals for a non-owner, a disabled owner and an unknown id with nothing saved; `OWNER_EXISTS`; the account changed meanwhile; the role, disabled and `OWNER_EXISTS` checks each fail when removed); unit (`cafe-form.test.ts` +5); e2e `platform.test.ts` (+4).

### D147: The build's heap set to 4 GB in CI, 2026-10-09
- **Context:** the staging deploy after PR 95 failed twice at "Build the staging Worker" with exit code 134, before migrations or the deploy ran (staging kept the previous Worker). Locally the build passed on Node 22 and on Node 24.21 (what CI installs). Measured: the build peaks at about 5 GB across its processes and needs about 3 GB of V8 heap; with `--max-old-space-size=2048` it aborts locally with "JavaScript heap out of memory", exit 134, as in CI. GitHub's runners for private repositories have 7 GB, and Node sizes its default heap from the machine's memory, so the growing build crossed that default.
- **Decision:** `NODE_OPTIONS: --max-old-space-size=4096` on the two CI steps that build the app (Build the staging Worker, and E2E tests, whose global setup builds the same app). The limit no longer depends on the runner's memory, with headroom above the 3 GB the build needs.
- **Alternatives:** a larger runner (paid); trimming the build's memory (source maps, chunking), worth it only if 4 GB is reached; re-running (the second run failed the same way: not a flake).
- **Verified:** locally, the staging build fails at 2048 MB (exit 134, heap out of memory) and passes at 3072 MB and at the default; the PR's e2e jobs build with the new setting; the staging deploy after the merge.

### D148: Each cafe's own Bakong token, sealed in the database (T2d), 2026-10-09
- **Context:** the Bakong Open API token that lets the counter confirm KHQR payments by itself (D131) was one Worker secret, `NUXT_BAKONG_TOKEN`: NUK Cafe's. Every cafe has its own Bakong account and its own token (tokens are free, from Bakong's developer portal, 90 days), and the platform team must not hold or paste cafes' tokens. Planned in T0 (D134): encrypted in the database, the key a Worker secret.
- **Decision:**
  - **`bakong_tokens`**, one row per tenant: the token **sealed** with AES-256-GCM under `NUXT_BAKONG_ENCRYPTION_KEY` (32 random bytes, base64, a Worker secret), a fresh 12-byte IV each time, and `bakong-token:<tenantId>` as additional data, so a sealed token copied into another cafe's row doesn't open. Stored as `v1.<iv>.<ciphertext>`; `v1` names the scheme for a later key or scheme. `expires_at` is read from the token (its JWT `exp`, D132) when saved and kept in plain beside it, so Payments and the reminders never need the key. WebCrypto only, the same in the Worker and Node. Migration `0032_bakong_tokens`.
  - **Payments → Automatic check with Bakong:** the cafe's owner (`settings: ['manage']`) **adds**, **replaces** or **removes** the token (`PUT` / `DELETE /api/c/<slug>/admin/khqr/token`, at the version read: 409 otherwise). It's sent once and never answered, logged or audited again (the audit keeps only the expiry); the page shows when it expires and who saved it. A token that has already expired is refused on its field. A server without the key says "Tokens can't be saved on this server yet" (503 `BAKONG_KEY_MISSING` on a save) and the counter confirms by hand.
  - **Every use is per cafe:** the counter's queue says the check is on when the cafe has a token; the check and Test connection open that cafe's token for the request. A token this server can't open (no key, another key, another cafe's row) answers the new problem `key` ("The saved token can't be read on this server any more: Replace token"), never another cafe's token.
  - **Reminders** (D132) per cafe: each active cafe with a token gets its own 14/7/3/1/0-day reminders, in its own chats, with its own Payments button.
  - **No fallback** to the old server secret: `NUXT_BAKONG_TOKEN` is no longer read. On staging the platform team sets `NUXT_BAKONG_ENCRYPTION_KEY`, then NUK Cafe's owner pastes the token on Payments; until then NUK Cafe's cashiers confirm by hand (as when Bakong is unreachable). Deleting the old secret afterwards is in operations.md → KHQR.
- **Alternatives:** keep `NUXT_BAKONG_TOKEN` as NUK Cafe's fallback (a second path to keep in step and remove later, for a gap of minutes on staging); Workers Secrets per cafe (secrets are set by deploy tooling, not by an owner on a page); KV with its own encryption (D1 already holds the cafe's data, in one place to back up and restore); key rotation now (no need yet: changing the key only asks each cafe to paste its token again).
- **Verified:** server (`bakong-token.service.test.ts` 10: sealing round trip, another cafe's id, another key, an altered text, a short key; save, replace, stale and racing saves, two first saves, an expired token, no key and a bad key, remove; each cafe asks Bakong with its own token; a copied row, another key and no key answer `key` without asking Bakong; the cafe binding and the replace's version guard each checked to fail when removed); the reminders' text and the counter's queue updated in their tests; unit; e2e `payments.test.ts` (+3: add a token sent once and never shown, an expired one refused on its field, remove asked first, a server without the key) and the counter's automatic check on the real server with NUK Cafe's token sealed in the e2e seed; the full e2e suite (461).

### D149: The reliability pack: the screen kept on, a new version offered, the queue's last update, its tab count, browser errors reported, 2026-10-10
- **Context:** the owner asked for more of what makes the app dependable without anyone noticing (the unsaved-changes guard, tabs that stay in step). The counter and the admin stay open all day on tablets and laptops, on cafe Wi-Fi, while new builds are deployed under them; errors on customers' phones were never seen.
- **Decision:**
  - **The counter keeps the screen on** while one of its pages is open (Screen Wake Lock through VueUse's `useWakeLock`, in `layouts/counter.vue`): a tablet that dims between customers misses the chime. The browser drops the lock when the tab is hidden and it's asked for again when visible; refused (battery saver, an old browser), nothing changes.
  - **A new build is offered.** Every tab asks every 5 minutes whether a newer build was deployed (Nuxt's `builds/latest.json`, `experimental.checkOutdatedBuildInterval`; Nuxt's default is an hour). Once it is: in the admin, the counter and the platform console, `<NewVersionBanner>` says "A new version of the app is ready" with **Reload** and **Later**; the next move to another page is a full page load anyway (Nuxt's own chunk-reload plugin, after the route guards, so a dirty form is asked about first). The customer site shows nothing: a visit is short, and its next page loads the new build. The banner and the offline notice stack at the bottom of the screen (`app.vue`).
  - **The counter's queue keeps its cards while a refresh fails** and says since when: "The queue may be out of date · Last updated 10:42 AM (3 min ago)…". **Found while building this:** Nuxt's `useAsyncData` resets `data` when a refresh fails, so every Wi-Fi drop emptied the board and showed "Couldn't load the queue"; the existing "Showing it as it was" alert never appeared. `useCounterQueue` now keeps the branch's last answer while the failure is the connection's or the server's (a refusal such as access removed still shows the error). Other lists keep Nuxt's behavior (a load error with Retry); changing `useApiQuery` for every list is a separate decision.
  - **The queue's tab counts the orders to pay:** "(2) Queue · NUK Cafe Counter", as Gmail counts unread mail, so a background tab still shows them.
  - **Browser errors are reported** to `POST /api/client-errors` (global, same-origin only through the origin check, rate limited with the public API) and written to Workers Logs as "Browser error" with the page (never its query: a reset link's token lives there), the build, the stack and the browser. Vue's errors, uncaught script errors and unhandled rejections; not our API's failures (the server logged them) or cancelled requests; each message once per page load, at most 10. **Not sent to Telegram:** the text comes from the browser, anyone can send one, and a cafe owner can't act on it; it waits for an alert channel of the platform team's own.
  - **Also fixed** (found by CI on this PR): the Bakong token dialog checked its field 300 ms after typing; a token pasted and saved at once could have the server's "expired" error wiped by that late check, leaving the dialog open with no message. The field is checked on blur and change now ([forms.md → Implementation traps](reference/forms.md)).
- **Alternatives:** our own route-change reload (Nuxt already does it: two would race); a toast instead of a banner (the session boundary clears toasts on every sign-in or sign-out); Sentry or a similar service (a paid account and a third party receiving customers' page data, for a volume Workers Logs holds); keeping the last queue in `useApiQuery` for every list (a shared building block's behavior, wider than this step); the favicon with a count (the title is enough and works everywhere).
- **Verified:** unit (`client-errors.test.ts` 5: what is sent, the query dropped, API errors and cancellations skipped, once per message, the limit; the counter's title and last-update text); server (`rate-limit.test.ts`, `routes.test.ts`: the route in the global list); e2e `reliability.test.ts` (7: Reload, Later and the next page as a full load, the customer site silent, no reload without a new build, a timer's error and a rejection reported once, the server's origin and validation checks) and `counter.test.ts` (+1 on the real server: the wake lock asked for, the tab's count, the board kept with "3 min ago" while the queue's requests fail, the alert gone once they work; checked to fail before the queue fix: the board was replaced by "Couldn't load the queue"); screenshots at 1440 and 390, light and dark. Nuxt's 5-minute check itself is compiled out of a test build (`import.meta.test`): the tests call its hook, and the check is verified on staging after a deploy.

### D150: Reliability pack 2: alerts sent at once, screens that keep their data, the counter's sound and stale warning, failed jobs alerted, 2026-10-10
- **Context:** the owner asked for more of D149, and for order and payment alerts on Telegram to arrive at once ("the info is important for the counter or admin to notice"). They arrived when the once-a-minute `platform:deliver-outbox` ran: a few seconds to a minute late. Checking D149's queue fix found the same bug on the customer's order page.
- **Decision:**
  - **Order and payment alerts right after the request.** Placing an order, recording a payment and a KHQR check that records one call `sendOrderEventsNow` (`server/utils/outbox.ts`): after the answer (`waitUntil`), it delivers the due order events (`deliverOutbox(…, { kinds })`), so the Telegram message arrives within seconds. Each outbox message is claimed before it's sent, so the scheduled run can't send it twice; anything that fails is retried by that run as before. The outbox handlers moved from the task to `outboxHandlers()`, shared by both.
  - **A failed refresh keeps the data, on every screen** (`useApiQuery`). Nuxt's `useAsyncData` resets `data` on any error. On the customer's order page that showed "Couldn't load this order" on a Wi-Fi drop **and stopped its polling** (polling ran only while an order was on screen). Now the last data is put back, in the same tick, when the failure is the connection's or the server's (retryable) and the key is the same. Only Nuxt's failure path (a new error, then the reset) is undone. Any other reset (`clear()`, the session boundary's `clearNuxtData`) forgets the kept copy, so another person's data never comes back; Nuxt 4.5 also drops the error first when clearing, and the check doesn't depend on that order. The counter's own fix from D149 is removed. The order page says "Can't reach the cafe right now, trying again · Updated 3 min ago". Admin lists still show their load error with Retry (their data is kept underneath, so the list returns at the next refresh).
  - **The counter's sound.** Browsers hold Web Audio back until a tap. After any reload (a new version, D149) the chime was silent and nobody knew. One audio context (`useCounterSound`), "Sound is off until you tap the screen" with **Turn on sound**, unlocked by the first tap anywhere.
  - **The counter warns louder after 2 minutes** without an update while refreshing fails: a red alert ("The queue isn't updating: check the connection"), "⚠" in the tab's title, and a warning sound once (three low notes, unlike the chime; not when muted).
  - **No pull-to-refresh or swipe-back on the counter** (`overscroll-behavior: none` on the page while the counter layout is open).
  - **A scheduled job that fails is alerted on Telegram.** Every scheduled task runs through `runJob` (`server/utils/jobs.ts`): a failure is logged with its stack, alerted, and thrown again. An outbox message given up on after its last try is alerted too. The alert names the job and what failed in our own words, never the error's text, at most once per job and chat every 15 minutes, to the chats with **Server errors and reminders** on. Jobs belong to no cafe: like a server error outside a cafe's address (D119), they go to the default cafe's chats until the platform has its own alert channel (Q24).
- **Alternatives:** a cron every few seconds (Workers cron is per minute at best); a Durable Object or queue for alerts (more moving parts for a few seconds); keeping data per screen as in D149 (a second screen had the same bug, so it belongs in the shared building block); Nuxt's `getCachedData` (it decides whether to fetch, not what to keep on failure); sending job failures to every cafe (they can't act on them).
- **Verified:** server (`platform.service.test.ts` +1: a run limited to order events, and the request's run with the scheduled one at once, each message sent once; `notifications.delivery.test.ts` +2: job failure alerts, their chats, our words, one per job in 15 minutes even when two runs fail at once); unit (the counter's stale helpers and title); e2e `shop-tracking.test.ts` +1 (the order stays while the order's requests fail, says so, and follows the payment once they work), `counter.test.ts` (the sound notice and Turn on sound, the louder alert, "⚠" and the warning tones after 2 minutes, overscroll none), both checked to fail with the restore removed; `session.test.ts` +2 (a list kept after a failed refresh never reaches the next person, signing out in the same tab or in another tab while the page stays open; the stricter check couldn't be shown failing because Nuxt 4.5 already drops the error first, so these prove the outcome, not that line); the full e2e suite.

### D151: Drafts kept in the browser: unsaved input offered back after a crash or a lost session, 2026-10-10
- **Context:** the unsaved-changes guard (D19) only helps while the page is alive and the person answers "Discard unsaved changes?". A browser crash, a dead battery, a phone dropping a background tab, a force reload, the new-version Reload (D149) or an expired session lost what was typed without a word. The menu item editor (option sets, a price grid, add-ons with their own prices) and a branch's weekly hours take minutes to enter.
- **Decision:**
  - **A long form keeps its unsaved input in `localStorage`** through `useUnsavedChanges(state, { draft: { key, version } })` (`app/utils/form-draft.ts`, the store; `app/composables/useUnsavedChanges.ts`, the wiring): written half a second after typing settles, and at once when the tab is hidden (a phone may drop it without another event). One draft per **cafe, person, form and record** (`nuk:draft:<cafe>:<userId>:<form>:<recordId | new>`). The key is fixed once known, so a session change can't write one person's input under another's key. Opted in: the **menu item editor** (page and slide-over share one draft per item, or one for a new item; none for an archived item) and **branch settings** (hours included).
  - **Offered back, never applied on its own:** `<FormDraftAlert>` says "Unsaved changes from 10:42 AM" with **Restore** and **Discard**. Restore fills the form, still unsaved and guarded; Save goes through the version check as always (D41). A draft identical to what's saved is dropped without a word.
  - **Someone saved the record after the draft** (the version differs): the alert turns amber and says restoring puts the draft's values over theirs. The form has read the latest version, so a save after Restore overwrites their change: the person is told before choosing. Refusing the restore instead would lose the typing again, which is what this step exists to stop.
  - **When a draft goes:** a successful save (`markSaved()`), Discard in the alert, or Discard in "Discard unsaved changes?" (route change, closing a modal, voluntary sign-out). **A voluntary sign-out removes every draft on the browser** (`plugins/form-drafts.client.ts`), so nothing typed stays on a shared tablet. **A session that ends on its own** (expiry, signed out in another tab) **keeps the draft**: the session boundary discards the form (`discard('session')`) after writing what was typed, and the same person is offered it after signing in again; nobody else is, the key holds their id. Drafts older than 7 days are dropped when the app starts.
  - **Storage that fails** (private windows, a full quota, blocked site data) does nothing: every call is guarded, and the form works as before.
  - Never for secrets: password and Bakong token forms don't opt in.
- **Alternatives:** `sessionStorage` (lost with the tab: no help after a crash or a dropped tab, the main cases); saving drafts on the server (another table and route for something private to one browser, and a server-side draft would need its own conflict rules); applying the draft automatically (surprising, and wrong when the record changed); drafts on every form (a category or a table takes seconds to retype: added when a form needs it).
- **Consequences:** a person's unsaved input sits in their browser for up to 7 days unless they save, discard or sign out; on a shared device another account never sees it. Two tabs with the same form: the last one typed in wins. An image picked in a draft is already uploaded (D122); if the draft is restored after its temporary upload was purged (24 hours), the form shows no image.
- **Verified:** unit (`form-draft.test.ts` 6: keys, expiry, sign-out removal, failing storage, the time text); e2e (`form-drafts.test.ts` 9: reload and Restore then Save, Discard, changed since, another person, leaving with Discard, sign-out, a session expiring during a save, a new item, branch settings). Checked to fail with the guards removed: a session end that drops the draft (the expiry test), sign-out without clearing (the sign-out test). The full e2e suite.

### D152: The UX pack: Undo instead of asking, Ctrl/⌘+K search, a sold-out switch that moves at once, an accessibility check, 2026-10-10
- **Context:** the owner asked to improve the UI and UX only. Five things from the review: confirm dialogs on every archive (slow, and clicked by reflex after a week); no way to jump to a record without opening its list; the counter's sold-out switch waiting on the server on cafe Wi-Fi; no check for accessibility; photos that showed a blank box while loading.
- **Decision:**
  - **Undo instead of "Are you sure?" where the reverse puts everything back the same.** Archive (one or in bulk) and Unpublish of a menu item, and archiving an option set, an add-on group, an availability rule or a dining table run at once; the toast offers **Undo** for 10 seconds (hovering pauses it). The reverse is a second request, never a delay: the server is the truth at once and nothing is lost to a closed tab. `useMutation({ undo })` puts the action on the success toast, and on a batch's summary beside Retry failed; `execute(input, { undo: false, confirm })` asks instead, for a call made inside a modal or slide-over whose toast can't be reached while it's open (the option set editor, the add-on group page).
  - **An item's Undo is exact:** `POST …/items/{id}/restore` takes `undo: { status }` and puts the item back where it was (its place was kept while archived) in the status it had; back to published passes Publish's checks (a leaf category, something to sell). A plain Restore still makes a draft at the end of its category. The undo names the version the archive answered, so someone's change meanwhile refuses it ("Could not undo: … stays archived").
  - **Kept as questions:** archiving a category (a main category archives its subcategories with it, and the undo would need to know which were active), and everything that can't be put back the same or touches money or people (cancel an order, a payment, disable or reset staff, New QR, disconnect Telegram, the Bakong token, the riel rate, Sample data reset).
  - **Ctrl/⌘+K search** (and a Search button at the top of the sidebar): Nuxt UI's `UDashboardSearch`. Typing two characters or more asks every source at once: menu items through the server's own search (archived too, opening the editor), categories, option sets and add-on groups (by their names and their values' or add-ons'); the pages and a few actions (New menu item, View menu, Keyboard shortcuts) are always there. Each feature exports its source from its `index.ts` and `utils/admin-search.ts` lists them, like the sidebar. A source that fails says so in its group; the others still answer.
  - **The counter's sold-out switch moves at once:** it shows the state asked for until the server answers, then takes the server's list from the answer (no flicker before the refresh); a refusal puts it back and the toast says why.
  - **An accessibility check:** axe-core's WCAG 2.1 A and AA rules in e2e (`accessibility.test.ts`) on Dashboard, Menu items, Categories, Staff, Options, Add-ons, the customer menu and the counter's queue with an order on it, at 1440 and 390 px. What it found, fixed: the record dropdowns (`RecordSelect`) were buttons named "Category" showing "All categories", now comboboxes like `USelect` (the text on them is their value); the staff and category rows and the counter's order cards were one button named only by the name but holding the description too, now the name (or "Open order" + the number) is the button, stretched over the row so the target is as large as before; the account buttons showing initials ("SK") now have names that start with them.
  - **Photos:** every photo already sat in a fixed box (nothing moves while one loads); the item's detail and the order's lines now show the same gray placeholder as the menu cards until the photo arrives.
- **Alternatives:** Undo by delaying the request (Gmail's) — loses the action when the tab closes within the window and other screens see the old state meanwhile; Undo for categories with the list of subcategories to restore (more API for a rare action); our own command palette (Nuxt UI's has the modal, keyboard and grouping); turning off axe's label-in-name rule (it found real problems: a voice-control user saying "click All categories" or "click SK" reached nothing).
- **Consequences:** an archive done by mistake is caught only if someone looks at the toast; afterwards Restore still works from the Archived tab (as a draft, the normal restore). Tests find the record dropdowns by `combobox`, not `button`.
- **Verified:** server (`items.service.test.ts` +2: an undo back in place and status, the version and Publish's checks; checked to fail with the guard removed); unit (`mutation.test.ts` +3); e2e: `list-bulk.test.ts` +3 (Undo of one, of a batch, refused), the libraries' and tables' Undo, `admin-search.test.ts` 4, `counter.test.ts` +1 (the switch moves before the answer and comes back on a refusal; checked to fail without the change), `accessibility.test.ts` 16; the full e2e suite.

### D153: Someone else saved first: Reload keeps your input on top, part by part, 2026-10-10
- **Context:** the owner asked for more UX work. The menu item editor, the longest form, answered a stale save (409, D41) with "Close it and open it again to see their changes (your input will be lost)". The add-on group page's Reload threw away the settings typed. Categories, Branch settings and Cafe profile already kept the input on Reload (D72), but by putting all of it over the other person's version, so their change to a field the person never touched was overwritten on the next Save.
- **Decision:**
  - **Reload takes their version and keeps this person's changes on top, part by part:** `mergeFormChanges({ base, mine, theirs, parts })` (`app/utils/form-value.ts`) compares each part of the form with what it opened with (`base`): a part this person changed keeps their input, any other part takes the newer version. A part is the fields that change as one: the menu item's `ITEM_FORM_PARTS` (Name, Description, Category, Image with its preview, **Prices** = the option sets with their grid, since one without the other would describe versions that don't exist, Add-ons, Availability); the add-on group's Name and Selection rule.
  - **Where both changed a part differently, this person's input wins and the editor says so:** "You both changed the prices: yours are kept. Check them, then save." (amber); otherwise "Their changes are in the form, with yours kept. Save to keep both." (blue). Nothing is saved by Reload: the form stays unsaved, measured against their version (`markClean(saved)`), and the next Save names their version.
