  - **Someone archived the item meanwhile:** Reload says so and keeps the input in the form and in this browser (D151); it's offered back once the item is restored and opened again. A failed Reload keeps the conflict, to try again.
  - **Not per field inside a part:** no merge UI choosing values one by one (a price grid has dozens of cells; the owner's cafes have a few people editing), and no change to the other forms: Categories, Branch settings and Cafe profile keep D72's "all of mine on top" until a part-wise merge is asked for there.
- **Alternatives:** keep "close and reopen" (loses minutes of typing); all of mine on top as D72 (silently undoes the other person's change to fields this person never touched); a side-by-side merge dialog (heavy for a rare event); locking a record while someone edits it (stale locks when a tab dies, and the server would need presence).
- **Consequences:** a part both changed takes this person's whole part, including the other person's edits inside it (their price for a version this person didn't touch, when this person changed another price): the amber message names the part to check. `useUnsavedChanges().markClean` takes an optional baseline.
- **Verified:** unit (`form-merge.test.ts` 6, `item-form.test.ts` +2); e2e `products.test.ts` +3 (the merge and Save naming their version, checked to fail when Reload takes theirs; nothing both changed; archived meanwhile), `modifier-groups.test.ts` +1 and one rewritten (Reload kept the old reset); the full e2e suite.

### D154: Duplicate a menu item, 2026-10-10
- **Context:** the owner's UX review: adding "Iced Latte" after "Latte" meant choosing the option sets, typing every version's price, the add-ons and their prices and the availability again.
- **Decision:** a menu item's row menu (not an archived one) has **Duplicate**: **New menu item** opens filled from it (`toItemCopyForm`): "Latte (copy)" (cut to fit the name's limit), the description, category, option sets with the price grid and which versions are on, add-on groups with this item's own rules and prices, availability rules. Nothing is sent until **Create** (or Create and publish): it's a new draft through the normal create, with its own checks. Phones open `/products/new?copy=<id>`, wider screens the slide-over. The copy counts as unsaved from the start (closing asks), and keeps its own draft in the browser (`copy-<id>`, D151), never the "New menu item" one.
  - **Not copied:** the photo, since an upload belongs to one record (`media_assets` is attached to one record and released with it); and what a new item can't take: archived option sets (the grid rebuilt from the others; their combinations' prices kept), archived add-on groups and add-ons, archived availability rules.
- **Alternatives:** a server route that copies the item (and the photo in R2) in one request: one more write path with its own guards and an R2 copy, for something the create already does; copying the photo by uploading it again from the browser (a download and an upload per duplicate). Both possible later if the owner misses the photo.
- **Consequences:** a duplicated item needs its photo added. The source is read when Duplicate is pressed; a change to it afterwards isn't followed (it's a copy).
- **Verified:** unit (`item-form.test.ts` +3: everything but the photo, archived parts left out, the name's limit); e2e `products.test.ts` +3 (the slide-over and the create body, asks before closing, the phone route).

### D155: The admin's home page: today, "Set up your cafe", shortcuts, 2026-10-10
- **Context:** `/admin`, the first page every admin sees, said only "Welcome back, …". A new cafe had no guide to what to do first; a running one had to open Reports for today's numbers. The owner chose to skip the mockup round and build it from the UI standard (as for the platform console, D142).
- **Decision:**
  - **Today, per branch:** the Reports summary for the branch's business date (`GET /admin/reports/summary`, the same numbers and definitions as Summary, D110): paid sales with the change against yesterday, orders placed, waiting for payment, preparing or ready; links to that Summary and to the branch's counter (a new tab). Several branches: one block each, by name. No new server route.
  - **Set up your cafe:** each feature with something a new cafe has to do exports one `AdminSetupStep` from its `index.ts` (`{ label, description, page, optional, check }`), and `utils/admin-setup.ts` lists them in order, like the sidebar and the search (D152). The checks read the existing admin routes: opening hours on a branch, a published menu item, the riel rate; optional: KHQR at the counter, staff besides the owner, the cafe's logo, Telegram (left out where the bot isn't set up on the server). Steps to do come first (required, then optional), then the done ones; a check that fails says so and doesn't count as done. While a required step is left, the checklist comes **before** today's numbers and can't be hidden; once only optional ones are left it moves below and offers **Hide** (kept in this browser per cafe and person: a preference, not data). Everything done: it's gone.
  - **Shortcuts:** New menu item, Sold out (the first branch's counter page, a new tab), Reports, View menu.
- **Alternatives:** one `GET /admin/setup` on the server (one request instead of seven, but a server feature reaching into six others for a page that is read now and then); a setup wizard that a new cafe must finish (blocks a cafe that wants to look around first); hiding the checklist for good with a server flag (nothing else needs it).
- **Consequences:** the dashboard makes about nine requests when it opens (two for today, seven checks), each small and already authorized; it re-reads them each time it opens, so a step done elsewhere is checked off on return. `changeText` ("+12%") moved from Reports to `utils/text.ts` for both. The e2e harness answers the dashboard's requests by default (an empty cafe).
- **Follow-up (2026-10-19, owner: UI/UX refresh from a mockup, shape only):** the page is named **Overview** (sidebar, tab title, assistant help); the route, the `dashboard` feature folder, `DashboardPage` and the query keys keep their names. Shape: a greeting with a one-line subtitle; **View menu** in the navbar; per-branch Today with outline **Summary** / **Open counter** buttons; `StatCard`s with icons; the checklist as a card with an "Essentials complete" badge, steps still to do first (**Open**, grouped "To do first" while a required step is left, else "Recommended"), then "Completed" rows each with **Manage** (the step's page); **Hide checklist** replaces **Hide**; Shortcuts as four `UPageCard` links with a description. Second pass (same day, owner): the panel body is `bg-elevated` (the middle gray: `bg-muted` was too light, `bg-accented` too heavy; `bg-muted` in dark mode, subtitle `text-toned` for contrast) and Today (one card per branch) and Shortcuts sit in white `UCard`s like the checklist, so the cards read against the ground; the owner-directed exception to ui.md "page is `bg-default`" and "no card inside a card" (the stat cards and shortcut tiles are soft tiles in a card), for this page only. Behaviour, copy "Set up your cafe" and the Today region are unchanged. Not built from the mockup: the Today date dropdown (the page reads today only).
- **Verified:** unit (`dashboard/tests/setup.test.ts` 3); e2e `dashboard.test.ts` 6 (a new cafe's order and links, today's numbers and links, Hide kept after a reload, a failed check, several branches, a phone), `accessibility.test.ts` (the dashboard at 1440 and 390 with the checklist); screenshots 1440/390; the full e2e suite.

### D156: Customers order without an account (guests), and without verifying their email, 2026-10-10
- **Context:** the owner's UX review (2026-10-10). A first-time customer had to create an account, open the verification email and come back before Place order worked: at a table, most give up and queue at the counter. The email can't reach a real customer yet (Resend's test sender, Q4). Accounts were required at launch (D45, "no guest ordering") mainly for points and vouchers, which the owner deferred (D115). The owner chose guest ordering over one-tap sign-in or keeping it as it was.
- **Decision:**
  - **Guests, through Better Auth's anonymous plugin** (library first): Place order without an account calls `/api/auth/sign-in/anonymous`, which makes a `user` with `is_anonymous` (migration `0033_guests`), a placeholder email, no password, and a normal session. Orders keep their owner, so every "only your own orders" rule, the unpaid limit (2 per person and cafe) and idempotency work unchanged.
  - **A guest gives a name** for the counter (`guestName` on `POST /shop/orders`, required from a guest, 40 characters), stored on the guest's user in the order's batch by a statement that only renames `is_anonymous` users (an account's name can't be changed this way). The next order fills it in.
  - **A guest is never more than a customer:** `authorizeSignedIn` returns `customer` (with `guest: true`) before reading memberships; no customer profile or member code is made.
  - **Signing in or up on the same browser moves the guest's orders to the account** (`onLinkAccount` → `moveGuestOrders`, every cafe); the guest stays, empty (`disableDeleteAnonymousUser`: orders reference it with `restrict`).
  - **A verified email no longer gates ordering** for accounts either (replacing D45/D51's rule): customers pay at the counter before anything is made, so an order proves nothing an email would. The banner now asks to verify "so you can always get back into your account".
  - **Abuse:** a fake order costs the cafe nothing (paid first, cancelled unpaid after 30 minutes). Limits per client address: a guest's sign-in 30 per 10 minutes (Better Auth), placing orders 20 a minute (a Workers binding, `POST` only, so order pages polling don't count). Generous because the cafe's Wi-Fi is one address for many people.
  - **The app:** Review order asks "Your name" (with "Have an account? Sign in") instead of a sign-in dialog; the verify dialog is gone. A guest is signed out in the header (Sign in, Create account) with **Your orders**; Your orders says they're kept on this device, with Create account; the menu's order bar shows for guests too.
- **Alternatives:** keep accounts with one-tap sign-in (Google/Telegram) and no verification (still a step before the first order, and needs those providers set up); guest orders without any session, tracked by a secret link only (every ownership rule would need a second path); our own guest table (Better Auth already does it, with linking).
- **Consequences:** guest users accumulate (one per browser that ordered; none for visitors who only browse, since the guest is made at Place order). A guest who clears the browser or switches phone can't see their orders (the counter still has them). An account that orders as a guest first and signs in later gets those orders added, even past the unpaid limit (the limit applies when placing). Moving orders is the one query across cafes (a person, not a cafe, owns the guest), by design.
- **Verified:** server (`orders.guests.test.ts` 8 against Better Auth's real anonymous sign-in and sign-up linking, the rename guard and the guest-is-never-a-member guard each checked to fail when removed; identity tests updated; rate limits +1); e2e `shop-checkout.test.ts` (a guest orders with a note, the name asked once, an account signs in from Review order, an unverified account orders, a guest who signs in finds their order, the phone layout), `shop-orders-api.test.ts` (a guest over HTTP: name required, `/shop/me`, someone else's 404, never admin); the full e2e suite.

### D157: "Your order is ready" on Telegram, for customers, 2026-10-10
- **Context:** agreed with the owner (2026-10-02, then 2026-10-10 after guest ordering): a customer who walked away from the counter, or sits at a table, has to keep the order page open to see "Ready". The cafe's bot already sends the staff's alerts (D112, D113). Customers have no app and many have no account (D156).
- **Decision:**
  - **Per order, a one-time link to the cafe's bot:** while an order waits for payment or is being prepared, its page offers **Message me on Telegram**. `POST /shop/orders/{id}/telegram` (the customer's own order only) makes `t.me/<bot>?start=o_<code>` (15 minutes; only the hash is stored, in `order_ready_alerts`, migration `0034_order_ready_alerts`, one row per order). **Open Telegram** is a real link (`target="_blank"`), so a phone opens the Telegram app; two taps on purpose, since a browser blocks a window opened after waiting for the server. Asking again makes a new code and the old one stops working.
  - **Start in the bot's private chat ties that chat to the order**, once: a guarded batch clears the code as it stores the chat (two chats with the same link at once: one wins). The bot answers "Done. NUK Cafe will message you here when order 042 is ready." A group, an expired or replaced link get a short answer. The `o_` prefix tells the webhook it's a customer's code; one not found falls through to the staff's links (a staff code can start the same way by chance).
  - **Mark ready writes `orders.ready`** in the order's batch (the outbox, like `orders.placed`/`orders.paid`), sent right after the counter's answer (`sendOrderEventsNow`, D150) and retried by the scheduled run: "✅ Your order 042 is ready · Collect it at the counter · NUK Cafe, Riverside" with **View order**. One message per order; a Telegram refusal other than "blocked" is retried by the outbox (at least once, so a lost answer can repeat it, like D113); a customer who blocked the bot is given up on. An order completed or cancelled before the event ran gets nothing. A chat tied after the order was marked ready gets "is ready now" at once.
  - **Nothing personal kept longer than needed:** no account is linked to Telegram; the chat id is cleared once the message is sent or given up; rows are deleted after 2 days (`notifications:purge-deliveries`, daily).
  - **The page** reads `GET /shop/orders/{id}/telegram` (`available` false without a bot: nothing shows) and, while a link waits, every 3 seconds until Start was tapped: "We'll message you on Telegram when it's ready."; afterwards "Sent to your Telegram.".
- **Alternatives:** link a customer's Telegram to their account once and message every order (more convenient for regulars, but guests have no account, and it keeps a personal chat id indefinitely); Telegram Login on the site (a bot domain to set up, and still an account step); web push notifications (no iOS support without installing the site as an app); SMS (costs per message and a provider).
- **Consequences:** a customer taps twice per order. The bot that sends the staff's alerts is the one customers talk to: its name is the cafe platform's (`NUXT_TELEGRAM_BOT_USERNAME`), and its messages name the cafe. A customer can still mute or block it; nothing else is sent to them.
- **Verified:** server (`notifications.customers.test.ts` 10: the link, refused once ready, single use and the race (checked to fail without the code guard), expired/replaced/group, the message with its button, sent once, nothing when nobody asked or the order moved on, blocked vs retried, tied after ready, another cafe, the purge); e2e `shop-tracking.test.ts` +2 (no bot: nothing shown and the link refused, on the real server; with a bot: the browser's two requests answered by the test, Open Telegram's link, the page following Start).

### D158: Menu photos: a photo editor, a square and a 4:3 file, blurred placeholders, 2026-10-10
- **Context:** the owner wanted a modern photo upload (a modal with cropping, previews, better loading). Before this, a picked photo was shrunk and uploaded as is, and every view cropped it its own way with `object-cover`: the item page showed it in a box of about 2:1, cutting the top and bottom off drinks. Owners couldn't see how the customer would see it. A review of a GPT-written plan (two separately adjusted crops, Cloudflare transformations, Nuxt Image) and of what popular apps do led to the design below; the owner skipped the mockup round.
- **Decision:**
  - **One photo, two views, made in the browser.** `PhotoEditorModal` (products feature): pick (choose, drop or paste; **Take photo** on phones), click the **middle of the dish** (a focus point, like Sanity's hotspot), and see the **Menu card** (square) and **Item page** (4:3) as customers will, side by side. Both views are suggested from the focus point: the card fills its square around it; the item page keeps the **whole photo**, fitted, with a fill colour taken from the photo's edges, unless filling 4:3 cuts at most 12% of it. **Adjust** on a view moves (drag, arrow keys) and zooms (slider, + and −, the wheel) that view only; the card can't zoom out past filling its square. **Rotate** turns a sideways photo. No brightness, filters or AI.
  - **What is stored** (migration `0035_menu_photos`, `media_assets`): the **source** (the photo the right way up, at most 2400 px, its metadata gone by redrawing it) under `t/<tenant>/source/`, which `/media/…` never serves, with `edit` (the focus and both views, to edit again exactly) and a **placeholder** (16 × 12, a data URL of a few hundred bytes); and three public files pointing at it: `card` 480 × 480, `detail` 1200 × 900, `detailSmall` 600 × 450, WebP (JPEG where the browser can't write WebP). One upload, one batch; the source is what a menu item attaches (`imageId`), so the existing attach, release and 24-hour purge rules hold for the photo and its files together. The server checks every size from the image headers (no decoding: a Worker's CPU), and a file is never attached on its own.
  - **Showing it:** `<MenuItemImage usage="card" | "detail">` everywhere a menu photo appears (menu card, item page, order lines, checkout, the admin grid and form): the blurred placeholder at once, the photo fading in, lazy below the fold, the item page eager with `srcset` (600/1200 w). The item page's photo box is **4:3** (it was ~2:1). The API adds `image: MenuImage` beside `imageUrl` (kept, the card file) on the public menu and the admin list; the admin item's `image` gains `display`. Photos from before keep working: one file for every view, no placeholder, and **Edit photo** opens them in the editor.
  - **Cache:** every edit makes new files at new keys, so the year-long `immutable` cache stays right.
- **Alternatives:** two crops adjusted separately (the first plan: twice the work per item, and every future shape would need a third); Cloudflare Image Transformations or the Images binding (needs a zone or a paid add-on, staging has neither, and the Free plan's CPU limit); Nuxt Image (its providers need the same); resizing in the Worker (10 ms CPU); keeping the raw upload as the source (location metadata, 3–10 MB); Cropper.js (a fixed frame with the photo moving under it is ~150 lines of tested math here, and the previews and the files use the same function, `placePhoto`).
- **Consequences:** a photo is four objects (the source ~0.5–1.5 MB, the files ~200 KB together). Editing again uploads the source again (a new photo; the old one is purged 24 hours after the item stops using it). A very large photo (48 MP) is decoded at full size in the browser before being made smaller: a phone with little memory may fail on it, said in plain words. The editor's `uploading` state left the item form (the editor uploads while it covers the form); back since D159, which moved the upload into the form.
- **Verified:** server (`media.photos.test.ts` 11: header sizes for PNG/JPEG/WebP ×3, stored privately with its files, wrong sizes refused with nothing stored, a failed object write leaves nothing, attach the photo and never a file, purge and reset take the files, edit data, a photo from before, another cafe's 404; menu and catalog tests updated); unit (`photo.test.ts` 9: placement, suggestions, moves, zoom limits, the fill colour); e2e `products.test.ts` (the form's photo, refusals, the files at their sizes from a clicked focus point, Adjust by keyboard, Rotate, the small-photo warning, Edit photo opens the saved edit, a failed upload keeps the old photo) and `photos.test.ts` on the real server (sizes checked, the source private, the item's card on the menu, the item page 4:3 with both sizes); screenshots 1440 and 390.

### D159: A menu photo uploads in the form, not in the editor, 2026-10-11
- **Context:** the owner found two problems with D158's photo editor on staging. (1) **Add photo** or **Edit photo** a second time, after closing the editor, showed "Unexpected error": the field opened one `useOverlay` instance made once with `destroyOnClose`, and a destroyed instance can't be opened again. (2) **Use photo** uploaded while the editor stayed open; closing it then hid the upload (nothing on the preview said a photo was on its way) and **Edit photo** couldn't show the photo until it had finished.
- **Decision:**
  - **A new editor instance on every open** (`overlay.create(…)` inside the click handler).
  - **The editor only prepares the photo** (`preparePhoto` in `photo-canvas.ts`: the source, the three files, the edit and the placeholder as one form body, and a local URL of the card). **Use photo** closes it at once, and `MenuPhotoField` uploads: the new photo on the preview, dimmed with a spinner, "Uploading the photo… You can keep editing." The form's Save waits for it ("Waiting for the photo upload…", the `uploading` model back on the form).
  - **Edit photo while it uploads** opens the same photo from memory, as it was left (the editor's `photo` prop; no request). Using it again replaces the upload: the earlier answer is ignored (each upload is numbered), and its files are purged after 24 hours like any unused upload.
  - **A failed upload** says so on the preview with **Try again** (the same files) or **Cancel** (the photo the item had, or none). Save is allowed meanwhile and keeps the old photo.
- **Alternatives:** keep uploading in the editor and stop it from closing until done (the owner's report is that people close it; a modal that can't be closed while a network call runs is worse); a background upload queue shared by every form (one field uses it today).
- **Consequences:** the prepared files stay in the browser's memory until the upload ends or is cancelled (their local URL is released then, or when the form closes). An upload still running when the form closes finishes but is never attached: purged after 24 hours.
- **Verified:** e2e `products.test.ts`: opened and closed three times without an error (failed before the fix: the second open never showed the editor); Use photo closes the editor, the preview shows the local photo with the spinner, Save waits, Edit photo reopens it with no request, a 503 shows Try again and Try again uploads it; a failed upload's Cancel keeps the item's photo. `photos.test.ts` on the real server unchanged.

### D160: Photos: unsaved while uploading, big photos opened smaller, and the items without one, 2026-10-11
- **Context:** after D159 the owner asked what would improve photos further. Two gaps were found in the code: closing the item form (or leaving the page) while a photo uploaded, or after it failed, didn't ask, because the photo isn't in the form's values until the upload answers, so the photo was lost without a word; and the editor decoded a photo at full size before making it smaller (a 48 MP photo takes about 200 MB), which can fail on a phone with little memory. And nothing showed an owner which items had no photo, although customers choose items with one more often.
- **Decision:**
  - **`useUnsavedChanges({ pending })`**: unsaved while `pending` is true, whatever the values. The item form passes the photo field's `pending` (uploading or failed), so closing or leaving asks. **Save waits** for a failed photo too ("The photo didn't upload: Try again, or Cancel it, before saving."), changing D159, where Save was allowed and then leaving asked about a photo nobody could see.
  - **Big photos opened smaller:** above 16 MP the editor reads the photo's upright size from an `Image` (no pixels decoded) and asks `createImageBitmap` for 2400 px wide while decoding (`resizeQuality: 'high'`). Only the width is given, so the shape is kept in any browser; Chromium resizes upright (checked with an EXIF-rotated JPEG: a 400 × 200 photo turned upright came out 50 × 100, not stretched). A browser that refuses the option decodes it whole, as before.
  - **The items without a photo:** `GET /admin/menu/items?photo=missing`; Menu items says under the tabs how many the filters show without one ("3 menu items have no photo"), with **Show them** (`?photo=missing`, a **No photo** chip to remove it); with that filter on, the slide-over's Save is **Save and next** and opens the next item on the page (the last one says Save). A new slide-over each time (`destroyOnClose`): a reused one kept the item it was set up with when the next opened while it was still closing. The home page's **Add a photo to each published item** (optional; done when every published item has one) opens `?status=active&photo=missing`.
- **Alternatives:** a camera badge on every card without a photo (the card's placeholder already shows it); a separate "Photos" page to go through items (the list with a filter does it with what exists); Save and next on phones too (the page editor returns to the list; the filter is kept, so the next one is one tap away); brightness and filters in the editor (left until the owners' real photos show a need).
- **Consequences:** Menu items makes one more request (the count) per filter change. A photo still uploading when the form closes, if the person chooses Discard, is purged after 24 hours as before.
- **Verified:** server (`items.service.test.ts` +1: `photo=missing` with other filters); e2e `products.test.ts` (closing during an upload asks, checked to fail without `pending`; a failed upload holds Save, Cancel frees it; a 24 MP photo opened at 2400 px wide, its source 2400 × 1600; the hint, Show them, the chip; Save and next to the last item) and `dashboard.test.ts` (the step and its link).

### D161: The photo editor frames each view like a crop; Rotate turns at once, 2026-10-11
- **Context:** on staging the owner found the "middle of the dish" point confusing: a solid dot on the photo, with nothing saying what it does or that the previews follow it, and a step people don't know from other apps. **Rotate** also felt stuck: each click redrew the 2400 px source and encoded it again as a JPEG for the screen before anything moved.
- **Decision:**
  - **A crop frame, as in the apps people know** (Instagram, a profile photo, Shopify): the tabs **Menu card** (square) and **Item page** (4:3) over one frame; the photo dragged inside it and zoomed with the slider, the wheel, arrow keys and + and −; the part left out shown dimmed around the frame, a thirds grid while dragging; **Reset** (only once the view was framed by hand). Clicking a preview frames its view. No focus-point step and no Adjust/Done mode.
  - **The item page follows the menu card** until it is framed by hand: moving the card sets the edit's `focus` to the card's middle (`withFocus`), so the saved `PhotoEdit` and the server are unchanged. Reset on the card goes back to the photo's middle.
  - **Rotate** turns the frame on screen at once (200 ms, a CSS transform; none with reduced motion), redraws meanwhile and swaps in the turned photo once decoded; the buttons wait for the turn. The editor's display copy is at most 1200 px (`sourceUrl`), a quarter of the pixels to encode; the files are still made from the source.
- **Alternatives:** keep the point with a label and an animation linking it to the previews (still a concept to learn, and a second step before framing); both frames drawn over the photo at once (two overlapping rectangles); Cropper.js (the frame and the files already share `placePhoto`).
- **Consequences:** a photo framed by hand on the card no longer moves when the item page is framed; editing a photo made before (with its point) opens with its views as saved.
- **Verified:** e2e `products.test.ts` (the files as framed, the item page following the card, a preview and the tab choosing the view, framing the item page stops it following, Reset, rotate by its frame's size, the saved zoom on Edit photo; 60 passed); screenshots 1300 and 390.

### D162: The photo editor's layout, from the owner's mockup, 2026-10-11
- **Context:** the owner sent a mockup of the photo editor with each part in its own place (the frame's column and the previews' column, one toolbar, previews as cards) and asked for the previews' header to be a grey strip like the dashboard's cards, and for the previews to be visible on phones without scrolling (they were under the whole frame).
- **Decision:**
  - **Two named columns** from `lg`: **Adjust photo** (the tabs **Menu card | Item page** beside the title: tabs, not the mockup's dropdown, so both choices show and switch in one click) and **How customers see it** ("The item page follows the menu card until you frame it."), with a line between them.
  - **One toolbar under the frame** in groups: **− slider +**, **rotate left / right**, **Reset** (an icon on phones); the frame in its own bordered panel.
  - **Each preview a `UCard`** with the app's grey header strip: the name and shape ("Menu card · Square", "Item page · 4:3"), **Editing** on the view being framed and **Edit crop** on the other; a caption that says what it does: "On the menu, in checkout and orders", or the item page's "The whole photo" / "Cropped to fill" (from where the photo sits).
  - **On phones:** the previews side by side under the toolbar, the frame smaller (30 % of the screen's height) and the captions shortened, so the whole editor shows without scrolling at 390 × 844; the title "Adjust photo" kept for screen readers only.
  - **Choose another photo** an outlined button ("Another photo" on phones). Names stay the app's own ("Item page", not "Product detail"); colours and type from Nuxt UI (D74).
- **Verified:** e2e (`products.test.ts` 60 with the preview test on the Editing badge and the tabs; `accessibility.test.ts`); screenshots 1300 × 900 and 390 × 844.

### D163: Promotions: a discount on the menu, the checkout and the order, with an uploaded poster, 2026-10-11
- **Context:** the owner asked for a Marketing area: run promotions and discounts in the app and post them to social media in one click. Posters are designed elsewhere (by the owner or with AI) and uploaded finished; the channels for now are a Telegram channel and a Facebook Page. The owner skipped the mockup round and delegated the design and the rules. Plan: [plans/marketing.md](plans/marketing.md).
- **Decision:**
  - **Two steps:** M1 promotions (this), M2 posting to channels (planned; Facebook needs Meta's App Review, Business Verification and a domain before other cafes can use it, Q4).
  - **A promotion** is a percent (1–100) or an amount off each item, on chosen items and/or categories (a main category covers its sub-categories, and items added later) or the whole menu, for calendar days on each branch's clock (or with no end), at every branch or chosen ones. **Add-ons keep their price; two promotions never add up: each version takes the bigger discount** (a tie: the older one). A percent rounds to the nearest cent, half up (`discountOff`, shared by the server and the editor's preview).
  - **Prices change in one place:** `getPublicMenu` applies the promotions running at the branch today (`applyPromotions`, pure); the quote and placing an order read that menu, so all three agree and a promotion starting or ending between them is caught by PRICES_CHANGED (D99). Each version carries `priceMinor` (paid), `regularPriceMinor` and `promotion`; the menu answers the live `promotions` for Offers.
  - **Orders keep a snapshot:** each line's price before the promotion and the promotion's id, name and badge (migration `0036_promotions`); a promotion's results count the lines of orders paid and not cancelled.
  - **States:** draft, published (scheduled / live / ended by date), ended by hand. A published promotion is never deleted (orders name it), an ended one never changes (**Run again** copies it); drafts are deleted. Publishing a promotion whose last day has passed is refused (`PROMOTION_DATES_PASSED`). Every write names the version read (409), in one batch with the links, the poster's attach / release and the audit row.
  - **Server code in the menu feature** (`promotions.*`): promotions change menu prices and link to menu items and categories by composite foreign keys inside the tenant. The results are an orders read joined in the route.
  - **Screens** (designed here, no mockup round): Marketing → **Promotions** (cards with poster, badge, coverage, dates and "Ends in 5 days"; phase tabs, running first); the **editor** (Details, Discount, What's included with a menu checklist, When with quick lengths, Branches, Poster; How customers see it beside it; a note when another promotion covers the same items); the **promotion's page** (results, details, poster with Download). Customers: a red badge on the card's photo and the sale price with the old one struck through, **Offers** at the top of the menu, "You save" in the order panel, the badge per line at checkout and on the order. Staff see the promotion under each line at the counter and in Order history.
- **Alternatives:** stacking discounts (surprising totals); promotions as their own server feature (it would reach into menu tables and the menu would import it back); a promotion stored as a price override per version (breaks when versions change); times of day now (left for later, through the availability rules); a poster maker in the app (the owner's tools do it better).
- **Consequences:** the public menu reads promotions on every request (one indexed query and two small ones while any runs); the menu page's 60 s cache (D122) can show a promotion's start or end up to a minute late, never at checkout. `CheckoutQuote` gains `savingsMinor`; `QuoteLine`, `OrderLine` and report lines gain `regularUnitPriceMinor` and `promotion`.
- **Verified:** server `promotions.rules.test.ts` (rounding, the bigger discount, ties, categories and sub-categories) and `promotions.service.test.ts` 15 (menu prices at once, first and last day on the branch's clock, chosen branches, archived and other cafes' targets refused, two cafes isolated, a race at one version checked to fail without its guard, ending, deleting drafts only, the poster attached and released, the quote and the order's snapshot and results, D1's 100 parameters at the most targets, the sample-data reset); unit `promotion-form.test.ts`; e2e `promotions.test.ts` 7 (mocked API), `shop-promotions.test.ts` 3 (the real server: Offers, the badge and struck price, the item, "You save", Review order), `accessibility.test.ts` +6 (which also found `StatCard` invalid inside a `<dl>`: fixed); the full e2e suite, 549.
- **Follow-up, 2026-10-19 (the "Choose what's included" picker):** a category box now shows ticked when every item under it is ticked one by one, and dashed when some are, but **those items stay items**: they are never saved as the category. A ticked category also covers items added to it later, so turning "every item now" into the category would widen a discount nobody chose. A box ticked by its items alone says "Every item now, not ones added later"; ticking the category itself is still how to include new items. Unticking an item inside a ticked category turns the rest into single items (it only narrows). Test: `promotions.test.ts` "creates a promotion" (Latte, all Coffee has, is sent as `itemIds`; it fails when items are merged into their category).

### D164: Promotions posted to the cafe's Telegram channel, with orders counted per channel, 2026-10-11
- **Context:** after M1 (D163) the owner asked for the next step of Marketing: posting a promotion to the cafe's social channels in one click. Telegram channels first: they need no approval from anyone. Facebook Pages (M2b) need Meta's App Review, Business Verification and a domain (QM1, Q4). The owner delegated the design. Plan: [plans/marketing.md](plans/marketing.md).
- **Decision:**
  - **Connecting a channel by a code posted in it.** Telegram's "add the bot to a channel" link (`?startchannel&admin=post_messages+delete_messages`) carries nothing back, so Channels shows a one-time `/connect CODE` (10 characters of base32, 15 minutes, only its hash stored). The admin adds the bot as an admin and posts the code; the webhook's `channel_post` checks the bot may post there (`getChatMember`), moves the code to **confirm** with the channel's title, @username and subscribers, and deletes the post; the admin confirms in the dialog (the same pattern as a staff group, D112). A code is used once (a guarded status change); only the admin who made it sees or confirms it.
  - **The webhook asks for channel posts by itself:** making a code reads `getWebhookInfo` and, when `allowed_updates` lacks `channel_post`, sets the webhook again with the same address and secret plus `channel_post`. Staging and production need no hand step; operations.md lists it too.
  - **Channels per cafe** (`marketing_channels`, unique per tenant and Telegram chat, so one channel can serve two cafes). The bot removed or demoted (`my_chat_member`) marks every cafe's row **removed**; Disconnect makes the bot leave only when no cafe still uses the channel.
  - **Posts are saved before they're sent** (`marketing_posts`: the caption, a message snapshot with the poster's key and the **Order now** button's link, when, tries). **Post now** sends in the same request and answers how each went; **Later** (up to 30 days) is sent by `marketing:deliver-posts` every minute. Each run **claims** a post first (pending and due, in one update), so two runs never send it at once. A post whose promotion ended is cancelled, a removed channel fails it; Telegram's 429 waits as long as asked without counting a try, a 400 fails at once, a 403 marks the channel removed; anything else retries (`retryDelayMs`) up to 5 tries. **Try again** starts a failed post over; **Cancel** only before its first try. Creating posts takes an `Idempotency-Key`. At least once: a lost answer after Telegram accepted a post can repeat it.
  - **The button's link** is the cafe's menu with `?promo=<id>&src=telegram`: the menu opens that offer and remembers the channel in the browser for 24 hours (per cafe); the order placed next sends `source` (`orders.source`, migration `0037_marketing_channels`), and the promotion's Results say "2 orders from Telegram posts". Not UTM tags: the app reads the source itself and nothing else consumes them.
  - **Where the code lives:** server feature `marketing` (channels, codes, posts, delivery; it reads promotions through the menu feature's `getPromotion`); app feature `marketing` (Marketing → **Channels**); the posts card and the Post to channels dialog in the promotions feature (posts belong to a promotion's page), reading the channels through `useMarketingChannels` from `~/features/marketing`. Routes: `/api/c/<slug>/admin/marketing/channels/**`, `…/marketing/posts/{id}/cancel|retry`, `…/menu/promotions/{id}/posts`.
- **Alternatives:** the admin typing the channel's @name (private channels have none, and it proves nothing about who owns it); sending through the outbox (posts need their own states, the cancel and the result per channel); UTM parameters; counting visits (needs a store of clicks; orders are what the owner asked about).
- **Consequences:** the bot is an admin of each connected channel and can delete messages there (only its own code post is deleted). A scheduled post can go out up to a minute late. `PlaceOrderInput` gains an optional `source`; `PromotionResults` gains `bySource`.
- **Verified:** server `marketing.service.test.ts` 21 (codes: connect and confirm, the rights check, wrong, expired and repeated codes, only the creator; removed and reconnect; disconnect at the version and the bot leaving only when no cafe uses the channel; posts now with the poster as a file and the escaped caption, text without a poster, scheduled with the 30-day limit and Cancel, the idempotency replay, draft and other cafes' promotions and channels refused, a post cancelled when its promotion ended; retries, 5 tries, 429, 400, 403; two overlapping runs send once, checked to fail without the claim's guard; Cancel refused when the task claimed the post, checked to fail without `requireOneChange`; the order's source and the results by source); unit `post-form.test.ts` 3; e2e `channels.test.ts` 7 (mocked), `shop-marketing.test.ts` 3 (the real server: the offer opened from `?promo`, the source on the order), `accessibility.test.ts` +2.

### D165: UI fixes: the order drawer's scrollbar, reorder chevrons that slide, AM/PM by a tap, each Pre-selected box its own, 2026-10-13
- **Context:** the owner's bug report from staging (2026-10-13, screenshots): the order drawer's scrollbar sat 16 px in from the edge; the reorder arrows on Categories and Options looked heavy and a row jumped to its new place; on a phone AM/PM in a time field couldn't be changed; in New add-on group, ticking the second "Pre-selected" ticked the first.
- **Decision:**
  - **The order drawer:** `UDrawer`'s container pads all sides (`p-4`), so the lines' scroll area ended 16 px short. The container keeps its vertical padding only (`px-0`); the header, lines and footer pad themselves (`px-4`), so the scrollbar is at the drawer's edge (phones and tablets).
  - **Reorder:** Move up / Move down are `i-lucide-chevron-up` / `-down` (Categories at both levels, an option set's values, New option set). A row moved by its chevrons or ↑/↓ slides to its new place: `useReorderTransition` gives `<TransitionGroup>` a move class (`.reorder-move`, 200 ms) while reordering, off during a Sortable drag (Sortable animates it and the dropped row is already there) and for `prefers-reduced-motion`. A root composable: two features use it.
  - **AM/PM:** Reka's time field changes AM/PM only from keys (A, P, arrows), and Android's keyboard reports letters as "Unidentified", so on a phone it couldn't change. `<TimeInput>` wraps `UInputTime`: **a tap or click on AM/PM moves the time 12 hours** (nothing while empty). Used for branch hours and availability rules; `UInputTime` elsewhere is a lint error.
  - **Checkbox ids:** a `UCheckbox` inside a `UFormField` takes the field's id unless it has its own. The Pre-selected boxes sat inside the Add-ons field, so every one had the same id and every label pointed at the first. Each now has an id from `useId()`. **Found the same way:** the promotion editor's branch checkboxes inside the `branchIds` field, fixed the same way. Rule: a labelled `UCheckbox` / `USwitch` in a `v-for` inside a `UFormField` gets its own `id`.
- **Alternatives:** a native `<select>` for AM/PM (a second control beside Nuxt UI's field); a 24-hour clock on phones (the cafe's staff read 12-hour); wrapping each checkbox in its own `UFormField` (extra markup, and no error to show there); animating with Sortable only (it animates drags, not data moves).
- **Consequences:** the add-on and product reorder lists keep their arrows and no slide until asked; the composable is ready for them.
- **Verified:** lint, typecheck. New e2e tests: `modifier-groups.test.ts` +1 (the second label ticks the second box), `availability-rules.test.ts` +1 (AM/PM tapped on both fields, the saved minutes), `shop-menu.test.ts` (the tablet drawer's lines reach its right edge); run with `categories`, `option-sets`, `branches`, `promotions`: 7 files, 177 tests passed. The new tests were not run against the code before the fixes.

### D166: Errors that leave when fixed, Preselected without waiting, drawer padding, a black backdrop, and the expected UI behavior written down, 2026-10-13
- **Context:** the owner's second report after D165 (2026-10-13, screenshots): in New add-on group the Pre-selected boxes stayed red and "Each add-on can be listed only once" and the At least / At most error stayed after being fixed; the add-on group page's Reorder still had arrows and no slide; ticking Preselected there blocked the page until each answer and showed nothing while saving (the counter's Sold out switches don't); the drawers' top and bottom spacing was the container's padding and gap, so the lines were cut against the header and footer; the backdrop behind modals and drawers was nearly white in light mode. The owner asked for the expected UI behavior to be part of the standard.
- **Decision:**
  - **Errors leave when fixed (`useFormErrorRecheck`, every `UForm`).** `UForm` validates only the field being edited, so an error another field caused (a rule across fields, a duplicate in a list, a removed row) stayed. After each change, the fields showing an error the schema gave are validated again by name, silently: fixed ones clear, nothing new appears on untouched fields. Errors from the server (`setErrors`) aren't confirmed by the schema, so they stay until their own field is edited, as before. It reads the form's `schema` and `state` props and its exposed API: one line per form.
  - **A checkbox inside a group's field gets its own `UFormField`.** The Pre-selected boxes were inside the Add-ons field: they took its id (D165) and its error colour. Each now has its own field (`modifiers.<i>.isDefault`), which replaces D165's `useId` there.
  - **Preselected never waits (`usePreselectQueue`).** A group's add-ons share one version (D59), so changes can't be sent in parallel like sold-out switches (separate rows). A box shows its new value at once; the changes are sent in turn, each with the version the last answer gave; ticking a box back before its turn sends nothing; a refused change puts the waiting boxes back (toast; a conflict also on the page). A spinner beside the box while its change isn't saved; no success toast (the new `setDefault` mutation); the group's maximum is checked on the boxes as shown. The rows stay usable while a Preselected change is sent; other changes still disable them.
  - **Reordering:** chevrons and the slide (D165) on the add-on group's Reorder, Menu items → Reorder and the add-on groups in the item form too, so every reorder list behaves the same.
  - **Drawers** (`app.config.ts`): the container has no padding or gap and doesn't scroll; the header `px-4 pt-4`, the body `p-4` and scrolling, the footer `px-4` with the safe area. Pages set borders only (the order drawer); the period picker's `pb-2` removed.
  - **The backdrop** behind modals, slide-overs and drawers is `bg-black/50` (`/60` in dark mode), in `app.config.ts`.
  - **The standard:** [ui.md §17 Expected behavior](reference/ui.md#17-expected-behavior-owner-2026-10-13-d165-d166) (overlays, form errors, controls that save when changed, reordering, times of day), the review checklist, forms.md (`useFormErrorRecheck`, two edge cases) and AGENTS.md.
- **Alternatives:** validating the whole form on every change (errors on fields not reached yet); parallel Preselected requests (409s on the shared version) or a server change to version add-ons separately (a data-model change for a UI fix); keeping each drawer's own spacing (the cut-off kept coming back per page).
- **Consequences:** a form whose schema isn't a Standard Schema gets no recheck (all of ours are Valibot). The queue keeps unsent changes only in the page: leaving the page while one waits its turn still sends it (the promise chain outlives the page), but closing the tab drops it.
- **Verified:** lint, typecheck, unit 353; e2e `modifier-groups.test.ts` +3 (errors clearing and the boxes not red; the queue: boxes change while the first change is held, sent in turn with versions 1 and 2, the box ticked back never sent; a refused change puts the box back); the full e2e suite: 565 of 566 passed, the one failure this step's own error test (its steps, not the app: At most was already 1, and a rule across fields runs only once the name is valid), fixed and the file rerun on the same build, 41 of 41. The new tests weren't run against the code before the fixes.

### D167: Task-based agent documentation loading, 2026-10-13
- **Context:** 42 KB root instructions and Claude imports of the 129 KB progress log made unrelated history startup context. Codex documents a default 32 KiB project instruction budget.
- **Decision:** keep universal security, tenant, payment, session and architecture invariants plus task routing in AGENTS.md; CLAUDE.md imports only it. Preserve detailed recipes in five on-demand guides and existing standards. Navigate history through current-state and decision indexes with bounded section reads; preserve all earlier entries/IDs.
- **Authority:** task reference/server standards own details; histories are evidence of past choices, not blanket current requirements. Unresolved documentation/status conflicts are flagged, not guessed. No implementation, dependency, test or deployment change.
- **Alternatives:** splitting large files into unconditional imports (same context cost); duplicating Claude/Codex skills or nested rules (different discovery, maintenance cost); wholesale archive moves (unnecessary anchor churn).
- **Verification:** sizes, loading behavior research, relocation coverage, links/import graph, eight scenario estimates and required repository checks are recorded in [context audit](context-audit.md). This is documentation evidence, not a live comparison of agent sessions.

### D168: Architecture reliability: bounded requests, owned delivery and durable cleanup, 2026-10-14

- **Context/authority:** the owner requested fixes on a new branch from main after the [architecture audit](reviews/architecture-audit-2026-10-14.md). Keep the modular monolith and existing business policies; [implementation scope](plans/architecture-reliability.md).
- **Requests:** count actual bytes before JSON/multipart parsing. Nitro 2's Cloudflare adapter buffers requests before routes, so wrap its preset entry with the same caps while preserving assets, cron and other handlers. Header checks alone cannot protect memory. A future Nitro upgrade must retest this integration.
- **Delivery:** give outbox, notification and marketing claims random owner tokens; require ownership for success, retry and failure. Re-read production time per claim and take attempt counts from the conditional write. Delivery remains at least once; provider deduplication remains necessary. Immediate order dispatch targets the tenant/order/event, not the shared event backlog. Provider and dispatch budgets bound work.
- **Media:** write provisional object keys before R2; remove plans in the successful asset batch. Removal queues source/variant object keys in the same batch that deletes asset rows. Clear deletion work only after R2 succeeds. This closes cleanup-loss and upload-crash gaps without allowing references to missing objects.
- **Jobs:** use an independent persistent cursor per platform task and process at most 25 active cafes per run, continuing after each failure. Work remains idempotent and can repeat if runs overlap. Existing notification delivery runs before generation; summaries and reminders fail independently. Migration `0038_delivery_reliability` adds only tables/nullable claim columns.
- **Database/lifecycle:** aggregate payments in SQL by method and UTC windows derived from the branch timezone, keeping report semantics. Use the existing timezone library to resolve repeated/skipped report hours; business dates compare the local wall clock with 04:00, avoiding the old four-elapsed-hours error on DST days. Index targeted dispatch and cleanup queries. Bound idempotency/history cleanup. Redact expired account-email payloads using the existing link lifetimes. Sent-metadata retention is opt-in, default zero; financial history/audit and guest-retention policy are unchanged.
- **Operations:** preserve D133's staging flow. Manual CI on main creates a checked-revision manifest only after checks/e2e pass. Provide a disabled-until-configured independent health workflow and optional scheduler heartbeat; production promotion enforcement, URL/domain, recipients, Cloudflare alerts, capacity and recovery/retention policy remain Q4/Q24. No external configuration is implied by this change.
- **Alternatives deferred:** microservices, realtime subscriptions, Queues and database partitioning until measurements justify them; catalog caching without a measured bottleneck/invalidation design; guessing guest/financial retention or configuring production accounts without the needed settings.
- **Verification:** see the D168 progress entry for actual check results, guard-removal probes and build/runtime evidence. No merge or deployment.

### D169: Server log privacy, fresh catalog reads and successful-job health, 2026-10-14

- **Authority:** the owner requested a new branch from main for audit S2, S3, S4, S7 and S8. Branch `fix/server-performance-security`, base `9159866`; [scope](plans/server-performance-security.md).
- **Logs:** sanitize query/fragment and URL credentials, sensitive token paths and common credential assignments; recursively redact structured secrets with depth/entry/string caps. Preserve trusted request context and useful stack locations. Callers must still avoid bodies and opaque secrets the logger cannot infer.
- **Catalog:** add explicit tenant scope to inner option/modifier queries using the existing tenant/status index. Batch twelve base relations in a fresh snapshot, and restrict checkout/placement item-owned relations to cart IDs via a single JSON bind. All validation data for selected items remains loaded; hours, sold-out state, promotions and final order checks remain fresh. Image/dynamic reads are outside the base snapshot. No cross-request cache or weaker freshness policy; measure deployed cost before introducing cache/invalidation infrastructure.
- **Jobs:** separate cursor activity from last useful success and record processed/failure counts. All-failed pages advance for fairness without refreshing success; partial progress or a successful empty page updates it. Existing optional scheduler age now measures useful success. Add an optional overdue pending outbox/notification age check, default zero; production chooses thresholds under existing policy. Migration `0039_job_success` adds nullable success and default-zero counts, preserving old writers/cursors without inventing past success.
- **Maintenance:** assistant usage, notification history and customer-alert cleanup use maximum 500-row batches, repeat under time and batch-count budgets, and resume on later runs. Retention remains 90/90/two days. Split notification history budgets so customer alerts cannot starve. Orders, ledgers, audit and unresolved retention policy are untouched.
- **Verification repair:** correct the known Bakong tamper test's mutation of an unchanged character. This changes tests only, not encryption or payment behavior. See progress for guard-removal and actual validation evidence.
- **Alternatives/follow-ups:** cross-request catalog caching, Queues, realtime and partitioning wait for deployed measurements. External alert recipients, monitoring configuration, capacity/billing and launch decisions remain Q4/Q24. No merge or deployment is included.

### D170: Tenant-scoped admin pricing and fresh counter batches, 2026-10-14

- **Authority:** the owner requested follow-up audit N4/N8. Branch `fix/admin-counter-query-performance`, base merged main `8a47cb3`; [scope](plans/admin-counter-query-performance.md). D169's staging deployment completed successfully before this work.
- **Admin prices:** the item-list price aggregate names its cafe and uses the existing tenant index. Preserve sellable min/max ranges, filters, ordering, counts and pages; no speculative index/migration is added.
- **Counter reads:** combine branch timezone, the current rate, KHQR capability and token presence in one context read, then batch four queue/detail/count reads after computing the branch business date. Use tenant/branch-scoped active-order subqueries instead of expanding ID lists, and group lines once. Every request remains fresh, with no hidden queue cap. The HTTP route no longer reads the sealed token just to determine presence. Explicit internal capability overrides stay compatible; authorization still runs normally.
- **Read consistency and cost:** context precedes the queue snapshot because business-date conversion needs the IANA timezone. The queue rows, lines, payments and finished count share one batch. Two database calls/five SQL statements replace the old eight separate nonempty-queue reads including token presence; this excludes access/session work and is not a production latency/billing claim.
- **D1 result mapping:** batch objects collapse repeated SQL output names. Alias branch/customer names and catalog add-on own/default rules explicitly, preserving decoders and behavior. The catalog fix is a necessary correction to D169 found with a populated local D1 probe; test empty results, populated object mapping and duplicate-name removal, not only libsql array results.
- **Validation/follow-ups:** see progress for actual checks, removal probes and build/browser evidence. Cross-request caching, conditional/delta responses, realtime and capacity decisions wait for measured workload and a complete freshness/invalidation design. Other N findings and business/security policies are outside this change; no merge or deployment is authorized here.

### D171: Errors of a removed or moved row follow the row, 2026-10-16

- **Authority:** the owner reported that removing a value in the option set form left its "Value is required" on the value that moved into its place. The same issue was in the add-on group form, and the owner asked for it to be written into the UI standard and made reusable across the app.
- **Decision:** a list's per-row error (`values.2`, `modifiers.2.price`) goes with its row when the row is removed or moved. Lists remove and move rows through `useFormRowErrors` (`app/composables/useFormRowErrors.ts`), built on the pure `followRowErrors` (`app/utils/form-rows.ts`). [ui §17](reference/ui.md#forms-and-their-errors) states the rule; [forms → Rows and their errors](reference/forms.md#rows-and-their-errors) gives the usage.
- **Alternatives:** validate the whole form after each change (rejected: it shows errors on rows the person hasn't reached, against D166); clear every row error on each change (loses the other rows' errors); key errors by row id (`UForm` names errors by path, so the id never reaches them).
- **Consequences:** a list whose rows have their own fields removes and moves rows only through the composable; a `splice` on such a list is a bug. Not yet fixed, follow-ups: `AvailabilityRuleFormModal` (`rows.N.*`, `rows.splice`), `ItemAddOnGroups` (`addOnGroups.N.*`, `groups.splice`), `StaffFormModal` (`memberships.N.branchId`, `memberships.splice`).
- **Verified:** unit `test/unit/form-rows.test.ts` (4) and `test/unit/use-form-row-errors.test.ts` (4); the option set and add-on group unit tests pass; lint and typecheck pass. **Not checked in a browser yet** (forms reference, edge case 32).

### D172: Faster and reliable local checks, 2026-10-17

- **Context:** the owner found small fixes slow to finish. Measured on a clean `main` (12-core Windows laptop): lint 97 s, typecheck 185 s, unit/server 99 s with one Windows-only failure, the full e2e suite 487 s with 4 failed tests and 2 files failing before they ran, and a stale e2e build about 5 min (Nitro bundling about 3.3 min). Earlier runs worked around the failures (the upgrade ran e2e serially and listed the unit failure as known).
- **Decision (owner):** quick wins first, the same gates as D83.
  - `routes.test.ts` compares route paths with forward slashes on every OS.
  - `pnpm lint` / `lint:fix` use ESLint's cache (`--cache-strategy content`, in `node_modules/.cache/eslint/`): 87 s cold, 4 s warm or after editing one file; a new error in a cached file is still reported (checked).
  - Local e2e runs at most 4 files at a time (`E2E_WORKERS` overrides; CI unchanged). Full suite: 11 workers (the default) 487 s, 6 failed; 6 workers 577 s, 3 cross-tab timeouts; 4 workers 661 s, 568/568 pass.
- **Rejected for now:** more than 4 local workers (faster but flaky). Still open, measured above: the stale-build cost on every source edit (e2e against `nuxt dev` locally, or a lighter e2e build) and the 3-minute typecheck. **The stale build is followed up by D177** (untraced locally: 2m23s → 35 s; `nuxt dev` rejected). Capping the server-test workers does not stop their native Windows crash (exit 3221225477), so it is not capped.
- **Where:** `package.json`, `vitest.config.ts`, `server/tests/routes.test.ts`; [development guide → Commands](guides/development.md#commands).

### D173: Reference data kept between visits; a query's first fetch joins a running one, 2026-10-17

- **Context:** the owner asked for fewer requests when admin pages open, without stale data after any change ([plan](plans/request-reduction.md), measured in `test/e2e/request-budget.test.ts`). Opening a menu item read the item, option sets, add-on groups (twice) and availability rules every time. Two causes in Nuxt 4.6's `useAsyncData`: `purgeCachedData` deletes a key's data when its last user unmounts, so nothing survives a page change or a closed slide-over; and the first fetch uses `dedupe: 'cancel'`, so a second component mounting a key while it loads cancels the load and starts another. D170 left cross-request caching for "a measured workload and a complete freshness/invalidation design": this is that design. It amends D22 (which keeps its three triggers).
- **Decision:**
  - `useApiQuery(key, fn, { staleTime })`, opt-in, set where the query is defined (every call site of a key passes the same options, D69). The key's data is kept after unmount (a custom `getCachedData`, which also stops Nuxt purging it). On the next first fetch: kept and younger than `staleTime` → shown, no request; older → shown at once and refetched behind it (stale-while-revalidate).
  - **Known changes beat time.** `invalidate()` (our write, or another tab's message) marks every matching key: kept ones are never shown again, so the next visit loads fresh with its loading state; keys on screen refetch at once. A failed load (any error) also marks its key. `invalidateAll()` (tab return ≥ 5 s, reconnect) marks kept keys stale, so they revalidate when next shown.
  - **A load started before a write never counts after it:** each invalidation bumps the key's generation; a load that started in an older one doesn't make the key fresh.
  - **First fetches join, refreshes cancel.** `useApiQuery` defaults to `dedupe: 'defer'`, so a second component joins the load already running. The `refresh`/`execute` it returns default to `cancel`, and `invalidate`/`invalidateAll` refresh the keys on screen through a registry of mounted queries with `cancel`, not `refreshNuxtData` (whose refresh would now join an older load). D30's watch behavior is unchanged.
  - Opted in, `REFERENCE_STALE_TIME` (60 s): `categories:all`, `option-sets:list`, `modifier-groups:list`, `availability-rules:list`, `branches:options`. Not: records opened for editing (forms send the version they read), paginated lists (one key across filters), settings edited in a form (cafe profile, rates, KHQR), `reports:branches` (carries each branch's business date and isn't invalidated by branch writes).
  - Product writes now also invalidate `categories`: categories carry `itemCount`, which `CategorySelect` uses to decide what it offers. The gap was hidden while every visit refetched.
- **Freshness, by case:** our write or another tab's → fresh on the next visit, at once on screen; another device's → within 60 s of a visit, on tab return, and a write based on old data is refused (409); sign-in/out → every key cleared (D29 session boundary, unchanged); another cafe → full page load.
- **Safety:** the bookkeeping (`utils/invalidate.ts`) runs in the browser only: module state on the server would be shared by every request. A refused read (403 after a permission change) marks the key, so the last good copy isn't shown again.
- **Rejected:** a server-side cache (KV) — reads are cheap, the cost is round trips, and it adds a tenant-isolation and invalidation risk; ETag/304 — still a round trip and a query; TanStack Query or Pinia Colada — a migration for one missing feature (and no Pinia); `defer` for every refresh — a save's refresh would join a load started before it and show the old data.
- **Evidence:** request budget: a second item opened reads 1 request (was 5), the first 4 (was 5), the shell's assistant 1 (was 2), repeat visits to Categories and Availability 0. Guard checks: without the invalidation mark, "changed meanwhile … loads it fresh" fails; without `defer`, the add-on groups read twice again; without `cancel` on refresh, D30's "older response can't replace the newer one" fails.
- **Where:** `app/composables/useApiQuery.ts`, `app/utils/invalidate.ts`; [data fetching → staleTime](reference/data-fetching.md#kept-between-visits-staletime-d173), [app behavior → data freshness](reference/app-behavior.md#data-freshness).
- **Amended 2026-10-18 (plan step 6, the customer site):** `menu:branches` and `menu:public:<branch>` opt in too: coming back to the menu from Review order or Your orders read the whole menu again. Safe because Review order (the quote) and Place order check sold out, prices and opening on the server. Data the server rendered with the page now counts as loaded at hydration (`queryHydrated`); without it, `staleTime` saw it as never loaded and refetched the menu right after the first paint. Customer request budget (`request-budget-shop.test.ts`): back to the menu 0 reads (was 1 from Review order, 2 from Your orders); the first load unchanged.


### D174: The server tests' database is built once per run, 2026-10-18

- **Context:** follows D172. `pnpm test:unit` (unit + server) took 171–188 s on the owner's 12-core Windows laptop and failed every run: 5–12 tests over the 5 s test / 10 s hook timeouts (health, migrations, tenant-jobs, d1-limits, identity, marketing, expiry, sample data), once a native worker crash (exit 3221225477). Each passes alone: CPU contention. `createTestDb()` replayed all 41 migration files (436 statements, one `execute` each) for every database, and about 38 files call it in `beforeEach` (~780 tests): 230–570 ms per database, about 3 CPU-minutes per run.
- **Decision:** `createTestDb()` replays a dump of the migrated database instead. Once per run, the server project's global setup (`server/tests/support/global-setup.ts`) runs every migration on a scratch database, `dumpSchema()` reads back `sqlite_master` (tables, indexes, triggers, views in creation order) and every row the migrations left, and the statements are `provide`d to the workers (a project without that setup builds them once per test file: Vitest isolates each file's modules); each new database gets those statements in one `batch` (one transaction), then `PRAGMA foreign_keys = ON` and the D1 parameter proxy, as before. The dump comes from the real migrations at run time, so a new migration is never missed; `server/tests/test-db.test.ts` checks the result equals a migrated database (schema and rows), that copied rows load in any key order, and that each call is its own database (both guards fail when the dump drops an object or the rows). `migrations.test.ts` keeps replaying migrations step by step (`createTestClient`/`applyMigration`).
- **Measured (same laptop, 2026-10-18):** a database 14 ms (was 230–570 ms), the migrations ~0.4 s once per run. Machine otherwise idle: 64 s → 30 s, both green (main 2 runs, this 3 consecutive runs plus 4 earlier). Busy machine (main 188 s, 5 timeouts): 64–81 s with 2–7 timeouts. Two `test:unit` at once (a reproducible load): main 126 s, a timeout in each; this 60 s, all pass. No native worker crash in 13 runs.
- **Rejected:** a template SQLite file copied per test (4 ms, but tests then write to disk, Windows kept the files locked so cleanup failed with EPERM, and temp files pile up); `executeMultiple` of the migration SQL (slower, 704 ms); Vitest's `fsModuleCache` (no measurable gain, 30 s either way, and a stale-cache risk); capping server workers (D172: it didn't stop the crash, and the run is now short).
- **Still open:** under heavy load a few tests still exceed 5 s. The slowest is the first `operationalHealth` call in `health.test.ts`: its dynamic `import('#server/features/notifications')` transforms that feature inside the test (4 s on an idle machine). `sample-data` "loads the standard menu in small steps" takes ~5.6 s alone. **Followed up by D175**, which also merged the once-per-run global setup: it was pushed to PR 129's branch after the merge and missed `main`, which still built the dump once per test file.
- **Where:** `server/tests/support/db.ts`, `server/tests/support/global-setup.ts`, `server/tests/test-db.test.ts`, `vitest.config.ts`; [development guide → Tests](guides/development.md#tests).

### D175: Heavy server tests tagged `slow`; quick test commands for the inner loop, 2026-10-18

- **Context:** follows D174. On the owner's laptop, shared with dev servers and other agents' e2e runs, `pnpm test:unit` still failed 1–4 tests a run at the 5 s default: d1-limits (up to 11 s), migrations.test.ts (13 s), the sample menus (14 s Standard, 25–31 s Large), staff password resets (6 s), and the first test of random files. That last group came from D174's dump being built once per test file on `main` (Vitest isolates each file's modules), not once per run: the global setup that fixed it (`7d1d6d1`) reached PR 129's branch after the merge. Small fixes also had no quicker command than the full run.
- **Decision:**
  - This branch includes that global setup (D174's text already describes it).
  - **`slow` tag** in the server project, timeout 60 s; every other test keeps Vitest's 5 s, which stays the global default. Tagged: `d1-limits.test.ts` and `migrations.test.ts` (`// @module-tag slow`), the six sample-data tests that load a whole menu (`loadAll`), and staff's "reset password" describe (several scrypt hashes per test). Tag a new test when its own work is heavy (data at the contracts' limits, a whole sample menu, migrations replayed, several password hashes) and it takes over ~1 s idle; a slow import or setup gets fixed, not tagged.
  - `server/utils/health.ts` imports the notifications feature statically. The dynamic import had no recorded reason and there is no cycle (nothing in notifications imports health); the import now happens at file load, not inside the first test (that test: 3–4 s → 72 ms).
  - **Inner-loop commands:** `pnpm test:changed` runs the unit and server tests that import, directly or not, a file changed since `origin/main` (commits, staged and unstaged). `pnpm test:fast` runs unit + server without `slow`. `pnpm vitest --project server <path>` is watch mode. They don't replace the gate: full `pnpm test:unit` before a PR, CI runs everything (D83).
  - `forceRerunTriggers` adds `package.json`, `vitest.config.*` and `server/db/migrations/**`, anchored at the project root. The server tests read migrations from disk, so with Vitest's defaults a migration-only change ran nothing under `--changed`. And the defaults' `**/` skips dot folders, so in a checkout under `.claude/worktrees/` even a config change didn't rerun everything.
- **Measured (same laptop, 2026-10-18):** `pnpm test:unit` 28–29 s three runs in a row, all 1,479 pass (before: 64–70 s and 2–3 timeouts a run while the machine was busy). Two at once, twice: 54 s each, all four green. `test:fast` 26 s (39 tests skipped; the slow ones run in parallel with the rest, so it saves little on an idle machine). `test:changed` after one edit: `server/utils/health.ts` 9 s (1 file), a notifications file 10 s (its tests plus health and tenant-jobs), an app util 3 s, a migration the whole run, nothing changed 2 s.
- **Guard checks:** with the tag's timeout at 100 ms every tagged test timed out (all 16 migration tests, the 6 sample menu tests, the 5 reset tests, 5 d1-limits tests over 100 ms) and untagged tests in the same files passed. Without the tag's timeout, two runs at once each failed the Large sample menu (8.7 s, 7.5 s); with it, the same load passed.
- **Rejected:** `experimental.recordDependencies` (not in Vitest 5.0.1, and not needed: `related`/`--changed` already follow dynamic imports; before this change `related` on a notifications file found `health.test.ts` through the dynamic import). `NODE_COMPILE_CACHE`: 27–28 s against 27–32 s without, within noise, and the native worker crash came in 3 of its 5 runs. A higher global timeout would also hide a hang in a fast test. Git hooks (husky, lefthook, e.g. `test:changed` before a push) wait for the owner's OK.
- **Still open:** the native worker crash (exit 3221225477, D172) came in 2 of 17 full runs without the compile cache, in a different file each time; D174 saw none in 13 runs before the global setup, so whether `provide`-ing the dump makes it likelier is untested. **Found and fixed in D176:** libsql crashes when it frees a database nobody closed. `--changed` ignores untracked files: `git add` a new test or source file first.
- **Where:** `vitest.config.ts`, `package.json`, `server/utils/health.ts`, the tagged test files; [development guide → Commands](guides/development.md#commands).

### D176: Every server-test database is closed, 2026-10-18

- **Context:** the native worker crash seen in D172–D175: the worker exits with code 3221225477 (0xC0000005, an access violation) and Vitest reports `Worker exited unexpectedly … during started state`. It came in 5 of 30 full `pnpm test:unit` runs on 2026-10-18, more often under load, in a different file each time (items.service, d1-limits, sample-data twice, staff). In 2 of the 5, every test in the file had finished, so the worker died while ending; in 3, tests were still pending, so it died mid-file. libsql 0.5.29 (via `@libsql/client` 0.18) is the only native module a test worker loads (checked with Node's diagnostic report). The tests never closed their in-memory databases: `createTestDb()` opened one per test and left it to the garbage collector.
- **Cause, reproduced outside Vitest:** a script that opens in-memory libsql clients and never closes them hits a segmentation fault when Node exits normally: 0 of 20 runs with 30 clients, 5 of 20 with 100, 9 of 20 with 300, 6 of 6 with 1,500. Closing each client, or all of them at the end, gave 0 crashes every time. The schema, the batch and transactions make no difference: plain queries crash too. `process.exit()` with 300 open clients didn't crash (0 of 10), and Vitest ends a worker by killing it, so this exit path alone doesn't explain crashes reported "during started state". The mid-file crashes fit the same fault reached through garbage collection, which can free a database and its statements in any order (a known way for SQLite wrappers to crash). This is not proven in libsql's source, and there was no Windows crash dump: Windows Error Reporting logged nothing, and turning on local dumps would change a system setting.
- **Decision:** no test database is left to the garbage collector. `server/tests/support/clients.ts` tracks every client that `createTestDb()` and `createTestClient()` open. The server project's setup file (`server/tests/support/setup.ts`) closes those opened during a test or its `beforeEach` after that test, and the rest (from `beforeAll` or the file's top level) after the file. The setup file's own hooks mark where each test starts and ends: `expect.getState().currentTestName` keeps the previous test's name between tests, so a `beforeAll` database looked like a test's. `test-db.test.ts` checks that a test's database is closed after it and a `beforeAll` one stays open; without either hook it fails (guard checks). The e2e helpers already close theirs.
- **Measured:** with the fix, 16 full runs and 0 crashes: 10 as two runs at once with `NODE_COMPILE_CACHE` (the heaviest load seen), 5 in a row (28–29 s, unchanged) and 1 more. Without it, 5 crashes in 30 runs (about 17%). At that rate 16 clean runs in a row would happen by chance about 5% of the time, so the evidence is strong but not conclusive. The smaller repros couldn't show it: without the fix, the five heaviest server files run 20 times, and a test opening 300 databases run 10 times, never crashed, so the crash needs a full run's load.
- **Not done:** no with/without comparison of D174's global setup. The cause doesn't involve the dump the setup hands to workers, and at this crash rate telling two variants apart would take about 50 runs each. `NODE_COMPILE_CACHE` stays rejected (D175: no speed gain); its higher crash rate was this same fault.
- **Where:** `server/tests/support/clients.ts`, `server/tests/support/setup.ts`, `server/tests/support/db.ts`, `server/tests/test-db.test.ts`, `vitest.config.ts`; [development guide → Tests](guides/development.md#tests).

### D177: The local e2e build leaves dependencies untraced, 2026-10-18

- **Context:** follows D172 (still open there: the stale-build cost). Any build input change made the e2e build stale. The PR rule runs the changed feature's e2e files, so every small fix paid for a rebuild: 3m03s on the owner's 12-core Windows laptop, 2m10s of it "Bundling Nitro server". A CPU profile of the build put most of that in filesystem `stat` calls from Nitro 2.13's `node-externals` plugin. For every server import that resolves into `node_modules`, it resolves the module again with exsolve, uncached, trying every module directory, extension and `/index` suffix, so that it can trace and copy dependencies into `.output/server/node_modules`. On this disk (OneDrive folder, Windows) each `stat` is slow.
- **Decision:** locally the e2e build runs with Nitro's `externals.trace: false`. The global setup passes `E2E_UNTRACED=1` to `nuxt build` and `nuxt.config.ts`'s `nitro:init` hook turns tracing off. The built server imports dependencies by absolute path from this checkout's `node_modules` (`.output/server` 12 MB instead of 35 MB, no `node_modules` copy), so it runs only from this checkout, which is all the e2e server does. libsql's native binary loads from its installed package (`copyLibsqlNativeBinary` finds nothing to copy). Everything else is unchanged: the same app and server code, minifying and sourcemaps. The fingerprint (D83) records traced vs untraced, so switching rebuilds.
  - **CI is unchanged:** with `CI` set the build traces, like a deployment. `E2E_TRACE=1` does the same locally, to rule out a tracing-only problem. Staging, `pnpm build` and every other build always trace.
  - The PR gate is unchanged (AGENTS.md, D83): lint, typecheck, unit and the changed feature's e2e files locally; CI runs the full suite on a traced build.
- **Measured (same laptop, 2026-10-18):** idle machine, a stale build: 2m23s → 35–36 s (Nitro 2m06s → 20 s). Categories + shop-menu with a stale build: 241 s traced, 94–95 s untraced (176 s on the first run, a cold client cache). While other sessions loaded the laptop, alternating builds: Nitro 6m52s and 7m57s traced, 1m19s and 51 s untraced. On the untraced build: categories + shop-menu passed 3 runs in a row (68/68 each), and the full suite passed 576/576 in 436 s. One earlier full run, while the laptop was overloaded, had 50 failures.
