All of them were Chrome closing, worker aborts (exit 134) or a 180 s Chrome launch timeout, not app errors. The rerun once the laptop was quiet passed.
- **Rejected:**
  - **Turning off sourcemaps or minify** for the e2e build: no measurable change (Nitro 2m12s–2m18s in all three variants).
  - **e2e against `nuxt dev` locally:** 146 s before the first page answered, no faster than an untraced build. Categories + shop-menu then failed 9 of 68 tests: 30 s timeouts while Vite compiled each page on first request. The customer site's tests also need the seeded database, which the dev server's own migrations conflict with ("table already exists"). It is not the production build CI tests either (no security headers, dev-only plugins).
  - **Patching Nitro** to cache its resolutions: a dependency patch to maintain, and the untraced build skips that work entirely.
- **Where:** `nuxt.config.ts` (`nitro:init`), `test/e2e/support/global-setup.ts`; [development guide → Commands](guides/development.md#commands).
### D178: A failed submit goes to the first invalid field, 2026-10-18

- **Context:** the New option set form (20 values allowed). With the 15th value empty and the dialog scrolled to the top, pressing Create showed nothing: the error was below the fold, so it looked as if the button did nothing. The sign-in and change-password forms already focused the first invalid field, each with its own copy; the other 13 forms with a schema did not.
- **Decision:** one shared `focusFirstInvalid(event)` (`app/utils/focus-first-invalid.ts`), used as `@error` on every `UForm` with a schema. It scrolls the first invalid field in page order (not the schema's order) to the middle of the view and focuses it, on the next frame (the form's fields are disabled until after its events). A group error with no input scrolls to its message. The menu item editor keeps its own handler (it opens the field's section first); the sign-in and password pages keep their own copies for now (same behaviour).
- **Rejected:** hooking it inside `useFormErrorRecheck` by watching the errors (can't tell a failed submit from typing, so focus would jump while typing); Nuxt UI's `UForm` has no such option.
- **Where:** `app/utils/focus-first-invalid.ts`; [forms → Errors on submit](reference/forms.md#errors-on-submit), [ui §17](reference/ui.md#forms-and-their-errors); test `test/e2e/option-sets.test.ts`.

### D179: Every list that removes or moves rows moves its errors, 2026-10-18

- **Context:** after D178 the owner asked for every `UForm` to be checked for row errors. D171's `useFormRowErrors` was used by the option-set and add-on forms only. Four lists still removed or moved rows with `splice`, leaving an error on its old position for the row that takes its place: a staff member's branches, an availability rule's times, the weekly hours' windows, and the add-on groups of the item form (removed and moved).
- **Decision:** all four use `useFormRowErrors`. The two inside child components (`BranchHoursEditor`, `ItemAddOnGroups`) get the page's `UForm` ref as `errors-form`. Not changed: the item form's price grid and option sets (rows keyed by id or fixed, never removed by position), and rows that only show a server error by position (availability times clear it on any edit).
- **Where:** `StaffFormModal`, `AvailabilityRuleFormModal`, `BranchHoursEditor` / `BranchSettingsTab`, `ItemAddOnGroups` / `ProductFormFields`; [forms → Rows and their errors](reference/forms.md#rows-and-their-errors).

### D180: One crop modal for every photo upload, 2026-10-19

- **Context:** the menu item photo editor (D158) crops and zooms, but only for menu items: two fixed views, a fill colour, files for `/admin/media/photos`. The logo and the promotion poster upload the photo whole, with no way to frame it. The owner asked for the capability as a reusable component for any photo upload.
- **Decision:** `<ImageCropModal>` (root): one frame of any proportion (`aspectRatio`), the photo dragged and zoomed inside it (always filling the frame), Rotate and Reset; the result is the cropped file (WebP, at most `maxEdge` px, never enlarged) or `null`. It takes the picked `File`; the picker stays in the input. `<ImageInput crop-ratio>` opens it after picking and uploads the cropped file through the unchanged `useImageUpload`; without `crop-ratio` nothing changes. The frame geometry (`app/utils/image-crop.ts`) and the canvas work (`image-canvas.ts`: open upright, turn, display copy, crop to a file) moved out of the products feature; the menu editor imports them and keeps its two views, fill colour and files. Opt-in per input. The cafe logo uses it (`crop-ratio="1"`, it is shown small and square beside the name); the promotion poster uses it only when needed: a design already 4:5 (within 2 %) or unreadable uploads as is (D163); any other shape (a square 1080 × 1080 is supported) opens the modal at 4:5 with `maxEdge` 2700 and quality 0.92, and **Keep whole poster** (`keepLabel`) uploads the picked file untouched. Never forced, because the stored poster is also what Telegram posts receive: cutting a design silently would change both. **Owner answer (2026-10-19):** Telegram posts get the framed poster, the same stored image the menu shows; the original is not kept. An owner who wants the whole design posted chooses **Keep whole poster**.
- **Rejected:** parameterising `PhotoEditorModal` (its previews, tabs, focus point and fill are menu rules); a crop library (the geometry already exists, tested, and must match the menu editor's); a pick/drop/paste phase inside the modal (the inputs already pick).
- **Where:** `app/components/ImageCropModal.vue`, `app/components/ImageInput.vue`, `app/utils/image-crop.ts`, `app/utils/image-canvas.ts`; [UI helpers → ImageInput](reference/ui-helpers.md#imageinput); test `test/unit/image-crop.test.ts`.

### D181: Staff cannot take the payment of their own order by hand, 2026-10-19

- **Context:** [security.md → Identity](server/security.md#identity) says staff never complete their own order's payment, but `payOrder` only checked `payment: ['collect']`, so a cashier could pay an order whose `customerId` was their own `userId`.
- **Decision:** inside `payOrder`'s command plan (after the status check, before the deadline, rate, charge and any statement) the persisted `order.customerId` is compared with the authenticated `actor.userId`: equal → 403 `OWN_ORDER_PAYMENT`, for every method (`cash_usd`, `cash_khr`, `khqr`). The plan throws before the batch, so no payment, event, audit entry, version change or idempotency record is written; another cashier pays the same order at the same version. `recordCheckedKhqrPayment` (Bakong-confirmed arrivals) is unchanged: the money itself is the verification, and no new automatic-settlement policy was invented.
- **Rejected:** a blanket check in `runOrderCommand` (it would also hit ready, complete, cancel and automatic settlement).
- **Where:** `server/features/orders/counter.service.ts`, `orders.errors.ts`; tests in `counter.service.test.ts` → "a cashier's own order".

### D182: One durable claim for the customer's "order ready" message, 2026-10-19

- **Context:** two paths sent it (the `orders.ready` outbox handler and the catch-up after Start), both reading `notified_at = null` and sending: concurrent duplicates; and when the event finished before the sign-up committed, a failed catch-up left a chat with no pending work.
- **Decision:** the delivery state lives on the order's `order_ready_alerts` row (one message per order; `notification_deliveries` needs a connected staff destination, which a customer's chat is not): `subscription_id` (new for every chat that uses a code), `claim_token`, `claim_until`, `attempts`, `retry_at` (migration `0040`, additive). Every sender (event, catch-up, `notifications:deliver` via `deliverOrderReadyDue`) takes one atomic claim (`UPDATE … WHERE chat set AND not notified AND lease free`), sends, and completes fenced by tenant, subscription and token. The sign-up batch sets `retry_at = now` and resets the claim, so the recovery work is committed with the subscription; a failed try releases the claim with the backoff (`retryDelayMs`, ≤ `DELIVERY_MAX_ATTEMPTS`, then the chat is forgotten without a "sent" mark). The claim's lease is 2 minutes: a stopped worker's claim is claimable again. The scheduled run parks an order still being prepared (`retry_at = null`: the ready event sends it). The event throws while another sender holds the claim, so the outbox retries it instead of losing it.
- **Not promised:** exactly-once. Telegram has no idempotency key; a send whose answer was lost can repeat after the lease or a retry (at least once), like the staff alerts (D113).
- **Rejected:** a new queue; reusing `notification_deliveries` (destination foreign key, staff-only history); a read-then-write check (the race itself).
- **Where:** `notifications.customers.ts` (`attemptOrderReady`, `deliverOrderReadyDue`), `notifications.repository.ts`, `tasks/notifications/deliver.ts`; tests in `notifications.customers.test.ts` → "one claim, durable recovery", `batch.test.ts` (D1 adapter), `migrations.test.ts`.

### D183: Searches match with `instr`, not `like`, because D1 limits patterns to 50 bytes, 2026-10-19

- **Context:** D1 refuses a `LIKE` or `GLOB` pattern over 50 bytes ("LIKE or GLOB pattern too complex"); libsql accepts 50,000, so the tests passed. The search pattern was `%` + the search in UTF-8 with every `%`, `_` and backslash escaped + `%`, so 49 ASCII letters (51 bytes), 17 Khmer letters (53) or 25 literal `%` (52) failed with a 500 on D1 although the contracts allow 100 characters. Affected: menu items (list and counts), staff (name, email) and the platform's cafes (name, address). No other `like` or `glob` is in the server.
- **Decision:** `containsText(column, search)` (`server/utils/search.ts`) is `instr(lower(column), lower(?)) > 0`, the search a bound parameter. No pattern means no wildcard, no escaping and no length limit of its own; the contracts (100 characters, trimmed) are unchanged and nothing is truncated. `lower` folds ASCII only, as `like` did, so case behaviour and Khmer matching are the same; `%`, `_` and backslash are plain characters as before. Page and count queries share the condition. It is still a scan of the tenant's rows: neither form uses an index, and this is not a speed-up.
- **Rejected:** a 48-character limit (UTF-8 and escaping still overflow it, and it would change the contracts); truncating; `glob` (same limit); FTS (a redesign nobody asked for).
- **Tests:** the test database now refuses a long text bound beside `like`/`glob`, so the old code fails in the service tests; `server/tests/d1-search.test.ts` runs the real repositories on real D1 (workerd through wrangler's platform proxy, Drizzle's D1 adapter), with a control that a 51-byte pattern is refused there; `test/unit/search-contracts.test.ts` pins the 100-character limit.
- **Where:** `server/utils/search.ts`, `items.repository.ts`, `staff.repository.ts`, `tenants.repository.ts`; [architecture → writes and D1 limits](server/architecture.md).

### D184: The staff list starts from the cafe's memberships, not from every account, 2026-10-19

- **Context:** `listStaff` selected from `user` and tested membership with a correlated `exists`, for the page and the count. `user` holds every customer and guest of every cafe, so `EXPLAIN QUERY PLAN` showed `SCAN user` (plus a temp sort) and a small cafe's list cost more with every sign-up elsewhere ([audit N5](reviews/server-followup-audit-2026-10-14.md#n5-staff-lists-scan-all-platform-users)). `member` has no unique `(organization_id, user_id)`, so a repeated row is possible; a person may also work at several branches.
- **Decision:** membership and branch checks are tenant-scoped `user.id in (select …)` semi-joins (`memberIds`, `workerIds` in `staff.repository.ts`). The planner builds the small set from `member_organizationId_idx` (or the `branch_staff` primary key) and looks users up by primary key, then the existing search, sort (name, id) and pagination apply. A semi-join matches a person once however many rows they have, so rows and totals can't multiply. The owner filter replaces the membership check (owners are members). Without a branch, the `branch_staff` subquery is bounded by the cafe's branches (`branches_tenant_id_unique`, archived ones too) because `branch_staff` has no tenant index; a row's branch always belongs to its tenant (composite key). Search (D183), filters, sort, response shape and the writes are unchanged. No migration.
- **Evidence:** query plans, not latency. On 20,000 unrelated users the old page and count plans were `SCAN user` in every case (unfiltered, search, admin, manager/staff, branch, combined); the new ones are `SEARCH user USING INDEX sqlite_autoindex_user_1 (id=?)` driven by `member_organizationId_idx` / `sqlite_autoindex_branch_staff_1`, with and without `ANALYZE`. No timings were taken and no production speed-up is claimed.
- **Rejected:** a join from `member` to `user` (needs `distinct`/`group by` against duplicate rows and several branches, and the count must be built differently); a new `branch_staff (tenant_id, …)` index (see limits); caching.
- **Limits:** the role filter without a branch still reads the tenant's `branch_staff` rows through its branches, and after `ANALYZE` on a table dominated by one big cafe SQLite may scan `branch_staff` instead (staff rows of all cafes, not customers). Search still scans the cafe's members (D183). The temp sort covers only the cafe's matching people. Add a `branch_staff (tenant_id, role, user_id)` index if platform staff counts ever make that visible.
- **Tests:** `staff.service.test.ts` → "list from the cafe's memberships" (customers and guests, two cafes, repeated membership row, three branches incl. an archived one, stable pages, totals, and the plan has no `SCAN user` for seven filter combinations; fails on the old query, and the isolation tests fail without the tenant condition); `server/tests/d1-search.test.ts` repeats the filters, isolation and the plan on real D1 (workerd).
- **Where:** `server/features/identity/staff.repository.ts`.

### D185: Create a cafe is one page; "Create new account" never reuses an account, 2026-10-19

- **Context:** the owner approved a single-page design (the frame is a layout reference; priority: functional requirements, project standards, Nuxt UI, frame). New cafe was a modal (D142, D146). Its "A new person" quietly gave access to any existing account with that email (D142): with the new labels ("Create new account: generate a temporary password") that promised a password for an account that keeps its own.
- **Decision:**
  - **One page, `/platform/cafes/new`** (route `app/pages/platform/cafes/new.vue`, thin; page and form in `app/features/platform/components/CafeCreatePage.vue`). Cafe details, First branch and First owner on the left, a live Creation summary and "After creation" steps on the right (from the panel's `@3xl` container width; one column below), and the shared `<BottomActionBar>` (`pinned` from `lg`, fixed below it) with Cancel and **Create cafe**. No stepper, tabs, review or confirmation. `CafeFormModal` is gone; the list's New cafe (`n` too) and a cafe's New cafe for this owner are links. The page sits in the existing sidebar; the frame's Overview, People, Activity and System health items are not built. Page background is `bg-elevated/50` on the panel body (a documented deviation from "no colour override on a page": the owner's frame asks for a gray page).
  - **Owner choice:** two radio cards, **Create new account** (name, email, "Password shown after creation") and **Use existing account** (a bounded server search by name or email, then the chosen identity with the cafes it owns). The summary and the steps follow the choice and never mention a password for an existing account. The result (`CafeCreatedModal`) shows the temporary password once for a new account and "Use your current password" for an existing one.
  - **Existing accounts:** any registered, not disabled account (customers, branch staff, owners of other cafes). `GET /api/platform/accounts?search=` (at least 2 characters, at most 8 results, `tenant: ['create']`; moved to `/api/platform/owner-candidates` by D187) and `GET /api/platform/accounts/{id}` (404 when not eligible; used by `?owner=<id>` from a cafe's page: an id, never a name, email or secret). Eligibility = not `banned` and not `is_anonymous`, enforced when reading **and** in the create batch (`requireEligibleAccount`, a guard statement), so an account disabled or turned into a guest between the check and the write aborts everything (422 `owner.userId`). The server reads the account's own name and email; the client's are ignored (the schema drops them). `GET /api/platform/owners` (every owner) is removed. A search is a scan of `user` with `instr` (D183), bounded by the limit: no index serves "contains".
  - **Create new account with an email that has an account** is 409 `ACCOUNT_EXISTS` on `owner.email` (replaces `OWNER_EXISTS`): checked before planning, after planning (an account created in between) and on a unique violation of the insert (an account created during the write). The page keeps every value and offers **Use existing account**, which switches the card and chooses the account with that email. An email that belongs to a disabled or guest account is 422 on `owner.email` (there is nothing to choose instead).
  - **Address check:** `GET /api/platform/slug-availability?slug=` answers from the address history (current and former addresses); only a slug that passes the address rules is asked, 300 ms after typing settles; each answer names its slug and is used only while that is still the typed one (a late answer for an older address never shows; `useApiQuery` also cancels the older request). Advisory: the atomic create still resolves a taken address (409 `SLUG_TAKEN`). A known-taken address stops the submit with the field error and no request.
  - **Submitting:** `useMutation` (`platform:create-cafe`, key = address: a double submit is skipped) behind a `saving` flag; Ctrl/⌘+Enter while saving does nothing more. The page may be left while saving (no question: `paused: saving`): the result still opens over the page the person is on (the only place the password can be read), and a failure after leaving offers **Reopen**, which returns to the page with the input (held in memory for that one click, no password, cleared with the toast on a session change). Cancel and a saved form go back to the list with its search and filters when the page was opened from it (`history.state.back`).
  - **A11y/UI:** `bg-primary/10` made a checked radio card's `text-muted` description 4.1:1 (under AA): `radioGroup.slots.description` is `text-toned` in `app.config.ts` (every radio group). On a phone the name field is not auto-focused: the keyboard would hide the bar (`BottomActionBar` steps aside while a field has focus).
- **Alternatives:** keeping the modal (the owner approved a page); a custom summary or bar (the shared bar and Nuxt UI cards are used); excluding super admins from the existing-account search (an authorization policy nobody decided then: QT4, answered by D190: they may be owners); loading every account into the browser (refused: bounded server search); a hard block on an address the check can't confirm (the check is advisory).
- **Verified:** see [progress](progress.md#create-a-cafe-one-page-d185-2026-10-19).
- **Where:** `shared/contracts/tenants.ts`, `server/features/tenants/`, `server/features/identity/staff.*`, `server/api/platform/{accounts,slug-availability.get.ts}`, `app/features/platform/`, `app/pages/platform/cafes/new.vue`.

### D186: The platform console manages the container, never the contents, 2026-10-19

- **Context:** the console created, renamed and paused cafes (D142, D185) but couldn't act on accounts or a cafe's ownership, and showed no platform activity or health. The owner asked for a fuller console and accepted the recommendations ("go with your recommendations", 2026-10-19). Research: comparable platforms grant support access only with the customer's consent and for a limited time; impersonation needs step-up auth, a banner and an audit trail.
- **Decision:** phase P, [plan](plans/platform-console.md): P1 Accounts (list, account page, ban/unban with a reason and optional end, sign out everywhere, temporary password; 2FA for super admins), P2 cafe owners (add, remove, transfer, at least one active owner kept in the batch; internal notes), P3 Overview and Activity (counts only; `platform.*` audit rows), P4 System health (read-only, no secrets), P5 consent-based support access after QP1.
  - The D134 boundary stands: the platform acts on cafes, accounts and ownership; never reads a cafe's menu, orders, customers' profiles or reports.
  - Platform writes go through our routes (one batch, guards, version, audit row); the `superadmin` role loses Better Auth's `user`/`session` admin grants in P1 so nothing bypasses the audit.
  - Banning an owner doesn't pause their cafe (a cafe without an active owner is shown as such); a super admin may add, remove or transfer ownership alone with a required reason; the account page shows the cafes an account works in, never the cafes a customer ordered from; no account or cafe deletion.
- **Alternatives:** Better Auth's admin endpoints called from the browser (no audit row in the same write, no version guard); plain `impersonateUser` for support (acts as the person in all their cafes, no consent); ban also pausing the owner's cafe (punishes the cafe's customers and staff for one account); delete instead of ban/pause (loses history; orders and ledgers are never deleted).
- **Where:** [plan](plans/platform-console.md); built in P1–P4.

### D187: The console's Accounts: ban, sign out, temporary password, through our routes only, 2026-10-19

- **Context:** step P1 of the [platform console plan](plans/platform-console.md) (D186). The platform team could create cafes but not act on an account: an abusive customer, an owner locked out while email can't be sent (Q4), a lost device. The `superadmin` role held Better Auth's `user`/`session` admin grants, so its endpoints could ban or change a password without our audit row or version guard. The owner skipped the mockup round.
- **Decision:**
  - **Accounts** in the console's sidebar: `/platform/accounts` lists every registered account (guests never), newest first, searched by name or email (`instr`, D183), with tabs Owners, Staff, Customers, Super admins, Banned (no counts: each would scan `user`). `/platform/accounts/{id}`: email and verified, joined, open sessions and the latest activity among them, the cafes it works in (owner or staff, from memberships; "Only owner" when no other owner can sign in), never the cafes a customer orders from (D186).
  - **Actions**, each one batch with the audit row (`tenant_id` null; `platform.account.ban`, `.unban`, `.sign-out`, `.password.temporary`; the reason in metadata, never a password): **Ban** (a required reason, until unbanned or 1/7/30 days; sessions deleted; refused while banned), **Unban** (asks first), **Sign out everywhere** (asks first; no version: nothing on the account changes), **Set temporary password** (a required reason; shown once, as D115; sessions deleted; refused for a banned account). Ban, unban and the password send the version (409 when stale); ban and the password are also guarded in the batch on "still registered, not a super admin, not banned", so a role granted by hand meanwhile, or a ban, is not missed even when the version didn't move.
  - **Never** on one's own account (409 `OWN_ACCOUNT`) or another super admin's (409 `PLATFORM_ACCOUNT`; unbanning one is allowed); the page shows them without actions.
  - **Banning an owner** keeps their cafe running (D186); the ban dialog names each cafe left without an owner who can sign in.
  - **Permissions:** platform statement `account: ['read', 'ban', 'sign-out', 'reset-password']`, granted to `superadmin`; Better Auth's `user` / `session` statements granted to nobody (a test calls `banUser`, `revokeUserSessions`, `setUserPassword` and `impersonateUser` as a super admin and sees each refused). The owner picker's search (D185) moved from `/api/platform/accounts` to `/api/platform/owner-candidates` so the management list takes the plain name.
  - 2FA for super admins, planned with P1, is its own step (P1b): it needs Better Auth's `twoFactor` tables, enrolment and a sign-in challenge.
- **Alternatives:** Better Auth's endpoints from the browser (no audit in the same write, no version); a date-and-time picker for the ban's end (four choices cover the cases); tab counts (five scans of `user` per visit); listing a customer's cafes (order data, D186).
- **Verified:** see [progress](progress.md#platform-console-p1-accounts-d187-2026-10-19).
- **Where:** `shared/contracts/accounts.ts`, `server/features/identity/accounts.*`, `identity.permissions.ts`, `server/api/platform/{accounts,owner-candidates}/`, `app/features/platform/{components/Account*,composables/useAccounts.ts,schemas/account-form.ts}`, `app/pages/platform/accounts/`, `app/layouts/platform.vue`.

### D188: Two-step sign-in, required for super admins, asked for at every sign-in, 2026-10-19

- **Context:** step P1b of the [platform console plan](plans/platform-console.md) (D186: "super admins sign in with 2FA"). With P1 a super admin can ban accounts and set passwords, so a stolen password alone must not open the console. Better Auth's `twoFactor` plugin makes 2FA a property of the **account**: once on, every password sign-in answers `twoFactorRedirect` and no session. Super admins can also own a cafe (QT4, allowed by D190; the seed's first owner is both), so the admin, counter and customer sign-ins would have stranded them.
- **Decision:**
  - Better Auth `twoFactor({ issuer: 'NUK Platform' })`: TOTP from an authenticator app and ten backup codes; Better Auth's limits (a 10-minute challenge, 5 tries, a 15-minute lock after 10 wrong codes); no "trust this device" (sessions already last 7 days). Migration `0041_two_factor` (the `two_factor` table, `user.two_factor_enabled`), additive.
  - **Required for the console:** `authorizePlatform` refuses 403 `TWO_FACTOR_REQUIRED` after the temporary-password check; `GET /api/platform/me` answers with `twoFactorEnabled`, and the console's route middleware sends every page to `/platform/two-factor` until it's on: the password, then the QR code (and its key to type), the backup codes shown once (copy, download), and a code from the app.
  - **Turning it on signs the account out on its other devices** (an after-hook on `/two-factor/verify-totp` when the session was swapped): a session from the password alone mustn't count as two-step.
  - **Every sign-in asks for the code:** the console's, a cafe's admin and counter, and the customer account's. Their password step returns `'signed-in' | 'two-factor'`; on `two-factor` the page shows the shared `<TwoFactorCodeForm>` (6 digits, or "Use a backup code instead"; a timed-out or over-tried challenge goes back to the password, saying why), then `finishSignIn` / `finishLogin` does what a password-only sign-in did. Shared root pieces: `app/utils/two-factor.ts`, `app/components/TwoFactorCodeForm.vue`.
  - **Recovery** for a super admin who lost the app and the backup codes: by hand in D1, by another team member (operations → Super admins).
- **Alternatives:** the code step only on the console's sign-in (super admins who own a cafe locked out of its admin); a separate super admin account per person (QT4 undecided then; D190: a super admin may be an owner); SMS or email codes (no email domain yet, Q4; SMS is phishable); "trust this device for 30 days" (more state for little gain with 7-day sessions).
- **Limit:** signing in from a guest's browser moves the guest's orders to the account on the password step (Better Auth's anonymous plugin); with 2FA that step makes no session, so they don't move. Only 2FA accounts (super admins) are affected.
- **Verified:** see [progress](progress.md#platform-console-p1b-two-step-sign-in-d188-2026-10-19).
- **Where:** `server/features/identity/{identity.auth.ts,identity.service.ts,identity.errors.ts}`, migration `0041_two_factor`, `shared/contracts/tenants.ts` (`PlatformSession.twoFactorEnabled`), `app/utils/two-factor.ts`, `app/components/TwoFactorCodeForm.vue`, `app/features/platform/{components/PlatformTwoFactorPage.vue,composables/usePlatformSession.ts}`, `app/middleware/platform.global.ts`, the four sign-in pages and their composables.

### D189: A cafe's owners and internal notes in the console: add, remove, transfer, one batch each, 2026-10-19

- **Context:** step P2 of the [platform console plan](plans/platform-console.md) (D186). A cafe whose owners are all banned or unreachable kept running with nobody able to change its menu, staff or payments, and the platform team couldn't fix its ownership or keep a contact for it. The owner decided earlier that a super admin may add, remove or transfer ownership alone, with a required reason (D186), and skipped the mockup round.
- **Decision:**
  - **On a cafe's page** (`/platform/cafes/{id}`), the Owners card has **Add owner** and, per owner, **Transfer ownership** and **Remove owner** (with Open account and New cafe for this owner). Add and Transfer choose an existing account with the D185 owner search (registered, not banned) or create a new account whose temporary password is shown once; each asks for a reason (required, at most 500 characters) kept in the audit row's metadata, never shown to the cafe.
  - **Routes**, each `requirePlatformPermission` with the new platform statement `owner: ['add', 'remove', 'transfer']`: `POST /api/platform/tenants/{id}/owners`, `POST …/owners/{userId}/remove`, `POST …/owners/{userId}/transfer`; each answers `{ tenant, temporaryPassword }`. Audit rows in the cafe's history by the super admin: `tenant.owner.add` (`ownerId`, `newAccount`, `reason`), `tenant.owner.remove` (`ownerId`, `staysStaff`, `reason`), `tenant.owner.transfer` (`fromId`, `toId`, `newAccount`, `staysStaff`, `reason`).
  - **One batch each:** the cafe's version (409 when stale; every ownership change moves it), each existing account's version and, for an account made owner, its eligibility (`requireEligibleAccount`, so a ban meanwhile aborts), the membership change, and `requireAnOwner` after a removal: the cafe always keeps an owner who can sign in, also against a removal in the cafe's own admin or a ban landing meanwhile. Remove is refused (409 `LAST_OWNER`) when no other owner can sign in, also for a banned owner of a cafe with no active owner: transfer then.
  - **Removing** keeps the person's branch roles (they stay a `member`) or leaves no access here, and ends every session of theirs, as disabling staff does. **Adding** keeps an existing account's password, branch roles and other cafes, and gives it a customer profile here (D137). **Transfer** = add + remove in the same batch, so it works from a banned owner.
  - **Never one's own ownership** (409 `OWN_ACCOUNT`, add or remove): ownership is one's own role, which the plan's self-protection leaves to another super admin; adding oneself would also be a way into a cafe's contents without its owner (D186's consent rule). Another super admin may be made an owner (D190).
  - **No active owner:** `TenantSummary.activeOwners` (owners not banned); a cafe with 0 shows a warning with Add owner on its page and a "No active owner" badge on the Cafes list, whose **Owner** filter (`?owner=none`) lists them (a semi-join over the owners' memberships).
  - **Internal notes** per cafe: contact person, phone (E.164, D127) and notes (2,000 characters), in the platform's own table `tenant_notes` (migration `0042_tenant_notes`, additive), not on `organization`, whose fields Better Auth's organization endpoints would answer to the cafe's members. `PUT /api/platform/tenants/{id}/notes` (`notes: ['update']`) at the notes' own version (0 before the first save; a second first save fails on the key), so a pause doesn't conflict with a note; the audit row `tenant.notes.update` names the fields changed, never the values. Returned in `TenantDetail.notes`.
- **Alternatives:** reusing `planStaffCreate` (it replaces branch roles and writes a `staff.create` row for an existing member); a version per account only (two removals at once in the console would then race, and the cafe's other actions wouldn't see ownership change); notes as `organization` fields (visible to the cafe through Better Auth); an ownership change that also unbans or pauses (ban and pause stay separate, D186); hiding Remove for a banned owner (shown, disabled with "transfer instead").
- **Verified:** see [progress](progress.md#platform-console-p2-cafe-owners-d189-2026-10-19).
- **Where:** `shared/contracts/tenants.ts`, `server/features/tenants/{tenants.service,tenants.repository,tenants.errors,tenants.schema}.ts`, `server/features/identity/{owners.service,staff.repository,identity.permissions}.ts`, `server/api/platform/tenants/[tenantId]/{owners,notes.put.ts}`, `app/features/platform/{components/CafeOwnerModal,CafeOwnerRemoveModal,CafeNotesModal,CafeDetailPage,CafesListPage}.vue`, `app/features/platform/schemas/owner-form.ts`.

### D190: A super admin may be a cafe's owner (QT4 answered), 2026-10-19

- **Context:** QT4 asked whether a super admin may be a cafe's owner: the platform team never reads a cafe's data (D134), but an account that is both (the seed's first owner) gets both. Create a cafe's "Use existing account" (D185) allowed it until decided, and P2's ownership changes (D189) made it pressing.
- **Decision (owner, 2026-10-19):** keep allowing it. A super admin's account can be chosen as a cafe's first owner (D185), added as an owner or receive a transfer (D189); the console shows it like any owner. It then holds the cafe's owner role through its membership, as any owner, and its two-step sign-in (D188) applies to the cafe's admin too.
- **Limits that stay:** a super admin never changes their **own** ownership from the console (D189: another super admin does it, audited with a reason); platform permissions still grant nothing inside a cafe (security → Roles and permissions).
- **Alternatives:** excluding super admins from the owner search and refusing them on the server (separate accounts per role: more accounts for a small team, and the seed's first owner would need splitting).
- **Where:** QT4 removed from the [open questions](progress.md#open-questions--waiting-on-others); D185, D188 and the [plan](plans/platform-console.md) point here.

### D191: The console's Overview and Activity: counts, and the platform's own audit rows by action, 2026-10-19

- **Context:** step P3 of the [platform console plan](plans/platform-console.md) (D186). The console opened on the Cafes list and showed neither how the platform was doing nor what the platform team had done. The audit log holds both the platform's rows and every cafe's own (menu, staff, orders), and some cafe rows use the `tenant.*` names too (an owner's `tenant.profile.update`), so the action prefix alone can't tell them apart. The owner skipped the mockup round.
- **Decision:**
  - **Overview** at `/platform` (the console's home; the Cafes list moved to `/platform/cafes`, and an old `/platform?…` list link opens it with its filters). Counts only, one read (`GET /api/platform/overview`, `tenant: ['read']` and `account: ['read']`): cafes active and paused, new this month (from the 1st at midnight in the platform's zone, `NUXT_PUBLIC_CAFE_TIME_ZONE`), active cafes with no order placed in 7 days (per cafe on `orders_tenant_placed_idx`; never the orders), cafes with no active owner (D189), registered accounts banned. "Needs attention" links each non-zero count to the list that shows it.
  - **Activity** at `/platform/activity` (`GET /api/platform/activity`, the new platform statement `activity: ['read']`): the audit rows whose action is one the console writes, `PLATFORM_ACTIONS` in `shared/contracts/platform-activity.ts` (`tenant.create`, `.suspend`, `.resume`, `.slug.change`, `.owner.add|remove|transfer`, `.notes.update`, `platform.account.*`, `platform.superadmin.grant`). The list of actions is the boundary, required by the platform repository's `listAuditEvents`, so a cafe's own rows never match, whoever wrote them (an owner who is also a super admin, D190, included). Newest first, paged, filtered by who (the super admins), what (one action), when (Any time, Today, Last 7 and 30 days, This month; the API takes `from`/`to` dates in the platform's zone, both included) and cafe (`?tenantId=`, a chip to clear). Each entry names the actor (or the system), the cafe, the account acted on and a transfer's previous owner, and only these metadata keys: reason, an address's from/to, a ban's end, the notes fields changed (never values), new account, kept branch roles.
  - **A cafe's page** lists the latest five platform actions on it, with **See all** opening Activity for that cafe.
  - The sidebar is Overview, Cafes, Accounts, Activity.
- **Alternatives:** filtering by action prefix (lets `tenant.profile.update` and other cafe rows in) or by the actor being a super admin now (a role changes; an owner who is also a super admin would bring their cafe's own edits); a calendar range for dates (the reports' picker is that feature's own; presets cover the cases, the API already takes any range); a cafe search in Activity's toolbar (opened from the cafe's page instead; the Cafes list finds a cafe); recent activity on the Overview (the plan keeps it to counts).
- **Verified:** see [progress](progress.md#platform-console-p3-overview-and-activity-d191-2026-10-19).
- **Where:** `shared/contracts/platform-activity.ts`, `server/features/tenants/activity.service.ts`, `server/features/platform/platform.repository.ts` (`listAuditEvents`), `server/features/identity/accounts.*` (`countBannedAccounts`, `accountIdentities`), `server/features/orders` (`tenantsOrderingRecently`), `server/api/platform/{overview,activity}.get.ts`, `app/features/platform/{components/PlatformOverviewPage,PlatformActivityPage,CafeDetailPage}.vue`, `app/features/platform/{composables/useActivity.ts,schemas/activity.ts}`, `app/pages/platform/{index,activity,cafes/index}.vue`, `app/layouts/platform.vue`.

### D192: The console's System health: read-only counts and dates, and a record of every job's run, 2026-10-19

- **Context:** step P4 of the [platform console plan](plans/platform-console.md) (D186). The platform team couldn't see whether messages were going out, whether scheduled jobs ran, which cafes' Telegram messages failed or whose Bakong token was about to expire; only one job (`notifications:generate`) recorded its progress, so a job that never ran (a missing cron) looked the same as a healthy one. The owner skipped the mockup round.
- **Decision:**
  - **`/platform/health`** (sidebar **System health**; `GET /api/platform/health`, the new platform statement `health: ['read']`), read-only, a snapshot with Refresh: a summary of what needs a look, then four cards, each cafe linking to its page:
    - **Scheduled jobs:** every job in `SCHEDULED_TASKS` (moved to `shared/scheduled-tasks.ts`, which `nuxt.config.ts` imports, so the list can't drift from the crons), with its schedule, last success, last failure, failures in a row and a state (OK, Failing, No run recorded).
    - **Outbox:** unsent messages per kind and cafe (`null`: the platform's emails): waiting (not tried), retrying (failed a try), given up, and the oldest. Sent messages aren't read.
    - **Telegram:** cafes whose deliveries failed in the last 7 days: given up, retrying, the newest failing one.
    - **Bakong tokens:** each saved token's expiry date and state (Expired, Expires soon within `BAKONG_TOKEN_WARNING_DAYS`, OK, No expiry date), never the token (D148).
  - **No secret in any answer:** each feature's query selects only counts and dates (never a payload, a message, a chat id or title, an error's text or a sealed token), and the service builds the answer from those fields alone; a test puts marker strings in every such column and checks none appears in the JSON. Error texts are left out on purpose: they can carry an email address or a URL with a token.
  - **Job runs:** a new table `job_runs` (migration `0043_job_runs`, additive): per job, the last run, last success, last failure and failures in a row, written by `runJob` after each run. A failure is always written; a success only when the last one is 5 minutes old or a failure came between (an upsert's `WHERE`), so the four every-minute jobs cost about 1,150 row writes a day, not 5,760 (D1's free plan allows 100,000). A failed write is logged and never fails the job.
- **Alternatives:** returning error texts (useful, but can hold personal data or a token: counts and dates point to Workers Logs instead); writing every success (a write per job per minute); reusing `job_cursors` (its row is a paging cursor: writing runs into it would reset the cursor); per-cafe outbox detail on the cafe's page (P4 keeps it in one place, linked by cafe).
- **Verified:** see [progress](progress.md#platform-console-p4-system-health-d192-2026-10-19).
- **Where:** `shared/contracts/platform-health.ts`, `shared/scheduled-tasks.ts`, `server/features/tenants/health.service.ts`, `server/features/platform/{platform.schema,platform.repository}.ts` (`job_runs`, `recordJobRun`, `outboxHealth`), `server/features/notifications` (`telegramDeliveryHealth`), `server/utils/jobs.ts` (`runJob`), `server/api/platform/health.get.ts`, `app/features/platform/{components/PlatformHealthPage.vue,schemas/health.ts}`, `app/pages/platform/health.vue`.

### D193: Support access: the owner allows it, a super admin reads with a fresh code, read-only, every request audited, 2026-10-19

- **Context:** step P5 of the [platform console plan](plans/platform-console.md) (D186). The platform team could manage cafes but never see what an owner sees (D134), so it couldn't help with "my menu looks wrong". QP1 (the support process) was open; the owner answered "use the defaults" (2026-10-19): read-only, 24 hours, granted by the cafe's owner in the admin, who sees it and can end it; and, asked further, any super admin may open it; no mockup round. Comparable platforms grant support access only with the customer's consent, for a limited time, with step-up auth, a banner and an audit trail.
- **Decision:**
  - **The owner allows it:** **Admin → Platform support** (`/admin/support`, owners only: tenant statement `support: ['grant']`), **Allow for 24 hours** (asks first) and **End now**; one grant at a time (a guard in the batch: two clicks or two owners make one grant). History of the last ten grants: who allowed and ended each, and who from the platform team opened the cafe and when. While a grant is on, every owner sees a strip above the admin ("Platform support can look at this cafe until …", Manage). Audit rows `support.grant`, `support.end` in the cafe's history.
  - **A super admin opens it:** the console's cafe page shows Support access; with a grant, **Open read-only** asks for a fresh 6-digit code from the authenticator app (`POST /api/platform/tenants/{id}/support/open`, platform statement `support: ['open']`), checked by Better Auth's `verifyTOTP` against the signed-in session. That check has no attempt limit when signed in, so at most 5 wrong codes per super admin in 15 minutes (counted from `tenant.support.code-failed` audit rows; 429 after). A backup code doesn't open it. A **visit** (`support_visits`) is bound to that Better Auth session, lasts at most 60 minutes and never past the grant; then a full page load into `/c/<slug>/admin`. Audit `tenant.support.open` / `.close` (in Activity). **One visit can be shut out (follow-up, 2026-10-20):** the History shows "Looking now" on a visit still open, with **End visit** (asks first; `POST /api/c/<slug>/admin/support/visits/{visitId}/end`, `support: ['grant']`): that visit closes, so its next request is refused, while the grant and the other visits stay and the platform team can open the cafe again with a fresh code (end the grant to stop that). One guarded batch (cafe, id, still open) with audit `support.visit.end`; unknown, closed, expired or another cafe's is 404. **The app stops edits before they start (same day):** during a visit `useMutation` asks nothing and sends nothing ("Nothing was changed", "Platform support can only look at this cafe"), so no confirmation dialog ends in a refused save; the server stays the guard. Edit buttons are still shown (the app has no per-permission hiding yet). **Owners are told (follow-up, 2026-10-20):** opening a visit queues a Telegram message "Platform support opened your cafe" (who, until when, **Open Platform support** button) to the cafe's chats with **Server errors and reminders** on (the `server_error` switch, as the Bakong reminders, D132: no new kind or migration), one per chat and visit, sent right after the answer; a failure is logged and never fails the visit.
  - **Read-only, enforced on the server:** `requirePermission` runs as before; only when it refuses (403 `FORBIDDEN`) does it look for this session's open visit, under a grant still in force, for a super admin with two-step sign-in. With one: a non-GET request is 403 `SUPPORT_READ_ONLY`; a GET is allowed only for the support permissions (`menu:read`, `branch:read|update`, `staff:read`, `report:read`, `settings:manage`, `audit:read`; on GET they only read), so report exports, the assistant, uploads, sample data and the Platform support page itself stay refused. Every allowed request writes `support.request` (visit, method, path without its query) in the cafe's audit history; the actor is `role: 'support'`. The cafe's own people are untouched (no extra query on their path).
  - **The admin app:** `/admin/me` answers a support visit with `role: 'support'`, the read permissions and when it ends; a strip above every page says "Platform support view: read-only until …" with **Leave** (closes the visit, back to the console). The admin's edit buttons still show (the app doesn't hide by permission yet): a save is refused with "Support access is read-only".
  - **Tables:** `support_grants`, `support_visits` (migration `0044_support_access`, additive; visits reference their grant within the cafe).
- **Alternatives:** Better Auth's `impersonateUser` (acts as the owner in every cafe they own, no consent, no time limit; D186 refused it); taking access without the owner (the boundary D134/D186); a separate read-only copy of the admin's screens for the console (duplicates every page); a grant naming one super admin (the owner chose any); recording only the opening (the plan asks for every request); write access (the default is read-only).
- **Limits:** the admin still shows edit controls, but a click now explains itself instead of failing on the server; no email to the owners when someone opens it (waits for Q4); the Telegram message goes to the chats with operational alerts on, which an owner may not have set up (the strip and the history still show it).
- **Verified:** see [progress](progress.md#platform-console-p5-support-access-d193-2026-10-19).
- **Where:** `shared/contracts/support.ts`, `server/features/support/`, `server/utils/access.ts` (`requirePermission`, `supportRequestOf`), `server/api/c/[slug]/admin/{me.get.ts,support/}`, `server/api/platform/tenants/[tenantId]/support/`, `app/features/support/`, `app/layouts/default.vue`, `app/features/platform/components/{CafeDetailPage,CafeSupportOpenModal}.vue`, `server/features/assistant/help/support.md`.

### D194: Repositories keep Drizzle's query builder; one repository standard, 2026-10-20

- **Context:** the owner asked for a server code standard, starting with repositories, and whether Drizzle's newer query style should replace the code in `platform.repository.ts`. The code uses Drizzle's query builder (`db.select/insert/update/delete`, `db.batch`), which Drizzle 1.0 keeps unchanged; the newer style is the relational query API v2 (`defineRelations()`, `db.query.<table>.findMany({ where: { … } })`). On 2026-10-20, `drizzle-orm@latest` is 0.45.4 and 1.0 is a release candidate (`1.0.0-rc.4`); 1.0 removes relational queries v1. NuxtHub 0.10.8 builds the database as `drizzle(binding, { schema })` and doesn't pass `relations`; support is nuxt-hub/core PR #825 (open since 2026-02-09). An audit of the 25 repositories followed ([report](reviews/repository-audit-2026-10-20.md)).
- **Decision:**
  - **The query builder stays** for every repository. No relational queries (v1 is removed in 1.0; v2 needs Drizzle 1.0 and NuxtHub support) and no raw statements (D53). **Revisit** v2 for nested reads when Drizzle 1.0 is `latest` and NuxtHub passes `relations`; writes stay on the builder (guards, conditional updates and upserts have no relational form).
  - **One repository standard**, [docs/server/repositories.md](server/repositories.md): file sections; names and parameter order; explicit return types; every function on a cafe table filters that table's own `tenant_id`, or says "Across cafes:" and is a task, webhook, platform-console or own-account read; named column lists, no casts; several reads for one answer in one `db.batch`; builder operators, `sql` only for what they lack, with interpolated columns and no hand-encoded times; a condition defined once and reused by guards; writes return statements, and the only executed writes are single conditional background-task statements.
  - **Building blocks, not a generic repository** (amended the same day): no `BaseRepository`, no `findOne(table, id)` / `create(table, values)` for any table, no query-parameter filter language: they would make the tenant, version and status conditions optional, lose result types and run writes outside the service's batch. A query shape repeated in two or more features becomes a small typed helper (`server/utils/query.ts`, `server/utils/batch.ts`, or `shared/utils/` when the app uses it too); a feature's own data rules are conditions exported by its `index.ts`. The planned catalogue is in [repositories.md → Shared helpers](server/repositories.md#shared-helpers).
  - **Official documentation first:** a library API is used as its current official docs describe, checked against the installed version's types. This replaced two helpers of our own with Drizzle's documented `.$dynamic()` pagination and `db.$count` (guards count with `requireCount(db, db.$count(table, condition), n)`; `$count` isn't a batch item, so a page's total stays a `select({ total: count() })` in the page's batch).
  - **Existing code migrates in steps** (the report's clean-up plan), all on branch `claude/repository-standard` until the repository standard is finished: shared helpers, then orders (money first, the owner), menu, identity and cafes, messages, the rest. New and changed repository code follows the standard now.
  - **Step 2, the shared helpers (built the same day):** `server/utils/query.ts` (`inTenant`, `byId`, `atVersion`, `withStatus`, `searchIn`, `byName`, `firstRow`, `withPage`, `readPage`, `hasAny`, `nextPosition`), `server/utils/batch.ts` (`updateAtVersion`, `insertAll`, `replaceAll`, `deleteOldest`), `shared/utils/collections.ts` (`groupBy`) and `shared/contracts/common.ts` (`toPage`, `slicePage`). Names say what they do because Nitro auto-imports `server/utils` into routes (`firstRow`, `readPage`, not `first`, `page`). `countBy` was dropped: most of its uses join and read in chunks, and what's left is one line. ESLint refuses result casts, raw statements and hand-written times in repositories, except the ones the later steps clean (`REPOSITORIES_TO_CLEAN`).
  - **Step 3, orders (same day):** the orders, reports and KHQR repositories follow the standard (audit plan step 3). Branch lookups for another feature are the branches feature's `findBranchClock` and `listActiveBranchClocks` (id, name, time zone), not a second query in orders. Because the services check the parent first, a removed repository filter fails no service test: the second layer is proven by repository-level two-cafe tests (the standard's Tests section now asks for them), and every batch read that joins tables by a D1-shaped test, which caught a D170 collision in the new order-history batch before it shipped.
  - **Step 4, menu (same day):** the nine menu repositories follow the standard. A rule is defined once in its owner's file and imported by the others in the feature (`listedItem` in items, `activeCategory` and `childrenOf` in categories); the item counts categories made of `menu_items` moved to items, so no two files query one table for the same thing. A condition with a subquery is a function of `db` (`sellable(db)`), because Drizzle's standalone `QueryBuilder` would need the snake_case setting repeated. Reads used as batch items keep their inferred builder types (the catalog's twelve). The menu item list's page and total now share a batch, and a D1-shaped test showed the category's `name` would have replaced the item's on D1 (D170): it is `category_name`.
  - **Step 5, identity and cafes (same day):** identity, accounts, staff, tenants, branches and customers follow the standard. Five missing cafe filters were added to branch hours and dining-table reads/writes; each separately fails the repository-level two-cafe tests when removed. Identity owns and exports `accountCanSignIn`, `registeredAccount`, `ownerCanSignIn(db)` and `hasActiveOwner(db, cafeIdColumn)`; tenants owns `activeCafe` and the sole cafe lookups/enumeration; branches owns `activeBranch` and active-branch lists. Tenants asks identity's condition for ownerless cafes instead of reading identity's private columns/tables. The membership-role lookup is defined once in identity. Legacy null flags/status and the oldest-cafe fallback (D140) retain their meaning. Account/staff/cafe pages read their totals in the same batch; account workspace roles share a joined batch, checked by `fakeD1` and real D1; an account's cafe list reads in chunks instead of querying once per cafe. Guards use `$count`, with the same shared conditions as reads. The three step-5 lint exemptions and all six result casts are gone. The existing SQL-plan tests now inspect batch items, because neither driver logs those statements. API use checked against installed Drizzle 0.45.3 types and the official [select](https://orm.drizzle.team/docs/select), [operators](https://orm.drizzle.team/docs/operators), [batch](https://orm.drizzle.team/docs/sqlite/batch-api) and [$count](https://orm.drizzle.team/docs/query-utils#count) documentation. Stop after this local commit for owner review; no push or PR.
  - **Step 6, messages (same day):** notification destinations/rules, links, deliveries and customer-ready work have separate repositories; marketing channels, links and posts likewise. Sixteen missing tenant predicates now protect id/deduplication reads, conditional transitions, upsert conflicts, claims and settlements, and each fails a repository two-cafe assertion when removed. `server/utils/claims.ts` supplies the shared atomic token/attempt fields, due/lease conditions and token ownership; all four queues return `WorkClaim` fields and claim with `until, now`. State, lease and subscription rules remain with each owner, including global outbox work and legacy null subscriptions. `server/utils/connect-codes.ts` shares typed cafe/id/state/strict-expiry transitions; cancellation still permits expired waiting/confirm codes. Writes return statements; the service's `tryGuardedBatch` owns code transitions and delivery success plus destination timestamp. Rule/retry audit is atomic with the change; retries guard the still-failed state. Telegram overview uses one joined read batch, with `fakeD1` and real-D1 decoding cases. Growing lists use chunks, link cleanup is bounded, and marketing connected links remain. Both feature lint exemptions are removed; platform's remaining cleanup waits for step 7. Checked against installed Drizzle 0.45.3 types and official [updates](https://orm.drizzle.team/docs/update), [upserts](https://orm.drizzle.team/docs/guides/upsert), [operators](https://orm.drizzle.team/docs/operators), [column selection](https://orm.drizzle.team/docs/select) and [SQLite batch](https://orm.drizzle.team/docs/sqlite/batch-api) docs. Stop after this local commit for review; no push or PR.
  - **Step 7, the rest (2026-10-21):** platform, media, support, assistant and sample-data finish the seven-step cleanup. Platform splits audit, outbox and jobs from idempotency; media splits asset rows from durable object work. The missing variant-key and assistant-completion tenant predicates are added, and completion now requires its cafe. Services own all write batches: media expiry/reset deletion with durable object work, provisional plans before R2, acknowledgement/retry, assistant completion, sample-run release and job cursor/run updates. Media uses `alias`/`exists` with shared typed expiry conditions; support and quota guards use `db.$count`. Joined source/file and support-history reads share batches; audit rows and totals share a batch, retaining the offset/limit API rather than converting arbitrary offsets to fractional pages. All results have checked return types and named columns. ID lists and provisional writes use chunks. `inJsonArray(column, strings)` extracts the catalog's existing JSON-bound membership condition for audit action/outbox kind sets, which can intersect another growing ID list without exceeding D1's bind limit. It uses documented [Drizzle operators](https://orm.drizzle.team/docs/operators) with SQLite's [json_each](https://www.sqlite.org/json1.html#jeach); there is no client-defined filter language. Aliases, insert-select/upserts, counts and batches were checked against installed Drizzle 0.45.3 types and official [joins](https://orm.drizzle.team/docs/joins#aliases--selfjoins), [insert](https://orm.drizzle.team/docs/insert), [$count](https://orm.drizzle.team/docs/query-utils#count) and [batch](https://orm.drizzle.team/docs/sqlite/batch-api) documentation. Both image batch predicates and the missing filters fail their two-cafe assertions when made ineffective; 34 isolated mutations cover tenant scope and guards, with source restoration after each. Joined batches have D1-shaped and real-D1 cases. Strict sample-data lease expiry, support expiry, quota counting, idempotency scope, global outbox work, object retention and job success throttling retain their semantics. The final three lint exemptions and exemption mechanism are removed. Stop after one local commit for review; no new push or PR requested.
- **Other features' tables (report R7, answered by the owner the same day):** writes only by the owning feature (one documented exception: identity's staff management writes `branch_staff` with the account's memberships in one batch). Another feature may read, in a join, only `user.id`/`.name`, `branches.id`/`.tenantId`/`.name`/`.timezone`/`.status` and `organization.id`/`.slug`/`.name`/`.status`, imported from `server/db/tables.ts`; conditions with business meaning come from the owner's `index.ts`; anything else goes through `index.ts`. Joins were kept over service calls because a separate names read costs a D1 round trip, loses the batch's snapshot and can't sort, filter or guard in SQL. Architecture rule 2 and the data model say so; a lint rule (step 2) refuses importing another feature's `*.schema` file. At the audit revision, reads fit the list except `tenants.noActiveOwner` (`member`, `user.banned`), which was replaced by identity's exported condition in step 5.
- **Alternatives:** moving to Drizzle 1.0 and relational queries v2 now (a release candidate, and NuxtHub's database client would have to be replaced); relational queries v1 on 0.45 (removed in 1.0); a query builder other than Drizzle (NuxtHub, Better Auth and the migrations are built on it).
- **Where:** `docs/server/repositories.md`, `docs/reviews/repository-audit-2026-10-20.md`, `docs/server/architecture.md` (repository layer, rule 2), `docs/server/data-model.md` (ownership), `docs/server/README.md`, `server/db/tables.ts` (owners and readable columns), `server/utils/{query,batch,claims,connect-codes}.ts`, `server/tests/messages.repository.tenants.test.ts`, `server/tests/remaining.repository.tenants.test.ts`, `shared/utils/collections.ts`, `shared/contracts/common.ts`, `eslint.config.mjs`, `server/tests/query.test.ts`, `server/tests/d1-search.test.ts`, `test/unit/shared-utils.test.ts`.

### D195: One date and time style: the day first, the 12-hour clock, 2026-10-21

- **Context:** timestamps were formatted by about twenty local helpers in two styles: day first (`en-GB`: the platform console, support, the date pickers, the KHQR token, form drafts; and, by hand, the reports' periods, promotions' dates and the server's Telegram messages) and month first (`en-US`: orders, Telegram deliveries, promotion posts, channels, the rate history, add-on groups). `en-GB` also shows 24-hour times, "Sept", and a comma after the weekday in current browsers. The owner delegated the choice ("everything that is good for the app").
- **Decision:** dates day first, as in Cambodia and as most screens and every Telegram message already show them ("21 Oct 2026", "21 Oct", "Wed 21 Oct 2026"); times on the 12-hour clock of the rest of the app ("9:05 AM", like `formatClock`); together "21 Oct 2026, 9:05 AM". One set of helpers, `app/utils/date-format.ts`: `formatDate`, `formatShortDate`, `formatTime`, `formatDateTime`, `formatShortDateTime` (an optional `timeZone`) and `formatDay` (a calendar day). The day's parts come from `en-US` (stable "Sep") and are put in day-first order, so the text matches the reports' and Telegram's hand-built dates in every browser. Formatters are built once and reused.
- **Alternatives:** month first everywhere (`en-US`): fewer files would change, but it differs from how dates are written locally, from the reports and promotions, and from the server's messages. `en-GB` as is: "Sept", 24-hour times and a weekday comma, unlike the rest.
- **Consequences:** month-first screens now read day first (orders' "28 Sep", Telegram, promotion posts, channels, the rate history, "Last updated 28 Sep 2026"); the console's times are 12-hour. `clockTime` (orders, counter), `dateOnly`/`dateTime` (console health) and the local `when`/`date`/`time` helpers are gone; `clockIn`/`stampIn` (reports, positional zone) use the shared helpers. **Enforced (same day):** ESLint (`DATE_FORMAT_RESTRICTIONS` in `eslint.config.mjs`) refuses hand-formatted dates in `app/`, scripts and templates; the helpers and `timezones.ts` are exempt, and the two `YYYY-MM-DD` keys say why on their line. Calendar-range text (reports' periods, promotions' `whenText`, `shared/contracts/marketing.ts`) keeps its own month list: already day first, shared with the server. `formatTime` puts a narrow no-break space before AM/PM, as `toLocaleTimeString` did.

### D196: Staff access is one choice; owners will add branches, at most 10 (2026-10-23)

- **Context:** the staff form asked two things at once: an **Admin** switch and branch rows each with a role. An admin's rows stayed on screen though an admin works at every branch, the role sat at the end of each row, and Manager and Staff were never explained. The owner also asked how branches relate to cafes: a cafe (Better Auth organization, D135) is a separate business; a branch is one location of it, sharing the menu, staff and customers. Today only the platform's New cafe (its first branch, D142) and the seed task create a branch; owners hold `branch: ['create']` but no route uses it (branch settings were edit-only, D91).
- **Decision (owner, 2026-10-23):**
  - **Step A (built):** the form asks **Access** first, as card radios: **Admin** (whole cafe, every branch; no branch picker), **Manager** or **Staff** (tick the branches, one role at all of them), and **Different per branch** (today's rows; offered with two branches or more, or when the person already has mixed roles). New people start as Staff. Switching keeps what was entered (`switchAccess`): leaving per-branch rows keeps their branches ticked, entering them keeps each branch's earlier role. Only the shown list is validated ("Pick at least one branch" / "Add at least one branch"). The API body is unchanged (`admin` + `memberships`); an admin is now saved with no branch roles (they had no effect: owners hold every branch permission). A branch that is no longer active but still on the person stays listed, so it can be unticked.
  - **Manager and Staff have the same access today:** the only branch permissions any route checks (`menu:read`/`setSoldOut`, `order:*`, `payment:collect`) are in both roles; tables are admin-only (`branch: ['update']`). The form says so ("For now, the same access as Staff") rather than describing permissions no screen uses. The help page's "manage dining tables" for managers was wrong and is corrected.
  - **Step B (next, its own PR):** owners add branches from the Branches page (`branch: ['create']`), **at most 10 per cafe**, counted inside the insert's batch; later the limit comes from the cafe's subscription (T3/QT2).
- **Alternatives:** keep the switch and rows with better copy (the overlap stays); one role per person only, dropping per-branch roles (needs a server rule and a data change for mixed people); a multi-select for branches (checkboxes show every branch at once, and a cafe has at most 10).
- **Where:** `app/features/staff/schemas/staff-form.ts`, `app/features/staff/components/StaffFormModal.vue`, `server/features/assistant/help/staff.md`; tests `app/features/staff/tests/staff-form.test.ts`, `test/e2e/staff.test.ts`.
- **Edge case found:** a ticked `UCheckbox` inside a labelled `UFormField` took the field's label as its accessible name ("Branches"); each checkbox now has its own `aria-label`. The e2e checks both boxes by their branch names.

### D197: Owners add branches, at most 10 active per cafe, names unique (step B of D196), 2026-10-23

- **Context:** D196 recorded the owner's decision: owners add branches, at most 10, later by subscription. Until now only a new cafe's first branch (D142) and the seed task created one; owners held `branch: ['create']` with no route.
- **Decision:**
  - **`POST /api/c/<slug>/admin/branches`** `{ name, timezone }` (`createBranchSchema`), `requirePermission({ branch: ['create'] })` (owners), 201 with its `BranchSettings`. `createBranch` writes the branch, a guard and the audit row (`branch.create`, name and time zone) in one batch. A new branch is active, has no hours (never open until they are set), no tables and no staff rows (owners work at every branch).
  - **The limit, `MAX_BRANCHES = 10` active branches per cafe,** is a guard in that batch (`activeBranchesAtMost`, `requireAtMost` after the insert), so two owners adding the last branch at once can't both pass (409 `BRANCH_LIMIT`). There's no check before the write: the guard is the one rule. Archived branches don't count; when archiving is built, restoring must use the same guard. Later the cafe's subscription sets the number (T3/QT2).
  - **Names are unique among a cafe's active branches, ignoring case:** a partial unique index (`branches_active_name_idx` on `(tenant_id, lower(name))` where active, migration `0045_branch_names`, additive), like dining tables' labels. Adding or renaming to a used name is 409 `BRANCH_NAME_TAKEN` on `name`. Before applying it, staging must have no two active branches of one cafe with the same name (one branch per cafe so far); the migration fails on a duplicate rather than hiding it.
  - **UI:** **Add branch** (name, time zone starting on the current branch's, else `cafeTimeZone`) on the Branches list when the cafe has several, and on the Branch page when it has one (the list hands a single branch straight to its page, D91, kept). After the save, the new branch's page opens to set its hours. At the limit the list's button is off and the list says why. The list explains what a branch is (shares menu, staff and customers; own hours, tables, counter and orders).
- **Alternatives:** a check before the write (two adds at the limit both pass, like tables' soft cap); counting archived branches (a closed location would use a slot forever); no name rule (two "Main" branches confuse the counter, staff and reports); always showing the list instead of opening the only branch (an extra click for most cafes, and it changes D91's approved flow).
- **Where:** `shared/contracts/branches.ts`, `server/features/branches/{branches.service,branches.repository,branches.errors,branches.schema}.ts`, `server/api/c/[slug]/admin/branches/index.post.ts`, `server/db/migrations/sqlite/0045_branch_names.sql`, `app/features/branches/{components/NewBranchModal.vue,components/BranchListPage.vue,components/BranchPage.vue,composables/useAddBranch.ts,composables/useBranches.ts,schemas/branch-form.ts}`, `server/features/assistant/help/branch.md`; tests `server/features/branches/tests/branches.service.test.ts`, `app/features/branches/tests/branch-form.test.ts`, `test/e2e/branches.test.ts`.

### D198: Manager tools on the counter: tables and QR codes, branch reports (step 1, the server), 2026-10-23

- **Context:** the Manager role (D45) lists `table: ['manage', 'rotateQr']`, `report: ['read']`, `voucher: ['issue']`, `loyalty: ['adjust']` and `audit: ['read']`, but no route used them: managers had the same access as staff (D196). Vouchers and points wait for phase 7 (D115); there is no cafe activity screen.
- **Decision (owner, 2026-10-23, the recommendations accepted, no mockup round):** managers get **dining tables and QR codes** and **reports to view and print** for their branches, **in the counter app** ([plan](plans/manager-tools.md)). The admin portal stays owners only (D52). CSV and Send to Telegram stay with owners (`report: ['export']`), and so do opening hours (`settings: ['manage']`).
  - **Step 1 (this):** routes under `/api/c/<slug>/counter/<branchId>/`: `tables…` (`table: ['manage']`, new QR `table: ['rotateQr']`) on the admin's table services; `reports/branches|summary|items|orders` and `reports/orders/{orderId}` (`report: ['read']`). The branch is always the path's: `readValidQuery` takes `fixed` values that replace the caller's, and `orderHistoryDetail` takes `{ branchId }` (another branch's order is 404).
  - `server/tests/routes.test.ts` now checks that every route under `counter/[branchId]` calls `requireBranchPermission` and never `requirePermission`, and that the tool routes name their permissions and the path's branch.
- **Alternatives:** the admin portal for managers (every owner-shaped page, the search and the assistant would need branch filtering; one miss leaks data); a reports query `branchId` checked against the path instead of replaced (two values to compare, and a forgotten check reads another branch).
- **Where:** `server/api/c/[slug]/counter/[branchId]/{tables,reports}/**`, `server/utils/validation.ts`, `server/features/orders/reports.service.ts`; tests `server/tests/routes.test.ts`, `server/tests/validation.test.ts`, `server/features/identity/tests/identity.service.test.ts`, `server/features/orders/tests/reports.service.test.ts`.
- **Step 2 (2026-10-23):** **Manage** in the counter header for managers and owners (the role at this branch), opening **Dining tables** and **Reports** for that branch. The admin's screens are reused, not copied: the reports feature's pages read a **surface** (`useReportSurface`: API, query keys, links, `exports`), the admin's by default and the counter's provided by `CounterReportsPage`; the tables tab and form take a `surface` prop (overlay modals render outside the page, so they can't inject). On the counter the report header has Back to queue, the reports as tabs and Print; CSV and Send to Telegram aren't shown and their API is never asked. The report period button's accessible name now begins with its visible text (axe, on the admin's reports too). Alternatives: copies of the pages in the counter feature (two versions to keep in step), or exporting the pages from the features (forbidden: pages and forms never leave their feature).
- **Step 3 (2026-10-23):** the staff form's Manager reads "Everything Staff do, plus their branches' dining tables, QR codes and reports" (D196's "For now, the same access as Staff" no longer holds), and the Staff help page lists what a manager can and can't do. Manager tools are complete; vouchers and points join them with phase 7 (D115).

### D199: Pause online orders: a branch state set from the counter, never past closing (step 1, the server), 2026-10-23

- **Context:** online orders follow the weekly hours (D45). Stopping them now (a broken machine, a rush) meant an owner editing the hours: permanent, owners only, slow at the counter. Branch settings deferred "pause orders" (D91).
- **Decision (owner, 2026-10-23, the recommendations accepted, no mockup round; [plan](plans/pause-orders.md)):**
  - Managers and owners pause a branch's online orders from the counter (`branch: ['pause']`, a new branch action; staff don't) for **15 minutes, 30 minutes, an hour or until closing**. The server computes the end from now, capped at today's closing; a closed branch is refused (409 `BRANCH_CLOSED`). No "until I resume": a pause can't outlast the day. Orders already placed carry on.
  - Stored on `branches` (`paused_until`, `paused_by` → user set null, `pause_reason`; migration `0046_branch_pause`, additive). drizzle-kit wrote `paused_by`'s reference without its `ON DELETE SET NULL`; the migration was corrected by hand to match the schema and snapshot, and a test deletes the account that paused.
  - **Outside the branch's `version`:** pause and resume set the state like sold out (D105), so an owner's settings save never conflicts with a pause. The last pause wins. One batch each with the audit row (`branch.pause` with the choice, the end and the reason; `branch.resume`). Resume when not paused changes and audits nothing.
  - **Read, not scheduled:** `toPublicBranch` gives `pausedUntil` only while it's ahead and the branch is open; `orderProblems` answers `ORDERING_PAUSED` ("Riverside isn't taking online orders right now. Back at 3:30 PM.") after `BRANCH_CLOSED` and before last orders, so the quote and placing an order (409 `ORDERING_CLOSED`) refuse it. Customers see only the end; who and why are staff-only (`BranchPause`, `GET …/pause`).
- **Alternatives:** editing the hours (permanent, owners only); bumping `version` (an owner's open settings would 409); a job that ends pauses (nothing to run: the end time is read); "until I resume" (a forgotten pause would stop tomorrow's orders).
- **Where:** `server/features/branches/{branches.schema,branches.repository,branches.service,branches.errors}.ts`, `server/features/orders/quote.rules.ts`, `server/features/identity/identity.permissions.ts`, `shared/contracts/{branches,orders}.ts`, `server/api/c/[slug]/counter/[branchId]/{pause.get,pause.post,resume.post}.ts`, `server/db/migrations/sqlite/0046_branch_pause.sql`; tests `server/features/branches/tests/branches.service.test.ts`, `server/features/orders/tests/{quote.rules,orders.service}.test.ts`, `server/features/identity/tests/identity.{permissions,service}.test.ts`.
- **Step 2 (2026-10-23):** the counter's **Manage** gets **Pause online orders…** (a dialog: 15 minutes, 30 minutes, 1 hour, until closing, an optional reason; off while the branch is closed) or **Resume online orders**; the header's badge reads **Paused**; the queue shows a banner with the end, who paused and why, and **Resume now** for managers and owners. The banner is `role="status"` (Nuxt UI's `UAlert` has no role, so a pause from another tablet wasn't announced). The pause is read from `GET …/pause` and refreshed with the board. The e2e seed gains **Zulu Pop-up** (open around the clock, last by name so the default branch stays Riverside) and its manager, so pausing never stops another test file's orders.
- **Step 3 (2026-10-23):** customers: the menu treats a pause like closed (adding off) and says **Not taking online orders right now** with **Back at …** on the branch's clock, the badge **Paused**; checkout shows the server's message. Owners: `BranchSettings` carries the `BranchPause`; the Branch page's status card reads **Paused until …** (who, why) with **Resume** (`POST …/admin/branches/<id>/resume`, `branch: ['update']`). Pausing stays a counter action. The feature is complete; the owner's Telegram alert on a pause is the next step when wanted.


### D200: Delete permanently only without history; Archive stays the bin; menu items first, 2026-10-24

- **Context:** records are archived by default (D43); architecture.md allowed a hard delete "only when nothing ever referenced the record" and said `DELETE` archives a record with history and deletes one without. Only draft promotions could be deleted (D163). The owner asked which data should be deletable for good, and about a page of deleted records restorable for 30 days before they're removed.
- **Decision (owner, 2026-10-24, the recommendations accepted, no mockup round):**
  - **The rule:** a business record is deleted for good only while it has **no history**: nothing that must stay explainable refers to it (an order line, a promotion, a ledger). With history it stays archived, restorable, forever. Orders and their lines, payments, ledgers, voucher redemptions and audit events are never deleted (unchanged).
  - **Two explicit actions, never one that guesses:** **Archive** (Undo, D152) and **Delete permanently** (always asks; it can't be undone). `DELETE` on a resource deletes it or answers 409; it never archives. This replaces architecture.md's "`DELETE` archives when it has history": the person pressing it couldn't know which would happen.
  - **No recycle bin page and no 30-day schedule for now.** Archive already is the bin: hidden, restorable any time, on each feature's Archived tab. A page across features would need one feature to read every other's tables (the feature boundary) or a generic endpoint that knows them all; a third `deleted` state would have to be excluded from every list, picker, count and guard (one miss shows deleted data); and only records without history could ever reach it. **If a mistaken delete shows the need:** a nullable `purge_at` on the archived row, a "Deletes on …" badge with Cancel deletion on the Archived tab, and a daily task calling the same delete (it re-checks the history). The delete below is that task's body, so nothing is rebuilt.
  - **Where it is offered:** in the feature's own list, in the ⋮ menu of an archived record (after Restore), and of a draft without history. The server says what blocks each one in the list; the client never decides. An archived record with history shows the action off with the reason, like "Restore (restore the parent first)".
  - **Menu items (this step):** a draft or archived item that **no order line names** and **no promotion of any status names**. `DELETE /api/c/<slug>/admin/menu/items/{itemId}` `{ version }`, `menu: ['write']` (owners): 204; 409 `INVALID_STATE` for a published item ("Archive this menu item before deleting it."), 409 `ITEM_HAS_HISTORY` with the reason, 409 `VERSION_CONFLICT`, 404. One batch: the delete with its own guards (version, status, `not exists` order line, `not exists` promotion link), `requireOneChange`, the image released (purged after 24 h), the audit row (`menu.item.delete`, name and status).
