Its option sets, versions with their sold-out switches, add-on groups and prices and availability links go by cascade. Summaries carry `deleteBlocker: 'ordered' | 'promotion' | null`.
  - **The order check belongs to orders:** `itemOnAnOrder(db, { tenantId, id })` (a correlated `exists` on `order_lines`, `order_lines_item_idx`) is exported by the orders feature, like `hasActiveOwner` (D194, rule 3). Menu now imports orders, which imports menu (the public menu for quotes): a module cycle like identity ↔ orders, safe because both only call each other inside functions.
- **Next, one PR each, in this order:** categories, availability rules, option sets and values, add-on groups (mostly `restrict` foreign keys already), then dining tables (`orders.table_id` has no foreign key, like items). **Deferred:** single add-ons (order lines keep them as JSON, so "was it ordered?" has no index); staff (removing access is their delete; the account may be a customer); branches (archived, never deleted, D47); customers (privacy erasure, Q24); a whole cafe (platform, retention).
- **Alternatives:** a page of all archived records with Delete (mostly disabled rows, and the boundary problem); a 30-day bin now (kept above as the later step); a `deleted` status on a separate page (every query must exclude it); `DELETE` that archives or deletes (the caller can't tell in advance); deleting sold items (lines keep names and prices, but reports by item, promotion results and the audit trail would point at nothing).
- **Edge cases and tests:** order lines name items without a foreign key, so the database wouldn't stop the delete: the check is inside the delete statement, and an order placed between the check and the batch is refused (race test). `menu_promotion_items` cascades from items, so a delete would silently change a promotion: refused for any promotion, including drafts (race test). The status guard is covered by the version (every status change moves it), so it is tested on the statement itself. The early checks are survivors: removing them leaves the in-batch guards giving the same answers. Two-cafe: another cafe's item isn't read or deleted with our id, and our cafe's line naming their item's id is not their history.
- **Where:** `server/features/menu/{items.service,items.repository,items.errors,index}.ts`, `server/features/orders/{orders.repository,index}.ts`, `server/api/c/[slug]/admin/menu/items/[itemId].delete.ts`, `shared/contracts/menu-items.ts`, `app/features/products/{composables/useItems.ts,components/ProductListPage.vue}`, `server/features/assistant/help/menu-items.md`; tests `server/features/menu/tests/{items.service,menu.repository.tenants}.test.ts`, `server/tests/batch.test.ts` (the fake D1 now reads a select list with subquery columns), `server/tests/support/db.ts` (`insertSoldLine`), `test/e2e/products.test.ts`.
- **Step 2, categories (2026-10-24):** an **archived** category is deleted for good only while it holds **no menu items and no sub-categories of any status** (delete those first; the `restrict` foreign keys say the same), **no order line was sold under it** (`order_lines.category_id`, the category as sold: an item moved away since still counts), and **no promotion of any status names it**. `DELETE /api/c/<slug>/admin/menu/categories/{categoryId}` `{ version }`: 204; 409 `INVALID_STATE` for an active one, `CATEGORY_HAS_HISTORY` with the reason, `VERSION_CONFLICT`, 404. `MenuCategory.deleteBlocker` is `items`, `subcategories`, `ordered` or `promotion`, the first that applies. Orders exports `categoryOnAnOrder`; a partial index `order_lines_category_idx` (`category_id` where not null; migration `0047_order_lines_category`, additive) keeps that check from scanning every line. A main category doesn't take its archived sub-categories with it: one rule, and each is shown and deleted on its own. Every guard sits in the delete statement and is tested on the statement itself, since items and sub-categories can't arrive under an archived category (only a promotion link can, tested as a race). Where: `server/features/menu/categories.{service,repository,errors}.ts`, `server/features/orders/{orders.schema,orders.repository,index}.ts`, `server/api/c/[slug]/admin/menu/categories/[categoryId].delete.ts`, `shared/contracts/menu-categories.ts`, `app/features/categories/{composables/useCategories.ts,components/CategoryListPage.vue}`, help `categories.md`; tests `categories.service.test.ts`, `menu.repository.tenants.test.ts`, `d1-search.test.ts`, `test/e2e/categories.test.ts`.
- **Step 3, availability rules (2026-10-24):** an **archived** rule is deleted for good only while **no item and no category of any status names it** (the `restrict` foreign keys say the same). Archived ones count: dropping an archived item's link would make it sell at any hour once restored, a silent change to what it was. Rules have no order history. `DELETE /api/c/<slug>/admin/menu/availability-rules/{ruleId}` `{ version }`: 204 (windows by cascade); 409 `INVALID_STATE` for an active rule, `AVAILABILITY_RULE_HAS_USES` with the reason, `VERSION_CONFLICT`, 404. `AvailabilityRule.deleteBlocker` is `items` or `categories`; the Availability page shows it under the disabled action, as the page already does for Archive. Where: `server/features/menu/availability.{service,repository,errors}.ts`, `server/api/c/[slug]/admin/menu/availability-rules/[ruleId].delete.ts`, `shared/contracts/menu-availability.ts`, `app/features/availability-rules/{composables/useAvailabilityRules.ts,components/AvailabilityRuleListPage.vue}`, help `availability.md`; tests `availability.service.test.ts`, `menu.repository.tenants.test.ts`, `test/e2e/availability-rules.test.ts`.
- **Step 4, option sets and values (2026-10-24):** an **archived set** is deleted for good, with its values, only while **no item of any status uses it and no item version uses any of its values** (retired versions count: an item that dropped the set keeps its old versions, which orders name). An **archived value of an active set** is deleted for good only while **no item version uses it**; it moves the set's version, like every value write (D58). Order lines name variations, not values, and a sold item can never be deleted, so its versions keep their values: the history holds. This revises D58's "values are never deleted": they still are, while anything uses them. `DELETE …/option-sets/{setId}` (204) and `DELETE …/option-sets/{setId}/values/{valueId}` (the set as it now is); 409 `INVALID_STATE`, `OPTION_IN_USE`, `VERSION_CONFLICT`, 404. A value of an archived set isn't deleted on its own: delete the set. The set delete is a guard statement first (still at the version, archived, unused), then its values, then the set, so a blocked delete is a clean 409, never a foreign-key error. `deleteBlocker: 'items'` on sets and values. UI: the card's ⋮ menu for a set; a bin button beside an archived value's Restore in the editor. No migration: `menu_variation_option_values_value_idx` serves the value check; `menu_item_option_sets` has no `set_id` index, but holds at most two rows per item, and "used by N items" already reads it so. Where: `server/features/menu/options.{service,repository,errors}.ts`, `server/api/c/[slug]/admin/menu/option-sets/{[setId].delete.ts,[setId]/values/[valueId].delete.ts}`, `shared/contracts/menu-options.ts`, `app/features/option-sets/{composables/useOptionSets.ts,components/OptionSetListPage.vue,components/OptionSetEditor.vue}`, help `options.md`; tests `options.service.test.ts`, `menu.repository.tenants.test.ts`, `test/e2e/option-sets.test.ts`.
- **Step 5, add-on groups and add-ons (2026-10-24; the owner delegated the remaining choices, the same day):** an **archived group** is deleted for good, with its add-ons, only while **no item of any status offers it and no item has its own price for one of its add-ons**. An **archived add-on of an active group** goes only while **no item has its own price for it**; it moves the group's version (D59). **Order lines don't count** (this replaces the deferral above): they keep each add-on as `{ id, name, priceDeltaMinor }`, and nothing reads that id back (reports, order history and Order again use the name and price; checking it would scan every line's JSON, with no index). A past order stays fully explained, so it isn't history in this rule's sense; an item id is, as Sales by item and Order again read it. `DELETE …/modifier-groups/{groupId}` (204) and `DELETE …/modifier-groups/{groupId}/modifiers/{modifierId}` (the group as it now is); 409 `INVALID_STATE`, `MODIFIER_IN_USE`, `VERSION_CONFLICT`, 404. The group delete is a guard statement, then its add-ons, then the group, as for option sets. `deleteBlocker: 'items'` on groups and add-ons. UI: the library card's ⋮ menu for a group; a bin button beside an archived add-on's Restore on the group page. No migration (`menu_item_modifier_groups_group_idx`; prices by add-on are a few rows per item). Where: `server/features/menu/modifiers.{service,repository,errors}.ts`, `server/api/c/[slug]/admin/menu/modifier-groups/{[groupId].delete.ts,[groupId]/modifiers/[modifierId].delete.ts}`, `shared/contracts/menu-modifiers.ts`, `app/features/modifier-groups/{composables/useModifierGroups.ts,components/ModifierGroupListPage.vue,components/ModifierGroupPage.vue}`, help `add-ons.md`; tests `modifiers.service.test.ts`, `menu.repository.tenants.test.ts`, `test/e2e/modifier-groups.test.ts`.
- **Step 6, dining tables (2026-10-24, delegated by the owner):** an **archived** table is deleted for good only while **no order (any status) was placed at it** (`orders.table_id`, without a foreign key; orders keep the label too, but the id is how history and reports find a table's orders). **Owners only:** `DELETE /api/c/<slug>/admin/branches/{branchId}/tables/{tableId}` (`branch: ['update']`); the counter has no such route and its Manage → Dining tables doesn't show the action, so managers (D198) archive and restore but never delete, like every other permanent delete. 204; 409 `INVALID_STATE`, `TABLE_HAS_ORDERS`, `VERSION_CONFLICT`, 404 (another branch's table too). Orders exports `tableOnAnOrder`; a partial index `orders_table_idx` (`table_id` where not null; migration `0048_orders_table`, additive) serves it. Branches now imports orders (orders already imports branches), a cycle like identity ↔ orders. `DiningTable.deleteBlocker: 'ordered'`. Where: `server/features/branches/branches.{service,repository,errors,index}.ts`, `server/features/orders/{orders.schema,orders.repository,index}.ts`, `server/api/c/[slug]/admin/branches/[branchId]/tables/[tableId].delete.ts`, `shared/contracts/branches.ts`, `app/features/branches/{composables/useBranches.ts,components/BranchTablesTab.vue}`, help `branch.md`; tests `branches.service.test.ts`, `test/e2e/branches.test.ts`, `test/e2e/manager-tools.test.ts`.
- **Complete (2026-10-24):** every business record the admin archives can now be deleted for good while it has no history: menu items, categories, availability rules, option sets and values, add-on groups and add-ons, dining tables (D200 steps 1–6, PRs #167–#172). Out of scope, unchanged: orders, payments, ledgers and audit (never deleted), branches (archived, D47), staff (removing access is their delete), customers (privacy erasure, Q24), a whole cafe (platform). A recycle bin with a 30-day schedule stays the recorded option above, unbuilt.
- **Fix: a delete's version goes in the query (2026-10-24):** on staging every permanent delete answered 400 (the owner tested them all). **Cause:** Nitro 2's Cloudflare handler forwards a request body only for POST, PUT and PATCH (`requestHasBody`, `/post|put|patch/i`), so a `DELETE`'s `{ version }` arrived empty and failed validation. Node (dev, e2e, the server tests) keeps the body, so nothing local showed it; the e2e mocks read the body the browser sent. The draft-promotion delete (D163) and removing the Bakong token (D148) had the same bug. **Fix:** every `DELETE` sends `?version=` (`deleteVersionQuerySchema` in `shared/contracts/common.ts`, read with `readValidQuery`), as `docs/reference/mutations.md` already showed; the routes' services are unchanged. **Guard:** `server/tests/routes.test.ts` fails on any `*.delete.ts` that reads a body (it failed on all 10 routes before the fix). The e2e mocks now record the query and expect no body. Alternatives: patching Nitro's Worker entry to forward DELETE bodies (a fork of framework behavior that the next upgrade can drop silently), or `POST …/delete` routes (a second convention beside the documented one).

### D201: Back closes the overlay on top, not the page (2026-10-24)

- **Context:** on a phone, people use the system's Back (Android's button or edge swipe, iOS's edge swipe) like in a native app: to close the sheet or form they opened. A modal, slide-over or drawer had no history entry, so Back left the page and the overlay vanished with it, unsaved input asked through the route guard. ui.md and `AppDrawer` already said drawers close "with Back", which was true only because the page went away. Nuxt UI 4.12 and Reka UI 2.11 don't handle Back (checked in the installed sources); browsers give a page nothing but a history back (Chrome's `CloseWatcher` has no Safari equivalent).
- **Decision (owner, 2026-10-24, the recommendation accepted, review waived):**
  - **Every open overlay has a history entry; Back closes the one on top** like Escape and stays on the page; the next Back leaves it. Unsaved input asks as on X (Keep editing keeps it open, and Back asks again); an overlay that can't be dismissed (a password shown once, a save running) stays; the confirm dialog takes Back as **Cancel**, as a phone's own dialogs do. Closing another way (X, Escape, Save) steps back over the entry, so no dead Back press is left behind.
  - **Kept out of the router:** the entry is a copy of the page's history state with our marker, so its position is the page's. Vue Router's own entries never share a position, so a `popstate` between equal positions is ours and the router never hears of it (a custom `history` in `app/router.options.ts`, Nuxt's extension point). Pushing route entries for overlays (`?sheet=1`) was rejected: every open and Back would run the route middleware, and the unsaved-changes guard would ask about a page form when only a clean modal over it closes.
  - **Route changes:** our entries are stepped back over before the router writes its own (a guard after the others, so after the unsaved-changes question), and overlays still open get new ones. Every history traversal, `router.back()` included, goes through one queue: two started together aren't reliable across browsers. An entry left behind (a reload with an overlay open, Forward after closing one) is stepped off.
  - **Wrappers, like `AppDrawer` (D126):** `<AppModal>` and `<AppSlideover>`; `UModal` and `USlideover` are lint errors elsewhere, all 41 files moved. `routed` for overlays the page keeps in its address (Menu items' `?item=`, D90; Order history's `?order=`): Back is already their route change. `@back` replaces the default (ConfirmDialog). `useOverlayBack(open, onBack)` for Nuxt UI's own overlays: the sidebar's phone menu (both layouts), the search palette, the assistant's sheet below `lg`.
  - **No custom swipe gestures, no drag-to-close:** the system's edge swipe is the gesture; a second one in JavaScript would fight it and horizontal scrolling. Sheets still don't drag (D126). Menus and popovers don't take Back.
- **Alternatives:** route entries per overlay (above); a synthetic Escape on Back (Reka's toast viewport is a dismissable layer too, so Back could close a toast instead of the modal); `CloseWatcher` (Chrome only, and both together would close twice); one wrapper per call site instead of a lint rule (drift).
- **Edges and limits:** opening an overlay drops the browser's Forward history, like any navigation (the unsaved-changes tests that pressed Forward or Back with a form modal open now go back past the page, as a long press on Back does). Chrome skips entries pushed without a recent tap ("history manipulation intervention"): an overlay opened on load (a linked `?item=` on the customer menu) or one kept open after Back may be skipped by a later Back, which then leaves the page with the old behavior (the unsaved-changes guard asks). The e2e suite runs Chromium only: iOS Safari's edge swipe is the same `popstate` but isn't covered by an automated test.
- **Where:** `app/utils/overlay-history.ts`, `app/router.options.ts`, `app/plugins/overlay-history.client.ts`, `app/composables/useOverlayBack.ts`, `app/components/{AppModal,AppSlideover,AppDrawer,ConfirmDialog,AdminSearch}.vue`, `app/layouts/{default,platform}.vue`, `app/features/assistant/components/AssistantPanel.vue`, `routed` in `ProductFormSlideover.vue` (from `ProductListPage.vue`) and `OrderDetailPanel.vue`, `eslint.config.mjs`; docs ui.md → Overlays, ui-helpers `<AppModal>`/`useOverlayBack`, forms.md edge cases 5/5b/14; help `general.md`.
- **Verified:** see [progress](progress.md#back-closes-the-overlay-on-top-d201-2026-10-24).

### D202: The counter and admin are installable apps; a manifest per cafe, no service worker yet (2026-10-24)

- **Context:** the owner asked for a PWA. The app is already one Nuxt web app; what a PWA usually adds is mostly built (wake lock, chime, stale warning, offline notice, refetch on reconnect, new-build banner: D22, D149, D150) or not wanted. What's missing is installing: the counter tablet runs in a browser tab all day, where it can be closed or swiped away and loses space to the address bar. The repo had no manifest, icons or service worker. Chrome lets a site without a service worker install from its menu since 108 (Android) and 112 (desktop); only its automatic install prompt still wants a fetch handler ([Chrome, "Revisiting Chrome's installability criteria"](https://developer.chrome.com/blog/update-install-criteria)).
- **Decision (owner, 2026-10-24, the recommendation accepted):**
  - **Step 1: a web app manifest per cafe and workspace, no service worker.** `GET /c/<slug>/counter.webmanifest` and `…/admin.webmanifest` (two static routes: no parameter to allowlist), built from the public cafe profile (`getCafeProfile`, D143) by a pure `webManifest(profile, workspace)` in the tenants feature; the routes set `application/manifest+json`, `no-cache`. The existing cafe-pages middleware answers unknown (404), paused (403) and former addresses (302, D142). Distinct `id`s (`/c/<slug>/counter`, `/c/<slug>/admin`), the cafe's name (`<Cafe> Counter`), `start_url` the workspace, **scope `/`** (Your cafes and the account pages live outside `/c/<slug>/`; **replaced by the workspace's own scope in D204**: with `/` one device could hold only one app from the site), `display: standalone`. `app.vue` links the manifest and the iOS meta on admin and counter paths only. Icons: a generic coffee cup (the `CafeLogo` fallback), committed PNGs rendered once from one SVG (no dependency). [Plan](plans/installable-app.md).
  - **Not built:** offline orders or payments (prices, versions, idempotency and the atomic batch stay the server's, D39–D45); caching `/api/**` or HTML (identity-bound data would outlive the session and cross cafes on a shared tablet); Web Push (live-updates plan; customers have Telegram, D157); a manifest for the customer site or the platform console.
  - **Step 2, a service worker, not scheduled:** only when slow loads on cafe Wi-Fi are a real complaint or Chrome's install prompt is wanted. Static build files only, updates through `NewVersionBanner` (D149).
  - **Rollout:** staging installs for testing; cafes install once the production domain exists (Q4), since an installed app belongs to its domain.
- **Libraries (checked 2026-10-24 on the npm registry and GitHub):** step 1 needs none: Nitro serves the JSON, `useHead` the tags. For step 2: `@vite-pwa/nuxt` (vite-pwa organization, the Vue ecosystem's module on nuxt.com) is the default, 1.1.1 (2026-02) on `vite-plugin-pwa` ^1.2 (1.3.0 accepts Vite 8; we run 8.3.4); Nuxt 4 works by user reports but isn't confirmed (vite-pwa/nuxt#222), and on 2026-10-19 a maintainer closed the Nuxt 4 PR saying the next version moves to `unplugin-pwa` (vite-pwa/nuxt#233; `@unplugin-pwa/core` is at 0.0.0). So wait for that rebuild. `@serwist/nuxt` 9.5.13 (2026-10, built on Nuxt kit 4) is the fallback if it fails with our Nuxt and Cloudflare build. Rejected: `@kevinmarrec/nuxt-pwa` (deprecated on npm), `@nuxtjs/pwa` (Nuxt 2, last release 2021), hand-written `workbox-build` (more of our own build code).
- **Alternatives:** one static `public/manifest.webmanifest` (can't open a cafe's own counter or carry its name; no hardcoded cafe name); scope `/c/<slug>/` (a cafe switch or an account page would leave the app or show a browser bar); `display: fullscreen` (hides the clock and battery); the cafe's logo as the icon (QW1: manifest icons are PNGs of declared sizes, logos are WebP up to 1600 px, square only since D185, and the Worker can't resize images; a later step making PNGs on save if wanted); adding a PWA module now (a dependency for a step we don't need, on a module about to be rebuilt).
- **Edges:** a cafe whose address changed keeps its installed app working through the 302 but under the old id until reinstalled; iOS keeps the name given at install; offline at start shows the browser's own page. Which page iOS's Add to Home Screen starts on (manifest or current page) is checked on a real iPad before rollout.
- **Verified:** step 1, see [progress](progress.md#installable-counter-and-admin-d202-2026-10-24).

### D203: "Install app" in the counter's and the admin's user menus (2026-10-24)

- **Context:** after D202 the counter and the admin install from the browser's menu, which staff don't know about (the owner installed it on Android and checked it, 2026-10-24). Chrome shows its own install prompt only to sites with a service worker's fetch handler, which we don't have (D202); Safari never prompts.
- **Decision (owner, 2026-10-24, the recommendation accepted, mockup review waived):** **Install app** (`i-lucide-monitor-smartphone`) in the counter's user menu and the admin's (above Log out), hidden inside the installed app (`display-mode: standalone`, Safari's `navigator.standalone`). It opens the browser's own install dialog when Chrome or Edge sent `beforeinstallprompt` (kept from page load by a client plugin, its default mini prompt held back); otherwise a modal, "Install <cafe> Counter/Admin", with three steps for the device (Android's ⋮ menu, Safari's Share → Add to Home Screen, a computer's address-bar icon) and a line that it still needs the internet. The device only picks the text (`installPlatform`), never a layout. `useInstallApp` is shared (the counter feature and the admin layout); nothing identity-bound is kept.
- **Alternatives:** a banner on the counter (always on screen in a workspace used all day; dismissing it needs stored state); a service worker only to get Chrome's prompt (D202's step 2, not justified by this); nothing, the help page only (staff rarely ask).
- **Edges:** an event can prompt once: after it, the steps show until the browser sends a new one; installing from the dialog fires `appinstalled`, which drops the kept event; iPadOS reports itself as a Mac, so a Mac with touch gets Safari's steps; the customer site and the platform console have no item (not installable, D202).
- **Verified:** see [progress](progress.md#installable-counter-and-admin-d202-2026-10-24).

### D204: Each installed app's scope is its own workspace; one platform icon for every cafe (2026-10-24)

- **Context:** the owner installed NUK Cafe's counter on Android, switched cafe and found no Install: Chrome said the app was already installed. Chrome offers Install only on a page outside every installed app's scope, and D202's scope `/` covered the whole site, so one device could hold one app from it. Worse than two cafes (switching cafes works inside one app): an owner who installed the counter couldn't install the admin on the same phone, and nothing in the counter links to the admin. Separately, QW1 (the app icon) was open.
- **Decision (owner, 2026-10-24, the recommendation accepted):**
  - **Scope = the workspace:** `/c/<slug>/counter` and `/c/<slug>/admin`, the same as `start_url` and `id`. A cafe's counter and admin, and other cafes', install as separate apps on one device; no scope contains another app's start page (`web-manifest.test.ts`, checked to fail with `/`). The counter's sign-in and the admin's login are inside their scope.
  - **QW1 answered: one platform icon for every cafe**, like other apps' single logo; the name ("<cafe> Counter") tells cafes apart. Today's coffee cup is generic: it's replaced by the platform's logo once QT1 names the platform, ideally before launch (four PNGs in `public/pwa/`, no code). No per-cafe icons.
- **Alternatives:** keep `/` and link the counter and the admin for owners (one app per device, but its name would be wrong in the other workspace, and renaming waits on QT1); scope `/c/<slug>/` (a cafe's counter and admin would still exclude each other).
- **Edges:** out-of-scope pages (Your cafes, account pages, View menu) open inside the app with Android's address bar and ✕; an app installed before this keeps scope `/` until it's uninstalled and installed again (Chrome keeps the scope from install).
- **Verified:** see [progress](progress.md#installable-counter-and-admin-d202-2026-10-24).

### D205: Patched Node 24 and exact pnpm from package.json (2026-10-25)

- **Context:** the owner requested a Node/pnpm security audit after the October 21 Node security releases, consistent local/CI/build requirements, minimal standard configuration and no production deployment. CI requested Node 24 without a minimum patch, while the root manifest had no Node requirement; pnpm was pinned at 12.3.4. Production uses Workers, not the build host's Node binary.
- **Decision:** `engines.node: ^24.22.1` and exact `packageManager: pnpm@12.15.0` are the source of truth. Node 24.22.1 contains the October security fixes and satisfies locked Nuxt/Nitro/Vite/Wrangler engines. pnpm stays on major 12 while gaining upstream security fixes, including registry-name validation and cumulative build-script/store hardening. Only the lockfile's bootstrap document changes; all application dependency resolutions stay unchanged.
- **CI:** preserve the pinned `pnpm/setup` action, which reads `packageManager`; each setup job passes `engines.node` read with `jq`. Preserve triggers, caches, heap settings and deploy behavior. For independent Workers Builds, document the conditional dashboard `NODE_VERSION` override; never confuse it with a runtime binding or change Workers compatibility dates/flags for a Node build upgrade.
- **Alternatives:** redundant `.nvmrc`/`.node-version`/Volta settings or a second runtime declaration would add another version source; a new major Node/pnpm or unrelated dependency upgrades would broaden compatibility risk. Corepack remains an optional local mechanism for selecting exact pnpm. The manifest does not install/switch a developer's Node, and pnpm 12's root-engine check was not reliable in isolated probes, so document version checks instead of adding an ineffective strict-engine switch or a custom guard.
- **Verification and limits:** [audit](reviews/toolchain-audit-2026-10-25.md), [progress](progress.md#secure-node-and-pnpm-toolchain-d205-2026-10-25). Existing audit suppressions and unrelated advisories remain explicit. No merge or deployment in this task.

### D206: A refetch shows a bar on the header's edge and dims the content, not a toolbar spinner (2026-10-25)

- **Context:** the owner found the refresh spinner bad UX (Sales by item, on a phone): with no room in the toolbar it wrapped onto a line of its own, pushing the page down and back on every keystroke in the search box; it sat far from the control that caused it; it flashed on fast responses; and the old numbers stayed at full strength, passing for the answer to the new filter.
- **Decision:** `<RefreshBar :active="refreshing">`, last in a `UDashboardPanel`'s header: a 2px indeterminate `UProgress` overlaying the header's bottom edge (no layout change), and the panel's body at 60% opacity (CSS `:has()` on the panel; still usable). `useRefreshIndicator` times it: nothing under 150ms, then shown at least 300ms, continuous across back-to-back requests. "Updating…" for screen readers (`role="status"`). Reports also read "Updating…" in place of "Updated 2:35 PM". Where no panel header is above the list (the branch's Tables tab), the composable drives the search box's own spinner and the list's dimming. An explicit Refresh button keeps its own `loading` (the control pressed shows it, UI rule).
- **Applied to:** the three reports, menu items, categories, add-on groups, option sets, availability rules, promotions, staff, the branch's Tables tab, and the platform's cafes, accounts and activity. Pages that filter on the client (counter Finished and Sold out, the customer menu) don't refetch and have none.
- **Alternatives:** the spinner inside the search box only (it doesn't cover period, branch, category, sort or page changes); a skeleton on every refetch (throws away rows the person is reading; the page jumps); keep the spinner and stop it wrapping (still unclear, still flashing, stale numbers still look final).
- **Verification:** unit `test/unit/refresh-indicator.test.ts` (no flash, minimum time, no blink between requests, scope cleanup). The e2e list tests keep checking that the refetch happens; the bar's look is checked by eye in light and dark, phone and desktop.

### D207: The Summary's sales chart uses Nuxt UI's UChart (2026-10-25)

- **Context:** D111 drew the Summary's bars as `div`s because a chart library (`@unovis/vue`) was a dependency for two bar charts. Nuxt UI 4.12 now ships `UChart`, themed with our tokens, with keyboard focus and a tooltip; its only addition is the optional peer `@tanstack/charts`. The owner asked to replace the hand-made chart with it.
- **Decision:**
  - `SalesChart.vue` renders `<UChart>` with our own TanStack Charts `definition`, not UChart's `data`/`index`/`categories` props: the bars are keyed by hour (`'07'`) or date (`'2026-09-30'`) and labelled through the x axis tick format. Day labels repeat across months ("Mon 1" is both 1 Feb and 1 Mar 2027), and a repeated label would merge two bars on the band scale; UChart's generated bar chart has no x tick format.
  - One series in `--ui-primary` (neutral in print, from the print tokens), bars at most 48px wide, dollar ticks without `.00`, `nice` y domain about 4 ticks (replaces our `niceScale`). Crowded day labels are thinned by TanStack's default collision thinning (replaces "every nth label").
  - The tooltip slot shows the hour or day, the paid sales and the order count (the old bars' `title`). The chart is named "Paid sales by hour/day"; the screen-reader table with the same numbers stays.
  - Print: the chart follows its container, and the SVG is `width="100%"` with a `viewBox`, so it redraws, or at worst scales, to the A4 width.
  - `@tanstack/charts` `^1.1.0` added as a dependency. Adding it made pnpm re-resolve a few floating peers in the lockfile (`srvx` 0.11.22 → 1.0.5 under `crossws`/`nitropack`, `magic-string` 1.4.2 → 1.4.3, `@oxc-project/types` 0.151 → 0.154); main's lockfile alone is stable, so the drift comes with any new dependency.
- **Alternatives:** UChart's `data` props with labels as the index (merges repeated day labels, no tick format); keep the `div` bars (the owner asked for UChart; no tooltip or keyboard focus).
- **Verification:** see [progress](progress.md#summary-sales-chart-on-uchart-d207-2026-10-25).

### D208: One address per page, without a trailing slash (2026-10-25)

- **Context:** `/c/nuk/` and `/c/nuk` both rendered the menu. Vue Router matches either form, so `route.path` kept the slash, while the app builds every address without one (`tenantUrl`) and compares paths as strings: on `/c/nuk/admin/` no sidebar item was active, a signed-in admin on `/c/nuk/admin/login/` stayed on the sign-in page (the counter's sign-in alike), and the menu's page cache (D122) rendered and stored each form separately.
- **Decision:** the form without the slash is the only one. `withoutTrailingSlash` (`shared/utils/address.ts`) drops the slashes and keeps the query and hash; `server/middleware/25.trailing-slash.ts` answers a GET or HEAD for the slashed form with a 301 to it, before the old and the cafe addresses (D141, D142) see the request; `app/middleware/00.trailing-slash.global.ts` does the same for a link inside the app, before the sign-in checks. Not the site's root, the API (`/api/**` answers as it is) or Nuxt's and Vite's files (`/_…`, `/@…`).
- **Security:** only paths made of non-empty segments of URL characters are redirected, so the target is always a path on this site: `//evil.example/`, `/\evil.example/` and whitespace are left alone (unit tests). One regex per request; no lookup.
- **Alternatives:** compare paths without the slash everywhere (every present and future comparison has to remember it); vue-router's `strict` option (a slashed address would be a 404 instead of the page); a `routeRules` redirect (matches patterns, can't strip the slash generically).
- **Verification:** see [progress](progress.md#one-address-per-page-without-a-trailing-slash-d208-2026-10-25).

### D209: Test accounts for every role on the dev server, one known password (2026-10-25)

- **Context:** `db:seed` gives one owner with a random temporary password that must be changed at first sign-in, again after every database reset. Managers, staff, customers and a second cafe were made by hand, and an account with a forgotten password or two-step sign-in was locked out (the reset link only appeared in the dev server's console).
- **Decision:** dev-only Nitro tasks behind pnpm scripts ([auth → Test accounts](reference/auth.md#test-accounts-local-dev)). `dev:accounts` creates or resets six `.test` accounts (super admin with no cafe access, NUK owner, manager, staff, customer, Second Cafe owner) with one password (`nuk-dev-password`, or `NUXT_DEV_ACCOUNTS_PASSWORD`); `scripts/dev-accounts.mjs` writes them to the git-ignored `dev-accounts.local.md`. `dev:reset-password` gives any account the password back, keeping its roles. `dev:mail` lists the newest account emails' links from the outbox. A reset clears a forced change, two-step sign-in and a ban, signs out, bumps the version and audits `dev.account.reset`, all in one batch per account, through identity's own statements (`dev-accounts.service.ts`).
- **Security:** the tasks refuse outside `import.meta.dev` and the services refuse without `dev: true` (tested; removing the guard fails the test). `/_nitro/tasks` exists only in dev. The super admin is a separate account without cafe access, so the D135 rule (super admins don't get cafe data) is what you see locally; the console's two-step requirement (D188) stays, so the super admin sets it up after each reset. `db:seed`'s first owner (also a super admin) is unchanged.
- **Alternatives:** extend `db:seed` (it also runs in disposable deployed environments; a known password there is a hole); "Sign in as…" buttons on the login page (a new screen element, owner mockup first; deferred until the commands prove insufficient); a local mail catcher (another service to run; the outbox already holds the links).
- **Verification:** see [progress](progress.md#test-accounts-for-every-role-d209-2026-10-25).

### D210: The password rules as a live checklist under every new-password field (2026-10-25)

- **Context:** the owner found the password rules hard to follow when creating, resetting or changing a password. The change-password pages had a static hint ("At least 8 characters. Passwords found in known data breaches are refused."), sign-up and reset only "At least 8 characters", and a breached password on a change-password page came back as Better Auth's English message in the alert above the form.
- **Decision:** a shared [`<PasswordRequirements>`](reference/ui-helpers.md#passwordrequirements-and-generatepasswordbutton) under the new-password field of sign-up, reset password and the admin, counter and platform change-password pages, replacing the hints. It lists only the rules the server enforces, in words without jargon: "At least 8 characters" (`PASSWORD_MIN`, ticks while typing), "Repeat new password matches" with a repeat field, and last "Not a commonly used password" with its reason underneath ("Checked against passwords leaked from other websites, the first ones attackers try."). That line is checked while typing: the browser hashes the password and sends only the SHA-1's first 5 characters to `GET /api/password-ranges/{prefix}`, which asks Have I Been Pwned's range API (padded) and answers the matching suffixes; the browser compares the rest. A generated password is never looked up (random, so it can't be on the list; each lookup is about 2,000 suffixes), so pressing Generate costs no requests. The password never leaves the browser for this; the server checks again on save, and the line is red while the field holds a password it refused. A **Generate** button inside the new-password field fills it (and the repeat) with a random password of four groups of five letters and digits without look-alikes (about 116 bits) and shows it, so it can be saved. Nothing is red before a refusal: a short password stays the field's own error. On the change-password pages a breached password is now the New password field's error ("This password is too common. Choose another one.", now also on the account pages), focused, instead of the alert.
- **Policy unchanged:** no composition rules (a digit, an upper- and lower-case letter). They would be a new password policy (an owner decision), would lock existing passwords out at their next change, and NIST SP 800-63B advises length plus a breached-password check instead, which is what the server already does. If the owner wants them, they belong in `newPasswordSchema` and Better Auth's server-side check first, then the checklist.
- **Live check through our server:** the CSP allows the browser to talk only to our own origin (`connect-src 'self'`), and a call from our server keeps the person's address away from a third party; the route is public (sign-up has no session), read-only, rate limited like the public API and answered from the browser's cache for a day. Pages without `crypto.subtle` (a phone on `http://<LAN address>`) don't check live; the save still does.
- **Alternatives:** wording like "Not in a known data breach" or "Not a common or leaked password" (the owner found them unclear); a line that only resolves on save (the owner expected it to tick); calling Have I Been Pwned from the browser (a CSP exception and the person's address sent to a third party); sending the whole password to our server to check (needless: 5 hash characters are enough); a strength meter (it would call accepted passwords "weak" or a breached one "strong"); putting the list in the field's `help` slot (Nuxt UI hides help while the field has an error, so the list would vanish exactly when it's needed).
- **Verification:** see [progress](progress.md#password-rules-checklist-d210-2026-10-25).

---

### D211: Production at angder.app, staging at staging.angder.app, deployed by hand behind a reviewer (2026-10-26)

- **Context:** step 8.2 waited on the domains (Q4) and the Workers plan (Q24). The owner bought `angder.app` (active on Cloudflare) and Workers Paid (5 USD a month), which also ends staging's Error 1102 (10 ms CPU on Free).
- **Decision:**
  - Production `https://angder.app` (Worker `nuk-cafe-production`), staging `https://staging.angder.app`, both as Workers Custom Domains with `workers_dev` and preview URLs off, so each environment has one origin (Better Auth trusts only `NUXT_PUBLIC_SITE_URL`). The root domain because printed table QRs carry it: it is chosen once.
  - Production has its own D1 and R2 (Asia-Pacific), rate-limit namespaces (2001–2003), secrets and Telegram bot (`@angders_bot`; staging keeps `@nuk_cafe_bot`: a bot has one webhook). No Sample data and no AI variables (Q43).
  - `$env.production` with `--envName production`: c12 applies `$env.<name>` and `$<name>`, so `$production`'s headers apply without repeating them. **Follow-up (2026-10-26, PR CI):** wrong: without `--envName`, c12's env name is `NODE_ENV`, so every plain production build (the e2e suite) became the Worker and its server didn't start. Renamed `$env.prod` (`--envName prod`), with the security headers repeated as for staging.
  - Mail from `no-reply@mail.angder.app`: a sending subdomain keeps the main domain's reputation apart. The sender's name "Angder" follows the domain until the platform's name is decided (QT1).
  - Deploy: CI run by hand on `main` with **production** ticked → `check` + `e2e` → `verified-release` → `deploy-production`, waiting for the GitHub environment's required reviewer; records the D1 bookmark, migrates, deploys, smoke-checks. No by-hand deploy script for production.
- **Alternatives:** `app.angder.app` (keeps the root for a marketing site; longer QR addresses); staging on another domain (more isolation, a second domain to pay for and set up; Cloudflare Access covers most of it); a production deploy on every merge (no human gate before real data).
- **Consequences:** the owner creates the resources, secrets and GitHub environment; API tokens need the zone `angder.app`. Changing the production domain later breaks printed QRs. Alerts and RPO/RTO remain Q24.
- **Verification:** see [progress](progress.md#production-environment-and-domains-d211-2026-10-26).

### D212: Account email through Cloudflare Email Sending instead of Resend (2026-10-26)

- **Context:** the owner asked to use Cloudflare's own email (owner, 2026-10-26). Cloudflare Email Sending (public beta since 2026-04-16) sends to any recipient from a Worker binding once the sending domain is onboarded, on Workers Paid (which the account has, D211): 3,000 emails a month included, then 0.35 USD per 1,000. One vendor, account and secret fewer.
- **Decision:**
  - `cloudflareEmailSender` (`identity.mail.ts`) sends through the Worker's `EMAIL` binding, found in `globalThis.__env__` (Nitro sets it for requests and cron runs; NuxtHub reads its bindings the same way). Resend's sender, `NUXT_MAIL_RESEND_API_KEY` and the CI steps that re-sent it are removed.
  - Each environment's binding may send only from its own address (`allowed_sender_addresses`): production `no-reply@mail.angder.app` ("Angder"), staging `staging@mail.angder.app` ("Angder (staging)"), on the onboarded subdomain `mail.angder.app`. `NUXT_MAIL_FROM` is the bare address, `NUXT_MAIL_FROM_NAME` the display name.
  - A refusal throws with Cloudflare's code only: the binding's message can carry the recipient's address and the log prints a `cause`, so none is attached. Each send has a 10-second deadline (the binding takes no abort signal).
  - Staging sends to anyone now (Resend's test sender reached only one address): it holds test accounts only.
- **Alternatives:** keep Resend (an idempotency key, a mature product; another vendor and secret); Cloudflare's REST API or SMTP (needs an API token as a secret, where the binding needs none).
- **Consequences:** no idempotency key: a lost answer can send the same email twice (the same one-time link; accepted, as for Telegram). The product is in beta and new accounts start with a small daily quota. The owner onboards `mail.angder.app` once and deletes the old Resend secrets. Local dev still prints mail.
- **Verification:** see [progress](progress.md#production-environment-and-domains-d211-2026-10-26).

### D213: Account emails name the platform, from configuration (2026-10-26)

- **Context:** the verification and password-reset emails said "NUK Cafe" (a constant) to every cafe's customers, against the rule of no hardcoded cafe name; account emails are the platform's, not a cafe's (D138), and since D212 their sender is "Angder".
- **Decision:** the emails' subject, closing line and HTML use `NUXT_MAIL_APP_NAME` (`runtimeConfig.mail.appName`, default "Angder": the owner's choice, 2026-10-26), passed to `accountMailHandlers(send, { appName })`; the name is escaped in the HTML now that it is configurable. Only the emails: "NUK Platform" stays in the console, its tab title and the two-step sign-in label for now (owner).
- **Alternatives:** the cafe's own name (the outbox message has no tenant, D138, and one account spans cafes); the sender's display name (staging's says "(staging)"); renaming "NUK Platform" everywhere now (deferred by the owner; existing authenticator entries would keep the old label).
- **Consequences:** QT1 is partly answered; the console's name is a later step. Other hardcoded "NUK Cafe" text (Telegram replies, the assistant's prompt, the origin check's message) is outside this step.
- **Verification:** see [progress](progress.md#production-environment-and-domains-d211-2026-10-26).

### D214: Bot Fight Mode stays off on angder.app (2026-10-26)

- **Context:** the owner turned on Cloudflare's Bot Fight Mode for the zone. The production launch's smoke checks from GitHub Actions then got 403 on `/api/health` while browsers got `ok`. Bot Fight Mode challenges automated-looking requests across the whole zone and, on the Free zone plan, runs outside the rule engine: no skip, allow or bypass rule exempts a path.
- **Decision:** Bot Fight Mode off for `angder.app` (staging and production share the zone). Abuse is bounded by Cloudflare's always-on DDoS protection and the app's own controls: per-address rate limits on the public API (D121), Better Auth's sign-in limits, sessions, permissions and the origin check on writes.
- **Alternatives:** keep it on (every deploy's smoke check fails; Telegram's webhook calls and the platform health check, both from datacenter servers, would be refused); Super Bot Fight Mode on the Pro zone plan with a skip rule for `/api/health` and `/api/webhooks/telegram` (about 20–25 USD a month; reconsider if Security → Events shows real bot abuse).
- **Consequences:** documented in operations → Production so it isn't turned back on; a smoke check answered 403 while a browser gets `ok` points at zone security first.
- **Verification:** see [progress](progress.md#production-environment-and-domains-d211-2026-10-26).

### D215: Cloudflare improvements within the existing plan, domain security and log privacy first (2026-10-26)

- **Context:** the owner approved implementing improvements using Workers Paid and free services, on a new branch from main. Live reads showed DNSSEC disabled, TLS 1.0 allowed, HTTPS redirection off, Full mode and unredacted query metadata in both Workers; the operations guide had described stricter zone settings as already active.
- **Decision:** follow the [Cloudflare plan](plans/cloudflare-improvements.md), one step per branch/PR. Step 1 aligns the shared domain baseline and sets `observability.redact_query_string: true` for staging and production in `nuxt.config.ts`, preserving every existing resource, binding, header, cron and sampling setting. Infrastructure state is recorded separately from source validation and live ingestion evidence.
- **Alternatives:** add paid security/image/realtime subscriptions immediately (no measured need); rely on the app logger alone (it does not control automatic invocation metadata); change dashboard logging without source config (a later deploy can undo it).
- **Consequences:** no extra subscription or business behavior change; $5 remains a minimum subject to usage. DNSSEC chain publication may lag signing. Query redaction does not cover URL paths, arbitrary log text or old logs. D214, tenant isolation, authoritative payment checks and the existing release process remain in force.
- **Verification:** see [progress](progress.md#cloudflare-security-baseline-d215-2026-10-26).



### D216: Measure public menu performance with privacy boundaries and independent health checks (2026-10-26)

- **Context:** D215 merged in PR #188; main CI/staging succeeded. The owner authorized the next existing-plan step. Automatic Web Analytics was already installed, but CSP did not allow its script; the health workflow skipped without a URL. We need measurements before changing polling, images or SQL.
- **Decision:** reuse free automatic Web Analytics, allowing only its official script file/version path on public menu document responses (`/c/*`), with reporting to same-origin `/cdn-cgi/rum`. Other direct documents retain their script restriction. Token entry documents also use `no-store, no-transform`; referrers disclose only the origin (`strict-origin`). No custom collector, analytics dependency, new table or paid subscription. Record aggregate Worker/D1/R2/RUM observations with sampling and small-sample limits.
- **Live configuration and limits:** the Free site's additional-rule request was refused; narrow its existing rule to `/c/*`. API read-back alone is insufficient: synthetic token pages still contained an injected script before source deployment. Validate CSP/no-transform after merge. A loaded beacon survives SPA navigation, so document controls are not universal private-route exclusion. Never put secrets or personal data in paths/dimensions.
- **Independent monitor:** the existing five-minute GitHub workflow defaults to production health, can manually check staging, and is explicitly paused with `PLATFORM_HEALTH_ENABLED=false`. Require HTTP 200 plus JSON `status: ok`, bound the body/retries, and report duration without logging the URL/body. Existing GitHub notification preferences apply; Q24 still needs a responsible owner and verified failure receipt. Scheduling starts on main, not this branch.
- **Alternatives:** custom Web Vitals ingestion adds code/storage/cost and maintenance; enabling a broad external CSP origin or manually adding another beacon broadens collection or duplicates it; choosing SQL/index/polling changes from this tiny mixed sample invents a bottleneck. Paid monitoring and extra RUM rules exceed this step's subscription scope.
- **Verification:** [baseline and rollout limits](reviews/cloudflare-monitoring-baseline-2026-10-26.md), [progress](progress.md#cloudflare-monitoring-baseline-d216-2026-10-26), [operations](server/operations.md#browser-performance-and-usage-d216). One branch/PR; no automatic merge or production deployment. PR #189 CI confirmed the eleven HTTP header tests; its unrelated counter label assertion now waits for rendered visibility after the optimistic switch changes.


### D217: Verify account actions with free Turnstile before the existing auth handler (2026-10-27)

- **Context:** the owner authorized the next Cloudflare step after monitoring (PR #189 merged; main/staging green at `1611b28`). Rate limits bound requests but do not establish that an account/email action came from a browser challenge.
- **Decision:** protect email/password sign-in across all four apps, sign-up, reset-email requests and verification-email resends. Distinct actions (`sign_in`, `sign_up`, `password_reset`, `verify_email`) share one transport (`useAuthRequest`) and an on-demand AppModal; the SDK loads only on a protected submit. Cancel/Back leaves the form and sends nothing; failures can be retried with a fresh widget/token. No new screen layout or dependency.
- **Server:** extend Better Auth's existing before hook with canonical Siteverify before hashing or email. Require strict success, the endpoint's action and the exact deployment hostname; bound tokens to 2048 characters and upstream work to ten seconds. Missing configuration/upstream failures fail closed. Never log keys/tokens or cache successful verification. Native captcha options support one expected action per plugin; the shared hook keeps the four action contexts and bounded/safe error behavior without replacing handlers.
- **Environments/cost:** separate free managed widgets for staging and production, no pre-clearance. Private keys were written directly to each existing Worker's `NUXT_HUMAN_CHECK_SECRET`; only public sitekeys enter source. Widget allowlists include local development hosts as required by the setup workflow, but deployed backends accept only their own exact hostname. Local/e2e is off by default; no client flag bypasses the server. No new subscription or storage product.
- **Existing flows:** guest checkout, OAuth, two-step sign-in, reset-token redemption, health, Telegram and polling remain outside this challenge scope. Existing rate limits remain; fix the creation rule's prefix match so cancellation/Telegram order actions do not consume creation slots. Query/trailing-slash collection paths still count. Existing low per-IP auth/guest limits remain a shared-Wi-Fi constraint, not a claim of 50 simultaneous sign-ins.
- **Verification:** [plan](plans/cloudflare-improvements.md#step-3-account-bot-protection-d217), [progress](progress.md#cloudflare-account-bot-protection-d217-2026-10-27). Browser SDK fixtures do not prove real challenges; real token success and replay rejection on deployed staging remain required before rollout is called complete. Production retains its manual reviewer gate.

### D218: Cache public image bytes at the edge while keeping current request security (2026-10-27)

- **Context:** the owner authorized step 4 after PR #190 merged. Main `a486b7e` deployed successfully to staging. `/media/**` already advertised a one-year immutable browser cache, but NuxtHub 0.10.8's `blob.serve` calls the R2 driver's body read and metadata head on every origin image request. The app had no shared edge image cache.
- **Decision:** wrap the existing public media read with Workers Cache API. Keep NuxtHub/R2 and the existing key allowlist. Only canonical HTTPS GETs without queries, authorization or conditional/range headers qualify. Cache full successful, bounded JPEG/PNG/WebP representations only; reject private/uncacheable responses, Set-Cookie, Vary and Content-Range. Full origin plus the entire tenant-prefixed path isolates environments and cafes; session cookies never enter cache keys. A hit runs the normal route/middleware and reuses only content headers, so it cannot replay another request's id or an old security policy.
- **Freshness/lifecycle:** edge lifetime is one hour; the existing browser lifetime stays one year. Photo edits already produce new keys (D158). Removed public URLs may still be served from an edge copy for an hour, and existing browser copies cannot be revoked by deleting R2. No immediate-revocation promise. Sources remain behind their existing authorized API and never enter this cache.
- **Failure/operations:** cache reads fall back to the unchanged storage path; writes run through `event.waitUntil` and failures are logged without request data. `X-Media-Cache` distinguishes HIT, MISS (write scheduled, not guaranteed), and BYPASS. Out-of-scope requests retain their current behavior without this diagnostic. Storage errors are never put into the cache. Local Node has no Workers cache and bypasses it.
- **Alternatives:** a public R2 custom domain adds another public origin and security surface; broad Cache Everything rules can accidentally include sensitive routes; replacing pre-generated image variants with paid transformations adds cost and unrelated behavior. Workers Cache API provides the narrowly scoped capability without a new dependency or subscription. It is local to each data center, not tiered or an account capacity guarantee, and hits still invoke the Worker.
- **Verification:** [plan](plans/cloudflare-improvements.md#step-4-public-image-edge-delivery-d218), [progress](progress.md#cloudflare-public-image-delivery-d218-2026-10-27), [operations](server/operations.md#public-image-delivery-d218). One branch/PR; normal review and production release process.


### D219: Index active counter orders without caching their state (2026-10-27)

Cloudflare improvement step 5 uses an additive partial index `orders_active_queue_idx (tenant_id, branch_id, placed_at, id)` over awaiting-payment/preparing/ready orders. The counter's shared active predicate includes that literal status set and retains the strict unpaid deadline check. SQLite requires a query term it can prove implies the partial index condition; parameterizing the fixed status set loses this plan in the regression test. Tenant, branch and deadline stay bound; no user input enters literal SQL.

This is a narrow exception to preferring builder operators for every expressible condition: the literal term is needed to select this SQLite partial index. The schema's index condition and repository term must stay aligned. One fresh batch still returns queue, lines, payments and today's finished count; no menu/checkout/payment freshness or authorization changes. Migration `0049_orders_active_queue` keeps existing indexes and rows, adds storage and active-order write maintenance, and is compatible with the previous Worker. Reverting the application predicate needs no destructive rollback.

[Measurement](reviews/cloudflare-d1-queue-2026-10-27.md) records local workerd plans/rows read with and without ANALYZE; the old query is already cheaper with statistics, so this is not a production latency or capacity guarantee. [Progress](progress.md#cloudflare-d1-counter-queue-d219-2026-10-27) owns checks and rollout limits. No new paid service; one PR, no automatic merge.


### D220: One setup path per environment; the db:seed task is retired (2026-10-27)

- **Context:** the owner asked how a fresh environment becomes ready to serve. Locally there were two overlapping paths: `db:seed` (one cafe, one owner with a temporary password from `NUXT_SEED_ADMIN_EMAIL`, who was also made a super admin) and `pnpm dev:accounts` (D209: every role, two cafes, a super admin without cafe access). `db:seed` mixed the platform and cafe roles on one account, against D135's separation. The staging runbook still made a cafe and its owner with hand-written SQL that skipped `tenant_slugs` (D142), the first branch and the audit, while the production runbook said the console does it. The super admin promotion matched on email alone, although sign-up doesn't require a confirmed email (D156).
- **Decision:** one order everywhere: infrastructure, one super admin by hand, then the app ([operations → Setting up an empty environment](server/operations.md#setting-up-an-empty-environment-d220)). Locally `pnpm dev:accounts` is the only setup; `server/tasks/db/seed.ts`, `seedSuperadmin`, `grantSuperadminStatement` and the `seed` runtime config (`NUXT_SEED_ADMIN_*`) are removed. `seedFirstOwner` stays as server test setup, no longer exported from the identity feature. Deployed environments: the first super admin signs up, confirms the email and is promoted with `… WHERE email = '…' AND email_verified = 1`, checked for one changed row; cafes and owners only through the platform console's New cafe. The `platform.superadmin.grant` audit label stays so earlier rows still display.
- **Alternatives:** a first-run setup page or bootstrap endpoint (Ghost/n8n style; a permanent public surface for a once-per-environment step; D49 already removed `NUXT_BOOTSTRAP_TOKEN`); a super admin from configuration (a secret that grants the platform on every deploy); keep `db:seed` beside `dev:accounts` (two local states to explain, one mixing roles).
- **Consequences:** a local database is set up with `pnpm dev:accounts` and Sample data; nobody needs `NUXT_SEED_ADMIN_EMAIL`. Existing staging and production data are untouched. Making further super admins stays a hand step with the same confirmed-email check.


### D221: Nothing guesses a cafe; the site's root is the platform's (2026-10-27)

- **Context:** with no cafe, `/` redirected to `/c/nuk` and failed. The default cafe (`NUXT_PUBLIC_DEFAULT_TENANT`, D140) and the oldest-cafe fallback (`currentTenant`) were left from before cafe addresses (D141) and, with several cafes, leaked across them: platform errors and job failures went to the oldest cafe's Telegram chats; every sign-up became a member of the oldest cafe; the account pages read NUK's `/shop/me`, making any signed-in visitor a NUK member; cafe API calls from a page without a cafe went to NUK's; a broken table QR, Your cafes and the account pages linked into NUK's menu. The owner chose: platform alerts in the logs and health check only until Q24; remove the pre-D141 redirects (production never had those addresses); the platform's page at `/` after a mockup.
- **Decision:** a cafe comes only from the address or a record that belongs to one. `currentTenant`, `findOldestTenant` and `defaultTenant` are removed; the dev/test `seedTenant` finds a cafe by its address. Server errors alert only the cafe the request resolved (`alertTenantOf`); job failures and outbox give-ups only log and record for System health (`queueJobFailureAlert` removed). No profile at sign-up; `ensureProfile` on first use per cafe. The account pages read Better Auth's session (`useAccountSession`); verification links carry `?redirect=`. In the app, `apiPath` throws for a cafe's route without a cafe, `useTenantSlug`/`useTenantPath` throw on a page without one, and shell code reads `useOptionalTenantSlug` (`null`). `/` is a route redirect to Your cafes (`app/pages/index.vue`); pre-D141 addresses are not found. Platform drafts use the `_platform` key. [Plan](plans/platform-root.md).
- **Alternatives:** keep a configurable default cafe (any value is a guess, and a hole once cafes multiply); send platform alerts to a named chat now (Q24 has no owner or verified receipt); build the landing page now (a new screen needs the owner's mockup first).
- **Consequences:** a fresh environment works without a cafe. Existing profiles and alerts already sent stay. A direct `/sign-in` with no cafe returns to `/`, then Your cafes. Job failures no longer reach Telegram, so the independent health check reports stuck work: production sets `NUXT_MAINTENANCE_NOTIFICATION_MAX_AGE_SECONDS=300` and `NUXT_MAINTENANCE_DELIVERY_MAX_AGE_SECONDS=900` as plain vars in `$env.prod` (follow-up, 2026-10-27, after production's Telegram secrets were set and generation succeeded); staging sets neither. Supersedes D140's fallback and D150's Telegram job alerts.
